# Eustress Financial Backbone

Status: build specification. The single authoritative design for how money enters Eustress, where it sits, how it reaches contributors, how it is taxed and how it is proven. Facts dated 2026-11-12. In production `PAYOUTS_MODE` is `off`, `SWEEP_MODE` is `off`, `ENROLLMENT_MODE` is `off`, and every switch that opens a money path which does not run today is off. The streams that sell today (Ticket packs, Delta packs, plans, treasury gifts, sponsorships) stay open; closing one is McKale's recorded choice (decisions D26 to D28).

Code it governs: `infrastructure/cloudflare/api` (the Worker), `infrastructure/relay` (the fixed-IP bank relay), `eustress/crates/web` (the site), and the earning inputs in `eustress/crates/engine` and `eustress/crates/bliss`.

Contents: 1 Purpose and scope. 2 The money map. 3 Revenue streams in. 4 The treasury sweep. 5 Contributor payouts from Mercury. 6 Taxes. 7 The fixed-IP relay. 8 Reconciliation, alerts and the admin view. 9 Investments. 10 Ticket cash-out reserve. 11 Build plan: retiring Stripe Connect. 12 Data model. 13 Routes. 14 Secrets and configuration. 15 Failure modes. 16 Build plan: build order. 17 What McKale does himself. 18 Questions for the accountant. 19 Questions for counsel. 20 Assumptions resting on unverified research. 21 Open questions with owners. 22 Review notes.

Sections 1 to 10, 12 to 15 and 17 to 21 describe the system. Sections 11 and 16 are the build plan: they describe the transition, and they leave this document for the archive when the build completes.

---

## 1. Purpose and scope

### 1.1 What this system does

1. Money comes in through Stripe Checkout: Ticket packs, Delta packs, plan invoices, treasury gifts, engine sponsorships. Stripe pays out daily and automatically to Mercury Engine Expenses. No code creates, schedules or alters a Stripe payout.
2. After each Stripe payout is proven to have landed, a sweep moves the contributors' share from Engine Expenses to Bliss Treasury.
3. Each night the settlement shares a drip of the funded pool among contributors by score. An eligible contributor's share is a payable in their name. Nobody is paid at night and no bank is called.
4. Pay runs send what is payable from Bliss Treasury: US payees by ACH every week, international payees by wire once a month with the wire fee deducted from the payout.
5. One identity check exists: Eustress's own KYC. No identity document or identity fact goes to Stripe, Mercury or any other provider for a second check.
6. Taxes are part of the system: a tax-forms page (W-9, W-8BEN, W-8BEN-E), a write-only vault for tax identification numbers that the Worker never sees in the clear, backup withholding where the IRS requires it, year totals per payee, and year-end information returns (1099 series, 1042-S where required).
7. Every cent is in a named bucket, every movement has an idempotency key and a posted journal entry, and every provider row (Stripe and Mercury) maps to exactly one cash posting or to Suspense. Reconciliation is arithmetic.
8. Treasury investments and the Ticket cash-out reserve have their plumbing built and their offers closed behind switches that only counsel's written sign-off opens.

Out of scope: any change to the BLS emission, the score weights or the 50 percent split; any Stripe payout schedule other than daily automatic; any second identity provider.

### 1.2 Hard constraints on the build

- Agents never write to live Stripe or Mercury, never handle a key or token, never deploy to production. McKale sets every secret and performs every live write.
- Money code is tested on fakes. Every money test has a recorded mutation that makes it fail (`tests/money_mutations.json`), or a line in the grandfather list of 16.1, which only shrinks and is empty by the end of build step 9. The suite passes under `npm run test:clock-shift`.
- Security reviews before any deploy.
- KV is never a source of record for money or for authority over money: one write per second per key, no compare-and-set, 1,000 operations per invocation. Anything that needs atomicity lives in a Durable Object.
- Documents carry no em dashes and read as if always so.

### 1.3 Design rules

A reviewer rejects any change that breaks one of these.

1. **Only landed money is dripped.** A sale's pool share sits in `transit_cents` until its Stripe payout is matched to a Mercury deposit and the sweep to Bliss Treasury has landed. The drip base is `max(0, cents - pending_reversal_cents)` and nothing else.
2. **No inbound message moves money.** A Stripe webhook, a Mercury webhook and a relay answer only schedule a read. The decision is taken on what the provider returns to a read the Worker makes itself. This holds for deposits, for plan changes and for reversals: goods are credited only after the Worker has read the paid session or invoice from Stripe, and a plan's state comes from a subscription read, never from an event body.
3. **Debit before send, an attempt committed before the call, one constant key, read before any second send, gate at the moment of sending.** A payment's cents leave `owed_cents` for `reserved_cents` in the same write that records the intent, and the intent is copied outside the Treasury before the bank is called. Before every relay call the Treasury commits, in a put of its own, the attempt count, the attempt time and the state `sending`, and that put is a check-and-set: the payment is read again in the same turn, after the awaited gate, and no call is issued unless it is still `reserved` with no park requested (5.6). A payment found in `sending` with no recorded answer is unknown. The idempotency key is derived from the payment id and never changes. `payeeGate(send)` runs again immediately before every relay call. An unanswered send is resolved by reading the bank: the transaction list and the send-money request list. A second send happens only after a read found nothing, with the same key, and only while the first attempt that ended unknown is younger than `RESEND_MAX_AGE_HOURS`. No operation parks, voids or fails a payment that has an attempt until a confirming read taken after that attempt shows nothing at the bank, and none does so at all once an attempt has ended unknown: that payment leaves `reserved` only by a transaction or a request found by read, or by review.
4. **One payment per payee per period.** A payment is found through the index `payidx:{rail}:{payee}:{period}`, and its id is `HMAC(pay id key, rail | payee | period)`, so the id is fixed by its period and tells a reader nothing. A second payment to the same person in the same ACH period (the week, the day or the month that `ACH_CADENCE` names) or in the same wire month has no id to exist under. A released hold joins the next run as a line.
5. **Stopping is instant, starting is slow.** Any finance administrator, any tripwire and McKale at four separate layers halt payouts at once. Lifting a halt needs a fresh administrator key signature, waits out the administrator delay, is announced to every staff address and on the relay's out-of-band channel, and can be cancelled. A halt stops what releases money and never what adds cover or what lifts it: a top-up of Eustress's own cash into Bliss Treasury, the acts that explain a Suspense item and the clock of the unhalt itself keep running (8.6). Stop-only acts are counted, and a burst of them is itself a tripwire.
6. **No one names a destination.** Not an administrator, not a request body. The relay takes account ids from its own configuration and the recipient from a record the payee created through Mercury's hosted page and confirmed with a key signature. Each payment is bound to a fingerprint of the bank details the payee enrolled.
7. **Secrets that move money never sit in the Worker.** The Worker holds a Mercury read token and a request-signing key. Mercury write tokens exist only on the relay. Tax identification numbers are encrypted in the payee's browser to a public key whose private half never exists on any server. The credentials that deploy the Worker and the relay are bank credentials and are handled as such (section 15.4).
8. **Unknown means stop.** An unreadable freeze counts as frozen. An unknown balance-transaction type goes to Suspense and blocks its sweep. A stale or incomplete sanctions list blocks sends. A response in an unexpected shape, a timeout, a transport error once the request was issued, a relay answer without a valid signature, a 400 that is not provably a schema error and Mercury's 24 hour duplicate refusal are all "outcome unknown": never a success, never a recipient fault, never a release. Once any attempt on a payment has ended unknown, only a confirmed transaction moves it forward and only review takes it off the automatic path: no park, gate failure or window close returns its cents.
9. **Caps live outside the thing they cap.** Per-payment, per-day and per-run limits are enforced twice: in the Treasury from the book, and in the relay from its own durable ledger and Mercury's own lists, under one lock. Raising a cap is a configuration deploy by McKale. No admin route raises a cap, names a destination, reads a TIN or moves money between bank accounts. The Treasury plans inside every rule the relay enforces, so the relay is the backstop and never the scheduler: a relay refusal on a timing rule is a wait, never a fault on a destination.
10. **Every cent has a named bucket and every movement a posting.** Each Treasury operation moves cents between named buckets and writes its balanced journal entry in the same `storage.put`. Every mapped bucket equals its journal account. The Cashbook observes providers and never posts; it submits facts to the Treasury under idempotent keys.
11. **One provider row, one cash posting.** A bank account in the journal moves only when a posted Mercury transaction is applied, under the key `mt:{txn}`. The Stripe balance moves once per balance transaction, from a Stripe object the Worker has read: whichever of the object's key and the row's key arrives first posts account 1010, and the other is a reclass. A payout moves it from the payout's own balance transaction, never from the compose. A later match is a reclass of the same entry, never a second cash posting.
12. **Authority over money comes from deploy configuration and keys.** Finance administrators are a fixed list of account ids and key fingerprints in configuration. Ban state, account type, plan, destination and freeze live in the per-account Durable Object. A KV record grants nothing.
13. **A page that holds a key or a TIN runs on its own origin.** An identity file is created and read only on the payments origin (15.4). Sign-up and sign-in by identity file, email change, passkey add and remove, key replacement, the tax step, the destination steps and the administrator finance page are all served there, from a minimal bundle under an enforced content security policy and Trusted Types. Pages there show some text that others wrote (a counterparty name on a Suspense item, a legal name in the sanctions queue, a payee's case text, the accounts of a destination conflict), and they render it only as text: a text node, never parsed markup. The main site holds a bearer session obtained by a hand-off that carries no key, and its bundle contains no code that parses an identity file or signs with one. The hand-off limits what honest code holds; what stops injected code is policy, so the main site enforces `script-src` and `connect-src` of its own (15.4). Every defence that rests on a key signature or on encryption in the browser assumes that page is honest, so its integrity is a gate, and its bundle hash and the policy headers both origins are served with are watched from outside.
14. **Every relay answer is signed.** The Worker signs its requests and the relay signs its answers with a key of its own. An answer without a valid signature is unknown. The transport proves nothing by itself.
15. **A delayed act is judged when it takes effect.** Its preconditions, its bank facts and its signer's authority are evaluated again in the write that applies it. What was true when it was queued decides nothing.

### 1.4 Launch gates

Gates are conditions on switches, not build tasks. The build proceeds on fakes and staging while they are open.

| Gate | Condition | Blocks | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | Written yes from Mercury (api@mercury.com) for the exact flow. Mercury's API terms list "building out marketplaces to facilitate payments" as an unacceptable use without advance approval | `ENROLLMENT_MODE` above `off` in production (enrollment creates Mercury recipients by API), `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue`, `PAYOUTS_MODE=live` | McKale |
| G2 | Counsel's written memo covering: money transmission (FinCEN and the states), with the list of states and countries where payouts may open; whether a paid plan as the condition for a pool share is a security, a profit-sharing interest or a business opportunity (section 19, items 26 and 27); whether an accrued payable may wait on a current plan (decision D3); and approval of the contributor terms wording. The memo cites the signed tax rules file of G3 | `ENROLLMENT_MODE=live` (terms wording), `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue`, `PAYOUTS_MODE=live` | McKale with counsel |
| G3 | Accountant's signed `tax_rules.{year}.json`: character and source of each income line, thresholds, floor logic. The file cites counsel's memo of G2; the two are closed together, on one stated legal relationship behind the value line (section 18, item 26) | `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue` | McKale with accountant |
| G4 | A second Mercury user with send-money permission, who creates the wire request token so McKale is the approver | First international wire | McKale |
| G5 | An IRIS Transmitter Control Code in hand. IRIS is the IRS's only e-file system for information returns from January 1, 2027, and a code takes up to 45 days to issue. Eustress's first information returns are for the first tax year in which it pays a contributor: no contributor was paid in 2026, so they are due in early 2028 for tax year 2027, unless payouts begin and a return is owed sooner. November 19, 2026 is the last filing day of the older FIRE system, which Eustress has never used, so that date binds nothing here | First year-end filing | McKale: apply well before the first filing season; not urgent this week |
| G6 | Security review of the relay, the tax vault, the payout routes and each deploy; the payments origin serving its enforced content security policy and the main site serving its enforced `script-src` and `connect-src`, each proven by the policy test of 15.4 and by the deploy tripwire's hourly read of the live header; and a main bundle that holds no identity-file code, proven by the bundle test of 15.4 | Every deploy; `ENROLLMENT_MODE` above `off` | Security session |
| G7 | End-to-end proof in production under `ENROLLMENT_MODE=staff`: McKale's own tax envelope is exported and opened offline; one ACH to his own enrolled account settles and reconciles; one deliberately returned ACH is handled | `ENROLLMENT_MODE=live`; raising caps above the starting values | McKale |
| G8 | Restore drill passed on staging (section 15.3), including the loss of the Treasury one hour after a run and its loss after an anchor that followed the last run | `PAYOUTS_MODE=live` | Orchestrator session; McKale deploys |
| G9 | The accountant's answer on sales tax and VAT for each product (section 18, item 16) | Reopening paid checkout in any place McKale has closed under decision D26. Paid checkout stays open wherever it sells today until he closes it | McKale with accountant |
| G10 | Counsel's answer on the gift stream (section 19, item 28) and Stripe's confirmation that it fits its restricted-business rules | Turning `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE` back on once McKale has turned it off under decision D27. The gift checkout is open today and stays open until he closes it | McKale with counsel |
| G11 | McKale's answer on the self-declared Full-node bonus (decision D12) | `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue` | McKale |
| G12 | Counsel's written answer on Tickets as closed-loop stored value, including the earned-Ticket ceiling (section 19, item 4) | Reopening Ticket checkout in any place McKale has closed under decision D28; raising `TICKET_WALLET_MAX_USD_CENTS`; `TICKET_CASHOUT_LIVE` | McKale with counsel |
| G13 | Counsel's written answer on whether a personal estimate may be shown before the program opens, and to whom (section 19, items 2 and 26) | `SHADOW_VISIBLE` | McKale with counsel |

While G1 to G3 are open, `PAYOUTS_MODE=shadow` is available: shares are computed as estimates into the administrator view, nothing posts to a payable account, and no debt to a named person exists before there is a lawful rail to pay it on. Computing needs no gate. Showing an estimate to an account is a separate switch, `SHADOW_VISIBLE`, behind gate G13: a personal dollar figure beside a plan requirement is an earnings representation, and counsel answers that before anyone sees one (5.1).

One locked decision cannot be met in full on Mercury's API as documented on 2026-10-28. Mercury's direct-send endpoint accepts `ach`, `check`, `domesticWire` and `realTimePayment` only. International wires exist only on `request-send-money`, which waits in the Mercury dashboard for an approver who is not the user that created the API token. US ACH and the sweep are fully automatic. International wires are automatic up to the queue, and one approval sitting a month releases them. Decision D1 (section 17.1) records McKale's acceptance or his alternative. If Mercury grants direct international send, `WIRE_MODE` is `direct` and nothing else differs.

## 2. The money map

### 2.1 Places where cash sits

| Code | Place | Holds | Who moves money out |
|---|---|---|---|
| STRIPE | Stripe balance, account `acct_1THu8jRgsC7hEeKM` | Receipts for about 2 business days | Stripe: refunds, disputes, fees, the daily automatic payout |
| EE | Mercury Engine Expenses, savings, ****0474 (Stripe `ba_1Ub4NGRgsC7hEeKMQIBkW3qR`) | Every Stripe payout (both halves of every sale, net of fees, refunds and disputes) until the sweep; then Eustress's own money | The sweep (relay); McKale's own automations to Checking; Stripe debits for negative payouts |
| CHK | Mercury Checking | Operating money | McKale and his vendors, and the IRS deposits he makes. The system only reads it |
| BT | Mercury Bliss Treasury, ****8817 | The funded pool, accrued payables, held and blocked payables, payments not yet debited, tax withheld, wire fees owed to Mercury, investment principal (gated), Ticket reserve (gated), Eustress's carry | The relay (ACH, wire requests); McKale, only for an act planned in the book first (an investor repayment, a carry return, a refund of a Suspense credit to its sender, or the move to Checking of a Suspense credit that cannot be returned) |
| IRS | EFTPS or IRS Direct Pay | n/a | McKale deposits withheld tax himself, from Checking |

The system reads Checking as a third Mercury account. Decision D13 confirms which account id it is. The relay has no route out of Bliss Treasury except an ACH or a wire request to an enrolled recipient, and no route into Bliss Treasury except from Engine Expenses.

Bliss Treasury receives no debit pulled by an outside party. Tax deposits are paid from Checking, so McKale asks Mercury for a debit block or filter on Bliss Treasury and for a receive-only account number for inbound wires (section 21, Q25). An outside debit on Bliss Treasury is its own alert (`external_debit`) and carries the dispute deadline in its text.

### 2.2 The book and the mirror

| Object | Instances | Job |
|---|---|---|
| `Treasury` (binding `TREASURY`, instance `treasury`) | one | Decides who is owed what. Holds the buckets, the posted journal, deposits, reversals, accruals, lots, payments, holds, year totals, sweeps, investments, the administrator act log, the halt flag. The only writer of the journal and of anything that changes a contributor's claim |
| `Cashbook` (binding `CASHBOOK`, instance `cashbook`) | one | Records what Stripe and Mercury report: balance transactions, payouts, bank transactions, expense rows, reconciliation results, unit meters. Never decides, never sends, never posts |

The link between them is one protocol. The Cashbook stores each provider row with `applied: null`. It submits the row's facts to a Treasury operation under an idempotent key (the table in 2.6). The Treasury posts once per key and answers `{je_seq, replay}`. The Cashbook stamps the row `applied: {op, key, je_seq, at}`. Every tick a replay sweep resubmits every row older than 5 minutes that is still unapplied. **Unapplied** has one meaning everywhere in this document: a row that is posted at its provider and whose `applied` field is null. A pending row is not unapplied; it is tracked. A bug in ingestion cannot move a cent, and a bug in the book shows as a wall difference at the next quiescent read.

Three rules govern bank rows:

- **Posted rows only.** A Mercury transaction is submitted only when it carries `postedAt`. A pending transaction (a card authorization, an ACH not yet debited) is tracked in the Cashbook and posts nothing. A pending row never counts as unapplied and never makes a wall indeterminate. A pending debit on any of the three accounts is still compared with the book the moment it is seen: on Bliss Treasury a pending outflow with no record behind it halts (R3), and on Engine Expenses or Checking one that nothing explains raises `unmatched_outflow` (R5), in both cases while it can still be cancelled at Mercury.
- **One row, one cash posting.** `apply_bank_row` is the only operation that changes the cash of a bank account in the journal: the balance of 1100, the balance of 1110, and the sum of 1120 and 1121. Operations inside the book move amounts between 1120 and 1121 (a claim released into carry, carry used to fund a claim) and never change their sum, which is the only figure the bank can see. It is keyed `mt:{txn}`. In the same write it classifies the row: a matched row posts against its counter-account and performs its business effect (a payout confirmed, a payment debited, a fee cleared), recorded under that effect's own marker key; an unmatched row posts against the Suspense account of its bank account. A later match is a reclass keyed `mtc:{txn}` (Suspense against the counter-account) with the business effect in the same write. The cash line is never posted again. A transfer between two of Eustress's own Mercury accounts is two rows and two postings, each against account 1130, like the two legs of a sweep.
- **A posted row that changes.** Mercury transactions change after first sight (amount, status). A change of amount writes a reversing entry of the first posting and the corrected posting, keyed `mt:{txn}:{resourceVersion}`, with `relates_to` naming the first. A change of status alone is no such correction. A payment debit that turns `failed`, `reversed` or `cancelled` is a return: its one posting is `payret` (Dr 1120 for the amount returned; Cr 2012), keyed `mt:{txn}:{resourceVersion}` and applied by the rule of 5.6 after the shape wait, and `paydebit` stays as it was, so withholding already taken stays withheld.
- **The Stripe side has the same rule.** Account 1010 moves once per Stripe balance transaction. A charge can reach the book from the webhook's read (`dep:{ref}`) and from payout ingest (`sbt:{txn}`); the index `sbtx:{txn}` records which arrived first and posted 1010, and the other posts the reclass `sbtc:{txn}` between Suspense 2900 and the sale's own lines (3.2, 2.6). A payout moves 1010 from its own balance transaction, read when the payout is created (4.3).

### 2.3 Buckets in the Treasury

All integer cents in the book state record. Each bucket maps to one journal account (2.5) or is a memo counter, and every mapped bucket equals its account balance at all times (checked by `verify_step` and R13). "Cash is in" says where the money behind the bucket physically sits.

| Bucket | Account | Meaning | Cash is in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `transit_cents` (signed) | 2001 | Pool share booked at sale, not yet funded. Reversals post here too, so it can run below zero until later shares net against it | STRIPE or EE |
| `sweep_deficit_cents` | memo | The part of composed reversals that no funded cents could cover. It is inside `transit_cents` as a negative amount and nets against the next positive sweep parts | n/a |
| `cents` | 2000 | The funded pool. Never negative. Less `pending_reversal_cents`, the only drip base | BT |
| `pending_reversal_cents` | memo, derived | The sum of `treasury_cents` over `rev:` records with `funded: true` and `released: false`: the pool part of reversals taken against deposits whose share is in `cents`, from the moment the share and the reversal coexist until the reversal ends (3.3). Kept as a counter and recomputed from the records by R13. Never negative | n/a |
| `unaccrued_cents` | 2003 | Pool money that a settlement did not accrue (shares that failed the gate, rounding), and every amount that returns to the pool because of one payee's standing (an adjustment, a hold returned to the pool, a netted clawback). Outside the drip base; folded into `cents` once a month (5.1) | BT |
| `recoverable_cents` | 2002 | Pool money paid to a payee and later reversed, recoverable by netting. Outside the drip base and outside `claims_bt`. Always equal to `clawback_receivable_cents` | gone |
| `daypool_cents` | 2009 | One score date's pool between reserve and accrual. Zero outside a settlement | BT |
| `owed_cents` | 2010 | Accrued to named payees, maturing or payable. Equals the sum of `payee:{id}` rows | BT |
| `held_cents` (`review_cents` is a part) | 2012 | Owed, stopped for a reason | BT |
| `blocked_cents` | 2015 | Blocked property after a confirmed sanctions match. Never paid, returned to the pool, netted or forfeited. It leaves only by `unblock`, with a recorded OFAC licence, delisting notice or counsel reference (5.5) | BT |
| `blocked_outside_cents` | 2016 | Money from outside the pool (an investor's funds, an outside credit on Bliss Treasury) blocked after a confirmed sanctions match. It is never pool money, and it leaves only by `unblock` (5.5) | BT |
| `reserved_cents` | 2030 | Gross of payments built whose bank debit has not posted (reserved, awaiting approval, submitted). Withholding and the wire fee stay inside it until the debit posts | BT |
| `watch_cents` | memo | Net of payments debited at the bank and inside their return watch window | gone |
| `paid_cents` | memo | Net settled to payees, lifetime | gone |
| `return_loss_cents` | memo | Sent minus returned on returned payments, lifetime | gone |
| `withheld_backup_cents` | 2040 | Backup withholding on posted payments, not yet deposited (Form 945) | BT |
| `withheld_nra_cents` | 2041 | Nonresident withholding on posted payments, not yet deposited (Form 1042). Dormant | BT |
| `withheld_remitted_cents` | memo | Deposited with the IRS, lifetime | gone |
| `wire_fee_clearing_cents` | 2050 | Fee deducted on posted wires, not yet matched to Mercury's charge | BT |
| `wire_fee_paid_cents` | memo | Charged by Mercury and matched, lifetime | gone |
| `invest_principal_cents` | 2100 | Investor principal outstanding (gated) | BT |
| `reserve_transit_cents` | 2201 | Ticket reserve earmark booked, not yet swept (gated) | STRIPE or EE |
| `reserve_cents` | 2200 | Ticket reserve, funded (gated) | BT |
| `carry_cents` (signed) | 1121 | Eustress's own position in BT. Positive: cash Eustress owns there (pool money that reversals took back after a sweep, interest, a claim released when Eustress paid it from another account, a Stripe payout that landed here, the opening balance). Negative: a cost Eustress bore that Mercury debited from BT, or an unexplained debit charged to it. Netted into the next sweeps; a negative carry is topped up even under a halt (4.5) | BT |
| `carry_unlocked_cents` | memo | The part of a positive carry that an administrator classified. A sweep does not use it until the month that holds it is locked. Carry posted by a Stripe payout that landed on Bliss Treasury is bound to that payout (its id, its exact amount, its trace where the row carries one) and is never counted here: that payout's own sweep uses it at once (4.3) | BT |
| `carry_return_planned_cents` | memo | The sum of open carry return plans (`xo:` kind `carry_return`, 4.7), from the write that plans each until it is matched or expires. A sweep and `legacy_fund` use no carry inside it | BT |
| `suspense_bt_cents` (signed) | 2930 | Rows on BT not yet explained, in two parts that never net against each other: `suspense_bt_credit_cents` (unexplained credits; never part of the pool) and `suspense_bt_debit_cents` (unexplained debits; cash that is gone until it is explained). The account balance is credits less debits | BT; gone |
| `disputed_debit_cents` | 1210 | Unexplained debits on BT that were charged to carry and are under dispute at the bank, until recovered or written off; and the part of a stray payment that its payee's payable could not cover, until later earnings net it (8.6) | gone |
| `clawback_receivable_cents` | 1200 | Amounts payees owe back after a reversal reached a row already paid | n/a |
| `underwithheld_recoverable_cents` | 1220 | Tax that should have been withheld on cents paid before a late US-work report showed them US source; recovered from the payee's next payments (6.4) | n/a |
| `underwithheld_tax_cents` | 2047 | The same tax, owed by Eustress until a recovery moves it into 2041 or Eustress deposits it from Checking | CHK |
| `platform_cents`, `fees_cents`, `reversed_cents`, `hwm_cents` | memo | Eustress's side and the high-water mark, at actual fees | EE, CHK |

### 2.4 The invariant equations

**Claims on Bliss Treasury** (one definition, used by every check):

```
claims_bt = daypool_cents + cents + unaccrued_cents + owed_cents + held_cents + blocked_cents
          + reserved_cents + withheld_backup_cents + withheld_nra_cents
          + wire_fee_clearing_cents + invest_principal_cents + reserve_cents + blocked_outside_cents
```

Bliss Treasury's cash is two journal accounts: 1120 (cash behind claims) and 1121 (Eustress's carry, signed). Three statements prove the account; none of them compares a bank read with bucket state taken at a different instant.

**Book identity** (pure arithmetic on journal balances, evaluated inside the Treasury in one read; asserted after every operation in tests, by `verify_step`, and by R13):

```
bal(1120) = claims_bt + suspense_bt_cents          and          carry_cents = bal(1121)
```

Every term is a journal balance. `suspense_bt_cents` is `suspense_bt_credit_cents - suspense_bt_debit_cents`; both parts are kept and neither is ever negative. A failure is a bug in the book and halts at once.

**Bank wall** (per account, evaluated only at a quiescent point):

```
Mercury BT currentBalance  = bal(1120) + bal(1121) + early_bt
Mercury EE currentBalance  = bal(1100) + early_ee
Mercury CHK currentBalance = bal(1110) + early_chk
```

The book posts posted rows only. `early_x` is the signed sum of the tracked pending rows of that account whose class Mercury moves out of `currentBalance` before `postedAt` is visible. Which classes those are is a per-account setting, `WALL_PENDING_CLASSES`, empty until it is fixed from evidence: the first ACH of gate G7, the first supervised wire and the first card authorization on Checking. This term is what keeps a morning of thirty pending ACH payments from reading as a shortfall, whichever way Mercury treats them.

A quiescent read is: read the balance and the event cursor; list the account's posted transactions since the last wall by `postedStart` and `postedEnd`, and its pending transactions; read the balance and the cursor again. The result is **determinate** only when both balances are equal, both cursors are equal, and no row of that account is unapplied (posted and not yet applied, 2.2). Pending rows never make a wall indeterminate. Otherwise it is **indeterminate**: nothing is compared and nothing halts. A determinate difference that equals the sum of a set of tracked pending rows is **explained**: it is recorded with the classes involved, raises `wall_class_unset` so the setting is corrected, and is never a shortfall. A determinate, unexplained shortfall on BT on two consecutive polls halts payouts (`bt_shortfall`). `BANK_WALL_INDETERMINATE_MAX` (6) consecutive indeterminate polls halt too, because a wall that can never be read is itself a fault. A surplus raises an alert only. A posting that straddles a poll is indeterminate for that poll and determinate at the next.

`availableBalance` is the second witness: it equals the book's cash less every tracked pending debit, and a difference no tracked row explains raises `bt_available_mismatch`. Summing the posted transaction list proves only that ingestion is complete (every posted row has its `mt:` record); it is never a wall, because it compares the book with its own inputs.

**Coverage** (every determinate wall and before every run). An unexplained debit is missing cash, so it counts in full against cover and never as cover:

```
min(0, carry_cents) - suspense_bt_debit_cents >= -CARRY_DEFICIT_MAX_CENTS
```

which with the two statements above is the same as

```
Mercury BT currentBalance - early_bt - suspense_bt_credit_cents - max(0, carry_cents) >= claims_bt - CARRY_DEFICIT_MAX_CENTS
```

A negative carry is a small, bounded amount Eustress owes the claims side (a fee Mercury took from BT above what a payee was charged); the next sweep adds it to its transfer, and a top-up alone is sent even under a halt (4.5). An unexplained debit stays in the formula until an administrator act charges it to carry (8.6), where the same bound and the same top-up govern it. Unexplained credits and unexplained debits are never netted: 300.00 USD pulled from the account is 300.00 USD of missing cover, whatever else sits in Suspense. A test posts an unknown BT debit beside an unexplained credit of the same size and requires coverage and the run preflight to refuse; its mutation subtracts the signed Suspense balance. A run's preflight also requires `Mercury BT availableBalance >= the run's net total plus its wire fees`. Because `reserved_cents` counts every payment whose debit has not posted, wires waiting for approval never show as surplus and are never treated as free cover for the next run.

**Stripe wall** (daily and at each payout): every Stripe balance transaction has an `sbt:` record that is applied or in Suspense, and the journal balance of 1010 equals Stripe's available plus pending balance. Account 1010 is credited for a reversal only from the reversal's own balance transaction, at its actual amount, and for a payout only from the payout's own balance transaction, the moment that row is first read, so the wall is exact between any two rows whatever the state of a compose. A balance transaction created before the genesis instant never posts 1010 at all: that cash is inside the opening balance (12.1). Eustress holds one Stripe balance: the payments balance that the automatic payout drains. A balance of any other type (a prefunded reserve, a Treasury financial account) has no account in the book and raises `stripe_settings_drift` the day it appears.

**Conservation identity** (asserted in tests after every operation in every money test):

```
pool shares deposited (after true-ups) - pool parts reversed + pool parts restored
  = transit_cents + cents + unaccrued_cents + daypool_cents + owed_cents + held_cents + blocked_cents
  + reserved_cents + watch_cents + paid_cents + return_loss_cents
  + withheld_backup_cents + withheld_nra_cents + withheld_remitted_cents
  + wire_fee_clearing_cents + wire_fee_paid_cents
  + recoverable_cents - clawback_receivable_cents
```

The last two terms are always equal, so a clawback neither creates nor destroys pool money: it records that a paid amount is owed back, and each netting turns that much of it into pool money in Bliss Treasury again (through `unaccrued_cents`, like every return that depends on one payee). Investment principal, the reserve and each Suspense account have an identity of the same form. The withholding terms above are the whole of accounts 2040 and 2041, which hold tax withheld from pool payments and nothing else. Tax withheld on an investor return (section 9) never enters Bliss Treasury: it is a payable of its own on the operating side (2045 for backup withholding, 2046 for nonresident withholding), and the cash behind it stays in the account that paid the return until McKale deposits it.

**Transit identity** (account 2001 has open items, and this is their sum; asserted after every operation in tests and by R13):

```
transit_cents = pool shares of deposits no payout has stamped
              - pool parts of reversals no payout has stamped + restored parts no payout has stamped
              + sum over every payout that is not superseded of (open part - swept_cents)
              - sweep_deficit_cents + legacy_unfunded_cents
```

The open part of a payout is its running `pool_in - pool_out + pool_restored`, the figure `compose_payout` stores as `part_cents`. A deposit or a reversal is therefore counted in its payout's term from the write that stamps it, before the payout composes, and never in two terms. A true-up or reclass that changes the pool part of a stamped item changes its payout's accumulators in the same write. Deferred cents (4.8) are part of the open part until they land. A `failing` payout stays in the sum with its own part and its own `swept_cents`; only a `superseded` payout, whose accumulators have moved to its carrier, is left out. A deposit or a reversal booked before the genesis instant carries the stamp `payout_id: legacy` and is in no term but the last: the pool share of everything sold before the book opened is `legacy_unfunded_cents`, once, until `legacyfund` funds it (12.1).

The book identity compares journal balances with buckets written in the same put, and the bank sees only the sum of 1120 and 1121, so neither can tell a payout part that was funded twice from one that was never funded. This identity can: a part funded twice leaves `transit_cents` below the open items, and a part never funded leaves it above. The reserve has the same identity over 2201.

Both sides move together through a failure, write by write (4.3 S5). Payout A has a part of 8,000.00 USD, 5,000.00 landed and an installment of 3,000.00 in flight when its failure row arrives in payout B.

1. The failure row marks A `failing`. Nothing posts to 2001, and A's term is still 8,000.00 - 5,000.00. Neither side moves.
2. The installment lands: `transit_cents` falls by 3,000.00 and A's `swept_cents` rise to 8,000.00, so A's term is zero. Both sides fall by 3,000.00.
3. `fail_payout` posts the reversing part in one write (`sweepneg:{A}:{n}`, under A's next installment counter). With m = min(8,000.00, `cents`), `transit_cents` rises by m and `sweep_deficit_cents` by 8,000.00 - m; A's `swept_cents` go to zero, its accumulators move to B and A becomes `superseded`. On the right, A leaves the sum (its term was zero), B's open part gains A's 8,000.00, and the deficit takes off 8,000.00 - m: a rise of m, the same as the left.
4. B composes and is swept once, for its own contents and A's. Funded in all: 5,000.00 + 3,000.00 - 8,000.00 + 8,000.00.

Had the installment failed at step 2, A's `swept_cents` would have stayed at 5,000.00 and step 3 would read: the left rises by m = min(5,000.00, `cents`); on the right A's term of 3,000.00 leaves, B gains 8,000.00 and the deficit takes off 5,000.00 - m, again a rise of m.

**Further invariants**, each a test with a mutation: every journal entry balances and the trial balance sums to zero; every mapped bucket equals its account; `cents >= 0`; `pending_reversal_cents >= 0`; `sweep_deficit_cents >= 0`; `transit_cents + cents >= 0`; the coverage statement; `suspense_bt_credit_cents >= 0` and `suspense_bt_debit_cents >= 0`; `pending_reversal_cents` equals the sum over funded, unreleased `rev:` records; the transit identity; `owed_cents` equals the sum of payee rows, and per payee the unfolded accrual rows plus the lots' remaining cents equal `maturing_cents + payable_cents` while the holds' remaining cents equal `held_cents`; lifetime funded cents never exceed the lifetime positive pool and reserve parts of bank-confirmed payouts, each payout counted once, a `superseded` payout not at all and a `failing` payout at its own part until the reversing part has zeroed what was swept under it (the same words as 4.5); no posting that depends on one payee's standing credits account 2000, so outside the monthly fold the drip base moves only by the day's pool, by sweep landings and by reversals (5.1); at a month's lock, `daypool_cents` (2009) and account 6999 are zero as of the month's last day, and every posting dated in the month to account 1130 or to accounts 2900 to 2930 has its exit posted, at any date; a locked month never changes; every `dep:` and `rev:` record has its `jref:`; per payee, year, certificate on file at payment, line and character, the year total, its US-source part included, equals the sum of its payment lines by bank posting date.

Suspense is one account per place cash sits, so each wall and each identity reads its own. Account 1010 is signed: a negative Stripe balance is a negative balance of 1010 until Stripe's negative payout debits Engine Expenses, and no separate "due to Stripe" account exists. A cost credits the bank account it was paid from. `labor_share` is a report figure, never a posting. Whether 2000, 2001 and 2200 are liabilities or designated equity is the accountant's call; the book treats them as claims either way.

### 2.6 Postings and idempotency keys

Each row is one Treasury operation: one `storage.put` holding the journal entry `je:{seq}`, its `jref:{key}`, the account balances, the bucket changes and any sub-ledger rows. G is gross, T tax Stripe collected, F Stripe's fee, N = G - T - F, S = floor(N / 2), W withholding, X a transfer amount. "Row" in the key column means the cash line belongs to the bank row's own `mt:{txn}` posting and the named key is the marker of the business effect written in that same put.

**Stripe side**

| Movement | Key | Journal lines | Detected going wrong by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sale booked | `dep:{ref}` | Dr 1010 G-F; Dr 5000 F; Cr 40x0 G-T; Cr 2300 T; Dr 5200 S; Cr 2001 S | Stripe wall |
| Gift booked | `dep:{ref}` | Dr 1010 G-F; Dr 5020 F; Cr 4030 G; Dr 5200 G; Cr 2001 G | Stripe wall |
| Sponsorship booked | `dep:{ref}` | Dr 1010 G-F; Dr 5000 F; Cr 4040 G-T; Cr 2300 T | Stripe wall |
| Reserve earmark (gated) | inside `dep:{ref}` | Dr 5210 r; Cr 2201 r | Stripe wall |
| Fee true-up | `feetrue:{txn}` | Corrects 1010, 5000 or 5020, 5200 and 2001 by the difference, and re-bases any reversal already taken on the deposit | Payout tie-out |
| Refund, claim side | `rev:refund:{re_id}` | Dr 4900 R - t; Dr 2300 t; Cr 2410 R; Dr 2001 p; Cr 5200 p (p is the pool part, t the tax part of the amount refunded) | Aging of 2410; 2300 against Stripe's tax report |
| Dispute, claim side (only once the dispute lists a balance transaction) | `rev:dispute:{dp_id}` | Dr 4900 for the part within the sale's remaining gross; Dr 4910 for any part beyond it; Cr 2410 for the whole; Dr 2001 p; Cr 5200 p | Aging of 2410 |
| Dispute won | `rev:dispute-won:{dp_id}` | Mirror of the claim side. Releases the original's pending amount when it is funded and not yet released | Aging of 2410 |
| Refund failed | `rev:refund-failed:{re_id}` | Mirror of the refund's claim side. Releases the original's pending amount when it is funded and not yet released | Aging of 2410 |
| Reversal, cash side | `sbt:{txn}` | Dr 2410; Cr 1010 at the row's actual amount. In the same write the claim side is trued to that amount, up or down, by the re-base `feetrue` performs: 4900 or 4910, the tax part and the pool part move by the difference, so 2410 clears to zero for that reversal. A dispute fee on the row or beside it: Dr 5010; Cr 1010 | Stripe wall; aging of 2410 |
| Other Stripe row (fee, FX fee, tax on fees) | `sbt:{txn}` | Dr 5000; Cr 1010. Unknown type: Dr or Cr 2900 against 1010 | Stripe wall |
| Charge row that finds no deposit | `sbt:{txn}` | Dr 1010; Cr 2900 at the row's net. The index `sbtx:{txn}` records that the row posted the cash | Stripe wall; Suspense at close |
| Late match on the Stripe side | `sbtc:{txn}` | Dr or Cr 2900 against the lines the matched object posts (revenue, tax, fee, the split). Account 1010 is not posted again | Stripe wall; Suspense at close |
| Other receipt (a genuine payment the Worker did not sell, or a row type Stripe adds) | `sbtc:{txn}` (administrator act, classification `other_receipt`) | Dr 2900; Cr 4090 at the row's net, with no pool share. Its refund is the mirror: Dr 4090; Cr 2900 at the refund row's amount | Suspense at close |
| Payout created | `sbt:{txn}`, the payout's own balance transaction | Dr 1030 A; Cr 1010 A (A signed), when the row is first read | Stripe wall; aging of 1030 |
| Payout composed | `po:{po_id}` | none: the tie-out and `part_cents` | Tie-out |
| Payout failed or cancelled | `sbt:{failure_txn}` | Dr 1010 A; Cr 1030 A. The row and the event are one posting, and it is the exact mirror of the payout's own row whatever the state of the compose | Aging of 1030 |

A charge posts 1010 once. When the webhook's read books the sale first, `dep:{ref}` carries the cash line and the payout row finds the deposit through `sbtx:{txn}` and posts nothing to 1010. When the payout row arrives first (a webhook that failed for days), `sbt:{txn}` posts Dr 1010, Cr 2900, and the deposit, whenever it is booked, posts its revenue and split lines against 2900 under `sbtc:{txn}`. A sale the webhook could not attribute (no account, an unknown type, a session the Worker did not create) is the same Suspense posting, keyed by its balance transaction, or by its payment intent until that exists, so the payout row finds it too. A balance transaction created before the genesis instant posts 3100 in the place of 1010 on whichever line would have moved the Stripe balance, because the opening entry already holds that cash (12.1).

| Day pool reserved | `drip:{score_date}` | Dr 2000; Cr 2009 | Zero of 2009 |
| Accrual step | `acc:{score_date}:{step}` | Dr 2009; Cr 2010 (a line per payee); Cr 2012 for a suspended payee | Payee row sum |
| Day pool end | `dripend:{score_date}` | Dr 2009 remainder; Cr 2003 | Zero of 2009 |
| Month fold | `fold:{YYYY-MM}` | Dr 2003; Cr 2000, the whole balance, in the settlement of the month's first score date | Zero of 2003 after the fold |
| Hold set on payable cents | `holdset:{hold_id}` | Dr 2010; Cr 2012 | Payee row sum |
| Hold released, whole or part | `holdrel:{hold_id}:{n}` | Dr 2012; Cr 2010 | Payee row sum |
| Payment reserved | `pay:{pay_id}` | Dr 2010 G; Cr 2030 G | Conservation identity |
| Payment settled | `payset:{pay_id}` | none (memo move `watch_cents` to `paid_cents`) | Conservation identity |
| Failed before the debit | `payfail:{pay_id}` | Dr 2030 G; Cr 2012 G | Conservation identity |
| Void, rejected approval, parked at the send gate, or a relay wait | `payvoid:{pay_id}` | Dr 2030 G; Cr 2010 G (back to the lots; with a waiting reason after a `refused_wait`), or Cr 2012 or 2015 when the gate names a hold or a block | Conservation identity |
| Payable reduced (value reversal, correction) | `adj:{reversal_ref}:{purchase_id}` | Dr 2010 or 2012 payee; Cr 2003 | Payee row sum |
| Clawback arises (the row was already paid) | `claw:{reversal_ref}:{purchase_id}` | Dr 1200; Cr 2002 | Recoverable equals receivable |
| Clawback netted from a payable (when it arises, at each later accrual, and in the build write of each payment) | `clawnet:{claw_id}:{n}` | Dr 2010 payee; Cr 1200; Dr 2002; Cr 2003 | Recoverable equals receivable |
| Clawback written off | `clawoff:{claw_id}` (administrator act) | Dr 2002; Cr 1200 | Audit |
| Under-withheld tax found (a US-work report covering paid cents) | `uwh:{payee}:{seq}` | Dr 1220; Cr 2047 | R19 |
| Under-withheld tax recovered | inside `paydebit:{pay_id}` of the recovering payment | Dr 2047; Cr 1220 for the part recovered, which that payment credits to 2041 with its own withholding | R19 |
| Under-withheld tax borne by Eustress | `uwhoff:{payee}:{seq}` (administrator act, on or after the Form 1042 due date) | Dr 6080; Cr 1220. The deposit that clears 2047 is the tax deposit reclass, with no carry line | Audit |
| Payable blocked | `block:{payee}:{seq}` | Dr 2010 or 2012; Cr 2015 | Payee row sum |
| Payable unblocked | `unblock:{payee}:{seq}` (administrator act with an OFAC licence, delisting notice or counsel reference) | Dr 2015; Cr 2012, into a review hold | Audit |
| Outside money blocked after a confirmed sanctions match | `blockout:{ref}` (the delayed block act of 5.5) | A Suspense credit on BT: Dr 2930; Cr 2016. Investor principal: Dr 2100; Cr 2016. Its `unblock` is the mirror | Audit; `ofac_report_due` |
| Hold returned to the pool (banned account, case reference) | `rtp:{hold_id}` | Dr 2012; Cr 2003 | Audit |
| Equity proceeds accepted (gated) | `invaccept:{inv_id}` | Dr 2100; Cr 3200; Dr 1121; Cr 1120 | Coverage |
| Legacy pool figure funded from carry | `legacyfund:{id}` (administrator act) | Dr 2001; Cr 2000; Dr 1120; Cr 1121 | Book identity |
| Adjusting entry | `adjje:{id}` | A balanced entry among the accounts of the one allowlist: 2300, 2500, 2600, 3000, 3100, 4090, 4800, 5000 to 5020, 5300, 6000 to 6999. Never a bank, clearing, claim, receivable or Suspense account | Trial balance |
| Opening entry | `open:genesis` | The only posting for everything before the genesis instant (12.1). Dr 1010 at Stripe's available plus pending balance; Dr 1030 for each payout created and not yet landed; Dr 1100, 1110 and 1121 at the three Mercury balances; Cr 2001 at the legacy pool figure; the balance to 3100. Bliss Treasury's opening cash is carry | Bank walls; Stripe wall; transit identity |
| Administrator act recorded | `act:{seq}` | none; a hash-chained record in the Treasury | Audit |

A webhook event is no ledger of work done. The Cashbook records `evt:{source}:{event_id}` only after the work the event triggered has committed, and nothing reads that record to skip work: every repeat is absorbed by the object-keyed idempotency above.

The journal is hash-chained (`prev_hash`, `hash` over the entry's fields). `verify_step` checks the chain, the balance of each entry, the account balances from the last checkpoint `ckpt:{seq}`, the book identity and the bucket-to-account equalities in bounded steps of 500 entries, and writes a checkpoint when it reaches the head. The check that matters is made outside the Worker: each hour the Treasury sends the relay the digests of every entry from the last anchored head to the last backed-up head, and the relay recomputes the chain from the head it stored itself (section 7.7). A full replay from `open:genesis` runs offline against the backup and the relay's own head list at every month close (section 15.3).

---

## 3. Revenue streams in

### 3.1 The streams

Every stream enters through Stripe Checkout.

| Stream | Route | Deposit kind | Deposit ref | Split of gross less tax less Stripe's actual fee |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ticket pack | `POST /api/tickets/checkout` | `ticket_purchase` | `stripe:{session.id}` | 50 percent pool (floor), remainder Eustress |
| Delta pack | `POST /api/delta/checkout` | `delta_purchase` | `stripe:{session.id}` | 50 percent pool, remainder Eustress |
| Plan invoice (Player Plus, Creator, Professional, Bundle) | `POST /api/plans/checkout` (`/api/subscriptions/checkout` is an alias) | `plan_invoice` (`subscription` accepted on read) | `stripe-invoice:{invoice.id}` | 50 percent pool, remainder Eustress |
| Treasury gift, once or monthly | `POST /api/stripe/checkout`, open in production while `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE` is true (gate G10) | `treasury_fund` | `stripe:{session.id}`, renewals `stripe-invoice:{invoice.id}` | 100 percent of gross to the pool. Stripe's fee is an Eustress expense (5020) |
| Engine sponsorship | `POST /api/sponsor/checkout` | `engine_sponsor` | same refs | 0 percent pool. All net to Eustress |
| Treasury investment (gated) | none; bank transfer into BT | not a deposit | `inv:{mercury_txn}` | Principal bucket, never revenue (section 9) |

Worked example, a 9.99 USD Ticket pack on a US card: fee 0.59, net 9.40, pool 4.70, Eustress 4.70. A 25.00 USD gift: pool 25.00, Eustress bears the 1.03 fee. A 50.00 USD sponsorship: fee 1.75, Eustress 48.25.

`splitSale` and the `CHANNEL_FEES` table are the display estimate and the fallback in 3.2 step 5. They never decide a funded amount.

**The deposit record is validated per kind.** A sale keeps the identity `fee_cents + treasury_cents + platform_cents == gross_cents - tax_cents` with every share non-negative. A gift stores `treasury_cents == gross_cents`, `platform_cents: 0` and a separate `eustress_fee_cents` that posts to 5020; the validator checks the identity of the record's own kind and refuses a negative share in any kind. Month totals (`agg:`), `receiptsOf` and the administrator statement show a gift's fee on its own line, "processing fee borne by Eustress", never as a negative platform share.

### 3.2 Booking a sale at the webhook

On `checkout.session.completed`, `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` and `invoice.paid`, `handleStripeWebhook` (in `stripe_webhook.mjs`) does this in order:

1. Verify the signature, the 300 second window and the livemode guard.
2. **Read before crediting.** One Stripe read of the object the event names (`GET /v1/checkout/sessions/{id}` with `payment_intent.latest_charge.balance_transaction` expanded, or the invoice with its payments). The read, not the event body, supplies `payment_status`, the amounts, the currency, the metadata and the payment intent. A failed read answers 500 so Stripe redelivers, and the redelivery does the whole job again: nothing has marked the event as seen. A forged or replayed event credits nothing.
3. **Credit only what the Worker sold.** Three refusals, each acknowledged with 200, written as a Suspense item and raised as `suspense_item`; none is ever booked as a gift and none credits goods. (a) `metadata.type` is missing or unknown. (b) The Worker holds no creation record for the object. Every Checkout Session the Worker creates, of every kind and with or without an account, is written before the buyer sees it as `cs:{session id}` in the Treasury: the kind, the account or none, the pinned price or the product of a line built inline, the amount and the time. The first payment of a subscription adds its subscription id to that record and writes the index `csub:{subscription id}`. A session must match its `cs:` record, and an invoice must match through `csub:`. **The invoice path is self-sufficient.** Stripe does not order `invoice.paid` and `checkout.session.completed`, and an invoice names a subscription, never a session. So an invoice that finds no `csub:` is not refused for that: in the same handling the Worker reads the subscription's Checkout Session from Stripe (`GET /v1/checkout/sessions?subscription={id}`), matches it to its `cs:` record and writes `csub:` and the subscription id itself, whichever of the two events is handled first. When that read fails, or shows a session that is not yet complete, the answer is 500 and Stripe redelivers. Refusal (b) is left for what it names: a subscription with no session the Worker recorded. (c) The lines differ from what was sold. For a session: each price id is in the pinned set for that kind and the quantity is 1. The price id fixes the amount, so no total of the session is compared: Stripe can present a pinned price in the buyer's own currency, the session's totals are then in that currency, and such a buyer is credited like any other. A line built inline (a gift or a sponsorship of a chosen amount) has no pinned price, so its product, its unit amount and its currency must equal those in its record, read from the line as the Worker created it and never from the session's presentment totals. For an invoice: every line's price is in the pinned set of the recorded subscription's kind, or carries its recorded product, and the amount is whatever Stripe charged, because a plan changed in the billing portal bills a proration that equals no catalog price. A session made with the restricted key by anyone but the Worker therefore buys nothing, however genuine its signed event is. The quantity of goods comes from the catalog entry of the pinned price, never from session metadata; no code path reads a Ticket or Delta count from `metadata`.
   - **Objects older than the records.** Some sessions and subscriptions are older than the records: plans, gifts and sponsorships sold by earlier code, whose subscriptions renew for years. `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` is deploy configuration, the UTC time McKale records at the deploy that begins writing the records, in the shape of `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER`. A session or a subscription whose own `created`, as Stripe states it in the Worker's read, precedes that time has no record to match and is judged as the code that made it judged it: on its metadata type, and on a line that fits the catalog entry the metadata names (a pinned price, the catalog amount of a pack whose line was built inline, compared on that line's own unit amount and currency, or the gift or sponsorship product). Nobody can make Stripe stamp an earlier `created`, so a session made with a leaked key after that time still buys nothing. For sessions the exception ends by itself once the last of them has expired or settled; a subscription that predates the records renews under it for as long as it lives. While the variable is unset, records are written and none is required, `/health` says so, and `checkout_records_open` fires after a day: forgetting it costs an alert, never a refused renewal. Tests: a plan bought before the time renews after it, grants its plan and books its deposit (mutation: require a creation record for every invoice); a session created before the time and paid after it is credited once; a session created after the time with no record credits nothing.
4. Credit the buyer's goods (Tickets through `walletCredit`, Delta through `deltaCredit`, the plan through `plan_apply`), keyed by the Stripe object. The account comes from the creation record, and for an object older than the records from its `user_id` metadata. A kind that credits goods needs one: a Ticket, Delta or plan object that names no account credits nothing, books the money to Suspense and raises an alert. A gift or a sponsorship credits no goods and needs no account: it is booked from its record whether or not the payer was signed in.
5. Book the deposit from the balance transaction, in USD settlement cents, with Stripe's actual fee. Two cases need care:
   - The balance transaction is not ready (delayed payment methods), and the payment is in USD: book with the estimated fee and `fee_basis: "estimated"`. Payout ingest trues it up (posting `feetrue:{txn}`), and the true-up re-runs the cumulative reversal arithmetic of any refund already taken on that deposit and posts the difference in the same write, so a sale that was refunded in full nets the pool exactly zero.
   - The payment was presented in another currency and the balance transaction is not ready: write `unbooked:{ref}` and retry on the tick. Only the balance transaction states the USD amount. Goods are credited either way.
   - A sale booked to Suspense (step 3, or a goods kind that names no account) posts its cash once, keyed by its balance transaction, or by its payment intent until that exists. Payout ingest finds it through `sbtx:{txn}` or `pi:` and posts nothing to 1010 a second time (2.6). A test books Suspense at the webhook, applies the charge row at ingest and requires 1010 to equal Stripe; its mutation posts both.
6. Store on the deposit: `currency`, `settled_cents`, `tax_cents`, `fee_basis`, `balance_txn`, `payment_intent` (never null for a paid amount: for a subscription first payment it comes from the invoice's payment), `buyer_country`, `buyer_region` (from the session's customer details), `reserve_cents`.
7. The pool share goes to `transit_cents`, never to `cents`.
8. Only when all of that has committed, record `evt:stripe:{event.id}` in the Cashbook, as an observation for the duplicate counter. Idempotency rests on the object keys (`dep:{ref}`, the wallet credit ref, the plan read), never on the event record.

### 3.3 Reversals

A reversal has two legs, because Stripe's message that a refund or dispute exists and Stripe's withdrawal of the cash are different facts.

**Claim side, at the webhook, after the Worker's own read.** `rev:refund:{re_id}`, `rev:dispute:{dp_id}`, `rev:dispute-won:{dp_id}` and `rev:refund-failed:{re_id}` are keyed by the Stripe object, so one reversal maps to one balance transaction. Each debits revenue, takes the pool's part, and credits account 2410 (Stripe reversals pending). Nothing touches 1010 here. `refund.created`, `refund.updated`, `refund.failed` and `charge.refunded` are all triggers of the same read of the charge's refunds, and none of them is more than a trigger (rule 2 of 1.3): a refund announced by one of them, or by all, is booked once, under its refund id.

- The pool gives back its proportional part p, by cumulative floor arithmetic over the deposit's reversals, as far as `transit_cents + cents` holds. Earlier reversals are already inside `transit_cents`, so nothing else is subtracted and two refunds that arrive before either is paid out both get their full part. Any remainder is a shortfall borne by Eustress and recorded on the reversal. The pool never goes negative in total.
- Eustress's side of a refund is `refunded gross - t - p`, not its original net share. t is the tax part of the amount refunded: the deposit's `tax_cents` in proportion to the gross refunded, by the same cumulative floor arithmetic as the pool part, so a full refund returns the whole tax. The claim side debits 2300 for t and 4900 for the rest, the reversal stores `tax_cents`, and `rev:refund-failed` carries it back. Tax that Stripe has already reversed is therefore never left owing in 2300 and never charged to Eustress as lost revenue. A dispute takes no tax part until the accountant says the tax on a lost dispute is recoverable (section 18, item 16). Stripe keeps its fee on a refund; this rule books that loss on every refund.
- Every pool posting of a reversal goes to `transit_cents`. The reversal record carries two flags, `funded` and `released`, and `pending_reversal_cents` is by definition the sum of p over records with `funded: true` and `released: false`.
  - **Funding is judged at two events, not one, and per deposit.** A deposit's share is in `cents` when an installment has landed on its payout and the deposit has no entry in that payout's `deferred` list (4.8). At the reversal: `funded` is set when the deposit's share is already in `cents`. At every sweep landing: for each deposit whose own cents the installment covers (a deposit stamped with a covered payout and not deferred on it, or a deferred deposit whose released cents this installment carries), every reversal of that deposit that is neither funded nor released becomes `funded` in that landing's reversal pass (4.4), because the landing has just put the reversed share into `cents`, and no drip is computed until the pass has closed. A reversal of a deferred deposit therefore stays unfunded: its share never reached `cents`, the drip base is left alone, and its cents net inside `transit_cents` in the installment that carries the reversal's row (4.8). A refund booked after its deposit's payout was created and before that payout's sweep lands is therefore held out of the drip base from the moment the money arrives, and stays out until its own row has been netted.
  - **Release happens exactly once**, in the first of: the reversal pass of the landing, or of the negative part (`sweepneg`), that nets the reversal's own payout; `rev:refund-failed`; `rev:dispute-won` before the dispute's withdrawal row has been netted. Each path sets `released` and skips a record that is already released, so the mirror and the landing can arrive in either order and the counter can neither go below zero nor eat another reversal's hold.
  - Between funding and release the drip base is lower by p, so no contributor is ever paid from money a refund has already claimed. A reversal of a deposit whose share is still unswept nets against that share inside `transit_cents` and leaves the drip base alone until that share lands.
  - Tests, each with a mutation: a 500.00 USD gift whose payout is created Monday, refunded Tuesday morning and swept Tuesday at 9:05 AM drips nothing from that 500.00 USD on Tuesday or Wednesday night, and `transit_cents + cents` never goes below zero (mutation: decide `funded` only at the reversal). A refund of a swept sale that stays pending 45 days and then fails restores the drip base (mutation: leave the counter alone at the mirror). A dispute won while Stripe payouts are paused releases once, whichever of the mirror and the landing comes first (mutation: release at both). A 1,500.00 USD gift that is deferred for dispute cover and refunded on day 10 leaves `cents` and the drip base unchanged (mutation: take the negative part from `cents` while the deferred cents stay in transit).
- **A dispute is booked only when the dispute object lists a balance transaction**, which is when Stripe has withdrawn the funds. An inquiry or early warning (`warning_needs_response` and its relatives) writes a watch record `dw:{dp_id}`, raises nothing against the pool and is re-read daily until it closes or withdraws funds.
- **A reversal beyond the sale.** When a buyer is refunded and the card is charged back anyway, the cumulative cap leaves the second reversal a pool part of zero; the amount beyond the deposit's remaining gross posts to 4910 (reversal losses beyond the sale). The loss is in an account, never an unexplained difference on 1010.
- A lost dispute on a plan invoice ends the plan's `paid_through` at once.
- `dispute_won` checks the deposit exists and answers `deposit_not_found` cleanly.
- Goods follow money: the Delta lot and the Ticket lot the payment bought are debited first, and the Ticket reversal starts the chain in section 5.10.

**Cash side, from the reversal's own balance transaction.** The posting `sbt:{txn}` debits 2410 and credits 1010 at the row's actual amount, whenever that row is first read (at the webhook's read when it is ready, otherwise at payout ingest). **The claim side is trued to the row.** The claim side was booked at the amount known at the webhook: for a USD sale the refunded amount, and for a sale presented in another currency the refunded fraction of the deposit's own USD gross, since no balance transaction states the refund's USD amount until Stripe executes it. When the row is first read, the same write re-runs the reversal's cumulative arithmetic at the row's actual amount and posts the difference, up or down, to revenue, the tax part and the pool part, and to `pending_reversal_cents` when the record is funded and unreleased, exactly as `feetrue` re-bases a reversal. Account 2410 therefore clears to zero for every reversal whose cash has moved, and 4910 holds only what exceeds the sale's remaining gross. A 20.00 EUR pack settled at 21.60 USD whose refund waits three days for balance and executes at 21.10 USD leaves nothing in 2410; a test holds that, and its mutation leaves the claim side at the webhook amount. A dispute fee is booked from the same read to 5010, whether Stripe shows it as the fee of the adjustment row or as a separate fee row. A refund that stays pending for lack of balance has its claim side booked and no cash side, which is the truth; `rev:refund-failed:{re_id}` mirrors the claim side if it fails and releases its pending amount. A reversal whose cash side posted more than `REVERSAL_CLEARING_MAX_DAYS` ago and that no payout carries raises `clearing_stuck`, because its release depends on that payout. A reversal Stripe reports at payout ingest that the book lacks has its claim side applied then, from the API read, under the same keys. An unmatched reversal row is a Suspense item, never a dropped log line. An item in 2410 older than `REVERSAL_CLEARING_MAX_DAYS` (10) raises `clearing_stuck`.

### 3.4 Stripe client and key

- `stripe_client.mjs` is the only Stripe client: a pinned `Stripe-Version` (`STRIPE_API_VERSION`, a GA version), a 20 second timeout, an HTTP status check, and a required idempotency key on every POST. The module contains no function that creates a payout, writes balance settings, changes an external account, creates a transfer, or creates or updates a customer. A test asserts those paths are absent.
- `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` is a restricted key (`rk_live_`, `rk_test_`). Its permissions: Checkout Sessions write; Billing Portal Sessions write; read on Customers, Prices, Payment Intents, Charges, Invoices, Subscriptions, Disputes, Refunds, Balance, Balance Transactions, Payouts and Balance Settings, and on External Accounts where a restricted key offers it. It has no write on customers, payouts, external accounts, transfers, Connect accounts, Files or refunds. With no write on customers it cannot set a customer's credit balance, so a renewal is paid from credit only when Stripe's own proration of money already paid created that credit, and a holder of a leaked key cannot renew a plan for cents. `SWEEP_MODE=live` and `PAYOUTS_MODE=live` refuse to start under an unrestricted key. A Worker compromise therefore cannot redirect Stripe's payouts away from Engine Expenses. It can open a billing portal session for any customer, and a portal can let its user change the customer's email, so no check rests on a customer's current billing email (7.7). The key can create a Checkout Session, so a leaked key could try to buy goods cheaply with a session of its own making; rules (b) and (c) of 3.2 step 3 make such a session credit nothing.
- Preview-version reads (the v2 payout-method reads, `2026-10-28.preview`) live in one module, are shape-checked and feed alerts only. Nothing that moves money depends on a preview API.
- Each account has one stable Stripe Customer (`customer_id` on the plan record). Stripe Checkout creates it at the account's first paid session (`customer_creation: always`), the Worker stores the id from its own read of that session, and every later checkout passes it. The Worker never creates or edits a customer.
- The live webhook endpoint receives, beside the checkout and invoice events: `payout.created`, `payout.updated`, `payout.paid`, `payout.failed`, `payout.canceled`, `payout.reconciliation_completed`, `charge.dispute.created`, `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn`, `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`, `charge.dispute.closed`, `refund.created`, `refund.updated`, `refund.failed`, `charge.refunded`, `customer.subscription.updated`, `customer.subscription.deleted`, `invoice.payment_failed`, `checkout.session.async_payment_failed`, `account.external_account.updated`, `balance_settings.updated`. Adding an event is a live Stripe write: McKale's, made before the release that handles it (16.2, step 3).

### 3.5 The plan record

The plan gates cash-out (locked decision 8), so it lives where writes are atomic. `plan` is a record in the per-account `AccountAuth` object, written only by the operation `plan_apply`:

- **An event is only a trigger.** `plan_apply` takes its state from a read of the subscription (and its latest invoice) made at apply time. Nothing is ordered by, or copied from, a field of an inbound event, so a forged or future-dated event changes nothing the read does not confirm;
- rejects a subscription id that is not the record's, unless the read shows the record's subscription ended and the customer is the record's own; an empty record takes the subscription the read shows only when that subscription's `user_id` metadata names this account;
- derives the plan from the current price's `eustress_plan` metadata, never from metadata frozen at checkout;
- stores `paid_through`; `trialing` and a zero-amount invoice do not open accrual; `past_due` keeps it for 3 days past `paid_through`;
- a plan started by checkout with no period end lapses after 35 days unless an invoice confirms it;
- a lost dispute on a plan invoice ends `paid_through` at once;
- `settlePlanInvoice` checks `billing_reason` and the matching line item before granting: Tickets and Delta are granted for the invoice that starts a period (`subscription_create`, `subscription_cycle`) and never for a proration, which books its money and changes only the plan;
- keeps a short history `plan_history [{from, to, plan, paid_through}]`, so the settlement can ask whether the plan was paid through a given score date.

KV `plan:{id}` is a display mirror. It is also where a plan sold by earlier code lives, so each account's record is seeded from it: `AccountAuth` runs `plan_apply` at the first plan read of an account whose record is empty and whose KV plan names a subscription, a renewal included, and the backfill of 12.5 sweeps the rest. The seed reads the subscription from Stripe and copies nothing from the mirror. `POST /api/plans/portal` opens a Stripe billing portal session for the stored customer, so a plan can be changed or cancelled, and Stripe shows the billing name, address, card digits and invoices there. The plan gates cash-out and the portal shows personal data, so a bearer token alone opens none: the route is served on the payments origin and needs a signature by the account key over a one-time challenge (purpose `plan_portal`). Whether that step meets the rule that cancelling be as easy as subscribing is put to counsel (section 19, item 33). Plan and pricing pages carry no payout figure, pool balance or earnings example until counsel has answered the questions of gate G2, and no personal estimate at any time: a page that renders an estimate carries no plan link and no plan price (5.1). Until the release that turns payouts on, every page that mentions payouts says they are not open yet, with no date and no figure, and a copy test walks every page of the site for that rule (section 11, item 7).

### 3.6 Ticket limits, sales regions, location evidence and unit meters

- **Daily load cap.** The Wallet reserves at checkout creation: `load_reserve {session_id, usd_cents, expires_at}`. Ticket checkout refuses an account whose paid total plus open reservations for the day would pass 1,000.00 USD (`TICKET_DAY_MAX_USD_CENTS`). The Checkout Session is created with a 30 minute expiry; a reservation is released when its session expires or its delayed payment fails, and is converted when the session is paid. Open sessions therefore count. The reservation is the cap's own record. The creation record that 3.2 step 3 requires is the session's `cs:` record, written in the same request. This cap limits the pace of loading. It is not the closed-loop limit.
- **Wallet value limit.** The federal closed-loop exclusion (31 CFR 1010.100(ff)(4)(iii)(A)) is a ceiling on the value that can be associated with a wallet on any day, not on what is loaded that day. The Wallet object therefore refuses a Ticket checkout when the wallet's Ticket value (every Ticket it holds, whether bought, granted by a plan or earned, valued at the highest pack price per Ticket) plus open reservations plus the pack being bought would pass `TICKET_WALLET_MAX_USD_CENTS` (200000). Three daily loads of 1,000.00 USD cannot pass it; a test holds that, and its mutation tests the day's load only. A creator's wallet also grows by earned Tickets with no purchase at all: the Wallet raises `wallet_value_over_limit` when earned Tickets take a wallet past the limit and refuses that wallet any further purchase. What must happen to the sale that would take a creator's wallet over the limit (refuse the sale, or hold the excess outside the wallet) is counsel's answer under gate G12 (section 19, item 4); until counsel answers, no sale is refused by code.
- **Ticket checkout by place.** Whether Tickets are closed-loop stored value at all, when third-party creators list the goods and receive 70 percent of the Tickets, is counsel's question (FinCEN's definition and A.R.S. 6-1201). `sales_regions.json` carries a `sell_tickets` field per place for the answer, and the field is enforced: Ticket checkout creation refuses, with `tickets_not_open`, a buyer whose account country or request country (and US state, where the file lists states) has `sell_tickets` false. Every other paid checkout in that place is unaffected, and a place that is not `sell` at all is refused first with `region_not_open`. Closing a place stops sessions being created there; a session created before the close and paid inside its 30 minute life is still credited, because that sale was made and its money taken while the place was open. Ticket checkout is open wherever it sells today; decision D28 records whether McKale closes it anywhere while the question is open, and gate G12 reopens a place he closed. A test sets `sell_tickets` false for a place and requires a Ticket checkout from it to be refused while a plan checkout from it succeeds; its mutation ignores the field in checkout creation.
- **Sales regions.** `sales_regions.json` is dated configuration: per country, and per US state where it differs, `{sell, sell_tickets, collect, rate_bps}`. Checkout creation refuses a buyer whose account country or request country is not `sell` (`region_not_open`). `automatic_tax` is on for a session when McKale has created a Stripe Tax registration for the buyer's jurisdiction; the tax Stripe collects posts to 2300 and is outside the split, and a refund takes its tax part back out of 2300 (3.3). Two facts shape the file: a seller outside the EU or the UK owes VAT on digital sales to consumers there from the first sale, with no registration threshold; and Eustress is in Arizona, so Arizona nexus exists from the first Arizona sale. The file ships with every place that sells today marked `sell`, so deploying it changes nothing. Closing a place before the accountant has ruled is McKale's recorded choice under decision D26, and gate G9 reopens it.
- **Location evidence.** Every deposit stores the buyer's country and region. The Cashbook keeps `geo:{YYYY-MM}:{country}:{region}` totals (count, gross and tax collected). For every place where sales are open and collection is off, the admin view shows the accrued tax exposure (gross times the configured rate), so the cost of selling there untaxed is a visible number that comes out of Eustress's half.
- **Unit meters.** Each Wallet and DeltaAccount balance change emits a meter event through its outbox, idempotent by its ref, to the Cashbook: Tickets issued for money, granted by plans, spent, burned (the 30 percent), earned and outstanding, negative; Delta sold, granted by plans, granted as trials, used, expired, outstanding. The totals are Eustress's unredeemed prepaid balances. They feed the admin view and the profit test, and a daily check compares Tickets and Delta issued against the `tickets` and `delta` fields of the deposits.
- **Value filings.** Each value-score filing (`creditValueScore`) carries `buyer_country`, because the US share of a creator's value earnings cannot be rebuilt later.
