The hub stores `score_day` on its sale record when it files, with no expiry shorter than `VALUE_MATURITY_DAYS` plus 30, and the Treasury keeps the per-buyer rows `vs:` for the same period, so a reversal on day 100 finds the day it scored and can recompute the capped score (section 5.10).

### 3.7 Correctness rules that real dollars depend on

- Creator share uses integer math: `floor(price * 70 / 100)` at every site.
- `paid_balance` is clamped to `max(0, balance)` on credit and refund.
- `bliss:burned_minor` is derived from spend entries, never from an unguarded KV counter.
- Studio keeps a stable chunk id and hash across a restart, so a chunk credited before a crash is not credited again, and it returns the uncredited part of a chunk the witness capped (`capped: true`) to its bucket.
- `/api/payouts/rate` carries no dollars-per-BLS figure (`bls_to_usd_rate` and `rate_display` are absent), and its dollar figures are the rounded public figures of 5.1.
- The Full-node 1.1 bonus is self-declared. Decision D12 settles it before `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue` (gate G11).

---

## 4. The treasury sweep

### 4.1 Why it is computed, not a bank rule

The amount differs every day: half of each sale's net, all of each gift, none of each sponsorship, less the pool's part of each reversal. Only the book knows it. Stripe cannot split an automatic payout across two banks, and its automatic transfer rules target only Stripe-held accounts. The split happens at Mercury, driven by what each payout contained.

### 4.2 Calendar of one sale

| Day | Event | Book |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Buyer pays; the webhook reads the session | Goods credited. Deposit booked. Pool share in `transit_cents` |
| about 2 | Stripe creates the automatic payout; `payout.reconciliation_completed` arrives | The Cashbook reads the payout, and the payout's own balance transaction moves its amount from 1010 to 1030 at once. When reconciliation completes the Cashbook reads the rows and the Treasury composes |
| about 2 to 3 | The deposit posts on Engine Expenses | The row is applied and matched; the payout is `bank_confirmed` |
| same day | The sweep goes through the relay | When the credit posts on Bliss Treasury, `transit_cents` falls and `cents` rises |
| next night | The drip uses the money | |

### 4.3 Payout lifecycle

The Cashbook holds `sp:{po_id}` with lifecycle `created`, `paid_expected`, `composed`, `bank_confirmed`, `swept_except_deferred`, `swept`, `failing`, `failed`, `superseded`. The Treasury holds the small record `po:{po_id}` with the accumulators the sweep needs.

**S1. Ingest.** Any `payout.*` event only enqueues the payout id. The Cashbook reads `GET /v1/payouts/{id}`, submits the payout's own balance transaction at that first read (S2), and, once `reconciliation_status` is `completed`, pages `GET /v1/balance_transactions?payout={id}` with `data.source` expanded. Each row is stored as `sbt:{txn}`. An hourly poll lists recent payouts, so a missed webhook costs time, never data. Only automatic payouts are accepted: a payout with `automatic: false` raises an alert and is never swept. `payout.paid` means expected, not arrived, and moves nothing. Every Payout object names its destination: ingest requires `payout.destination` to equal `STRIPE_EE_BANK_ID`. A payout to any other destination raises `stripe_settings_drift` the same hour. It is composed like any payout, and it is packed into no sweep until its bank row has been matched where it landed (S4). Ingest takes payouts created at or after the genesis instant; one created before it belongs to the opening entry (12.1).

**S2. Apply each row.** The Cashbook submits each row to the Treasury operation `apply_stripe_row {po_id, txn, ...}`. The posting is idempotent by `sbt:{txn}` and the accumulation into the payout is idempotent by `pobt:{po_id}:{txn}`. By `reporting_category`:

- `charge`: finds the deposit (by `sbtx:{txn}`, then by payment intent), stamps it with the payout, runs the fee true-up when the fee differs, and adds the deposit's pool share and reserve share to `po.pool_in` and `po.reserve_in`. The deposit's own booking posted 1010, so the row posts no cash. A charge with no deposit posts Dr 1010, Cr 2900 under `sbt:{txn}`, records `sbtx:{txn}` and is never guessed into a kind. It is a row of a known type, so it does not hold the payout: its net counts in `po.net_sum` and it contributes nothing to the part. When its deposit is booked later (a redelivered webhook, or `suspense/classify`), the deposit posts its lines against 2900 under `sbtc:{txn}`, joins the payout and changes its accumulators in that write (S3). A charge whose deposit carries the stamp `legacy` posts nothing and adds nothing to `po.pool_in`: its share is in `legacy_unfunded_cents` (12.1).
- `refund`, `dispute`: finds the reversal's claim side (or applies it at that moment from the read), posts its cash side (3.3), stamps the reversal with the payout, and adds its pool part to `po.pool_out`. A reversal row that finds no deposit is Suspense of a known type and holds nothing either.
- `dispute_reversal`, `refund_failure`: the same in mirror; adds the restored part to `po.pool_restored`.
- `payout_reversal` (Stripe's own row for a failed or cancelled payout, the payout's `failure_balance_transaction`): this row is the failure posting, `sbt:{failure_txn}` (Dr 1010, Cr 1030), and the payout that carries it is the failed payout's carrier. What else the write does depends on the failed payout. When its `swept_cents` are zero and no installment in flight covers it, the same write moves its accumulators (pool, reserve, net) to the carrier and marks it `superseded`. Otherwise the write marks it `failing` with `carried_by`, its accumulators stay where they are, and the move waits for `fail_payout` (S5). A carrier is not composed while a payout it carries is `failing`. The row is a known type and never Suspense.
- `fee` and its relatives: booked once to 5000 or 5010.
- `payout`: the payout's own row. It is the cash posting of the payout, `sbt:{txn}` (Dr 1030, Cr 1010 at the payout's signed amount), submitted when the payout is first read and so ahead of every other row of it. It is left out of `po.net_sum`. The minimum-balance hold and release pair nets inside the payout.
- anything else (an unknown `reporting_category`): account 2900 Suspense, `suspense_item`, and the payout is not composed until an administrator classifies the row. No row of a known type holds a compose for sitting in Suspense.

`suspense/classify` for a Stripe row is the reclass `sbtc:{txn}`. It accepts a closed list, each on what a read of the row's source object supports: a sale the Worker created; a known fee type; a reversal of a known deposit; and `other_receipt`, for a genuine payment the Worker did not sell (an invoice McKale sent from the Dashboard, a session someone else made) or a row type Stripe adds. `other_receipt` posts the row's net to 4090 with no pool share, and its refund is the mirror. It is refused for a charge whose session has a creation record, or predates `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` with a known type: that one is a sale and is classified as one. Every Stripe row in Suspense therefore has an exit, and 2900 can reach zero.

Every row adds its `net` to `po.net_sum`.

**S3. Tie-out and compose.** When every row of the payout is applied, `compose_payout` requires `po.net_sum == payout amount` to the cent. A failure raises `stripe_tieout_failed` and blocks the sweep. Success writes `po:{po_id}` as composed and stores `part_cents = po.pool_in - po.pool_out + po.pool_restored` (and the same for the reserve). It posts no journal line: the payout's cash moved when its own row was read. `compose_payout` runs again whenever a row of the payout is reclassed: a payout that is not fully swept takes the recomputed `part_cents`, and a payout already swept takes the difference as a part of its own in the next plan. `part_cents` is never edited under a landed installment.

**S4. Match to the bank.** A posted Mercury credit on EE inside a window around the payout's `arrival_date` (1 business day before to 5 after), matched in ranked order: (a) Stripe `trace_id` equals Mercury `trackingNumber`; (b) exact amount, date in the window, counterparty or bank description naming Stripe. Payouts of one amount are fungible in cash, so when k unmatched payouts and j unmatched Stripe credits share an amount they are paired oldest `arrival_date` to oldest posting date, and min(k, j) are confirmed; the pairing only orders confirmation. A credit that names Stripe with no payout of its amount, or an amount that differs, waits for an administrator. The payout's own row put its amount in 1030 in the hour the payout was created, so a credit matches 1030 directly, composed or not. A credit that reaches the Treasury before the book has read its payout posts to 2910 first, and the match is the reclass `mtc:{txn}`, so 1100 is posted once whatever the order. A match sets the marker `pobank:{po_id}`, the state `bank_confirmed`, and `match_key` (`trace` or `amount`) on the payout. A `paid` payout can still fail within 5 business days; the match, not the event, is the proof.

**A payout that lands somewhere else.** A Stripe payout that lands on Bliss Treasury (the default external account was switched, by one wrong click or otherwise) is matched against BT rows by the same ranks, and who performs the match depends on the rank. A BT credit whose `trackingNumber` equals the payout's `trace_id` matches by itself: the row posts directly (Dr 1121, Cr 1030) with the marker `pobank:{po_id}` and `bank_account` BT. A match by amount alone, or of a row that already sits in Suspense, is the delayed act `suspense/classify` (8.6), bound to the payout's id and exact amount. Either way the cash is carry bound to that payout (2.3, 2.6): it is never counted in `carry_unlocked_cents`, so the payout's own sweep uses it at once and funds the part with a transfer of zero, or of the remainder when the part exceeds the payout (a gift's fee). `stripe_settings_drift` is raised. It is a named posting, never a Suspense item with no exit, and once matched and funded it leaves R8, R9 and R26 green, so the month can lock. A payout that lands on Checking is matched there the same way (Dr 1110, Cr 1030) and its part is swept from Engine Expenses as usual. A payout that lands outside Mercury matches no row: it stays unmatched under R8 until McKale has moved the money into Engine Expenses, where `suspense/classify` binds that credit to the payout by its exact amount and a recorded reference.

**S5. Failure and retry.** A failed payout raises `stripe_payout_failed` to McKale: Stripe halts every payout until the bank details are re-entered or resumed in the Dashboard. Stripe records the failure with its own balance transaction, and that row rides a later payout, the carrier (S2). The failed payout's contents are swept exactly once, under the carrier. Two writes do it, and each has a name.

- **The failure row** (S2) finishes the job by itself for a payout with `swept_cents` of zero and no installment in flight: it moves the accumulators and marks the payout `superseded` in that one write. That is the payout that never reached the bank (its creation posting is undone by the failure row, whether or not it had composed), and the payout the bank sent back before any sweep (the return debit on EE is a bank row with the marker `poret:{po_id}`). In every other case the row marks the payout `failing`. No installment is planned over a `failing` payout, and its own part and `swept_cents` stay on it.
- **`fail_payout`** runs for a `failing` payout once no installment in flight covers it: that installment has landed or failed. One write, `sweepneg:{po_id}:{n}` under that payout's next installment counter, posts the reverse of what was swept under the payout (a part equal to minus its `swept_cents` as they then stand), sets `swept_cents` to zero, moves the accumulators to the carrier and marks the payout `superseded`. The carrier then composes and carries the contents in full. A payout that was already the newest payout of a net-negative installment, or that took a negative reclass difference as a part of its own (S3), posts this part under a fresh counter and never meets its own earlier key as a replay. The net is zero and nothing is funded twice: an installment that lands on a payout after its failure was read can never escape the reversing part, because the part is taken only after that installment has landed. The part is signed. For a negative payout that had already been netted it is a positive part, funded in the next installment like any other, and the write that lands it is the one that zeroes `swept_cents`, moves the accumulators and marks the payout `superseded`. A reversing part is planned even while the cumulative bound of 4.5 blocks transfers, since it only lowers what counts as funded.
- **A mispaired credit is re-pointed, not reversed.** The failed payout had been confirmed by amount pairing (`match_key` is `amount`) and no return debit followed on Engine Expenses: the credit was never this payout's. When an unmatched payout of the same amount exists inside its window, the `pobank` marker and the failed payout's `swept_cents` are re-pointed to that sibling, in the same write that moves the accumulators to the carrier and marks the failed payout `superseded`, and no reversing part is posted. The `failing` mark and the wait for an installment in flight come first here too, so no installment lands on a payout that is leaving the books. The sibling keeps its own `part_cents`, because equal payout amounts do not mean equal pool parts (a gift against a plan renewal): re-pointed `swept_cents` below the sibling's part are finished by the next installment, and re-pointed `swept_cents` above it are packed as a signed negative part of the sibling. With no such sibling the reversing part of `fail_payout` applies.

A deposit or reversal stamped with a `superseded` payout is read through `carried_by` as stamped with its carrier, and a `deferred` entry moves with the accumulators. The transit identity holds after each of these writes, and 2.4 walks them.

**S6. Negative payouts.** `payout.amount` is signed. A negative payout is Stripe debiting Engine Expenses; the creation and bank entries run in reverse and the bank match looks for a debit. It contributes its (negative) part like any other. If the debit fails, the failure row of S5 carries the same contents forward.

### 4.4 The sweep amount

`plan_sweep` runs after each bank confirmation and once a day. One installment is in flight at a time. It packs the payouts in `bank_confirmed` or `swept_except_deferred` that still have sweepable cents, oldest first and never a `failing` one, taking each payout's sweepable part (its unswept part less any cents deferred for dispute cover, 4.8; signed) until the installment reaches its cap or covers `SWEEP_COVER_MAX` payouts; a payout larger than the cap is covered in part and finished by later installments. Deferred cents are skipped, never waited on: packing continues to the payouts behind them. Every run tests each `deferred` entry again (4.8): an entry whose cover Engine Expenses holds, or whose deposit has passed `DISPUTE_COVER_DAYS`, is removed and its cents join that payout's sweepable part. Cents of a deferred deposit that a reversal has taken back are packed in the same installment as the payout that carries the reversal's row, whole and never apart from it, so the two net inside `transit_cents`.

```
part     = sum of the covered cents of each payout                  (signed; pool and reserve kept apart)
applied  = min(sweep_deficit_cents, max(part, 0))                    # earlier uncovered reversals net first
fund     = part - applied
if fund > 0:
    use      = min(max(0, carry_cents - carry_unlocked_cents - carry_return_planned_cents), fund)
                                                                     # Eustress-owned cash already in BT, less open carry return plans
    topup    = max(0, -carry_cents)                                  # a cost Eustress owes the claims side
    transfer = fund - use + topup
if fund <= 0:
    m        = min(-fund, cents)                                     # never below zero
    transfer = 0                                                     # a negative part never transfers
```

An installment is `sweep:{po_id}:{n}`: the `po_` id of the newest payout it touches and a counter. It records `covers [{po_id, pool_cents, reserve_cents}]`, at most `SWEEP_COVER_MAX` (60) payouts, and a negative part is an installment like any other, under the same id and counter. **A planned installment is immutable.** Once written, its covers and its amounts never change: `plan_sweep` does not repack it when a later payout confirms or a deferral is released, and a reclass that changes a covered payout's part follows as a part of its own (4.3 S3). One thing can happen to it before it is sent or settled: a payout it covers fails. It then ends `failed`, whole, and its other payouts are packed again under the next installment number. In live mode it is sent in the step after it is planned. In shadow mode it waits, later payouts are planned as later installments computed as if the earlier ones had landed, and `sweeps/manual` settles the oldest unsettled installment only, so the amount in the morning's digest is the amount the act checks when it takes effect. A payout carries `swept_cents`, so it is `swept` exactly when every cent of its part has landed, and `swept_except_deferred` when everything but its deferred cents has. Either state completes the payout for the release of reversals below. A payout leaves `swept_except_deferred` for `swept` in the landing write of its last deferred cents.

**Landing.** When the credit on Bliss Treasury is applied (`sweepland:{po_id}:{n}`), one write moves only that installment: `transit_cents -= fund`, `cents += fund` (the reserve likewise), `carry_cents -= use`, `carry_cents += topup`, `sweep_deficit_cents -= applied`, each covered payout's `swept_cents` rises, and the installment is marked `rev_pass: open`. That write holds the journal entry, the buckets, the installment and the covered `po:` records and nothing else, so its size is fixed by `SWEEP_COVER_MAX` and never by how many refunds and disputes a week of payouts carried. An installment whose transfer is zero lands in the planning write.

**The reversal pass.** The reversal records are updated by the scheduler's `revpass` stream, at most 50 records a step. It walks the index `revx:{po_id}:` of each covered payout: every reversal stamped with a payout this installment completes is released, and every unfunded reversal of a deposit this installment funds becomes funded (3.3). `pending_reversal_cents` moves by exactly the records of a step, in that step's write, and the last step closes the pass. A record that is already funded, or already released, is skipped, so the pass, a reversal booked while it runs and a mirror can arrive in any order. `accrue_setup` waits while any pass is open, so the drip base is never computed with a landing's money in `cents` and its reversals not yet counted against it; a release that comes an hour late only keeps the base lower for that hour. Three hundred refunds on one week's installment are six steps of the pass and never one put. A test lands an installment whose payouts carry 300 reversals under the operation-counting fake and requires the landing put and every pass step to stay inside the key limit, and the night's drip to wait for the pass; its mutation updates the reversal records in the landing write.

**A negative part.** Nothing is transferred. In one write (`sweepneg:{po_id}:{n}`): `cents -= m`, `transit_cents += m`, `carry_cents += m` (that cash is already in Bliss Treasury and belongs to Eustress until a later sweep nets it), `sweep_deficit_cents += (-fund - m)`, each covered payout's `swept_cents` moves by its covered cents, and the installment opens its reversal pass, which releases the covered reversals as above. The remainder that no funded cents could cover stays inside `transit_cents` as a negative amount and is netted against the next positive parts, so `cents` never goes below zero and `carry_cents` never claims cash that Bliss Treasury does not hold. A carry that is still negative afterwards is repaired by the `topup` of the next positive installment, or by the top-up installment of 4.5 when no positive part is waiting.

A gift contributes its gross. A sponsorship contributes nothing.

### 4.5 Preconditions and the send

All required: every covered payout `bank_confirmed` or `swept_except_deferred`, and none `failing`; tie-out passed; no row of an unknown reporting category in the covered payouts (a row of a known type in Suspense holds nothing: it adds nothing to the part, and its later reclass is a part of its own); no halt (a top-up alone is the exception below); `SWEEP_MODE` is `live`; no installment in flight; EE available balance at least `transfer + ee_floor` (4.8); `transfer <= SWEEP_MAX_CENTS`; the day's transfers at most `SWEEP_DAY_MAX_CENTS`; and the cumulative bound: lifetime funded cents (transfers less top-ups, plus carry used, less the negative parts posted for failed payouts) never exceed the lifetime positive pool and reserve parts of bank-confirmed payouts, each payout counted once, a `superseded` payout not at all and a `failing` payout at its own part until the reversing part has zeroed what was swept under it. A negative part transfers nothing and is planned whatever the bound says. No bound compares a transfer with the payouts' own amounts: a gift's pool part is its gross while its payout carries gross less fee, and the fee is an Eustress cost that Engine Expenses' other cash covers by design.

**A top-up alone runs under a halt.** When `carry_cents` is negative and no positive part can be funded (a halt is on, or nothing is waiting), the planner builds an installment that covers no payout: `sweep:topup:{YYYY-MM-DD}:{n}`, with `transfer = -carry_cents`, inside the same caps and the same Engine Expenses floor. It moves Eustress's own cash from Engine Expenses to Bliss Treasury, the only direction the relay knows, and can only add cover. It is sent under a halt of scope `payouts` and never under a halt of scope `all` (8.6). A carry that fees have pushed past its bound is therefore repaired while payouts stay stopped, and the halt it caused can then be lifted.

The Treasury writes the installment as `submitted` (a record, no journal entry; the journal moves only when the bank does) and calls the relay route `POST /v1/sweep`. The relay moves money from EE to BT only; both account ids are in its configuration. Before sending, the relay lists BT credits and EE debits in a date window and looks for one whose note equals `eus-sweep-{po_id}-{n}` exactly, and answers with it if found, because the replay behaviour of Mercury's `/transfer` on a repeated key is unverified. Its answers are `accepted`, `replayed`, `refused_cap`, `disarmed`, `refused_bank` (a definitive refusal by Mercury before anything was created: insufficient funds, a transfer limit) and `unknown`.

| State | Meaning | Leaves it by |
|---|---|---|
| `planned` | Computed; in shadow mode, shown only | Send, or a manual settlement |
| `submitted` | Sent to the relay | The Worker's own reads |
| `landed` | The debit row and the credit row are both applied | Final |
| `failed` | A definitive refusal; or the Worker's read shows a terminal failure; or the debit posted and Mercury returned the money to EE (marker `sweepfail:{po_id}:{n}`) | The next installment number |
| `manual` | Settled against a transfer McKale made himself | Final |

An unanswered send is resolved by the Worker's own read of both accounts, by exact note first and then by amount inside the time window, never by a new send. A failed installment frees its payouts for installment `n + 1` only after that read of both accounts proves no transfer of it exists beyond rows already applied. The two legs can sit at different partner banks: a debit that posts while the credit fails shows as a return credit on EE, which closes account 1130 by its own row.

### 4.6 `SWEEP_MODE`

| Value | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `off` | Payouts are ingested and composed. No sweep is planned. Pool shares wait in `transit_cents` |
| `shadow` | Installments are planned and their amounts appear in the daily finance digest and the admin view. Nothing is sent and nothing is required of McKale: `transit_cents` simply waits. If McKale chooses to make an internal transfer of exactly a planned amount from EE to BT in Mercury, both rows are applied the moment they post, like any row the book cannot yet explain: the EE debit to Suspense 2910 and the BT credit to Suspense 2930. `POST /api/admin/finance/sweeps/manual` then settles the installment as a Suspense reclass: it names the two rows, requires them to be an internal transfer of the installment's exact amount between the two accounts, and posts `mtc:` on both through account 1130 with the landing effect (2.6). Every posted row is applied throughout, so the walls stay determinate while the act waits out its delay. A transfer of any other amount is no installment: the own-transfer classification of 8.6 settles its two rows as carry, which later sweeps net once the month that holds it is locked |
| `live` | The relay sends them. `sweeps/manual` can also settle a `submitted` installment against the same pair of Suspense rows |

Shadow runs the whole cash model in production before the relay holds a Mercury token or answers a money route, and the manual settlement is the first money path anyone uses. The relay itself is deployed by then, with its announce, anchor and health routes alone, so the act is announced out of band and the witness anchors the journal from its first entry (8.6). It is tested end to end with its real delay; the test's mutation requires a row that is still unapplied at the effective time, which cannot exist.

### 4.7 Refunds and disputes after a sweep

The claim side moves at the webhook (3.3). Cash follows: Stripe pays the refund from its balance, so a later payout is smaller. When that payout is composed its part is lower by the pool part, so the next transfer is smaller, or `carry_cents` grows. No money moves from Bliss Treasury back to Engine Expenses through the relay; the relay has no such route.

If `carry_cents` stays above `CARRY_ALERT_CENTS` for 14 days, `sweep_carry_stale` asks McKale to return it. The return is planned first: `POST /api/admin/finance/carry/plan` creates an expected-outflow record `xo:{id}` for an exact amount no larger than the carry a sweep may use, with an expiry. From the write that plans it until it is matched or expires, its amount is in `carry_return_planned_cents`, which no sweep (4.4) and no `legacy_fund` may use, so the transfer McKale then makes can never take carry below zero. McKale then makes one internal transfer of that amount from BT to EE in Mercury; `bank_match` pairs the two rows with the plan and R3 accepts them. A transfer with no plan behind it trips R3 like any other outflow. A payable already accrued to a contributor is never clawed back for a refund of someone else's purchase.

### 4.8 Engine Expenses, McKale's automations and Stripe debits

- The system reads Mercury's auto-transfer rules daily (`GET /auto-transfer-rules`) and never edits one. The admin view shows **safe to move from Engine Expenses = EE available - unswept pool and reserve share - `ee_floor` - entity tax set-aside target**. `ee_below_unswept` fires when EE cannot cover the next installment and names the rule that ran. Any rule on Bliss Treasury raises an alert.
- **The floor is computed.** `ee_floor = EE_FLOOR_MIN_CENTS + dispute cover`. Dispute cover is the pool part of every swept deposit whose pool part is at least `DISPUTE_COVER_MIN_CENTS` (100000) and which is younger than `DISPUTE_COVER_DAYS` (120). A gift's whole gross is swept, so a dispute of a swept gift lands entirely on Engine Expenses; the cover is what makes that debit clear. A deposit at or above the threshold is swept only when EE holds the transfer plus the floor including its own cover. Until then it is **deferred on its payout**, not waited on: `po.deferred [{dep_ref, cents, reason}]` carves those cents out, the rest of that payout and every later payout are swept as usual, the payout rests in `swept_except_deferred`, the reversals stamped with it are released on that state, and R9 does not count deferred cents as overdue. `large_deposit_waiting_cover` tells McKale the figure. A deferral has three exits, and `plan_sweep` tests every entry on every run. Engine Expenses holds the transfer plus the floor including the cover: the entry is removed and its cents are swept. The deposit passes `DISPUTE_COVER_DAYS`, counted from the deposit's date and not from any sweep, and needs no cover: the same. Or the deposit is refunded or disputed while it is deferred: the entry is reduced by the reversal's pool part p, and those p cents are packed, with no cover asked, in the same installment as the payout that carries the reversal's row. A full reversal therefore nets to a transfer of zero, and `cents` is never debited for a deposit that was never funded (3.3). A restored part of such a deposit (a dispute won) is deferred on the payout that carries it under the same test. When the last deferred cents of a payout land, the payout is `swept`. What a large gift costs Eustress in tied-up cash is decision D29: a 1,500.00 USD gift either keeps 1,500.00 USD of Eustress's own money idle in Engine Expenses for 120 days or waits those 120 days before it reaches the pool. The same figure sizes the Stripe minimum balance of decision D9.
- A Stripe minimum balance set in the Dashboard is compatible with the daily automatic schedule and stops refunds going pending; tie-out handles its hold and release rows.
- Engine Expenses is a savings account. If Mercury confirms it cannot accept Stripe's ACH debits, the operating plan is, in order: a Stripe minimum balance sized to make negative payouts rare; and only then McKale's own choice to point Stripe's default bank account at Checking (decision D23, which touches locked decision 2 and is his alone). Stripe's separate prefunded balance for refunds and disputes is not part of the plan: a refund paid from it rides no payout, so the pool's part of that refund would never return through a sweep, and the book keeps no account for a second Stripe balance (2.4). If the savings account limits outgoing transfers per statement cycle, `SWEEP_MIN_INTERVAL_DAYS` is 7 so one installment covers a week of payouts.
- A drift monitor reads `GET /v1/balance_settings` daily and raises `stripe_settings_drift` when the interval is not `daily`, payouts are disabled, `debit_negative_balances` changes, a minimum balance changes, an automatic transfer rule appears or a balance of another type exists. Detecting a redirected payout does not rest on an event: every payout's own `destination` is compared with `STRIPE_EE_BANK_ID` at ingest (4.3), and the daily job reads the default external account where the restricted key allows it. `account.external_account.updated`, if Stripe sends it for the platform's own account, only schedules that read sooner. Nothing writes.

### 4.9 What a failed sweep costs

Time only. The share stays in `transit_cents`, the pool does not grow, the drip is computed on a smaller base that day, and the money is added when a later installment lands. A failed installment never blocks the ones after it: it ends in `failed` and its payouts are packed again. No payable is ever created against unswept money.

---

## 5. Contributor payouts from Mercury

Earnings are an obligation of Eustress LLC, paid from Eustress's own account. The words in code, records, pages and mail are "earnings payable" and "payout of earnings". Never "your funds", "held for you", "redeem" or "cash out Bliss". The USD payout does not burn BLS and no dollars-per-BLS figure exists. No feature lets a user send USD or BLS to another user or direct a payout to someone else's bank account.

### 5.1 The nightly settlement accrues

The settlement makes no provider call, so a Mercury or relay outage can never delay a settlement or the BLS emission. It is one work stream of the Treasury scheduler (8.9).

The emission phases run `init` to `supply`. Two rules tie them to the USD phases. `init` marks a job `emission_skipped` when its score date is older than the 6 day checkpoint horizon and continues through the collect phases, so the scores exist; the credit and supply phases are skipped for that job. `total` always proceeds to `accrue_setup`, for an `already_distributed` day too. A week of missed ticks therefore loses BLS emission for the days past the horizon, as the checkpoint rule requires, and loses no USD accrual, because the journal has no checkpoint horizon. `settlement_not_started` fires on the second unsettled day.

| Phase | What it does |
|---|---|
| `accrue_setup` | `drip = floor(max(0, cents - pending_reversal_cents) x rate)`, `pool = floor(drip x utilization)`. Posts `drip:{date}`: the whole pool moves from `cents` to `daypool_cents`, so a refund landing mid-settlement cannot shrink shares already planned. Skips for no contributors, for a pool of zero or less, or when `PAYOUTS_MODE` is `off` (shares stay in the pool). It waits while a landing's reversal pass is open (4.4), so the base never counts money whose reversals are not yet counted against it. Under `accrue` and `live` it waits, with `tax_rules_missing`, while the score date's year has no activated rules file, and runs when the file is activated (6.5); the emission phases are not held by that wait. On the first score date of a month it posts `fold:{YYYY-MM}` before anything else, on a skipped day too: the whole of `unaccrued_cents` returns to `cents`. The high-water mark decays here once per score date, on skipped days too |
| `accrue_plan` | Each contributor in good standing gets `cents = floor(pool x weight / weight_total)`. Weights count everyone, so an unpayable share is never handed to others that day |
| `accrue` | 20 payees a step. For each, `payeeGate(env, id, "accrual", score_date)` with strong reads. One journal entry per step, key `acc:{date}:{step}`. A passing share moves from `daypool_cents` to `owed_cents` and two accrual rows are written, each with the source stamp of 6.4 for a W-8 filer. A suspended payee's share moves to `held_cents` and its rows carry the hold (5.2). A row that already exists is skipped |
| `accrue_finish` | Posts `dripend:{date}`: everything not accrued (shares that failed the gate, and rounding) moves from `daypool_cents` to `unaccrued_cents`, outside the drip base. Writes the public day record |

Under `PAYOUTS_MODE=shadow` the same phases compute each share and write it as an estimate (`est:{payee}`); `drip:{date}` is not posted, nothing leaves `cents`, and no row, payable or hold exists. **The shadow gate is stated, not implied.** An estimate is computed only for an account that is a verified adult human, is not banned, and holds a Creator, Professional or Bundle plan paid through the score date. The tax form, the destination, the terms and the region are ignored, because none can exist while enrollment is off. An account with no qualifying plan has no estimate at all, so no figure exists that a plan purchase would unlock. Estimates feed the administrator view. An account sees its own only while `SHADOW_VISIBLE` is true (gate G13), only when it already holds a qualifying plan, and only on a surface that carries no plan link, plan price or checkout control. A pinned copy test fails when any page renders both an estimate and a plan checkout link; its mutation renders both.

**The public day record** carries the day's pool, rounded to two significant digits, and nothing else: no split, no count, nothing per person. Per-user scores are public, so every share is computable from an exact pool, and two channels would then give away who is enrolled, which is a list of who has passed KYC, holds a paid plan, filed a tax form and enrolled a bank account. The first is a published daily split into accrued and returned: an observer differences one day against the next and reads off who enrolled. The second is the pool itself, which would carry that same split one day late if the unaccrued remainder went straight back into the drip base: on a night with no sweep landing and no reversal, which is every weekend, returned(t) = base(t+1) - base(t) + pool(t). Three rules close both.

- **The unaccrued remainder is parked.** `dripend` moves it to `unaccrued_cents` (account 2003), and every amount that returns to the pool because of one payee's standing (an adjustment, a hold returned to the pool, a netted clawback) is credited there too, never to `cents`. From one night to the next the drip base therefore moves only by the day's own pool, by sweep landings and by reversals, none of which depends on who is enrolled. The bucket folds into `cents` once a month (`fold:{YYYY-MM}`, in the settlement of the month's first score date), the moment the monthly statement is cut. It stays pool money throughout: it is a term of `claims_bt` and of the conservation identity, and it is shared by everyone from the fold on.
- **Public pool figures are coarse, and the list of them is closed.** On the day record: `pool_usd`. On `/api/payouts/rate` and `/api/ledger/summary`: `treasury_usd` (the whole undistributed pool: `cents` plus `unaccrued_cents` plus `daypool_cents`, so the fold does not move it), `daily_drip_usd`, and the emission figures, which involve no money. On the monthly statement: the figures of 8.5. Every dollar figure among them is rounded to two significant digits. No public route exposes `cents`, the drip base, `unaccrued_cents`, `owed_cents`, `held_cents` or any figure from which the remainder can be computed to the cent, and `platform_usd` and `deposit_count` are not public (decision D24). Exact figures are in the administrator view and, for a payee, in that payee's own statements.
- **The split is monthly.** It is published in the public statement (8.5): `accrued_usd` and `returned_usd` for the month, each rounded to two significant digits, and only when both groups held at least `PUBLIC_K_MIN` (10) contributors on every day of the month and the enrolled set changed by at least `PUBLIC_SET_CHANGE_MIN` (5) people during it. Otherwise the month carries its pool total alone, and the absence of a split says nothing about group size, since two independent conditions can cause it.

What remains is stated plainly. The month's fold raises the next day's rounded pool by a visible step, so one coarse figure a month about the whole unenrolled group can be read from the series, even in a month whose split is withheld. It names no day and no person. Where that group is a handful of people it is still a hint about them, and it is the cost of returning unaccrued shares to the pool at all (decision D7).

Gate reasons are never public: they are administrator-only and shown to the affected payee. No sanctions figure is published anywhere. A test builds the public series from the real `drip`, `dripend` and `fold` arithmetic over a multi-day series that includes consecutive nights with no sweep landing and no reversal, in which one member joins or leaves at a time. Its attacker holds everything on `/api/payouts/rate` and the day records, rebuilds every share from the public scores, solves returned(t) = base(t+1) - base(t) + pool(t), and tries to recover who is in which group. It must fail. The test has two mutations and must fail under each: publish the daily split; return the remainder to the drip base nightly with exact public pool figures.

### 5.2 One gate, two stages

`payee_gate.mjs` exports `payeeGate(env, accountId, stage, asOf)` with `stage` of `"accrual"` or `"send"`. It is the only predicate any money path uses. It asks the `AccountAuth` and `TaxVault` objects directly, never a KV record; an unreadable answer is a refusal. Ban state and account type live in `AccountAuth` (`ban_set`, `ban_clear`) and come back in `money_view`; the KV user record is a display mirror, and a test whose mutation reads the ban from KV fails. It returns `{ok, outcome, reasons[], view}` where `view` is a narrow identity view (`legal_name`, `dob`, `kyc_country`, `verified_at`), never the whole KYC record.

At accrual the plan, the tax form and the terms are judged **as of the score date**, so a settlement that runs late neither grants nor forfeits a day by accident. A condition marked "required" fails the share. A condition marked "suspends" is a state that is transient or on Eustress's side: for a payee who has passed the whole accrual gate at least once (`first_qualified_at` on the payee row), the share accrues into a hold with that reason and is released when the condition clears; for a payee who has never qualified it fails the share.

| Condition | Accrual | Send |
|---|---|---|
| `account_type` is `human`. The KYC exemption of administrators and agents does not reach this gate | required | required |
| The account's `AccountAuth` state is migrated: `money_migrated` is set (12.5) | required | required |
| Not banned | required. Suspends (`banned`) for a payee who has qualified: a ban holds the share and never forfeits it by itself | required |
| Eustress KYC verified adult (`requireVerifiedAdult`, the one identity check) with a legal name read from the document | required | required |
| Active Creator, Professional or Bundle plan, paid through the score date (locked decision 8) | required | required while `PLAN_GATE_AT_SEND` is true (decision D3) |
| The payee's state or country is open in `payout_regions.json` (from counsel's memo, gate G2) | required | required |
| The income line is open for the payee's form in the signed tax rules | required, per line. A year with no activated rules file is not a failed condition: the accrual run waits for the file (6.5) | required |
| Current contributor terms accepted | suspends (`terms_update`) after a published version change | required |
| Valid tax form with no open review | required. Suspends (`tax_hold`) for a form expired less than `TAX_FORM_GRACE_DAYS` (30) ago and for a review the system opened | required, and the form on file is the one in the payment's tax snapshot |
| Sanctions status `clear` | suspends (`sanctions_hold`) on `potential`; a confirmed match blocks | required, re-screened against the current list |
| Payout destination active | required to have been set once. Suspends (`destination_hold`) while it is `pending`, cooling, `needs_reconfirm`, `needs_attention`, `name_mismatch`, `under_review`, `bad` or removed | required: active, past cooling, reconfirmed, fingerprint unchanged, version equal to the payment's snapshot |
| Value not frozen | suspends (`key_replaced`) | required |
| First payout hold after enrollment has passed | not required | required |
| The first payment to this destination version has settled with no return; no payment of an earlier period is still undebited; 24 hours since the last send to this recipient | not required | waits (`first_payment_in_watch`, `earlier_payment_open`, `sent_within_24h`): the cents stay in the lots and nothing is built |
| The TIN envelope of the current form, where the form carries one, has passed the offline check (6.6) | not required | waits (`tin_check_pending`) for any amount that would take the payee past `TIN_UNVERIFIED_PAY_MAX_CENTS` |
| Ticket wallet balance not negative; no open clawback or stray-payment remainder exceeds the payable (the build nets each one first, 5.6) | not required | required |
| No legal hold, estate hold or open payee dispute on the account | not required | required |

A suspended state has a limit: after `SUSPENDED_ACCRUAL_MAX_DAYS` (30) consecutive days in one suspended state, later shares return to the pool until it clears. What accrued inside the limit stays owed.

**A ban suspends; it does not forfeit.** The route that bans an account is a moderation route, outside the finance prefixes and behind the moderation role, so the money system gives a ban no power to move anyone's share to other people. For a payee who has qualified, a ban accrues the share into a `banned` hold for up to `SUSPENDED_ACCRUAL_MAX_DAYS`; clearing the ban releases it, and it returns to the pool only by the delayed `return` act with a case reference (5.9). Every ban of an account that has `first_qualified_at` is announced on the finance alert path (`payee_banned`), and more than `BAN_PAYEE_DAY_MAX` (3) such bans in one day raises `ban_tripwire` on both channels. An honest moderation mistake therefore costs the payee nothing, and a hostile one is seen the day it starts.

Three rules follow and are stated in the contributor terms.

- **A share that fails the accrual gate is owed to no one.** It returns to the pool, through the unaccrued remainder that folds in once a month (5.1), and is shared by everyone from then on. Nobody has a claim until a share is declared to them by name.
- **A suspended share is declared and held.** A payee who did the work while a name-similarity review, a bank return, a form renewal or a key freeze was open is paid for it when the state clears. Decision D7 records both choices.
- **A share once accrued stays owed.** A later lapse does not take it back. With `PLAN_GATE_AT_SEND` true, a lapsed plan pauses payment and the payable is paid when the plan is active again; whether that condition is lawful is put to counsel (section 19, item 27).

### 5.3 Accrual rows, maturity and lots

**Rows are evidence.** Each accrual writes two rows: `acc:{payee}:{score_date}:e` holds the effort part and `acc:{payee}:{score_date}:v` the value part, split by each part's share of the day score (`value = floor(share x value_score / score)`; effort is the remainder). Each row keeps what cannot be rebuilt later: `cents`, `score`, `plan`, `form_seq`, `line`, `character`, `mature_on`, the hold it accrued under if any, and on the value row `value_us_cents` (the part that came from buyers whose recorded country is the United States) and the counted Tickets. The accrual fields never change. `form_seq` is evidence of the certificate on file that night and decides nothing about reporting: one certificate governs a payment, the one on file when it is made (6.8). Three small fields move: `adjusted_cents` (a reversal shrank it), `folded`, and `us_cents`, the cents of the row that are US source under its line's own rule (6.4): for a `services` line, by where the payee was on the score date; for a line the rules mark US source with basis `slice`, the row's `value_us_cents`; with basis `whole`, the whole row. It is stamped at accrual for a W-8 filer, raised by a later US-work report or by the status-switch review of 6.3, never lowered by the system, and never more than the row's cents less `adjusted_cents`.

- Effort rows mature `EFFORT_MATURITY_DAYS` (7) after the score date.
- Value rows mature `VALUE_MATURITY_DAYS` (120) after the score date, and only while `WALLET_LOTS_LIVE` is true.

The 120 days cover the card dispute window. A Ticket bought with a stolen card and spent with a creator raises that creator's share; the chargeback arrives weeks later. Until then the value part waits, and the reversal chain (5.10) shrinks it before it can be paid. Stated plainly: **a creator's earnings from Ticket sales are first payable 120 days after the day they were earned**, and not at all until Ticket lots are live. Decision D6 records the 120 days.

**Lots are what is paid.** The scheduler's `mature` stream folds each matured row that is not under a hold into the payee's lot `lot:{payee}:{year}:{line}:{character}`, in writes sized by keys and not by rows. A folded row writes its own `acc:` row and can write a lot, a `payee:` row and a `payq:` entry, so a write folds rows until it holds 100 keys: about 25 rows on a night when every row belongs to a different payee, and far more when they share payees. A payee has a handful of lots however long they have been earning. A lot holds `remaining_cents`, `us_cents`, `folded_cents`, `consumed_cents` and the mature date of its oldest unpaid cent. A payment consumes cents from lots, oldest first, in any amount, so a cap splits a balance cleanly and the rest stays in the lot for the next run. Which rows a payment covered is derived from the lot's running totals; no row is stamped by a payment. A payment that covers 400 days of earnings writes the same few keys as one that covers a week, far inside the 128 key limit of a put.

**A hold never changes a clock.** A row that accrued under a hold keeps its own `mature_on`; while the hold stands the row is not folded, and when the hold is released the row is maturing or payable exactly as its date says. A hold on cents that are already in lots names the lots and the cents taken from each, and its release returns them, in whole or in part. The builder takes cents only from lots, whatever their history. A payee who freezes their own account by rotating a key gains nothing: value earnings accrued during the freeze still wait their 120 days.

`reverse_value` and `adjust_payable` act on the cents wherever they are: an unfolded row (maturing or held), a lot, or a hold on a lot. Only what has already been reserved or paid is a clawback.

### 5.4 Enrollment

One page, `/payouts`, shows five gates with live status; `/bliss` and the Settings "Billing" tab lead to it. Its tax step and its destination steps are served from the payments origin (15.4), never from the origin that renders forums, profiles and listings. `ENROLLMENT_MODE` governs it:

| Value | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `off` | The page shows the gates and what each needs. The tax form and destination routes answer `not_open`. The relay's `CAP_INVITE_DAY_COUNT` is 0. No TIN is collected and no Mercury recipient is created for a rail that may never run |
| `staff` | Only accounts in `FINANCE_ADMINS` can enroll. Requires G1 in writing and G6. Used for gate G7 |
| `live` | Open to every account that meets the gates. Requires G1 in writing, the terms half of G2, G6 and G7 |

1. **Identity.** The Eustress KYC. No further upload. A payee needs a document-read legal name. The single KYC call reads the document's issuing country, number and expiry, enforces `name_matches` and `dob_matches` for a payee, and never interpolates user-typed text into the model prompt. The facts the money system needs (`legal_name`, `dob`, `verified_at`, `id_type`, `issuing_country`, `id_hash`) are copied into `AccountAuth.kyc_facts` with no expiry, so the 7 year lifetime of the KYC key cannot unverify a payee. "Verified" has one meaning everywhere money or securities are involved: `requireVerifiedAdult`.

   **Reverification is narrow.** `POST /api/kyc/reverify` exists for one case: an account verified with an empty legal name. It is VALUE class, needs an identity-file session and a signed challenge, and is accepted automatically only when the fresh document is the same person: its `id_hash` equals the claimed one, or the date of birth and document number match. Anything else goes to administrator review. The one-person-one-account `id_hash` claim is taken again for the fresh document, so one document cannot verify several accounts. Any change of `legal_name` sets `tax_hold`, marks the destination `needs_reconfirm`, runs the sanctions screen again, starts the 30 day destination pin and notifies every address on the account. An account cannot be handed to another person and keep what the first person earned.
2. **Plan.** Shows the plan, its paid-through date and the portal link.
3. **Contributor terms and tax form.** `POST /api/payouts/terms` records acceptance of the current terms (`terms:{version}` with time, IP, session and text hash; acceptance is compared by version and the hash is evidence). Then the tax form (section 6).
4. **Bank details, entered at Mercury, bound by a key signature.**
   - Preconditions: an identity-file session opened on the payments origin (a session the main site holds by hand-off is refused, 15.4); a verified email address that was verified at least 30 days before the request, during all of which the account showed the in-product money notice of that address (5.11), so an address a thief added is announced to the owner on the site and in Studio for a month before it can receive a hosted link or a notice; no 30 day pin (refused when the pin cannot be read); gates 1 to 3; a clear country check.
   - `POST /api/payouts/destination/invite` (VALUE). The request carries an Ed25519 signature by the account key over a one-time challenge (AccountAuth purpose `dest_invite`). The Worker asks the relay for a Mercury recipient invite with `name` set to the KYC legal name, `contactEmail` set to the verified address, `sendEmail: false`, `requireTaxDocument: false`, and one method chosen by the rail rule. The relay never accepts a recipient id on this route. The hosted link goes only to this authenticated response and the site opens it only when it starts with `https://mercury.com/recipient-onboarding/`. That prefix proves the page is Mercury's, not that the invite belongs to Eustress's Mercury organization, so before a link is shown the invite is confirmed two ways: the relay's answer carries a valid response signature (7.4), and the Worker reads that invite id with its own read token and finds it (an invite carries no bank details). A link whose invite Eustress's own Mercury does not hold is never shown, so nobody who gets between the Worker and the relay can send a payee to type bank details into another Mercury customer's form. An invite not completed within 60 minutes is dead to Eustress: `confirm` refuses it and a fresh one is needed.
   - The payee types bank details on Mercury's page and accepts the payout authorization shown on the Eustress page first: the account is in their own name, they authorize credits to it, text version, time and IP stored as `auth:{seq}` and kept at least 6 years after the destination is removed.
   - **Confirm shows, then signs.** The relay reads the invite and the recipient and returns a whitelist: status, recipient id, name, method, bank country, account type, last four digits, bank fingerprint with its version, the relay's first-seen time, and for a wire recipient the bank's name and SWIFT code. The page shows the last four digits and the bank country it received. `POST /api/payouts/destination/confirm` (VALUE) then requires an Ed25519 signature (purpose `dest_set`) over `{recipient_id, bank_fp, bank_fp_v, last4, bank_country, dest version}`. A stolen bearer token cannot set where money goes, and a person who completes the hosted page in the payee's place is caught when the payee is shown an account that is not theirs. The Worker never requests a recipient object itself.
   - Checks at confirm: bank country fits the rail and is not blocked; the wire recipient's bank passes the entity screen (5.5); the recipient name still equals the legal name Eustress supplied (a change at Mercury parks the destination as `name_mismatch`); and the fingerprint is not bound to another payee. The binding is the `DestClaims` object keyed by the fingerprint (one instance per fingerprint, create-if-absent inside the object), so one bank account serves one payee and no bucket listing reveals who has a destination. **A conflict never touches the incumbent.** The payee who holds the earlier claim is not parked, proven or not: a proven one keeps being paid, and an unproven one stays on its normal path to its first payment. Only the newcomer is parked. **Every conflict opens a review and an alert.** A review naming both accounts opens at the first conflict on a fingerprint, whether or not either destination is proven, and `dest_conflict` goes to the finance alert path the same day. **Arrival decides who waits, never who owns.** An unproven earlier claim is no title. Between two claimants the claim goes to the one who shows the account is theirs: a bank document (a statement header or a bank letter) that carries the holder's name equal to the claimant's KYC legal name and the same last four digits, received through a payee case and recorded by case reference. Eustress keeps the reference and not the document. The delayed act `dest-claims/resolve` (13.2) then releases the losing claim, marks the loser's destination `removed`, and returns the winner's destination to `pending` with a fresh cooling period and every other gate. The act names an account and never a destination: each claimant's destination is the one that claimant enrolled and signed. Someone who enrolls a stranger's numbers first therefore delays the true owner by one review, and does it from a verified, identified account that the review can ban. **A conflict is not shown at the keyboard.** Every destination that was just confirmed, clean or conflicted, shows the same `pending` state for the whole cooling period; a conflicted one then reads as a generic bank verification failure with a support link, the words any parked destination shows. Knowing someone's routing and account number therefore does not stop their payouts. It remains a slow membership test, and this document does not claim otherwise: a clean enrollment turns `active` when cooling ends and a conflicted one does not, so a prober learns after 72 hours whether one bank account is enrolled. The price bounds it: a KYC-verified account with a paid plan and a signed tax form, at most `DEST_CONFIRM_MONTH_MAX` (3) confirms a month, and a review that names the prober to McKale on the first try. A claim is released when its destination is removed. Conflicts are also counted per account as an abuse signal (`dest_conflict_repeat` at an account's second).
   - The `AccountAuth` object writes destination version `n` in the same critical section that checks the pin: `{recipient_id, rail, bank_country, account_type, last4, bank_fp, bank_fp_v, set_at, first_seen_at, usable_after, proven_at}`. `usable_after` is the later of the cooling period and 72 hours after the relay's first-seen time, so the book and the relay agree on when a recipient is old enough.
   - A durable notice goes to every address on the account, and the in-product money notice shows on the site and in Studio (5.11): bank details were set or changed, when, last four digits, and a control that disables the destination.
5. **Sanctions screen** (5.5). Runs when gates 1, 3 and 4 are complete, and again before every payment.

**Rail rule.** ACH if and only if the tax form is a W-9 and the bank is in the US. Wire if the bank is outside the US: a W-8 filer, or a US person banking abroad; both bear the wire fee. A W-8 filer with a US bank account is offered no rail until counsel and Mercury answer the international ACH question. Rail follows the bank's country; form follows certified tax status; neither comes from an IP country.

**What the name check proves, and what it does not.** Eustress sets the recipient name in the invite, and receiving banks post ACH credits by account number. The name comparison therefore detects a later edit at Mercury; it does not prove the account belongs to the payee. Five controls bound the gap, and the relay enforces the first two by itself from Mercury's own history (7.5):

- **First payment cap.** The first ACH to any destination version is at most `FIRST_PAY_MAX_CENTS` (2500), and the first wire at most `FIRST_WIRE_MAX_CENTS` (10000, above the wire minimum). Nothing more goes to that destination until that payment is `settled` with no return. It is a real payment acting as the prenote; the rest of the balance stays in the payee's lots and follows in later runs.
- **Cooling and first payout hold.** No payment goes to a destination younger than `DEST_COOLING_HOURS` (72). A payee's first payment also waits `FIRST_PAYOUT_HOLD_DAYS` (14) after enrollment completes.
- **The key signature at confirm**, over the last four digits the payee was shown.
- **Notice** to every address on the account at every change.
- **One bank account per payee**, and the signed authorization that the account is the payee's own.

The residual risk (a payee enrolls a third party's account, or mistypes into a live account) is bounded by the first payment cap and is a question for counsel (section 19, item 9).

**Bank fingerprint.** The relay computes `HMAC(BANK_FP_KEY, canonical routing tuple)` with a key only it holds, over every field the rail uses to deliver the money. For ACH: routing number, account number and `electronicAccountType`. For a wire: `swiftCode`; the IBAN or account number; every non-null field of the recipient's `countrySpecific` block in sorted key order (an Indian IFSC, an Australian BSB, a Canadian transit number are what identify the account outside IBAN countries); the SWIFT code and routing number of `correspondentInfo`; and `bankDetails.bankCountry`. The formula has a version, `bank_fp_v`, stored with every fingerprint. A destination whose stored version is not the relay's current one is `needs_reconfirm`, so a change of formula makes the payee see and sign the account again and is never accepted silently. Every payment request carries the fingerprint and its version from enrollment; the relay recomputes it from Mercury's live record and refuses on a mismatch (`recipient_changed`). A change of any delivery detail at Mercury by any route other than a fresh, gated, signed, noticed Eustress enrollment stops the payment: a branch code or a correspondent bank edited in the dashboard while a wire waits for approval fails the hourly re-check exactly as a changed account number does. Two unrelated payees whose account numbers coincide at different branches of one bank have different fingerprints and never collide on the claim. A test changes a fake recipient's `countrySpecific` block between queue and approval; its mutation hashes SWIFT and IBAN only. This also meets the duty to apply a bank's correction before the next entry: nothing is originated to a changed record until the payee enrolls again.

**Changing details** is a fresh invite and the next version. Payments pause for the cooling period. The key-replacement freeze (72 hours, or 7 days when the account holds value) and the 30 day destination pin apply against this record. `accountHoldsValue` counts a tax form on file, a destination or a payable balance as value, so the longer freeze applies.

**A key replacement distrusts the destination it finds.** A thief holding the identity file enrolls their bank account; the owner recovers the account by replacing the key, days or months later, and believes the account is safe. So every key replacement marks the current destination version `needs_reconfirm`, whatever its age and whether or not a payment to it has settled. A `needs_reconfirm` destination is unpaid until the holder of the current key signs it again (purpose `dest_set`, same payload) after the freeze ends. The cost to an honest owner is one signature over the last four digits they are shown. The key-replacement notice, by mail and in the product, names the destination's last four digits and carries the disable control. The send gate also refuses any destination whose `set_at` falls inside a pin.

**Stopping is always allowed.** `DELETE /api/payouts/destination` and a `not_my_account` case are FRESH class, work from any session, and are exempt from the freeze and the pin, because removing a destination only stops money. Setting one again needs every gate above. Removal by key signature is instant and uncounted. Removals and `not_my_account` cases from sessions that carry no signature are counted across all accounts: more than `DEST_REMOVE_DAY_MAX` (10) in one day raises `dest_removal_tripwire` on both channels, and until a finance administrator acknowledges it each further unsigned removal parks the destination as `needs_reconfirm` (unpaid, and restored by one signature) and sends no removal notice. Money still stops for the person who asked; a forged-session wave can neither delete every destination nor mail every payee a genuine notice to enroll again. An unsigned `not_my_account` case past the limit is treated the same way: it parks the destination as `needs_reconfirm` and parks payments in flight under the rule of 5.6, and it does not set `payee_dispute`, so a forged wave cannot put a dispute hold on every payee. Nobody is left unpaid without knowing. The administrator's acknowledgement of the tripwire (`dest-removals/ack`, a delayed act, made once the session key has been rotated) sends `destination_reconfirm` to every payee whose destination the tripwire parked, and one signature by each restores it. Nothing restores parked destinations in bulk, because some of those requests were genuine.

**Fallback capture, designed and not built.** If the sandbox shows Mercury's hosted invite does not behave as assumed (section 20), US bank details are captured on an Eustress page that encrypts them in the browser to a relay public key; the Worker passes ciphertext it cannot read; the relay decrypts and creates the recipient with `POST /recipients`. Mercury offers no direct creation for international wire recipients, so that rail has no fallback and decision D22 returns to McKale.

### 5.5 Sanctions screening, inside the one identity check

No document goes to a second provider. `sanctions.mjs` runs on data Eustress already holds.

- **List sync.** A daily job fetches OFAC's SDN and consolidated lists (public, no key, no fixed IP; it follows the redirect). The exports are tens of megabytes and a Worker has 128 MB and no XML parser, so the raw response streams straight into R2, and a streaming tokenizer then reads that object by ranges in resumable scheduler steps with a bounded buffer (at most 4 MB held at once), building a compact index (names, aliases, dates of birth, nationalities, programs, and the entity list of banks) as `sanctions/index-{dataAsOf}.json`. A test feeds a full-size recorded file in small chunks and asserts the peak buffered bytes.
- **A sync must prove itself before it is current.** The entry count is within 10 percent of the previous index; `dataAsOf` is not earlier than the previous one; a fixed set of canary names and one Crimea address are present. A sync that fails any of these keeps the previous index and raises `sanctions_sync_rejected`; the age gate then stops sends if it persists. A truncated download can never become a small, fresh list.
- Inputs per payee: legal name, date of birth, KYC country, document issuing country, citizenship and residence from the tax form, bank country, for a wire recipient the bank's name and SWIFT code (screened against the entity list, since a clear-named payee can bank at a designated institution), and sign-in location history for the period. Sign-in locations are kept as `loc:{YYYY-MM}` in the payee's `TaxVault` (country and region per day, no raw address) for the life of the screening records. Logins from a blocked region are reason to know.
- The blocked-country list is dated, versioned configuration with a source for each line: the union of OFAC's territory embargoes (Cuba, Iran, North Korea, and the Crimea, Donetsk and Luhansk regions matched on address, bank location and sign-in history) and Mercury's own unsupported destinations. Syria is list screening only.
- Outcomes, stored as `screen:{seq}` with the list version and the inputs themselves (encrypted in the vault, so the 10 year record shows what was checked, not a hash of it): `clear`; `potential` (name similarity above threshold, compared with date of birth and country: hold `sanctions_hold`, review); `region_blocked` (a rejected transaction); `blocked` (a person confirmed the match). The money stops the moment a match is `potential`, by `sanctions_hold`. Confirming it is a delayed, signed, announced act (8.6), because it starts a federal reporting clock on a named person and must not be something a stolen key can do to every payee in a minute. When it takes effect the payable moves to `blocked_cents` by `block:{payee}:{seq}` and the account enters `sanctions_block`.
- **A block reaches the whole account, not only the payable.** OFAC prohibits all dealings with a blocked person and treats all property in which they have an interest as blocked. `sanctions_block` is a state in `AccountAuth`. While it stands the account is refused checkout creation, Wallet spend and Wallet credit, sales through its commerce hub, Delta use, plan changes, and every VALUE and FRESH route except reads. Every Ticket in the Wallet (bought, granted by a plan or earned) and every Delta lot is frozen in place: nothing is spent, credited, expired or burned while the block stands. The blocked-property inventory lists them (units and purchase cost) beside `blocked_cents`, so the initial and annual reports state everything Eustress holds. The subscription is neither cancelled nor refunded by code, since each of those is itself a dealing; what to do with it, and with a renewal charge that arrives after the block, is counsel's answer (section 19, item 17). A test holds that a blocked account can neither spend nor receive Tickets; its mutation gates only the payable.
- **Undoing a block.** `unblock` exists for a match that was wrong, or for a person OFAC has delisted or licensed. It is a delayed, signed, announced act that requires a recorded OFAC licence number, delisting notice or counsel reference; it moves the blocked cents to a review hold and clears `sanctions_block`. Nothing else moves blocked cents.
- **Who is screened.** Payees are screened as this section describes. Eustress already holds the document name, date of birth and issuing country of every verified account, so screening every account at verification and on each list change needs no second identity check. Whether screening only payees is defensible is counsel's question (section 19, item 16); `SANCTIONS_SCREEN_SCOPE` (`payees` or `all_verified`) carries the answer and the engine supports both. Under `all_verified` the screen of an account that is no payee lives where a payee's does: in that account's own `TaxVault` object, created at its first screen and holding only `screen:` records until the account enrolls. A block of such an account moves no cents (it has no payable); it writes `block:{account}:{seq}` as a record and sets `sanctions_block`, and the inventory and both reports follow from the block, not from a balance.
- **Every counterparty money is accepted from or sent to is screened, whatever `SANCTIONS_SCREEN_SCOPE` says.** An investor is screened when a subscription is accepted for funding, when its funding wire is matched, and when each repayment or return is planned (section 9). The originator name and bank of a Suspense credit on Bliss Treasury are screened, the bank against the entity list, before a refund to its sender or a transfer to Checking is planned (8.6). The screen is the name screen of this section (`screenCounterparty`), its record is kept on the act or the `xo:` record it gates, and it runs again when a delayed act takes effect. A `potential` result holds the act until review. A confirmed match blocks the money where it is, by the same delayed act as a payee's block: Dr 2930 or Dr 2100, Cr 2016 (`blocked_outside_cents`). It is never returned, repaid or transferred, it starts the same `ofac_report_due` clock, and it is listed in the same inventory and reports.
- A confirmed match or a rejection starts a 10 business day reporting clock (`ofac_report_due`). Any account in `sanctions_block` on June 30, whatever its `blocked_cents`, raises `ofac_annual_report_due`, and the alert carries the blocked-property inventory of each (blocked cents; Wallet Tickets by kind; Delta lots with units and purchase cost): the annual report of blocked property is due September 30. A test blocks an account with a zero payable and a Wallet balance and requires the annual alert; its mutation keys the alert on `blocked_cents`. Whether blocked amounts must sit in a separate blocked, interest-bearing account, and who opens it, is counsel's question (section 19, item 17). Blocked amounts are excluded from year-end returns until released.
- When: at enrollment, at every run build, immediately before every send, on every poll of a wire awaiting approval, and for every payee with a balance whenever the list version changes; for a counterparty outside the payee path, at each act that accepts or sends its money.
- A list older than `SANCTIONS_MAX_AGE_DAYS` (3) blocks every send. Screening records are kept 10 years.
- A one-page written sanctions policy signed by McKale and a yearly test (a known SDN name and a Crimea address must be caught, each with a mutation) complete the program.

### 5.6 US payouts by ACH

**Cadence.** Weekly, Tuesday at 7:00 AM Arizona time (2:00 PM UTC), which is before Mercury's same-day cutoff under either published time. A bank holiday moves it to the next business day. `ACH_CADENCE` accepts `weekly`, `daily` or `monthly`; decision D4. The ACH period token follows it: the ISO week (`2026-W46`), the day (`2026-11-17`) or the month (`2026-11`), and the relay's `CAP_ACH_RECIPIENT_PERIOD` names the same kind (7.5). Under every cadence no payment is sent to a recipient within 24 hours of the previous send to it, so two equal payments never fall inside Mercury's 24 hour same-amount guard. `ACH_CADENCE` and the relay's cadence settings (`CAP_ACH_RECIPIENT_PERIOD`, `ACH_RUN_WEEKDAY`, `CAP_ACH_WEEK_CENTS`, `CAP_ACH_30D_CENTS`; 7.5) are changed together; the preflight refuses a run when any of them does not fit the cadence. Accrual is daily in every case.

**Year end.** No ACH run starts in the last 3 business days of December or on January 1. Year totals and the withholding liability are both written when the bank debit posts, by its Arizona calendar date, so a form and a deposit can never disagree about the year. A payment whose debit posts in a later calendar year than it was built is flagged `tax_year_crossed` for the pre-filing report.

**Trigger.** The hourly tick calls the Treasury's `tick`; the scheduler derives the period from the clock (`business_days.mjs`, Arizona time) and calls `start_run {rail: "ach", period: "2026-W46"}`, a no-op when repeated. No cron string exists for it. The run is the scheduler's `run` stream, with its own backoff: ten payees a step, sends strictly one at a time.

**Step 1, preflight.** All required, or the run waits and raises one alert naming the reason: `PAYOUTS_MODE` is `live`; `MERCURY_ENV` matches `DEPLOY_ENV`; halt off; relay healthy, armed and reporting the same environment; Mercury readable; sanctions list fresh and accepted; tax rules for the year loaded; the tax key fingerprint pinned; a determinate bank wall for BT under 26 hours old with no shortfall; coverage; `verify_step` green; every Bliss Treasury outflow has its `pay:` record (15.3); the relay's period token kind, run schedule and weekly and 30 day caps fit the cadence (R27). A weekly run stays open for `ACH_RUN_WINDOW_BUSINESS_DAYS` (3): one that cannot start inside that window is skipped and the payables wait for the next, and one that has started re-examines, once each business day of the window, the payees it skipped for a reason that clears by itself.

**Order.** Payees are taken by the mature date of their oldest unpaid cent, oldest first (the index `payq:`). When `RUN_MAX_COUNT` or `RUN_MAX_CENTS` binds, the payees not reached are first in the next run; nobody is passed over twice by an accident of their id.

**Step 2, send gate and the timing rules.** `payeeGate(env, id, "send")`. A failure that clears by itself (cooling, first payout hold, a lapsed plan, below the minimum) leaves the cents in the payee's lots with a waiting reason on `payee:{id}`. A failure that needs an act sets a hold on the payable cents (`holdset`, 5.9).

The builder then applies, from the book, every timing rule the relay enforces from Mercury's history (7.5). The relay is the backstop, never the scheduler, and a rule of Eustress's own is never turned into a fault on a good bank account.

- **A first payment in its watch.** A payee whose destination version has a first payment that is not yet `settled` is skipped with the waiting reason `first_payment_in_watch`. The second payment to a destination is built only after the first has settled with no return, which for a first ACH that posts on a Wednesday is late in the next week's window or the run after it.
- **An earlier period still open.** A payee with a payment of an earlier period whose bank debit has not posted (`reserved`, `sending`, `submitted`, `awaiting_approval`, `review`) is skipped with `earlier_payment_open` and re-examined while the run's window is open. A run that slipped across a week boundary therefore never meets its own successor at the bank.
- **First payments per day.** At most `FIRST_PAYMENTS_DAY_MAX` (5, equal to the relay's `CAP_FIRST_PAYMENTS_DAY`) first payments are built per day. The sixth waits for the next business day of the window with `first_payments_day`; established payees behind it are built as usual and the run never pauses for it. A launch cohort is paid over several days, and nobody already proven waits for it.
- **Recipient age.** The destination's `usable_after` already includes the relay's 72 hours from its first-seen time (5.4).
- **24 hours between sends** to one recipient, under any cadence.

Tests, each with a mutation: the week-2 build after a first payment is skipped, not refused (mutation: cap the second payment and build it); a W46 payment sent on the Monday of W47 does not block W47 and the two are never inside 24 hours (mutation: count the week by send date); the sixth first payment of a day is deferred while the seventh, established payee is paid (mutation: pause the run).

**Step 3, build.** The build write first nets every open clawback and every open stray-payment remainder of the payee against the lots, oldest first (`clawnet:{claw_id}:{n}` and the netting of `straypay`, in the same put as `pay:{pay_id}`), so cents that came back into the payable after a receivable arose (a void, a rejected request, a released hold, a fee credit) never leave while the receivable stays open. `gross` is then taken from what remains in the payee's lots, oldest first, up to `PAY_MAX_CENTS` and, for the first payment to this destination version, up to `FIRST_PAY_MAX_CENTS`; what a cap leaves stays in the lots for later runs with no manual step. Below `ACH_MIN_CENTS` nothing is built. Withholding comes from `withholdingFor` (6.5), called for every line with the form of the payment's tax snapshot, on the gross less any cents tagged `withholding_settled`; it also carries any tax the payee owes as under-withheld from a late US-work report, recovered first (6.4). `net = gross - withholding`. One write posts `pay:{pay_id}`: the lots fall by the cents netted and the cents taken from each, `owed_cents -= gross`, `reserved_cents += gross`, and the payment record is written as `reserved` with its lines (`{lot, cents, us_cents}`), the destination snapshot, the tax snapshot, its index `payidx:ach:{payee}:{period}` and its history row. Withholding is not a liability yet: it becomes one when the bank debit posts. Before any bank call, the intent is copied outside the Treasury (`pay-intents/{pay_id}`, encrypted, in the finance backup bucket), so a lost Treasury can be rebuilt without paying anyone twice (15.3).

Run limits: `RUN_MAX_CENTS`, `RUN_MAX_COUNT`, and a tripwire that halts when the run total exceeds three times the median of the last four runs. The tripwire arms only after four completed runs and only for a run above `RUN_TRIPWIRE_FLOOR_CENTS`.
