An administrator's acknowledgement of that one run (`runs/{id}/ack-anomaly`, a delayed act whose clock runs under the halt the anomaly set) makes the cause false for that run id; the run finishes after the unhalt, inside its window (8.6).

**Step 4, send.** Immediately before every relay call (a first send, a resend, a send in a run that resumed after a pause or a halt), the Treasury runs `payeeGate(send)` again, requires the destination version to equal the payment's snapshot, and requires a fresh sanctions screen. A failure sends nothing and parks the payment under the parking rule below: `payvoid` routes its cents to the hold the gate names (or to `blocked_cents`), at once for a payment with no attempt, after the confirming read for one whose attempts were all definitely refused, and never for one whose attempt ended unknown, which only records the request.

**The attempt is committed before the call, by check-and-set.** `payeeGate(send)` is awaited I/O (`AccountAuth`, `TaxVault`, the sanctions index), and a Durable Object opens its input gate during every await, so an event can be delivered between the gate's read and the attempt. The put is therefore conditional. When the gate has returned, the Treasury reads the `pay:` record again and, with no await between that read and the put, writes `attempts += 1`, `last_attempt_at` and the state `sending` only when the record is still `reserved` (or is the unknown payment the resolver is sending again), carries no `park_requested`, and its destination version still equals its snapshot. Otherwise no put is made and no call is issued. A ban that arrived during the gate found no attempt and parked the payment at once; the step finds it `void` and stops. A Durable Object opens its input gate while it awaits the relay, so a ban, a `not_my_account` case, a key replacement, a destination removal or an administrator void can run in the middle of a send, and a deploy can reset the object mid-fetch. The committed attempt is what each of them finds. A payment read in `sending` with no recorded answer is unknown by definition: the latch is set, it has a resend age, and the resolver takes it.

**Parking a payment that has an attempt.** Three cases, one rule each. A payment with no attempt is parked at once. A payment whose every attempt ended in a definite, signed refusal before Mercury is parked after a confirming read, taken at least `RESEND_READ_GAP_MINUTES` after the last attempt, that shows nothing at the bank: no transaction carrying the payment id and no send-money request that is this payment's. Until then `park_payment`, `void_payment` and every `payvoid` and `payfail` path record `park_requested {reason, by, at}` on the payment and return, and the resolver applies it when the read allows. A payment with `unknown_seen` is never parked, voided, failed or returned to the lots by an automatic path, whatever a read shows: one empty read ten minutes after a lost answer proves nothing about a request still on its way. A park, a gate failure and the close of its window only record `park_requested` on it. It is not sent again while that request stands, the reads continue, and it leaves `reserved` by one of two doors: a transaction or a request found by read, or `review` at the resend age, where the review entry carries the park that was asked for. When the read finds the payment at the bank instead, the payment is `submitted`, nothing moves to a hold, and the alert of the event that asked for the park carries the Mercury transaction id so McKale can cancel it before its cutoff. Money that has left is never also sitting in a hold. Tests: a ban delivered during the awaited send pays once and holds nothing; an object reset mid-send resumes as unknown; the mutation of both writes the attempt after the call. A ban delivered during the awaited gate pays nothing; its mutation writes the attempt from the stale copy. A send that times out on the last day of its window and lands twenty minutes after an empty read is still `reserved` when its debit posts; its mutation returns an unknown payment to the lots at the window close.

Then relay `POST /v1/ach` with payment id, recipient id, bank fingerprint and its version, amount in integer cents and the period.

| Relay answer | Meaning | Book |
|---|---|---|
| `accepted` or `replayed` (Mercury 200, a 409 on the same key, or a transaction the relay found carrying this payment id), with recipient and amount equal to the request | Mercury has the payment | `submitted`; bank transaction id stored |
| `mismatch` (a transaction carries this payment id with another recipient or amount) | Something else used this id | `review`; halt; `duplicate_payment` |
| `awaiting_approval` (Mercury turned the send into an approval item, or the relay's look-up found a send-money request that is this payment's under the resolver of 5.7) | Mercury holds the payment for a person | `awaiting_approval` with the request id; required alert `ach_awaiting_approval`; tracked like a wire request (5.7) and approved against the same live manifest with its re-check: approved becomes `submitted`, rejected is a `payvoid` back to the lots |
| `refused_recipient` (the recipient is deleted, was not created through an invite, or its fingerprint differs) | Fault on the destination; this request created nothing | After the confirming read: `payfail` to a hold `recipient_refused` or `recipient_changed`; destination `needs_attention` |
| `refused_wait {reason, not_before}`, with reason `too_young`, `first_in_watch`, `period_used`, `first_payments_day` or `outside_run_window` | A timing rule of Eustress's own that the builder applies first; this request created nothing | Stays `reserved` and is sent again under the same key at `not_before` when that falls inside the run's window (a definite, signed refusal has no resend age). Otherwise, after the confirming read, `payvoid` back to the lots with that waiting reason, and the cents join the next period's run. Never a hold, never a change to the destination, never a pause of the run. `relay_wait_disagreement` is raised, because the book and the relay disagree about a clock |
| `refused_cap`, `disarmed` (signed relay answers), `not_sent` (the request was never issued: signing failed, the step's operation budget could not cover the call, or a preflight refused, all before the attempt was committed) | Our limit or our outage; nothing reached Mercury | Stays `reserved`; the run pauses; alert |
| `permission` (401 or 403 from Mercury on a write) | The token lost its scope | Stays `reserved`; the rail that token serves pauses; `bank_token_degraded` with the runbook. A refusal of the send token halts, because sweeps use it too |
| `bank_rejected` (a 400 the relay can prove is a schema error) | Mercury refused the request shape | Stays `reserved`; retried next step; after 5 refusals and the confirming read, a hold `provider_rejected` |
| `unknown` (timeout, 5xx, 429, an unexpected shape, any transport error from the binding once the request was issued, an answer without a valid relay signature, any other 400 including Mercury's 24 hour duplicate refusal, or the relay rejecting the request itself) | Outcome not known | Stays `reserved`; resolved as below |

**Unknown outcome.** The payment is never moved back to `owed_cents` and never given another key. `first_unknown_at` is written once, at the first attempt that ended unknown, and no later attempt moves it. The next steps read Mercury two ways, every time: the BT transaction list over a date window, comparing the note field exactly against the payment id (it is the note of every payment); and the send-money request list for Bliss Treasury (`pendingApproval` and `approved`) under the one resolver both rails use (5.7), because a direct send that crosses a Mercury limit can become an approval item that is not yet a transaction. A request is this payment's only when its recipient, amount and memo code equal the payment's, it was created at or after the payment's first attempt, and its request id is stored on no other `pay:` record. A transaction found with the same recipient and amount: `submitted`. A request found: `awaiting_approval`. Either found with another recipient or amount: `review`. Nothing found in either list on two reads at least `RESEND_READ_GAP_MINUTES` (10) apart, no park requested, and `first_unknown_at` younger than `RESEND_MAX_AGE_HOURS` (20): send again with the same key, after the check-and-set of step 4. The age is measured from the first unknown attempt because that is the attempt that may have landed. The 20 hours sit inside Mercury's 24 hour same-amount guard only when they are counted from it, so a resend that races a landed first attempt is refused by the bank, and a resend that itself ends unknown never buys another 20 hours. Older than that: the payment goes to `review` as `outcome_unknown` and is never resent automatically. **A refusal by Mercury ends resends at once.** An `unknown` answer that carries a Mercury 400 (any 400 the relay cannot prove is a schema error, the 24 hour duplicate refusal among them) means Mercury received the request and refused it. A duplicate refusal is evidence that a payment exists, and a resend inside 24 hours can only be refused again, so the reads continue and, when two of them find nothing, the payment goes to `review` instead of to a resend. The Mercury fake has an approval-converting mode; the test's mutation resolves from the transaction list only. Two more tests. A first attempt that landed unseen and is answered by duplicate refusals is in `review` before the guard lapses and is never accepted twice (mutation: measure the age from the last attempt). A capped payee's payment of the same amount as last week's approved request is sent and is not captured by that request (mutation: match a request on recipient and amount alone).

**The unknown latch.** Once any attempt on a payment has ended unknown (`unknown_seen`), only `accepted`, `replayed`, or a transaction or a request found by read may move it forward. Every other answer, however definite it looks, sends it to `review`: a later `bank_rejected` or `refused_recipient` may be the echo of a first attempt that landed. No automatic path releases it: not `payfail`, not a park, not a gate failure at resend time, not the close of its window. For a payment with no unknown attempt, `payfail` requires the confirming read by note and of the request list, taken at least `RESEND_READ_GAP_MINUTES` after the last attempt, that shows no transaction and no request of its own. A payment released to a hold or to the lots is therefore one that Mercury definitely never received.

**Resuming.** A payment whose every answer was a definite, signed refusal before Mercury (`refused_cap`, `disarmed`, `permission`), or that was never issued (`not_sent`), has no resend age: it may be sent at any later time under the same key, after the gate re-check of step 4. A payment the object finds in `sending` after a restart is not one of these; it is unknown. A paused run resumes at the first tick after its cause clears (the relay's cap period rolls over, the halt lifts, the token is restored), inside the run's window. A payment still `reserved` when its run's window closes, with no unknown attempt, is returned to the lots (`payvoid`, after the confirming read when it has an attempt) and joins the next run. A payment with `unknown_seen` is not: it stays `reserved` past its window, its payee is skipped by the next run as `earlier_payment_open`, and it ends as `submitted`, `awaiting_approval` or `review` within the resend age. Nothing else waits in `reserved` for a window that has passed. An outage that Mercury or the relay answered definitely costs its own length when it is shorter than the window and until the next run when it is longer, never a queue of manual reviews; only a payment whose own answer was lost can reach one.

**Review.** An administrator who has read Mercury either records the transaction found, or voids the payment (`payvoid`, a delayed and announced act as in 8.6), which returns its cents to the payee's lots for the next period's run. A void on either rail requires the confirming read of both lists with zero open requests for that recipient, and the read is taken again in the write that applies the void: a transaction that posted overnight, or a request McKale has yet to clear from the Mercury dashboard, cancels the void. When the review entry carries a park request, the void routes the cents to the hold that request named instead of to the lots.

**Step 5, track.** Driven by the Cashbook's own reads; a webhook only schedules them. The branches follow the state of the book, never the status word alone.

- The debit row posts (`sent` with `postedAt`): `paydebit:{pay_id}` in the row's own posting. `reserved_cents -= gross`, `watch_cents += net`, the withholding bucket and `wire_fee_clearing_cents` rise; the year totals and month totals are written in the same put by posting date; the trace number is stored; a `payout_sent` notice is queued. A second posted debit carrying the same payment id goes to Suspense and halts (R4). So does a posted debit whose note names a payment in `void`, `failed` or `returned` state: money left under an id the book had closed. Either one, when it could not be cancelled, leaves Suspense as a stray payment to the payee whose recipient received it (8.6).
- Before any debit has posted, Mercury shows `failed` or `cancelled`: `payfail` to a hold `provider_rejected`, subject to the latch and the confirming read. No tax entry exists to undo.
- Mercury `blocked`: hold `bank_compliance_hold`, required alert; the payment stays in `reserved_cents` until Mercury resolves it.
- **After the debit has posted, any of `failed`, `reversed` or `cancelled` on the original, or any credit linked by `relatedTransactions` (`ReturnToOriginalTransaction`, `PaymentRefundToFailedPayment`), at any later time, is a return.** It is keyed by the payment: `payret:{pay_id}`, once. Mercury can show one return three ways: as a linked credit row, as a status change on the original with no separate row, or as both. The cash is posted from the linked credit row when one exists. A status change alone marks the payment `return_pending`; if no linked row appears within `RETURN_SHAPE_WAIT_HOURS` (24) and a determinate wall shows the cash back, the changed row posts the return alone (`payret` under `mt:{txn}:{resourceVersion}`, never the reversing entry of 2.2) and the return is applied. A second, different return fact for a payment that already has its `payret` goes to Suspense and to review; it never adds to the hold twice. Matching is by Mercury transaction id, never by amount and name.
- **A return Mercury does not link.** A returned wire commonly comes back as a fresh incoming wire, and a returned ACH can arrive as an unrelated credit, for less than was sent, while the original still reads `sent`. Such a credit posts to Suspense like any unexplained credit. It leaves by the classification `payment_return_unlinked`, a signed, delayed act bound to bank facts: the credit's counterparty or originating details match the payment's recipient; the amount is no greater than the payment's net; it posted after the payment's debit; the payment has no `payret`; and a Mercury reference (a trace number or a support case) is recorded. It applies `payret:{pay_id}` with the same hold and year-total effects as a linked return. While an unexplained credit sits in Suspense on Bliss Treasury, `payset` is held for the payments the credit names: one debited before the credit posted whose recipient id, legal name, memo code or trace number appears on it. A credit that names no payment holds no settlement, however many payments are at least its size: two micro-deposits of a few cents from a stranger must not freeze every payment in its watch. Each of those payments settles at the end of its own watch window on its fresh read, and a return proven later reopens it (step 6). A test posts an unlinked credit that carries a payment's trace number and requires one hold and no settlement; its mutation settles on the status word alone. A second test posts a credit of three cents that names nothing and requires every payment in its watch to settle on time; its mutation holds every payment of at least the credit's amount.
- On a return the amount that came back goes to a hold; the destination is disabled; a notice is sent; nothing is retried automatically. **The year totals of the year in which the payment's debit posted are reduced by the cash that came back (`returned_cents`) and by nothing else**, in the same write, whenever the return posts. When that year is already frozen as a draft, furnished or filed, a correction flag is set on it as well, and the offline correction export carries the figure. Withholding already taken stays withheld, deposited and reported, so the gross reported for a fully returned payment equals its withholding (plus a wire fee and any unrecovered loss). **Only cents that tax was taken on are tagged.** The payment record holds the withholding of each line. Cents restored from a line that carried nonzero withholding are tagged `withholding_settled`, so they are not withheld on twice when they are paid again. Cents restored from a line that carried none go back untagged: that payment was never made, its repayment is the payment, and `withholdingFor` judges it under the certificate and the B-notice state in force on the day it is paid again (6.8). A test returns a payment that carried no withholding, moves the payee's B notice to `withholding_on`, repays, and requires 24 percent; its mutation tags every return.

**Return reasons.** Mercury's `reasonForFailure` is stored raw on the payment and branched: closed account, no account, invalid number, non-transaction account (R02, R03, R04, R20): destination `bad`, hold `ach_returned`, a fresh enrollment required. Frozen account (R16): hold `ach_returned`, review, sanctions re-screen. Credit refused by the receiver (R23): hold, review. Anything unrecognised: review.

**Step 6, settle.** Posted plus `RETURN_WATCH_BUSINESS_DAYS` (5 for ACH, 10 for wires) with no return, a fresh read showing the transaction still `sent`, and no unexplained Suspense credit that names it: `payset`. `watch_cents -= net`, `paid_cents += net`, and the destination version is marked proven (`proven_at`). The record is kept for good. **A late return reopens it.** A `payret` that arrives after `payset`, linked or unlinked, undoes the memo move in the same put (`paid_cents -= net`, the returned amount to a hold, `return_loss_cents` for any difference), reduces the year totals of the payment's debit year by the cash that came back, and clears `proven_at` when the payment was the destination's first.

No code path creates a Nacha reversal. Reversals are allowed for four error types within five banking days; the constant payment id removes the one that matters (a duplicate). Clawback is contractual netting (5.10).

### 5.7 International payouts by wire, monthly

**Calendar.** Built on the 3rd of each month at 7:00 AM Arizona time (next business day if needed) for everything matured by then. Queued the same morning. Approved in Mercury by McKale. Mercury sends within 1 to 3 business days; arrival can take 5 more. The payout page says: "Paid monthly. Allow up to 8 business days after the 3rd."

**The month stays open.** A wire period is the calendar month, named by its token (`2026-11`). Every rule that counts wires per month counts by that token, in the Treasury and in the relay: a November wire approved on December 1 is November's, neither blocks December's wire nor counts against December's cap. If the run cannot start on the 3rd (a halt, a stale wall, a degraded token), `start_run {wire, YYYY-MM}` is tried again each business day until `WIRE_RUN_CUTOFF_DAY` (the 24th; the 19th in December). Only then is the month skipped, with a `payout_delayed` notice to each payee. **The run is not a single pass.** While the month is open, each business day the run re-examines every payee it skipped for a reason that clears by itself (an earlier month's payment not yet debited, a first wire in its watch, a transient gate failure, a list that was stale that morning) and builds them once the reason is gone. In December nothing is built after the 19th, by a first start or by a re-examination: a payee not built by then waits for the January run. A payee whose November request is approved on December 4 is built for December on the first business day of the open month after November's debit posts, not on January 3; a test holds that, and its mutation builds in a single pass. A short outage or a slow approval costs days, never a month. The payment index stays `payidx:wire:{payee}:{YYYY-MM}`, so one wire per payee per month holds however often the run looks.

**Build.** The same preflight, order and send gate, plus: the W-8BEN (or W-9 for a US person banking abroad) is valid; the destination country is `enabled` or `trial` in `wire_countries.json`; and, for a W-8 filer only, the foreign-services certification stands and the payee's income lines are open for foreign payees in the signed tax rules. Where the payee was is not tested at the build: a build pays cents earned months earlier, so each row carried its source from the night it accrued, and the build reads `us_cents` from the lot lines and nothing else (6.4). Those conditions follow the form, never the rail: a W-9 filer on the wire rail is reported on a Form 1099 wherever the work is done, signed no such certification, and passes the same tax conditions as an ACH payee. `gross` is taken from the lots up to `WIRE_PAY_MAX_CENTS`, and for a first wire up to `FIRST_WIRE_MAX_CENTS`. `gross - withholding` below `WIRE_MIN_GROSS_CENTS` (5000) builds nothing, so the fee is never more than 30 percent of what the payee is due after tax. `fee = WIRE_FEE_CENTS` (1500). `net = gross - withholding - fee`. One write: `owed_cents -= gross`, `reserved_cents += gross`; the payment is written as `reserved` and its intent copied out, as for ACH.

**One fee per wire.** The recipient bears the fee of each wire (locked decision 5). A balance larger than a cap goes out over more than one month and each wire carries its own 15.00 USD. The payout page and every statement say so in plain words before the first wire: "Your first wire is limited to 100.00 USD. A balance above a wire limit is sent in later months, and each wire has its own fee." Decision D5 records the alternative in which Eustress bears the fee of a wire its own cap forced.

**Queue.** Relay `POST /v1/wire-request`. The relay always sends `chargeType: "ours"` (a test fails if the field is ever absent, because the fee policy is a stated choice), `paymentMethod: "internationalWire"`, a purpose of `contractor` with the payee's legal name read from Mercury's recipient, and a memo it builds itself from a fixed template, such as "Eustress earnings November 2026 EP-4F2A91". The amount is `net`. The payment is `awaiting_approval` with Mercury's request id.

**A request is not a transaction, so requests have their own resolver.** A request Mercury accepted is no transaction until it is approved; it carries no note and no idempotency key that a read can search; and Mercury documents the 409 replay and the 24 hour guard for direct sends only. So:

- Before it creates a request, the relay lists `GET /request-send-money` for BT (`pendingApproval` and `approved`, created in the month) and looks for one with the same recipient, amount and memo code. Found: it answers `replayed` with that request. The relay refuses a second open request for one recipient.
- An unknown wire answer is resolved by the Worker's own read of that same list, since the first attempt, matched on recipient id, amount and memo code, and of the transaction list for approved ones. A wire request is never sent again before that list has been read and shows nothing.
- **Both rails use this resolver.** On the ACH rail a direct send that Mercury turned into an approval item is found the same way: recipient id, amount and memo code, created at or after the payment's first attempt. And a request id belongs to one payment: a request already stored on another `pay:` record never matches. `approved` is a terminal status, so without those bounds last week's approved request for the same recipient and the same capped amount would be taken for this week's payment, every week.
- The relay's monthly wire cap and its one-per-recipient rule count open requests as well as transactions, each by the month token in its memo, never by the date it was approved.
- A void of a wire payment requires zero open requests for that recipient. The wires list and check R4 flag any recipient with two open requests.
- Whether `request-send-money` honours a repeated key is tested in the sandbox before the wire step is built, and the Mercury fake models a request endpoint that does not.

**Approval is a live decision, not a snapshot.** The request waits days; the payee's standing can change while it waits.

- On every poll, each `awaiting_approval` payment passes `payeeGate(send)` again with its snapshot check and a fresh sanctions screen. A failure marks the row `do_not_approve` on the wires list and raises a required alert on both channels naming the request id to reject. The ban route and the `not_my_account` case notify the Treasury at once, without waiting for the poll. After McKale rejects the request, `payvoid` routes the cents to the matching hold (or to `blocked_cents`). The mark follows the gate in both directions: when a later poll passes again (a sanctions list that was stale is fresh, a plan's renewal gap has closed), `do_not_approve` clears by itself, the alert is withdrawn on both channels, and the next manifest says so beside the row. McKale never rejects a payee for a failure that has already healed.
- The relay re-verifies the bank fingerprint of every pending request's recipient, on either rail, hourly, again immediately before it issues the manifest, and every `WIRE_LIVE_RECHECK_MINUTES` (5) while a manifest is live. A change posts "do not approve request X" to the out-of-band channel, declares the live manifest void there, and the Treasury marks the payment for void.
- McKale approves against a **ready-to-approve manifest** that the relay issues on request to `ALERT_WEBHOOK` and that expires after `WIRE_MANIFEST_TTL_MINUTES` (30): request id, recipient id, amount, fingerprint tail, and for a first-time recipient the legal name and country, so he can check them against the recipient announcement posted when that payee enrolled. Both names are the one the Worker supplied at the invite, so the comparison shows only that the recipient was not renamed at Mercury since; whether the person is a real payee is the month close's question (7.7). The list is generated on the day of approval; nothing is approved from the list of the day of queueing. `GET /api/admin/finance/wires` shows each request id with an opaque payee reference, country, gross, fee, net and the current do-not-approve set (the payee behind a reference is a signed read, 8.8); the two lists come from systems that cannot alter each other. The approval window is therefore at most one re-check interval wide, not closed: a bank detail edited at Mercury in the minutes between the last re-check and McKale's approval is paid unless a send-money request snapshots the details it was created with (Q7), and is found after the wire posts by `wire_details_mismatch`. The same manifest, re-check and window cover an ACH payment that Mercury turned into an approval item (5.6), which McKale also approves in the dashboard.
- One required alert per run: "N wires totalling X await approval in Mercury". Reminders on business days 3 and 6 carry the current do-not-approve set. The Worker polls each request with the read token (no webhook exists for approvals). `approved`: the transaction is found by `requestId` and tracked as in 5.6. `rejected` or `cancelled`: `payvoid` returns the cents to the lots (or to the hold a gate named), no fault recorded. Each request stands alone, so a partly approved batch simply has some payments moving and some waiting. A payee whose earlier payment has not yet debited, its request open or approved and not yet posted, is skipped by the next month's build that morning (`earlier_payment_open`, 5.6) and re-examined each business day while that month is open. After `WIRE_APPROVAL_DEADLINE_BUSINESS_DAYS` (10) each waiting payee gets a `payout_delayed` notice and the alert repeats daily. A stand-in approver is decision D2.
- After a wire posts, the relay fingerprints the same routing tuple (5.4) from the Mercury transaction's details and compares it with the enrolled fingerprint; a difference raises `wire_details_mismatch`.
- **No request stays open across December 31.** In December no wire is built after the 19th, and every request still open on or after the 20th is flagged for rejection, whenever it was built; its payment is voided when McKale rejects it and is built again in January under that year's rules, so the tax computed at build and the year of the bank posting agree. The same December 20 sitting confirms that next year's rules file is activated (6.5).

**The recipient bears the fee, exactly.** The statement shows gross, withholding, a wire fee of 15.00 USD and net. When the wire's debit posts, the fee moves into `wire_fee_clearing_cents`. That account clears only on evidence: the wire transaction's `feeId` or a linked fee row, looked for on all three Mercury accounts; or an administrator act (`wirefee-none`) after Mercury confirms no fee was charged. There is no refund on a timer. At 10 business days with no fee found, `wire_fee_missing` fires and the cents stay in clearing. A fee below the deduction returns the difference to the payee's payable as a **fee credit**; it is never Eustress income. A fee credit is treated like a return: in the same write the payment's year-total gross and wire fee fall by the credited cents, so they are reported once, when they are paid, and the cents enter the lot of the payment's first line under the tagging rule of 5.6. They are tagged `withholding_settled` when that line carried withholding, so tax is not taken on them twice, and untagged when it carried none, so the rules in force at their payment decide. `wirefee-none` is a fee credit of the whole deduction. A fee above it is borne by Eustress (6050, against the carry), `wire_fee_mismatch` fires and the setting is corrected. A fee billed on Checking or Engine Expenses is an expense there, recovered from clearing, with the cash it leaves in Bliss Treasury released into carry. A fee that arrives after a `wirefee-none` undoes that credit as far as the payee's payable allows (`wirefee-late`, 2.6): the cents come back out of the payee's lots, `withholding_settled` cents first, and the year totals the credit lowered are restored. What the payable cannot cover is borne by Eustress as a Mercury fee no payee owes (6050, against the carry). No receivable is opened and nothing is netted from later earnings. When and on which account Mercury bills the fee is settled by the first supervised live wire, before wires open to payees.

**What the fee does not cover.** The statement and the payout page state: the amount received can be lower than net; a receiving or intermediary bank's own charges and any currency conversion at the receiving bank are the recipient's. A currency conversion fee charged by Mercury itself (1 percent when a wire leaves in local currency) is debited from Bliss Treasury against the carry, raises `fx_fee_unrecovered`, and never opens a shortfall against claims; wires are assumed to leave in USD until the sandbox shows how currency is chosen, and decision D20 says who bears that fee.

**Countries.** `wire_countries.json` is dated configuration: per country `{state, purpose_code, local_currency_only, notes}` with `state` of `disabled`, `trial` or `enabled`. McKale sets a country to `trial` by configuration: one wire, at the first-wire cap, may be built to it. A clean settlement of that wire moves the country to `enabled`. A country that needs a purpose-of-payment code or local-currency delivery stays `disabled` until the relay route supports it. A country is also enabled only when counsel's answer on local platform-reporting rules for it is on file (section 19, item 29).

**Rejected or returned wire.** Before the debit: hold `wire_rejected`. After: the amount that came back (which can differ from the amount sent when a foreign bank converted it) goes to a hold; a fee Mercury charged stays with the payee and a second attempt carries a second fee; `return_loss_cents` records sent minus returned for the accountant's rule on who bears it. The destination is disabled.

**Trace.** Every statement carries the bank trace number or wire reference and Mercury's transaction id. A payee who has not received a wire 8 business days after it posted opens a trace from the statement (`POST /api/payouts/case`, kind `not_received`); the hold `trace_open` is set, `payout_trace_requested` goes to McKale, and he asks Mercury for the trace or recall.

### 5.8 Settings

All integer cents, hours or days. Changed only by a reviewed deploy.

| Setting | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `PAYOUTS_MODE` | `off` | `off`: shares stay in the pool. `shadow`: shares are computed as estimates for the administrator view; nothing posts. `accrue`: eligible shares are payables; nothing is sent. `live`: pay runs send |
| `SHADOW_VISIBLE` | `false` | Whether an account that holds a qualifying plan sees its own shadow estimate (gate G13) |
| `ENROLLMENT_MODE` | `off` | Section 5.4 |
| `SWEEP_MODE` | `off` | Section 4.6 |
| `WIRE_MODE` | `request` | `request`: wires are queued for dashboard approval. `direct`: only if Mercury enables it |
| `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE` | `true` | The gift checkout sells today and stays open. Decision D27 records McKale's choice to close it; gate G10 reopens it |
| `LEGACY_CONNECT_PAYOUTS` | `false` | Section 11, item 1 |
| `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET` | unset | The book schema the Treasury may migrate to on open. Unset: no migration runs, whatever code is deployed (16.1) |
| `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` | unset | The UTC time from which every checkout object must have its creation record (3.2). Unset: records are written and none is required |
| `STRIPE_EE_BANK_ID` | the Engine Expenses bank id | The only destination a Stripe payout may carry (4.3) |
| `MERCURY_ENV` | unset | `sandbox` or `production`. Must match the token prefix, the base URL and `DEPLOY_ENV`, or every Mercury call is refused. Unset fails closed |
| `ACH_CADENCE` | `weekly` | Decision D4 |
| `PLAN_GATE_AT_SEND` | `true` | Decision D3 |
| `EFFORT_MATURITY_DAYS`, `VALUE_MATURITY_DAYS` | 7, 120 | Days before a row can be paid |
| `WALLET_LOTS_LIVE` | `false` | Value rows mature only when true |
| `SCORE_SETTLE_DAYS` | 14 | Days after a sale before it files value score (the refund window is 72 hours) |
| `SUSPENDED_ACCRUAL_MAX_DAYS`, `TAX_FORM_GRACE_DAYS` | 30, 30 | Section 5.2 |
| `PUBLIC_K_MIN`, `PUBLIC_SET_CHANGE_MIN` | 10, 5 | Smallest group a public figure may describe; smallest change of the enrolled set in a month whose split is published |
| `ACH_MIN_CENTS` | 1000 | Smallest ACH payment |
| `WIRE_MIN_GROSS_CENTS`, `WIRE_FEE_CENTS` | 5000, 1500 | Smallest balance wired, after withholding; fee deducted from each wire |
| `PAY_MAX_CENTS`, `FIRST_PAY_MAX_CENTS` | 50000, 2500 | Largest single ACH; largest first ACH to a destination. The rest follows in later runs |
| `WIRE_PAY_MAX_CENTS`, `FIRST_WIRE_MAX_CENTS` | 200000, 10000 | Largest single wire; largest first wire to a destination. The rest follows in later months |
| `WIRE_RUN_CUTOFF_DAY`, `WIRE_MANIFEST_TTL_MINUTES`, `WIRE_LIVE_RECHECK_MINUTES` | 24, 30, 5 | Last day of the month a wire may be built, by a first start or a re-examination (the 19th in December, 5.7); life of a ready-to-approve manifest; the relay's fingerprint re-check interval while a manifest is live (both set on the relay as well) |
| `RUN_MAX_CENTS`, `RUN_MAX_COUNT`, `RUN_TRIPWIRE_FLOOR_CENTS` | 200000, 50, 50000 | Per run; the run total below which the anomaly tripwire stays quiet |
| `FIRST_PAYMENTS_DAY_MAX`, `ACH_RUN_WINDOW_BUSINESS_DAYS` | 5, 3 | First payments built per day (the relay's `CAP_FIRST_PAYMENTS_DAY`); how long a weekly run stays open for a late start and for re-examination |
| `SWEEP_MAX_CENTS`, `SWEEP_DAY_MAX_CENTS`, `SWEEP_MIN_INTERVAL_DAYS`, `SWEEP_COVER_MAX` | 500000, 1000000, 1, 60 | Per installment, per day, batching; the most payouts one installment covers, which bounds the landing write (4.4) |
| `OUTBOX_STEPS_MAX` | 8 | Steps one Wallet alarm runs before it re-arms (5.10) |
| `EE_FLOOR_MIN_CENTS`, `DISPUTE_COVER_MIN_CENTS`, `DISPUTE_COVER_DAYS` | 20000, 100000, 120 | The fixed part of the Engine Expenses floor; the deposits that add dispute cover to it |
| `CARRY_ALERT_CENTS`, `CARRY_DEFICIT_MAX_CENTS` | 10000, 10000 | Positive carry that alerts after 14 days; the most Eustress may owe the claims side |
| `BANK_WALL_INDETERMINATE_MAX` | 6 | Consecutive indeterminate walls before a halt |
| `WALL_PENDING_CLASSES` | empty | Per account, the classes of pending rows Mercury moves out of `currentBalance` before they post (2.4) |
| `REVERSAL_CLEARING_MAX_DAYS` | 10 | Age of an item in 2410 that alerts |
| `DEST_COOLING_HOURS`, `FIRST_PAYOUT_HOLD_DAYS` | 72, 14 | A destination just set or changed; a payee just enrolled |
| `RESEND_MAX_AGE_HOURS`, `RESEND_READ_GAP_MINUTES` | 20, 10 | Section 5.6 |
| `RETURN_WATCH_BUSINESS_DAYS`, `RETURN_SHAPE_WAIT_HOURS` | 5 (ACH), 10 (wire); 24 | Posted to settled; how long a status-only return waits for its linked row |
| `WIRE_APPROVAL_DEADLINE_BUSINESS_DAYS` | 10 | Payees are told after this |
| `SANCTIONS_MAX_AGE_DAYS` | 3 | An older list blocks sends |
| `BANK_POLL_MINUTES` | 10 | Mercury events poll; doubles up to 60 on a 429 |
| `ADMIN_DELAY_MINUTES`, `ADMIN_DAY_MAX_CENTS` | 720, 100000 | Delay for administrator acts; daily total for acts that send money toward a payee (8.6) |
| `STOP_ACT_DAY_MAX`, `BAN_PAYEE_DAY_MAX`, `DEST_REMOVE_DAY_MAX`, `DEST_CONFIRM_MONTH_MAX` | 5, 3, 10, 3 | Stop-only acts a day before further ones are delayed; bans of enrolled payees a day before the tripwire; unsigned destination removals a day before they park instead of removing; destination confirms per account per month |
| `ADMIN_READ_TTL_MINUTES`, `ADMIN_READ_DAY_MAX` | 5, 200 | Life of a signed read challenge; per-payee and export reads per administrator key per day (8.8) |
| `TIN_UNVERIFIED_PAY_MAX_CENTS` | from the rules file | Cumulative payments to a payee whose TIN envelope has not passed the offline check. For a W-9 filer: the lowest reporting threshold of the payee's open lines, less one cent. For a W-8 filer: no limit on a foreign-source line, and zero on a US-source line until the envelope passes. The setting can only lower it (6.6) |
| `SANCTIONS_SCREEN_SCOPE` | `payees` | `payees` or `all_verified` (5.5) |
| `TAX_INVALIDATE_DAY_MAX` | 5 | Forms invalidated or form requests sent in one day before the tripwire of 6.6 |
| `TICKET_DAY_MAX_USD_CENTS` | 100000 | Ticket purchases per account per day, open sessions included |
| `TICKET_WALLET_MAX_USD_CENTS` | 200000 | The most Ticket value a wallet may hold after a purchase (3.6) |

The starting caps are deliberately small (decision D14). McKale raises them as volume proves itself.

### 5.9 Holds and review

An administrator never names a destination, and the pin rule applies to every release.

- A hold is `phold:{payee}:{seq}` with a reason, a status, `cents`, `remaining_cents`, the destination version current when it was made, and its source: accrual rows by date range (a suspended payee's nightly shares add to one open hold per reason), lot lines (`{lot, cents}`), or a payment's lines (a failed or returned payment restores by line).
- Postings: an accrual under a hold credits 2012 directly; `holdset:{hold_id}` moves payable cents from 2010 to 2012; `holdrel:{hold_id}:{n}` moves them back, in whole or in part. A payment never draws on 2012: a released hold is in 2010 before any payment reserves it.
- Reasons: `key_replaced`, `terms_update`, `destination_hold`, `recipient_refused`, `recipient_changed`, `name_mismatch`, `ach_returned`, `wire_rejected`, `provider_rejected`, `bank_compliance_hold`, `outcome_unknown`, `tax_hold`, `us_presence`, `kyc_lapsed`, `sanctions_hold`, `ticket_debt`, `legal_hold`, `estate_hold`, `trace_open`, `payee_dispute`, `banned`, `sanctions_released`, `account_gone`.
- **Release joins the next run.** A hold whose cause has cleared is released: its rows return to maturing or payable by their own dates, its lot cents return to their lots, and the payee's next weekly or monthly payment takes them under the same caps as any other cents, after its build has netted any open clawback or stray-payment remainder (5.10, item 7). A 300.00 USD returned payment whose payee enrolls a fresh bank account is repaid as a 25.00 USD first payment and the rest in later runs. A release is never a payment of its own.
- An administrator's `pay` marks a review hold releasable to the account's own current destination, subject to the pin, the cooling period and every send gate. `return` (back to the pool) is a delayed act, allowed only for a banned account with a case reference, the only way a `banned` hold ever reaches the pool, and never for `sanctions_hold`, `blocked`, `legal_hold` or `estate_hold`.
- `legal_hold` (an IRS levy, a garnishment, a bankruptcy stay) and `estate_hold` (a deceased payee) are set by `POST /api/admin/finance/legal-hold` with a case reference. Setting one is immediate, and counted against `STOP_ACT_DAY_MAX` (8.6). Lifting one is a delayed, signed, announced act that never counts against the daily cents total. They are never released by a timer and never returned to the pool. The procedure for each is counsel's (section 19).
- A destination conflict (5.4) holds nothing of the incumbent's. The newcomer's shares accrue under `destination_hold` like those of any parked destination, and the conflict ends only by `dest-claims/resolve` or by the removal of one of the two destinations.
- No hold returns to the pool on a timer. Earned and declared money does not vanish. A dormancy report lists holds and payables untouched for 12 months, with the payee's state or country and last contact date, for counsel's answer on unclaimed property.

### 5.10 The stolen-card chain and clawback

Value score from Ticket sales moves the drip, and a disputed Ticket pack must reach the creators the buyer paid.

1. **Lots.** The Wallet tracks paid Tickets by lot (the Stripe session or invoice that bought them). A purchase records which lots it drew.
2. **Reversal.** A Stripe refund or dispute of a pack debits the buyer's Wallet (lot first) and returns the purchases that lot funded. One reversal can return several purchases, from one creator or many, scored on different days. The loop has a resumable home, and it is a cursor, never a fan-out. Each purchase writes an index entry `lotp:{lot ref}:{n}` for every lot it drew. In the same put as `lot_reversal`, the Wallet writes one record, `lotrev:{reversal_ref}` (the lot, the reversal and the next index), and the webhook answers after that one put. Each step of the Wallet's alarm reverses one purchase from the lot's own index and advances the cursor, and an alarm runs at most `OUTBOX_STEPS_MAX` (8) steps of any kind before it re-arms. A pack spent as 540 purchases of 20 Tickets is one record and 540 alarm steps, each idempotent and each retried by itself. It is never 541 keys in one put, which the 128 key limit refuses and every redelivery would refuse again, and never 1,080 subrequests inside one invocation. Splitting spend into small purchases is something a colluding creator controls, so the number of purchases must be neither a way to outlast an invocation nor a way to break the put that starts the reversal. A test reverses a lot that funded 600 purchases under the operation-counting fake, with its 128 key put limit. It has two mutations and must fail under each: run the loop inline in the webhook; write one outbox key per returned purchase in the reversal's put. For each purchase, in its own step, the seller's hub runs `cancel_settle {purchase_id}` (a pending `settle:` entry is deleted and never scores) or, when the score was already filed, `reverse_score {purchase_id}`, which answers with the `score_day` stored at filing. The Treasury then runs `reverse_value {purchase_id, reversal_ref}`, keyed **per purchase** (`adj:{reversal_ref}:{purchase_id}`): three purchases under one dispute are three adjustments, never one adjustment and two replays.
3. **The amount is recomputed, not prorated.** The value score is capped per buyer (1,000 Tickets) and per creator (9,600), so a proportional cut is wrong whenever a cap was binding. `reverse_value` reads the per-buyer rows `vs:` for that creator and day, recomputes the capped score with and without the reversed purchase, and shrinks the value row by the difference in cents, with cumulative floor arithmetic per row so the sum of adjustments never exceeds the row.
4. **The race.** The settle step reads the buyer's copy of the purchase before filing, so a refund in the last minute of the window can never score.
5. **Before lots exist**, the same reversal walks the buyer's later live purchases newest first up to the reversed amount of paid Tickets and cancels each pending settle; `SCORE_SETTLE_DAYS` gives it 14 days to arrive.
6. **Where the cents are.** The adjustment takes the cents from wherever they sit: the unfolded row (maturing or held), then the lot, then a hold on the lot (`adj`, back to the pool). Cents already reserved or paid are a clawback: `claw:{reversal_ref}:{purchase_id}` books the receivable and an equal recoverable amount of pool, outside the drip base, so the pool is never credited with cash that is not in the bank.
7. **Netting.** A clawback is netted at the moment it arises against the payee's existing unpaid cents, oldest first (`clawnet`), after that against each later accrual, and again in the write that builds each of the payee's payments, before the build takes its gross (5.6). The last is what catches cents that come back into the payable after the clawback arose: a voided or rejected payment's cents, a returned payment's cents released from their hold, any released hold, a fee credit. Each netting moves that much from recoverable to the pool's unaccrued remainder (2003) in the same write, because that cash stayed in Bliss Treasury; it reaches the drip base at the month's fold (5.1). The send gate blocks a payee while any open clawback or stray-payment remainder exceeds their payable; a payee who owes 4.00 USD and has 900.00 USD matured is paid 896.00 USD. A payee with a negative Ticket wallet is not paid. Netting is a contract term; counsel confirms it (section 19). An administrator may write a clawback off (`clawoff`), a delayed act.
8. **Tax.** A netted amount is earnings applied to a debt. When the clawed-back payment was reported in an earlier tax year, the netted amount is recorded as reportable gross in the year of netting (6.8); netting within the same year needs nothing.
9. A dispute rate of 0.5 percent of charges over 30 days (with at least three disputes) pauses the maturing of value rows and raises `dispute_rate_high`.

### 5.11 What the contributor sees

- `/payouts`: the five gates; balance split into maturing, payable and held, each with its reason in plain words (a gate reason is shown to the payee it concerns and to nobody else); next payment date; rail; bank last four; the fee, cap and minimum rules; plan state and renewal date. With `SHADOW_VISIBLE` on, an account that already holds a qualifying plan sees its estimate, labelled as one, with the statement that no amount is owed, on a view that carries no plan link, price or checkout control. Every other account sees the gates and no figure. The bank's last four digits and the TIN's last four are shown on the payments origin after a signed read (challenge purposes `dest_read` and `tax_doc_read`): no route returns them to a session alone, whatever its class. Two deliberate exceptions are stated. The in-product money notice below shows the last four digits of a destination set or changed in the last 30 days, so that an owner can recognise an account that is not theirs. And `/api/payouts/destination/pending` shows the payee's own open invite its last four digits and bank country for the 60 minutes the invite lives, because those digits must be seen before they can be signed.
- A statement per payment (`GET /api/payouts/statement/{id}`): gross by line (effort, value), tax withheld, wire fee, net, rail, status, expected arrival from Mercury's own estimate, the trace number. The id is the opaque payment id. The owner comes from the session, and every miss answers the same 404, so nobody can learn from this route whether, when or by which rail another person was paid.
- History from `pay:` records through the `payx:` index. History and statements are served to a session. A lifted token, or one forged with a leaked session key, therefore reads one account's payment amounts, dates, rail and statuses per request, and nothing that identifies a bank account or a person: no digit, no name, no address, no tax form. That exposure is accepted and stated (15.1, row 7); the remedy for a leaked key is its rotation.
- **The in-product money notice.** Mail is not the only signal, because an account can have no address, or only one a thief added. A change of destination, tax form, email address or key raises a banner on the site and in Studio for 30 days, shown at every sign-in with the date, the last four digits where there are any, and the control that disables the destination. It is read from `AccountAuth` (`money_notices`), never from mail state.
- Durable notices (mail kind `payout`, its own daily cap of 10 per account and address, sender name "Eustress Payouts", retried from an outbox): `payout_sent`, `payout_returned`, `payout_held` with the reason, `payout_delayed`, `destination_set`, `destination_changed`, `destination_reconfirm`, `key_replaced_destination`, `tax_form_received`, `tax_form_expiring` (60 and 14 days), `tax_form_required`, `backup_withholding_on`, `year_end_form_ready`. Destination and tax form notices go to the whole notice set, since those are the two changes a thief makes. No mail carries bank digits beyond the last four or any TIN digit.
- **Payee cases.** `POST /api/payouts/case` (FRESH, any session, exempt from the freeze and the pin) opens a case of kind `statement_wrong`, `not_received` or `not_my_account`. `not_my_account` disables the destination at once, sets `payee_dispute`, parks any reserved or awaiting-approval payment under the parking rule of 5.6 and alerts McKale. Unsigned cases are counted with unsigned removals, and past `DEST_REMOVE_DAY_MAX` they park the destination and set no dispute (5.4). A support view for administrators (`GET /api/admin/finance/payees/{id}`) shows payments and statuses with masked bank and tax data, and is itself a signed, logged read (8.8).

---

## 6. Taxes

### 6.1 One certificate before the first accrual

No tax form, no accrual. "No form on file" is a blocked state, never a default to US or to foreign. This single rule removes the missing-TIN branch of backup withholding, the undocumented-payee presumption and the annual TIN solicitation duty. What remains is the IRS-notice path (6.5). Where a form must carry a TIN (every W-9, and a W-8BEN that claims a treaty or gives one), the rule holds only if the envelope on file really contains it, which nothing online can know. The offline envelope check of 6.6 is what makes it true, and a payee's payments stay under the limit of 5.8 until that check has passed. A W-8BEN is complete with no TIN at all when no treaty is claimed and the payee ticks "not legally required"; such a form has no envelope and nothing to check.

Eustress's own tax-forms page is the system of record. A tax form is a certification, not an identity check, so it adds no second KYC. No vendor sits in the payout path and no vendor webhook sets a payout gate. The page collects nothing while `ENROLLMENT_MODE` is `off`.

Forms: W-9 for US persons; W-8BEN for foreign individuals. Accounts are natural persons aged 18 or over, so these two cover every payee at launch. The W-8BEN-E screen (a reduced substitute: Part I, Part III when a treaty is claimed, certification) and the W-9 entity classifications are built and stay behind `TAX_ENTITIES_ENABLED` until decision D11.

### 6.2 Interview and cross-checks

Three questions select the form: US citizen or resident for tax purposes; individual or entity; country of tax residence. A US citizen living abroad gets the W-9.

KYC data is knowledge the IRS will hold Eustress to, so the form is checked against it automatically. Refused or sent to review: a certified name that does not equal the KYC legal name after `normalizedName`; a W-8BEN from an account with a US-issued identity document, a US address, a US bank or sign-in history placing the person in the United States for the period; a blocked country. The IP-derived KYC country is an indicium only, never residence or citizenship. Legal name and date of birth are typed by the payee and compared with `kyc_facts` on the server. Nothing is prefilled: no route returns a legal name or a date of birth to a session (5.11). Country is asked, never taken from IP.

### 6.3 The forms page

`/payouts` shows the tax step; the routes are `/api/tax/*`. Requirements, each one a test:

1. Reachable only by the signed-in, KYC-passed account, and only on the payments origin (15.4).
2. Every line of the current official revision is captured. W-9 (Rev. March 2024): line 1 name, line 2 business name, 3a classification with the LLC code, 3b, 4 exempt payee code, 5 and 6 address as structured fields, TIN type and TIN, certification. W-8BEN (Rev. October 2021): name, country of citizenship, permanent residence address, mailing address, US TIN if any, foreign TIN or the "not legally required" box, date of birth, Part II when shown, certification, capacity.
3. The perjury certification is the official text, verbatim, stored as versioned content with its form revision, rendered directly above the signature. The signature is the last entry.
4. The certification has its own signing act, separate from the contributor terms. Where one signature ever covers more, the IRS sentence about consent sits immediately above it.
5. **The signature is real, and it never fingerprints the TIN.** Signing requires an identity-file session and a fresh one. The account's Ed25519 key signs a one-time challenge (AccountAuth challenge purpose `tax_form_sign`) bound to the SHA-256 of the exact payload. That payload carries every certified field and, in the place of each TIN, a **commitment**: `HMAC-SHA256(salt, TIN)` under a 256 bit random salt the browser generates. The salt exists only inside the encrypted envelope beside the TIN (6.6). The certification therefore covers the TIN, the offline tool can prove it by opening the envelope and recomputing the commitment, and nothing stored online can be tested against a guessed number. The challenge record in `AccountAuth` and the vault's `log:` hold the same commitment-bearing digest and nothing else. The record carries the signer's name, a server time stamp, the statement that the certificate was signed electronically, the key fingerprint and the signature. A typed name alone is never accepted.
6. The W-9 offers "the IRS has notified me that I am subject to backup withholding". Choosing it removes item 2 from the signed text and is recorded. Its effect follows the law line by line (6.5): it turns withholding on only for income whose character is interest or dividends. There is no "applied for" option. The FATCA code shows as not applicable.
7. The stored record `form:{seq}` is immutable: every field as submitted, each TIN as an envelope with its commitment, the payload hash, form revision, the hash of the certification text shown, time, account, session, IP, key epoch. A renderer reproduces a hard copy on demand, with the TIN filled in only by the offline tool. A server-side check refuses a submission missing name, signature or date, or missing a TIN envelope where the form requires one, and refuses any body that carries a field it does not expect. A TIN envelope is required on every W-9, and on a W-8BEN only when Part II is claimed or the payee enters a TIN. A W-8BEN with the "not legally required" box ticked and no envelope is complete: a resident of a country that issues no individual TIN certifies, accrues and is paid like anyone else.
8. Every submit, renew and modify event is logged in the vault's `log:{seq}`.
9. A W-8BEN's `expires_at` is December 31 of the third year after signing. Renewal notices start 60 days before. A form expired less than `TAX_FORM_GRACE_DAYS` ago suspends accrual into a hold; past that it blocks accrual. A change of country, a US address or a changed KYC name invalidates the form and opens a `tax_hold`.
10. Electronic delivery of year-end forms is a separate, versioned consent (`consent:{seq}`) carrying the eight required disclosures and a withdrawal control. Without consent the payee gets paper, so a mailing address is mandatory.
11. A tax form change is VALUE class (a frozen account cannot replace the form that sets its rail), is noticed to every address like a destination change, and a switch between W-9 and W-8 opens a review. That review has one job. One certificate governs a payment, the one on file when it is made (6.8), so rows earned under the earlier status will be paid under the new one. Before it releases, the review stamps every unpaid row and lot earned under the earlier status by the one rule of 6.4: for a former W-9 filer who now files a W-8, a `services` row of a US work day carries its whole cents unless the payee documents work done abroad, and a row of a US-source line carries its basis (`value_us_cents` under `slice`, its cents under `whole`).
12. A fresh IRS revision of a form is a content update with a grace window, never a schema change. Each stored form keeps its `form_revision`.
13. **Hashes are taken over one canonical form.** Every hashed text (certification, terms, payout authorization, consent) is UTF-8, with carriage returns removed, in NFC, with exactly one trailing newline. Every hashed JSON file (the rules files) is hashed over its sorted-key serialisation. A checkout with CRLF line endings and one with LF produce the same hash; a test hashes each text under both, and its mutation hashes raw bytes.

Mercury's own tax-document upload in the invite is off (`requireTaxDocument: false`): the payee fills one form, in one place.

### 6.4 Foreign payees: character first, then source

For each income line the signed tax rules state its **character** first and then its **source under that character**, because the source rule depends on the character. Compensation for services is sourced where the work is done. A royalty is sourced where the property is used. A prize, award or other fixed income paid by a US payer is generally sourced by the payer's residence, which makes it US source wherever the recipient lives. The accountant may give any of these answers for either line (section 18, items 1 and 2), so the engine supports all of them.

**Foreign source.** A documented foreign person earning a line the rules mark foreign source: no withholding, no Form 1042-S, the W-8BEN kept on file. The system holds that answer honest three ways. Payment is in arrears only (the monthly run pays closed periods; there are no advances). The W-8BEN signing carries a standing certification, stored as `attest:standing`: "I perform all work for Eustress outside the United States and will report any period in which I work while physically in the United States", with a "Report US work" control (`POST /api/tax/us-work`). And source is decided once, per score date, at accrual, never by a look back from the month a wire is built. A wire pays rows that are at least 7 days old on the effort line and 120 on the value line, out of lots that merge every earlier month a payee spent under a minimum, a cap, a hold or a lapsed plan, so the month of the build says nothing about the days the work was done. In `accrue`, for a W-8 filer, every row is stamped with `us_cents` by one rule, its line's own: a row of a line whose source is where the work is done (character `services`) carries its cents when the payee's latest recorded sign-in location on or before the score date (`loc:`, 5.5) is in the United States, or when a US-work report covers the date; a row of a line the rules mark US source carries its basis, `value_us_cents` under `slice` and its cents under `whole`; a row of a foreign-source line carries nothing. `POST /api/tax/us-work` names dates and stamps every unpaid row and lot cent of those dates. It also finds the cents of those dates already paid, from the lots' running totals (5.3), and treats them as US source after the fact. The year total of the payment that paid them gains them in `us_gross_cents` (with the correction flag when that year is frozen or filed), so they reach Form 1042-S. The tax that should have been withheld on them, from `withholdingFor` under that payment's tax snapshot, is recorded as under-withheld (`uwh:{payee}:{seq}`; Dr 1220, Cr 2047) and raises `nra_underwithheld`. The payee's next payments withhold it first, before anything else is paid (Reg. 1.1461-2(b)), whatever `NRA_WITHHOLDING_ENABLED` says, since the tax is owed either way; what they recover moves into 2041 with their own withholding. What is still unrecovered on the Form 1042 due date is deposited by Eustress from Checking and borne as its own expense (6080), by the act `tax/underwithheld/bear`. The stamp travels with the cents into the lot and the payment line, and the build reads it and nothing else. US-source cents are withheld on at 30 percent and reported on Form 1042-S, as the next paragraph states, under `NRA_WITHHOLDING_ENABLED`. While that switch is off they are not paid as foreign source: the `mature` stream sets the hold `us_presence` on every stamped cent, of any line, and the foreign-source cents beside them are paid as usual. Eustress holds the sign-in record, which is reason to know, so a W-8 filer who spends January and February in the United States and is wired in June is wired January's and February's cents as US source. A test places US sign-ins in January and a wire in June and requires January's rows to carry `us_cents`; its mutation tests the month of the build. A W-9 filer banking abroad signs no such certification and is never held for being in the United States. `FOREIGN_ATTEST_MODE` is `standing`; `monthly` requires a click per payout month and exists if the accountant asks for it (decision D21).

**US source.** For a line the rules mark US source, the rules also state the basis: `slice` (only the part traced to US buyers, `value_us_cents`) or `whole` (the entire line, as under a payer-residence rule). The basis is applied once, at accrual, as the `us_cents` stamp above, and a `services` line needs no basis from the rules. Every line's US-source part is the `us_cents` its rows carry and nothing else. The engine withholds 30 percent or a treaty rate on that basis into `withheld_nra_cents` with its own deposit clock, writes Form 1042-S rows for every such payee at any amount (a treaty rate of zero still needs one, exemption code 04) with a ten-digit unique identifier from a Treasury counter, and keeps a Form 1042 worksheet. Part II of the W-8BEN (treaty claim, with the foreign or US TIN the claim requires) appears for a payee with a US-source line.

- **The treaty rate comes from a table, never from the payee.** `treaty_table.{year}.json`, signed by the accountant and hash-pinned like the other rules files, holds one row per country, income character and Form 1042-S income code (the royalty codes 10, 11 and 12 carry different rates in many treaties): article and paragraph, and the rate in basis points. The income code is the accountant's as well: each income line in `tax_rules.{year}.json` carries its own (6.5). The engine selects the row by three facts, none of which the payee chooses: the country of permanent residence, the line's character and the line's income code. The payee's Part II entry only opens the claim. A claim is refused unless the country on line 9 equals the country of permanent residence, the article and income type entered agree with the selected row, that row exists, and a US or foreign TIN is present. A resident of a country whose treaty taxes copyright royalties at zero and industrial royalties at 10 percent, earning on a line the rules code as industrial, is withheld at 10 percent whatever subtype the claim names. The income code on Form 1042-S always comes from the rules line, with or without a treaty row. No row means 30 percent: a resident of a country with no US income tax treaty who types 0 percent is withheld at 30 percent, and a resident of a treaty country gets the table's rate, not the one typed. A withholding agent that accepts a claim it has reason to know is wrong owes the tax itself (section 1461), so the table is what Eustress relies on. Tests: a no-treaty country claiming 0 percent is withheld at 30 percent (mutation: use the payee's rate); a resident of a country with two royalty rows at different rates who claims the lower-rate subtype is withheld at the rate of the line's own income code (mutation: select the row by the payee's stated income type).
- A treaty row applies only to lines of character `royalty` or `other`.
- A treaty claim on **compensation for services performed in the United States** cannot be made on a W-8BEN. That slice is withheld at 30 percent unless an administrator has recorded an accepted Form 8233 for that payee and year (`POST /api/admin/tax/form8233`). The record holds the treaty article the form claims and the exempt amount for the year, both from the accepted form and never from a later entry. `withholdingFor` then returns 0, with exemption code 04, on `services` US-source cents up to the exempt amount still unused, and 30 percent on the cents above it. The use is counted on the Treasury's `f8233:{payee}:{year}`: a build reserves what it exempts in `pending_cents`, the payment's debit moves it to `used_cents` in the same put, and a void releases it, so two payments in flight never exempt the same dollar.
- A payee's reported US work days stamp `us_cents` on the unpaid cents of those dates and record the paid ones as under-withheld, as above, and the Form 8233 rule governs that slice. Nothing waits for a person.
- The wire minimum is tested after withholding. At 30 percent, a 50.00 USD balance would leave 20.00 USD after tax and the 15.00 USD fee, so no wire is built until the balance after tax reaches 50.00 USD (71.43 USD gross); the payout page states both figures to a payee on this branch.

This branch is a probable launch requirement for international payouts, not a remote one, so it is built and fixture-tested with the year-end work (the 1042-S export against the tax year's own IRIS template) and stays behind `NRA_WITHHOLDING_ENABLED` until the accountant rules. A line that the rules have not opened for foreign payees neither accrues to nor is paid to a W-8 filer.

### 6.5 Withholding

**Rules are data, and they come from the deploy.** `tax_rules.{year}.json`, reviewed by the accountant and committed with his sign-off recorded, holds: thresholds by form (`nec_cents: 200000` for 2026 and `210000` for 2027; `royalty_cents: 1000`; the interest threshold per instrument), `backup_bps: 2400`, `nra_bps: 3000`, and for each income line (`effort`, `value`, later `tickets`, `interest`) its character (`services`, `royalty`, `other`, `interest`), its Form 1042-S income code (10, 11 or 12 for a royalty; the code the accountant names for every other character), its source rule, its US basis (`none`, `slice`, `whole`), whether the statutory floor applies, whether a payee's own certification triggers withholding, and whether it is open for foreign payees. `treaty_table.{year}.json` holds the treaty rows of 6.4. The files ship inside the deployed bundle and their canonical hashes are pinned in source; the administrator route only activates a hash that the bundle contains, so no request can lower a rate. The accrual run, the payout runs and the year-end job refuse to run for a year with no activated rules file (`tax_rules_missing`). For accrual that is a wait, never a failed gate. A missing file is a state on Eustress's side, so the settlement of a score date in that year stops before `accrue_setup` and runs when the file is activated, as it does on `tax_key_mismatch` (6.6); no share returns to the pool for want of a file, and a late settlement loses no USD accrual (5.1). `tax_rules_missing` is raised every day from December 1 while next year's file is not activated, and its activation is on the December 20 checklist (5.7), which leaves the administrator delay room before January 1. Nothing is hard-coded. Each November the next year's figures are added.

**One pure function**, table-tested, integer arithmetic only:

```
withholdingFor({form_type, bnotice_state, self_certified, character, source, us_basis_cents,
                gross_cents, year, reportable_ytd_cents, prior_year, treaty_row, form8233})
  -> {cents, kind, rate_bps, reason}
cents = floor((basis_cents * rate_bps + 5000) / 10000)
```

- **W-9, no B notice.** Zero for `services`, `royalty` and `other`, whatever the payee ticked. The notified-underreporting and certification-failure triggers of section 3406 reach only reportable interest and dividends, so the payee's own certification turns withholding on only for a line whose character is `interest` or `dividend` (the gated investment return line). A creator who once had an underreporting notice about bank interest keeps every dollar of earnings.
- **W-9, B notice past its deadline** (`withholding_on`): 24 percent of gross, kind `backup`. Where the rules give the character a floor, it is tested on one figure: `reportable_ytd_cents`, the result of `payee_year_reportable(payee, year, form family, reporting section)`, which sums every payment to that payee of that reporting kind across lines, across characters that share a reporting form, and across W-9 sequences, and includes payments reserved or submitted and not yet posted. Withholding applies to the whole payment when `reportable_ytd_cents + gross_cents` reaches the year's threshold, or when a return or withholding was required for that same payee in the prior year. For `royalty` there is no floor. The accountant confirms the floor logic (section 18, item 7).
- **W-8, foreign source:** zero.
- **W-8, US source:** 30 percent of the US basis, or the rate of the treaty table's row under the limits of 6.4, kind `nra`. The row is selected from the payee's country of permanent residence and the line's character and income code; the function never receives a rate or an income type the payee typed.
- **W-8, US-source services under an accepted Form 8233:** zero, exemption code 04, up to the exempt amount the form leaves unused for the year (`exempt_cents` less `used_cents` and `pending_cents`), and 30 percent above it (6.4).
- **No valid form:** never reached; the gate refuses first.

Withholding is computed at the pay run on the gross, before the wire fee, for every line with the form of the payment's tax snapshot, and is carried inside the payment until the bank debit posts. Cents tagged `withholding_settled` are outside the basis. The tag marks cents that tax was actually taken on: what a return or a wire fee credit restores from a payment line that carried nonzero withholding. Cents restored from a line that carried none are untagged and inside the basis, under the certificate and the B-notice state in force when they are paid again (5.6, 6.8).

**B notices.** A per-payee state machine in the vault's `bnotice` record, driven by the business-day calendar: `none` to `first_notice` when an administrator records a CP2100 for the payee with its date (`POST /api/admin/tax/cp2100`); the first B notice and a W-9 request go out within 15 business days, by mail unless counsel approves electronic delivery (`POST /api/admin/tax/bnotice/mailed` records it); `withholding_on` at 30 business days unless a freshly signed W-9 arrives; `cleared` within 30 calendar days of receiving one. A second notice within three calendar years moves to `second_notice`, which needs an uploaded Social Security card copy or IRS Letter 147C (Letter 685C for an ITIN) before withholding stops; that evidence is encrypted in the browser to the offline key like a TIN. Each deadline raises `bnotice_deadline` before it passes. Every information return carries an opaque payee reference in its account number box, so a CP2100 line maps to a payee without opening a TIN.

**Withheld cash.** Withholding becomes a liability in the same write that records the payment's bank debit and its year totals (`withheld_backup_cents` for Form 945, `withheld_nra_cents` for Form 1042). The liability, its deposit clock, the Form 945 or 1042 figure and the box on the payee's form therefore all carry one date, the bank posting date, and a payment that is voided or rejected before it posts never touched a tax account. Withholding on a payment still in flight sits inside `reserved_cents` and is outside the deposit worksheet.

- **Form 945.** Once any tax is withheld in a year, it is deposited monthly, by the 15th of the following month. The exception for a year under 2,500 USD is used only when nothing was withheld before December, because the year's total is unknown until the year ends and a missed monthly deposit is a penalty on every earlier month. Accumulated withholding of 100,000 USD raises `withholding_next_day`: the deposit is due the next business day and Form 945-A is filed.
- **Form 1042.** 2,000 USD or more undeposited at the end of a quarter-monthly period is due in 3 business days; 200 to 2,000 USD at month end is due 15 days after; less with the return.
- The admin view shows each amount, its due date and a worksheet, and `withholding_deposit_due` fires ahead of it.
- **McKale makes each deposit through EFTPS from Checking.** Bliss Treasury therefore needs no inbound debit and can carry a debit block. A debit whose counterparty is the IRS or EFTPS is never an expense: it posts at apply to its account's Suspense (2920 for Checking). `POST /api/admin/tax/withholding/remitted` binds the act to that Suspense row: its counterparty is the IRS, and the act names each payable the deposit clears (2040, 2041, 2045, 2046, 2047) with an amount no greater than that payable's balance, the amounts summing to no more than the row. The act is the reclass of the row (`mtc:{txn}`: Dr the named payables; Cr 2920), so Checking's cash is posted once and its wall holds, and for 2040 and 2041 the claim is released into carry in the same write (`taxdep`, 2.6). One Form 945 deposit that covers contributors' withholding and a holder's is one act with two named amounts, and nothing is guessed. The next sweeps net it, so the cash returns to Eustress without any transfer out of Bliss Treasury. An IRS debit that is Eustress's own tax (an estimated payment) is classified from the same Suspense row to 6080. The tax deposit test asserts the Checking wall after the act; its mutation credits 1110 a second time. EFTPS enrollment takes about a week by mail and is one of the acts that start at once (17.2).
- **Withholding on an investor return stays on the operating side.** An investor return is paid from Engine Expenses or Checking (section 9), and its withholding never enters Bliss Treasury. The matched bank row credits the payable of its kind: 2045 when `withholdingFor` returns `backup` (a W-9 holder under the rules above), 2046 when it returns `nra` (a W-8 holder). The cash stays in the account that paid, no carry moves and no pool identity is touched. The Form 945 worksheet and its clock read 2040 plus 2045, the Form 1042 worksheet and its clock read 2041, 2046 and 2047, and the holder's year totals are written by the same row.

### 6.6 TIN storage: a write-only vault the Worker cannot read into

No server ever holds a TIN in the clear: not at rest, and not in transit through the Worker.

- **Key ceremony.** McKale generates the RSA key pair on the offline machine with the repository script; the private key never touches a networked machine. He keeps it offline in two places. The public key goes into the Worker's and the site's configuration as `TAX_PUBLIC_KEY_V1`.
- **The key is pinned.** The SHA-256 fingerprint of each permitted key version is hard-coded in the Worker's source and in the site bundle, each with a test. The site refuses to encrypt to a key whose fingerprint is not pinned; the Worker refuses a tax submission or an accrual run when the configured key does not match (`tax_key_mismatch`). `/health` and the relay's daily digest report the active fingerprint, and every envelope stores it. A changed blob in configuration cannot silently redirect TINs to a key someone else holds.
- **The browser encrypts.** On submit the page, served from the payments origin, validates the TIN's format, makes a fresh AES-256-GCM key, a fresh 96-bit nonce and a 256-bit salt, encrypts `{TIN, salt}` with additional data binding it to its place (`eustress-tin-v1|{payee}|{form seq}|{field}`), wraps the AES key with RSA-OAEP SHA-256 under the pinned public key, and computes the commitment of 6.3. The Worker receives the envelope `{key_version, key_fp, wrapped_key, nonce, ciphertext}`, the commitment, the last four digits and the TIN type, and stores them in the payee's `TaxVault` object. The US TIN, the foreign TIN of a W-8BEN and second B-notice evidence all travel this way.
- **No fingerprint of the TIN is stored.** An SSN has too little entropy: any unsalted or server-keyed digest of it is a lookup table. The commitment's salt is inside the envelope, so the stored commitment cannot be tested against a guess. A test models the attacker: holding every storage write, every log line and the last four digits, it enumerates candidates for a planted TIN and must fail; its mutation commits with an unsalted hash. Duplicate-TIN detection runs offline in the year-end tool. One person, one account is already enforced by KYC.
- **Leak tests plant real values.** The suite plants known TINs and searches every captured log line and every storage write for them after stripping non-digits, and in base64, hex and URL-encoded forms, including the formatted `123-45-6789`. A body that carries a TIN in the clear (a client bug) is refused without being logged.
- **A tripwire on re-asking.** The Worker controls the states that make payees submit again. More than `TAX_INVALIDATE_DAY_MAX` forms invalidated, or that many `tax_form_required` notices, in one day stops those notices and alerts on the out-of-band channel (`tax_reask_tripwire`).
- **Year-end and any IRS request.** An administrator requests the export by a signed act that carries no delay (8.6); it is audited and announced before it is produced, then streamed once, in the answer to that request, with the whole bundle (profiles, year totals and envelopes) encrypted to the offline key. It is never persisted in a bucket. McKale runs the year-end tool on the offline machine.
