That restore loads a journal that contains the relay's head, because no anchor runs ahead of the backup, so the entries replayed after it extend the witness and the next anchor is accepted |
| A refusal of the send token | The relay's health answer, read after the refusal, shows a send token that reads and is not degraded |
| A relay environment or base URL mismatch | The relay's health answer reports the Worker's own environment and base URL |
| An administrator's halt | It has no condition of its own; the signed unhalt is its acknowledgement |

An unhalt whose cause still stands is cancelled and the halt stays, with two exceptions, which exist so that a remedy and its unhalt never chase each other. An unhalt that takes effect under scope `all` and finds every `all` cause false and only `payouts` causes standing narrows the halt to scope `payouts`, which lets the top-up and the cover-adding acts run and releases nothing, and is otherwise cancelled. And an unhalt whose only standing causes have their remedy in flight (a top-up installment that is `submitted`, or a remedy act applied in that same hour whose effect a poll has yet to confirm) is judged again at each poll until 5:00 PM Arizona time that day, and is cancelled only then. Nothing suppresses a standing cause. The way out of a persistent condition is the remedy the halt leaves running (a top-up, a classification, a charge to carry, the acknowledgement of a run), and then the unhalt. A stolen session fails on the signature; a stolen key fails on the delay and the announcement.

**Each act is bound to facts the bank or the book already holds**, so an act cannot explain away money that left:

- `withholding/remitted` needs a Suspense row on Checking or Engine Expenses whose counterparty is the IRS, and names each withholding payable it clears with an amount no greater than that payable's balance. It is the reclass of that row (6.5).
- `sweeps/manual` needs a Suspense-classified internal transfer from EE to BT of the installment's exact amount: the debit row in 2910 and the credit row in 2930. It is the reclass of both (4.6). A transfer of any other amount is refused here and settled as an own transfer into Bliss Treasury.
- `suspense/classify` for a credit on BT accepts only: an investment receipt that meets the match of section 9; a return linked by Mercury to a payment; `payment_return_unlinked` under the five facts of 5.6; the recovery of a disputed debit, matched to its `btdebit` record by amount and counterparty; a Stripe payout that landed on Bliss Treasury, bound to an unmatched payout the Worker read from Stripe, by its id and exact amount (4.3); or an **own transfer into Bliss Treasury**. That is one act naming two rows, the debit on Engine Expenses or Checking and the BT credit of equal amount, each read from Mercury as a leg of one internal transfer between Eustress's own accounts, and it reclasses both through 1130 into carry (`xferbt`, 2.6). A BT credit is never classified as carry by itself, because its debit would then have no exit but an expense account. A transfer of an amount no planned installment has, a duplicated sweep transfer and cash McKale moves in ahead of `legacy_fund` all leave Suspense this way, and 1130, 2910, 2920 and 2930 return to zero. A credit from outside is never carry and never pool. One that fits none of these and cannot be returned to its sender still has an exit. An ACH credit carries no account to return it to: two micro-deposits from a service a payee linked, a stranger's mistake. `suspense/transfer`, after the screen of its originator (5.5), plans its move to Checking (an `xo:` record, marker `suspxfer:{id}`, 2.6), McKale makes that internal transfer, and the amount rests in 2600 as a payable to its sender until counsel's unclaimed-property answer says where it goes (section 19, item 10). Carry created by classification is not used by a sweep until the month that holds it is locked, with one exception: carry bound to a Stripe payout is outside `carry_unlocked_cents` and funds that payout's own part at once, because the payout, its amount and its contents come from Stripe and not from the administrator.
- `suspense/classify` for a row on Engine Expenses or Checking. Those two accounts hold only Eustress's own money, so a row there has a closed exit of its own. A credit: a Stripe payout's arrival, only for an unmatched payout of that exact amount, with a recorded reference (a payout that landed outside Mercury and was brought back, 4.3); or an ordinary credit, posted against one of 3100 (an owner contribution), 4090 (another receipt) or the 60xx account of the expense it refunds, and against no other account. A debit has four exits. A card charge or a vendor-pulled debit: the expense account its rule names (8.4). A payment sent to a recipient that no plan announced: an expense account and a vendor named in the act for that one row, which is McKale's signed statement that he sent it; no rule does this in bulk. The debit leg of an own transfer into Bliss Treasury, named together with its BT credit. And 6095 for a debit the bank did not return, with the dispute reference.
- `suspense/classify` for a Stripe row accepts only what a read of the row's source object supports, `other_receipt` among them (4.3).
- `suspense/classify` for a debit on BT accepts one thing, a **stray payment**: a duplicate, a debit under a closed payment id or a mismatched debit that could not be cancelled and whose Mercury recipient is the one an enrolled payee's signed destination names. The act is bound to that recipient id, read from the row, and to a recorded Mercury reference, and it names no destination. The debit is booked as what it is, a payment to that payee (`straypay`, 2.6). The payee's payable falls by the amount covered, and the payment's year totals are written in the same put under the form on file on the posting date; where `withholdingFor` returns tax for that payee, the payable consumed is the smallest gross whose net is the cash covered, and the tax is credited to 2040 or 2041. Nothing is credited to the pool, so Eustress is made whole by the payee's own payable and no contributor gains from the error. What the payable cannot cover is charged to carry and rests in 1210 under the payee's name. It is netted as a clawback is (5.10, item 7): at once against the payee's unpaid cents, then against each later accrual and in the build write of each payment, each netting a payment recorded in the netting year's totals under `netted_cents`, since its cash left with the stray debit. R12 therefore ties at every moment: the cash the bank shows for a recipient in the stray debit's year equals that year's totals plus the part the payable did not cover. The payee is asked to return the remainder; a returned amount is the recovery of a disputed debit, and `suspense/write-off` ends what is neither returned nor earned.
- `suspense/charge-debit` is the exit for a debit on BT that matched nothing, could not be cancelled and went to no enrolled payee's recipient. It records the cause and the bank dispute reference, charges the amount to carry and books it as a debit in dispute (1210). It restores no cover by itself: the carry bound then governs the amount, and the next top-up pays for it out of Eustress's own cash. `suspense/write-off` moves a disputed debit the bank did not return to 6095. No adjusting entry can touch either.
- `carry/plan`, `investments/{id}/repay`, `suspense/refund`, `suspense/transfer` and `expenses/plan` create expected-outflow records before the money moves; R3, and R5 on Engine Expenses and Checking, accept exactly the transfer that was planned. `investments/{id}/repay`, `suspense/refund` and `suspense/transfer` first screen the counterparty the money goes to (5.5), and the re-check at the effective time screens again; a `potential` result holds the act, and a confirmed match blocks the money.
- `tax/rules` activates only a rules file whose hash the deployed bundle contains.
- `dest-claims/resolve` names one of the two accounts of an open destination conflict and a case reference for the bank document (5.4). It releases one claim and re-opens the other claimant's own signed destination at `pending`; it names no destination. `dest-removals/ack` acknowledges the removal tripwire and sends the reconfirm notices; it restores nothing.
- `runs/{id}/ack-anomaly` names one run. It makes that run's anomaly cause false and moves nothing.
- An adjustment can only reduce a payable. A hold `pay` acts only through the send gates. No act names a destination.

### 8.7 The 10 percent rule

Nothing in the bank enforces locked decision 9; the book measures it and a guard acts on it. The build default, for McKale to confirm (decision D10):

```
eustress_net = receipts (40xx) - refunds and disputes (4900, 4910)
             - fees (5000, 5010, 5020) - Treasury share (5200) - reserve share (5210)
profit       = eustress_net - operating expenses (6000 to 6999) - investor returns (5300)
margin       = profit / eustress_net            rule: margin >= 0.10
```

Measured on each closed month and on the trailing three months, every day, from the posted journal. Withheld tax, investor principal, the reserve and payables sit in claim accounts and are never counted as Eustress's money. Wire fees are recovered from payees and net to zero. Unredeemed Tickets and Delta (the meters of 3.6) and the accrued sales tax exposure are shown beside the figure; the first enters it only under the accountant's deferred-revenue policy.

Below the floor the guard: raises `margin_below_floor` with the lines that moved; refuses to plan an investor return payment (principal repayment is not an expense and follows its terms); refuses any increase of the Ticket reserve rate. It never touches contributor money: the pool is the contributors' half by rule, an accrued payable is a debt, and pausing the drip would not raise the margin by a cent. Whether the rule gets a stronger lever is part of decision D10.

### 8.8 The admin view

`GET /api/admin/finance/overview` feeds the administrator finance page on the site (it renders only when the Worker's admin answer arrives; the page holds no admin flag of its own). It shows:

- every bucket beside its account balance, the three Mercury balances and the Stripe balance, the book identity and each wall with its last determinate result, and the result of each check R1 to R27;
- the sweep pipeline: payouts by state, installments by state, `transit_cents`, `sweep_deficit_cents`, `carry_cents`, the computed Engine Expenses floor with its dispute cover, safe to move from Engine Expenses;
- pay runs: next dates, the last run's counts, payments by state, wires awaiting approval with the do-not-approve set, holds by reason and payees stopped at each gate, both as counts;
- taxes: forms on file by type, forms expiring, payees under backup withholding, withheld amounts and deposit dates, B-notice clocks, the year-end status, the tax key fingerprint and its last drill, the entity tax set-aside;
- the margin with its lines, the unit meters, sales by buyer location with the accrued tax exposure;
- Suspense and uncategorized items, expected outflows, pending administrator acts, the halt state, open alerts, the journal head, the last anchor the relay accepted, and the deploy tripwire's last report;
- (gated) investor principal and repayments, the Ticket reserve.

Other administrator reads: `/api/admin/finance/runs`, `/runs/{id}`, `/payees`, `/payees/{id}`, `/wires`, `/sweeps`, `/suspense`. **A read is an act too.** A stolen administrator token, or one forged with a leaked session key, must not be able to copy the roster of who is paid, how much and who is under sanctions review, and leave no trace. List views return counts and opaque references only: runs, payees, wires, sweeps, Suspense, holds, and the books close, which carries account totals and no payee at all. A per-payee view, one hold's detail (its payee, its reason, its masked destination), the sanctions queue with its names, the destination conflict queue, the pre-filing tax report and the books export each require a signed challenge by the administrator key (purpose `admin_read`, valid `ADMIN_READ_TTL_MINUTES`). The challenge is bound to the route and to the target reference and is single use, so one signature reads one thing. Each signed read writes an entry to the act chain (`act:{seq}`, with the route and the opaque target in `args_digest`) and counts against `ADMIN_READ_DAY_MAX` per key; a bulk pattern raises `admin_read_bulk` on both channels. The books export carries opaque payee references, never account ids. Every finance GET declares its response fields in `route_policy.mjs`, and the route-policy test fails any GET under the finance prefixes whose declared response carries an account id, a name, a bank digit or a hold reason per payee without being marked a signed read, so a route added later cannot open this again. The administrator stats page shows payout-ready accounts and tax forms on file as counts. One place holds the roster outside the Worker, by design: the out-of-band channel, where the relay announces each recipient by legal name, country and fingerprint tail and posts each manifest. Its account is held like a credential and its history is kept short (15.4).

### 8.9 The hourly finance tick and the scheduler

**The Worker's part is two calls.** `scheduled()` shares its hourly invocation with the takedown sweep, evidence retries, legal reports, the moderation sweep and Space deletions, and the limit of 1,000 operations is per invocation. `financeTick(env, ctx)` therefore does a fixed, small amount: one call to the Treasury's `tick` and one to the Cashbook's `tick`, in its own `try` and its own `ctx.waitUntil`. No Stripe read, Mercury read, R2 write or KV write happens inline. A test runs a scheduled invocation in which the other sweeps have already spent 990 operations and requires both ticks to be delivered; its mutation performs a Stripe read inline.

**One scheduler in each object.** A Durable Object has one alarm, and the limit of 1,000 operations applies to one invocation, which an alarm is. The Treasury keeps a table `due:{stream}` of work streams, each with `next_at` and its own backoff: `settlement`, `mature`, `run`, `sweep`, `revpass`, `verify`, `backup`, `adminq`, `unhalt`, `anchor`. `alarm()` runs **one step of one stream**, the most overdue, and then sets the alarm to now when another stream is due and to the smallest `next_at` otherwise. Streams due at the same time run in a fixed order in which `sweep` and `adminq` come before `unhalt`, so a top-up, a remedy act and the unhalt queued on one evening are applied in that order the next morning and the unhalt is judged on their result (8.6). The `anchor` stream never sends a digest past the last completed backup: when the journal has moved since, its step is a `backup_step`, and it anchors once that backup's manifest names the head (7.7). No stream sets the alarm itself. A settlement collect step and a pay run step therefore never share an invocation's budget. A settlement step that is backing off for an hour does not slow a pay run that steps every few milliseconds, and a run does not hammer a settlement that is waiting. The Cashbook has the same table for `bank_poll`, `stripe_ingest`, `walls`, `sanctions_sync`, `meters`, `replay`. Every step carries one operation budget (900 KV operations and subrequests together, 128 keys a put) and checks it before each provider call: a relay call the budget cannot cover is not issued, which is a preflight refusal (`not_sent`) and leaves no attempt. Once a fetch is issued, any exception from it is unknown (5.6). A test makes every stream due at once and requires each invocation to stay under the limit; its mutation runs every due stream in one alarm. A job stored in a phase the code does not know fails loudly (`settlement_phase_unknown`) and never retries forever. The same rule holds outside the two finance objects: the reversal of the purchases a Ticket lot funded runs from one cursor record on the Wallet's own alarm, one purchase a step and a fixed number of steps an alarm (5.10).

---

## 9. Investments: plumbing built, offer gated on counsel

`INVESTMENTS_ENABLED` is `false` in both environments. With it off, every route below answers 404 and no page, button, email or copy mentions an investment. Securities counsel's written sign-off is the only thing that turns it on. Every instrument of this kind is treated as a security from the first line of code: a public page describing a return is itself an offer.

The gift flow ("Fund the treasury") is a gift. Its code and copy carry no investment word: `GiftTier` and `gift_body` in `bliss.rs`; no "Invest in", "your investment" or "cryptocurrency". A sponsorship or a gift never implies a return, and the gift page states that a gift is not tax deductible. Whether the gift stream itself needs a different legal shape is counsel's question (section 19, item 28) and gate G10.

**Record.** `inv:{id}` in the Treasury: investor (an account id with a tax profile, address and TIN envelope in its `TaxVault`, collected at subscription), instrument (`note` or `unit`; counsel decides), hash of the signed terms, exemption relied on, investor state or country, accredited-status evidence reference, first-sale date (it starts the Form D clock and is surfaced as a task), expected amount, `received_cents` kept apart from the expected amount, funding source (`bank_wire`, `portal_disbursement`), the Mercury transaction that funded it, receipts, repayments, returns paid, outstanding principal, status.

**Reference code.** One format serves notes and units: `EUS-` plus 12 hexadecimal digits, the `payment_ref` the Regulation A module issues, taken from 48 bits of a cryptographic random source. Every investment record and every subscription shows that code and no other. A code is single-use and expires with its expected record; a second credit carrying a used code never pairs.

**Instructions are shown late.** Bliss Treasury's account and routing number are enough for an outsider to attempt a debit, so `payment_instructions` are shown to a subscriber only after an administrator has accepted the subscription for funding, which first screens the investor (5.5), never to anyone who merely opens one. Where Mercury provides a receive-only account number, the instructions carry that number. Bliss Treasury carries the debit block of 2.1, and R3 raises `external_debit` with its dispute deadline.

**Receive and reconcile.** Money arrives at Bliss Treasury by wire, never by ACH and never through Stripe. The name on an ACH credit is whatever its originator typed into the company name field, so an ACH proves nothing about who paid; the originator name on a wire is the sending bank's own record of its customer. Every credit to BT that is not a sweep, interest, a linked return or a Stripe payout matched by its trace (4.3) posts to Suspense (2930). A match needs three facts, all of them: the credit is a wire that carries the reference code in its memo or bank description; the amount equals the open expected investment to the cent; and the wire's originator name equals the investor's KYC legal name after `normalizedName`. Then, after a fresh screen of the investor (5.5), an administrator confirms (a delayed act) and `inv:{mercury_txn}` moves the cents from Suspense to `invest_principal_cents` and records the funding transaction on the investment.

- A credit with the right code from the wrong sender, or by ACH, is third-party money. It stays in Suspense and is returned to its sender: once the originator's name and bank pass the screen of 5.5, an administrator plans a Suspense refund (`susprefund:{id}`, an expected outflow), and McKale sends it in Mercury to the originating account only. A confirmed match is blocked in 2016, never returned. Money that was never accepted is never refunded to an enrolled payout destination. A credit that cannot be returned, because an ACH credit carries no account to send it back to, leaves Bliss Treasury by the planned transfer to Checking of 8.6 and rests there as a payable to its sender.
- A credit with no readable code (a bank truncated the memo) can be classified only with the sender-name match and the exact amount, by the delayed act; two open records of one amount and one name wait for the investor's own confirmation.
- A short receipt (an intermediary bank kept a fee) is recorded as received and stays in Suspense until it is completed, refunded, or treated as counsel's terms for the instrument say; the treatment of the difference is in those terms, never an administrator's guess.
- A credit from outside is never carry and never pool (8.6). Nothing is ever credited to the pool from the bank side.

The Regulation A module's `sub_funds` and `sub_refunded` use this same match against a Mercury transaction id in place of a typed bank reference; acceptance there is a deliberate administrator act because it issues securities.

**Acceptance of equity.** When the instrument is a unit and a subscription is accepted, the proceeds stop being repayable principal: `invaccept:{inv_id}` moves them from 2100 to equity (3200) and releases the cash into carry, from where the sweep netting returns it to Eustress's use. A note stays in 2100 until it is repaid.

**Ring fence.** `invest_principal_cents` is outside `cents`, so the drip never touches it, and it is a term of `claims_bt`, so no pay run can spend it. Both are tests with mutations.

**Repay.** The relay has no route for repayments. After a fresh screen of the holder (5.5), an administrator plans a repayment, which writes two expected-outflow records: `invest_repay` on Bliss Treasury for the principal, and `invest_return` on the account that pays the return (Engine Expenses or Checking), carrying `gross_cents`, `withholding_cents` and `withholding_kind`. McKale sends each from Mercury; the Cashbook matches each outflow to its own record by reference code, account and amount. **Money goes back to the account it came from.** Principal and returns are sent to the originating account of the funding wire, which the plan names by its Mercury transaction, never to an enrolled payout destination. Funds that entered from one account under one name and left to another would make Eustress a pass-through. Principal leaves BT and reduces `invest_principal_cents` (`invrepay:{mercury_txn}`). A return is an Eustress expense (5300), is paid from Engine Expenses or Checking, never from Bliss Treasury, and is refused at planning time while the margin is below the floor. Its `invest_return` record carries gross, withholding and net: withholding comes from `withholdingFor` on the line `interest`, McKale sends the net, and the matched bank row posts `invret` from that record's fields: the gross to 5300, the withholding to the operating payable of its kind (2045 for `backup`; 2046 for `nra`, which a W-8 holder's return carries on the Form 1042 clock) and the holder's year totals, all in one put (2.6, 6.5). No line touches Bliss Treasury. An outflow from BT with no plan behind it trips R3 like any other.

**Holder ledger.** The `record` operation of `holder_ledger.mjs` writes holder detail changes as chained entries (`holder_update`, with `by`), so `verify` sees them. `sub_expire` runs on the hourly tick. A holder who receives a return has a tax profile like any payee, and is paid at the account that funded the investment, not at a payout destination. Whether an investment is the Regulation A unit or a separate repayable note is counsel's call; the cash record lives here either way.

**Tax.** For a note, returns are reported from the year-totals machinery under the line `interest`, with the threshold the rules file gives that instrument (a private note falls under the general threshold, not the 10 USD rule for registered obligations). For a unit, neither Form 1099-INT nor 1042-S is the answer: a second member makes Eustress LLC a partnership for tax from the date of issue (Form 1065, a Schedule K-1 to each holder, section 1446 withholding on a foreign holder), which also changes the payer classification behind every contributor form that year. `entity_tax.{year}.json` carries the classification as an effective-dated list for that reason, and the accountant's questions cover what holder data must be collected at subscription (section 18, item 22).

---

## 10. Ticket cash-out reserve: gated on counsel

Two Mercury accounts exist, so the reserve is a sub-ledger of Bliss Treasury, never a bank account. The Tickets line, if it ever pays, uses the pay runs of section 5; no batch is approved by hand and nothing runs through Stripe Connect.

`TICKET_RESERVE_MODE` has three values:

| Value | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| `off` | Nothing is computed |
| `shadow` | Numbers only. For every Ticket pack deposit, and for the Tickets part of each Player Plus or Bundle invoice (valued at the cheapest pack price per Ticket, capped at the invoice), `reserve = floor(Eustress's share x 15 / 100)` is recorded in a shadow counter. A shadow credit ledger per creator (`tr:{creator}`) measures eligible earned Tickets (the paid part of each sale times 70 percent, 120 days after the sale, web Tickets only, while enrolled), what a rate of 1.50 USD per 1,000 Tickets would have cost, and what fraud would have reached it |
| `funding` | McKale's decision (D17). The reserve part is earmarked at sale (Dr 5210, Cr 2201), rides the sweep into BT as `reserve_cents`, and is a named term of `claims_bt`. Reversals take the reserve's part back in proportion, and a negative reserve part follows the rule of 4.4: it never takes `reserve_cents` below zero. The pool stays exactly 50 percent of net. The reserve never goes negative, never borrows from the pool and never returns to operating money |

`TICKET_CASHOUT_LIVE` is `false` until counsel's written sign-off (stored value, money transmission, tax form, unclaimed property) and McKale's answers to the open items of `CREATOR_CASHOUT.md`. Tickets that redeem for cash may take Tickets out of closed-loop stored value; that question comes first.

Prerequisites, all of which section 5.10 provides: paid Tickets tracked by lot; `cancel_settle` and `reverse_score`; the refund-against-settle race closed; integer 70 percent math; `paid_balance` clamped; and for the payout itself a separate Redeemable balance with a one-way move to spendable.

When live, the Tickets line is one more line on the same payment record: eligible earned Tickets times the published rate (it starts at 1.50 USD per 1,000 and only rises), 25 USD minimum, pro rata when the reserve is short with the rest carried, paid in the monthly run for wire payees and the first weekly run of the month for ACH payees. It has its own year-totals line and its own income character. A negative Tickets balance is paid down from the Tickets line only, never from the earnings line. The 0.5 percent dispute tripwire pauses it.

---

## 11. Build plan: retiring Stripe Connect

This section and section 16 are the build plan. They describe a transition, name what the code holds on 2026-11-12, and leave this document for the archive when the build completes.

The payout rail through Stripe Connect cannot pay anyone under the daily automatic payout schedule, and its onboarding runs a second identity check at Stripe. It leaves in this order, so nothing can recreate what is removed and no deploy ships half of it.

1. **Stop the old rail first (build step 1).** The settlement phases `payout_setup`, `payout_plan`, `payout`, `payout_finish`, `release_holds` and `record_holds` exist in `treasury.mjs` and drip `state.cents` through `stripeTransfer`; the route behind the "Connect your bank" card (`handleStripeConnectOnboard`) creates a Custom account and `syncKycDocsToStripe` uploads the KYC images from R2 to Stripe, which is the second identity check locked decision 3 forbids. Step 1 puts one guarded line in each, in its own commit: `payout_setup` skips straight to `backup` unless `LEGACY_CONNECT_PAYOUTS` is on (off by default in both environments); the Connect onboarding route answers 410 "payouts are not open"; `syncKycDocsToStripe` returns before any upload. From that release a funded pool can never be paid out through Connect, no identity document leaves Eustress, and the restricted key of step 3, which has no Connect or Files write, never meets a live button. The neutral site copy of item 7 ships in the same release. Tests: with the switch off no Stripe transfer is attempted (mutation: default the switch on); the onboarding route answers 410 and no file upload is attempted (mutation: remove the guard).
2. **Code, in one change with the accrue phases (build step 7).** The Connect deletion and the accrue phases land together, so no phase that calls `stripeTransfer` outlives it.
   - Delete `handleStripeConnectOnboard`, `handleStripeConnectStatus` and `syncKycDocsToStripe`, their routes and the route rows their module exports. No identity document or identity fact leaves Eustress again.
   - Delete `stripeTransfer`, `findTransfer`, `DESTINATION_FAULTS`, `STRIPE_MIN_TRANSFER_CENTS`, `STRIPE_SHORTFALL_ALERT_SHARE`, the six phases above and every `stripe_*` field of the payout record from `treasury.mjs`. Hold reasons carry no Stripe name.
   - **Phase aliases.** A job persisted in a retired phase must resume, since `runStep` looks its phase up by name. An alias table maps `payout_setup`, `payout_plan`, `payout` and `payout_finish` to `accrue_setup`, and `release_holds` and `record_holds` to `backup`. A phase name with neither a handler nor an alias fails the job loudly. Test: a job stored in each retired phase resumes and finishes; mutation: remove the alias.
   - Replace every reader of `stripe_connect_id`: `accountHoldsValue` and `payout_connected` in `identity_routes.mjs` read the destination record and the payable balance; the credit phase, `resolve_hold`, the administrator stats and the holds view follow. `payee_gate.mjs` is the only predicate.
   - `u:{date}:{id}` rows lose `connect` and `transfer_id` and gain `gate_reasons`.
   - In `tests/route_policy.test.mjs`, the three expectations that name the Connect route move to `/api/payouts/destination/invite` (VALUE; a frozen account gets 423), proven by mutating the rule to FRESH.
   - Site: remove `connect_bank`, `safe_connect_url`, `onboarding_words`, their tests and the "Daily payouts" card from `bliss.rs`; add `safe_mercury_url` beside `safe_checkout_url`. The Settings "Billing" tab leads to `/payouts`.
   - Delete `src/index.js.bak`.
3. **Legacy cost and accounting surfaces (build step 4).** `econ:costs:*`, `costs:*`, `cost:*`, the legacy seed keys, `PUT /api/admin/economics/costs`, `/api/accounting/costs`, `/api/accounting/dashboard` and the hard-coded `INFRA_COSTS` table are retired in favour of the expense ledger. A route is retired only in the Worker release that follows the site release which stops calling it: the admin Economics page calls `PUT /api/admin/economics/costs` (`eustress/crates/web/src/pages/economics_live.rs`) and the Bliss page reads `rate_display` (`pages/bliss.rs`), so the Worker serves the old and the current fields side by side for one release.
4. **Data.** Before cutover the `holds` operation is read in production; it returns none (the live account has never transferred). Any hold found is converted to a payable first. **In build step 7**, with the Connect removal and after step 6 has made `AccountAuth` the source of record for bans, `user.stripe_connect_id` stops being written or read, and a one-time audited operation (`POST /api/admin/connect/archive`) copies each value to `legacy:connect:{user}` in the Treasury and removes the field. The ban lives in `AccountAuth` by then, so a whole-record rewrite of the KV user that races a ban can drop nothing but a display mirror.
5. **People.** Eustress sends its own note to the three account holders first: payouts come directly from Eustress by bank transfer, no Stripe account is needed, and the one identity check is Eustress's own.
6. **The three live connected accounts (McKale only; live Stripe writes).** Read each account's `controller` fields. The code created them as Custom accounts, never paid, so balances are zero and `DELETE /v1/accounts/{id}` applies; a Standard account is removed from the platform instead. Never `reject`: it stamps a fraud status on people who did nothing wrong. Ask Stripe whether the identity files uploaded to those accounts are deleted with them and whether the empty Connect platform profile can be switched off.
7. **Copy, in two releases.** The release that stops the old rail (step 1) also stops the site selling it, on every page. The pages that state or sell payouts on 2026-11-12 are `pages/bliss.rs` (the "Daily payouts" card and "Get paid every day at UTC midnight"), `docs_earning.rs` (the Stripe Connect account text), `economics.rs` (the "Stripe" and "$0.50" wording it pins by test), `pages/premium.rs` (the "Bliss cash-out" line of the three plans, the comparison row and the "Who can cash out Bliss?" answer), `pages/trust_registry.rs` ("Contributors are paid daily at UTC midnight"), and `pages/support.rs` and `pages/currency.rs` (the cash-out sentences). All of them change to a neutral statement that payouts are not open yet, with no date and no figure; the pinned tests change in the same commit. The list is not the control. A copy test walks every file under `eustress/crates/web/src/pages` and fails on any user-facing string that matches payout, cash-out, "paid daily" or "get paid" without the not-open wording. Its allowlist is short and each line carries its reason (the processor list and the data-use text of `legal_acts.rs`, which step 12 rewrites; the support line that tells people where to send a private question), and its mutation restores one old line. A paid plan is never sold on a payout statement the system has been built to make false. The final wording, naming Mercury bank transfers, fees, caps and schedules, ships in the release that turns payouts on (section 16, step 12). Stripe issues no tax forms for contributors; every information return is Eustress's. The site's statements that identity documents are used only for verification are true.

## 12. Data model

### 12.1 Durable Objects

| Object | Binding | Instances | Migration tag | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Treasury` | `TREASURY` | one (`treasury`) | the class's own; the book is schema 3 under `book:` keys | The book and the journal; the checkout creation records |
| `Cashbook` | `CASHBOOK` | one (`cashbook`) | `v10-cashbook` | Mirror of Stripe and Mercury; walls; reconciliation; expense rows; meters |
| `TaxVault` | `TAX_VAULTS` | one per payee (`tax:{account id}`); under `SANCTIONS_SCREEN_SCOPE=all_verified`, one per screened account | `v11-taxvault` | Tax profile, forms, TIN envelopes, consents, terms, authorization, B-notice state, screening with its inputs, sign-in locations, cases |
| `AccountAuth` | `ACCOUNT_AUTH` | one per account | the class's own | KYC facts, ban state and account type, the sanctions block, the plan record, payout destination versions, the money notices, the notice outbox for money kinds |
| `DestClaims` | `DEST_CLAIMS` | one per bank fingerprint | `v12-destclaims` | Which payee holds a bank destination, and the later claimants of an open conflict with its review reference. Nothing lists it |
| `Wallet`, `CommerceHub`, `DeltaAccount` | their own | per account | their own | Ticket lots with their purchase index, load reservations, the reversal cursor, settle cancellation, meter events |

Every class is bound at the top level and in `[env.staging]`, exported from `index.js`, and listed in `tests/deploy_bindings.test.mjs` together with `HOLDER_LEDGER`, `KYC_STATUS`, `KYC_BUCKET`, `FINANCE_BACKUPS`, `EMAIL` and `BANK_RELAY`. `wrangler.toml` and that test have one writer, the orchestrator: a binding, its migration and its class export land in one commit at the completion of the step that owns them, after McKale has created the outside resource the binding names (16.1). All amounts are integer cents. No record below has an expiry unless one is stated.

**The book lives under its own keys, and code that predates it is fenced out.** The book state is `book:state`. Pre-journal code reads and writes a key named `state`, seeds a fresh one from KV whenever it is missing, and adds a sale's pool share straight to the funded pool with no journal entry; a rollback to it against a schema 3 object would book deposits and run its payout phases beside the book. Four things prevent that.

- **A forward guard ships first.** One release before any migration (step 1), the pre-journal code learns one rule: when `book:state` exists it refuses every operation and every settlement step, and seeds nothing. It is the backstop for a rollback across the floor: the runbook's floor (15.2) is that release until McKale sets a later switch, and from the cutover on it is the step 4 release or later.
- **Migration needs McKale's word.** The Treasury migrates on open only when `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET` names the schema (16.1). A deploy of the tree, by any session for any reason, never migrates. Until the migration runs, the object serves every book operation through its pre-journal path, whole: one switch at the door on whether `book:state` exists, never a branch inside an operation.
- **No shared key space.** Schema 3 hold records live under `phold:`, a prefix the pre-journal code never lists; its own `release_holds` lists `hold:` and finds nothing of the book's.
- **A halt and a replay, for what comes after genesis only.** Once the book exists, the appearance of a legacy `state` key, or of any `dep:` or `rev:` record without its `jref:`, is a halt of scope `all`, and the missing postings are replayed idempotently into `transit_cents`. Every record that predates the genesis instant already has its `jref:` (below), so the replay can only meet a record made after genesis by code that should not have run, and it never posts a sale the opening entry already holds.

**The cutover is one instant.** The book opens with one entry, `open:genesis`, which is the only posting for everything before it (2.6). The migration runs when McKale has set `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET=3`, in the first release that can read both providers (build step 4). It runs in bounded steps and writes `book:state` last. Its first step makes the Treasury refuse every deposit and reversal operation and every settlement step; `checkout_record` and `checkout_get`, which post nothing, are served throughout, so checkout keeps selling. A webhook refused in that window answers 500, and Stripe redelivers it after the cutover, when it books as an ordinary deposit after the genesis instant (a charge created before the instant posts 3100 in the place of 1010, below). The genesis read is taken only after the refusal has begun, so no sale is booked by the pre-journal path after the read and none falls between the legacy figure and the opening balance.

- **The figures are read, not typed.** Figures copied by hand from two dashboards cannot describe one instant while sales continue. The Cashbook takes one quiescent read in the sense of 2.4: the Treasury's version; Stripe's available plus pending balance; the payouts created and not yet landed (those whose credit has not posted on a Mercury account, by the ranks of 4.3 S4); the three Mercury balances with their event cursor; then the Stripe balance, the cursor and the Treasury's version again. Unequal reads are taken again. **The read also requires a quiet moment.** It lists the pending transactions of all three Mercury accounts and is taken again until there are none. Whether Mercury has already taken a pending row out of `currentBalance` is unknown at this point (`WALL_PENDING_CLASSES` is empty until evidence fixes it, 2.4), so a row pending at the instant could sit inside an opening balance and then post a second time, and no posting could repair that: the opening entry and `apply_bank_row` are the only writers of a bank account. The cutover therefore runs on a morning with no card authorization, ACH or transfer in flight on any of the three accounts, and McKale holds card spend on Checking until it has run. The instant is the time of that read, stored on `book:state` as `genesis_at`.
- **The entry.** Dr 1010 at the Stripe balance; Dr 1030 for each payout in transit; Dr 1100, 1110 and 1121 at the Mercury balances; Cr 2001 at the legacy pool figure; the balance to 3100. **The legacy figure is computed, not decided.** Inside the refusal the migration reads the pre-journal `state.cents`: the live account has never transferred (section 11, item 4), so that figure is every pool share the pre-journal path booked less every reversal it booked, and nothing changes it while deposits and reversals are refused. It is stored on `book:state` as `legacy_pool_cents`, shown in the admin view and announced on the out-of-band channel. `transit_cents` and `legacy_unfunded_cents` both open at that figure, so every bucket equals its account and the transit identity holds from the first entry. `legacy_fund` is the one audited operation that funds the figure from carry, and it funds no more than a sweep could use (4.4): the carry less its classified, unlocked part and less `carry_return_planned_cents`.
- **Every earlier record is closed in the same pass.** Each `dep:` and `rev:` that exists gets a `jref:` pointing at `open:genesis` and the stamp `payout_id: legacy`. A legacy reversal is keyed `rev:refund:{payment intent}:{cumulative}`; the pass takes each payment intent's refunds, which the genesis read fetched from Stripe, maps each legacy record to the refund id whose cumulative amount it matches, and rewrites it as `rev:refund:{re_id}`, posting nothing, so the next read of that refund finds it and the pool never gives the same part back twice. Each payout in transit is written as a `po:` record in state `legacy`: it is matched to its bank credit like any payout and is never composed or swept.
- **What straddles the instant.** A balance transaction's own `created` time orders it against `genesis_at`. One created before it never posts 1010, because that cash is inside the opening balance. Its row at ingest posts nothing and adds nothing to `po.pool_in` when its deposit or reversal is `legacy`. A sale whose webhook arrives after the instant for a charge made before it posts its lines with 3100 in the place of 1010 and is otherwise an ordinary deposit, stamped and swept with its payout. A balance transaction created after the instant posts as this document describes. A legacy deposit refunded after the instant gives back its pool part through `transit_cents` like any other.
- **The cursors start there.** Payout ingest takes payouts created at or after `genesis_at`, and the Mercury poll starts at the cursor the read returned.

Tests, on a fixture with 40 legacy deposits, 180.00 USD of legacy pool, two days of sales in the Stripe balance and one payout in flight: after the cutover every wall and every identity holds, the in-flight payout's credit clears 1030, and a later payout that carries charges made before the instant leaves 1010 equal to Stripe (mutation: replay legacy deposits into transit; mutation: post 1010 for a balance transaction created before the instant). A legacy refund is found under its refund id at the next read and is not booked again (mutation: keep the old key). A card authorization pending on Checking at the read postpones the cutover, and the book that opens once it has cleared equals the bank after every later posting (mutation: open the book while a row is pending).

### 12.2 Treasury records

| Key | Fields |
|---|---|
| `book:state` | Every bucket of 2.3; `hwm_decayed_for`, `deposit_count`, `version`, `seeded_at`, `genesis_at`, `schema: 3`, `halt {on, scope, causes [{kind, ref, at}], by, at, unhalt_at}` (`ref` names the run of a run anomaly or the row of an unmatched outflow), `next_seq {je, hold, act, form1042s}`, `sweep_in_flight`, `legacy_unfunded_cents`, the day counters of the tripwires (`stop_acts`, `payee_bans`, `dest_removals`, `first_payments`, `admin_reads` by key) |
| `secret:pay_id_key` | 256 random bits, generated in the object at genesis. Leaves it only inside the encrypted backup |
| `je:{seq, 12 digits}` | `seq`, `at`, `eff_date`, `kind`, `ref`, `lines [{acct, dr_cents, cr_cents, payee?, tag?}]`, `meta`, `relates_to?`, `digest`, `prev_hash`, `hash` (`hash = SHA-256(prev_hash, digest)`, the form the relay recomputes) |
| `jref:{key}` | `seq` |
| `bal:{acct}` | Signed integer cents |
| `mon:{YYYY-MM}:{acct}` | `dr_cents`, `cr_cents` |
| `ckpt:{seq}` | `seq`, `head_hash`, `balances_hash`, `at` |
| `chg:{put seq}` | The change log: the keys one Treasury put wrote or deleted. Every put of the Treasury writes its own `chg:` record, so a put holds at most 127 other keys. `backup_step` ships the current value of each key the log names, or a tombstone, and deletes the entries it shipped (15.3). The log does not record itself: `chg:` entries and `bkp:head` are the backup's own bookkeeping, written outside it and rebuilt by a restore |
| `bkp:head` | The last completed backup: `put_seq`, `je_seq`, `head_hash`, `at`, and the prefix of the last full pass. The `anchor` stream sends no digest past `je_seq` |
| `lock:{YYYY-MM}` | `at`, `by`, `replay_recorded` |
| `dep:{ref}` | `ref`, `kind`, `gross_cents`, `fee_cents`, `treasury_cents`, `platform_cents`, `eustress_fee_cents` (gifts), `tickets`, `delta`, `user_id`, `payment_intent`, `channel`, `at`, `refunded_cents`, `reversed {gross_cents, treasury_cents, platform_cents, tickets, delta}`, `currency`, `settled_cents`, `tax_cents`, `fee_basis` (`actual` or `estimated`), `balance_txn`, `payout_id` (or `legacy` for a deposit booked before the genesis instant), `buyer_country`, `buyer_region`, `reserve_cents`. Validated per kind (3.1) |
| `pi:{payment_intent}` | Deposit ref |
| `cs:{session id}` | The creation record of a Checkout Session the Worker made, of any kind: `kind`, `account` or none, `price_id` or `product`, the `amount_cents` and `currency` of a line built inline, `created_at`, `subscription_id?`, `paid_at?`. Written before the session is shown and read by the webhook before anything is credited (3.2). It is served on both sides of the cutover. An unpaid record is deleted 35 days after it was written |
| `csub:{subscription id}` | The session id whose record created this subscription; written at its first payment, by whichever of the session event and the invoice event is handled first (3.2) |
| `sbtx:{txn}` | Which key posted account 1010 for this balance transaction (`dep:{ref}` or `sbt:{txn}`), and the deposit or Suspense item it belongs to |
| `rev:{kind}:{stripe id}` | `kind`, `deposit_ref`, `gross_cents`, `tax_cents`, `treasury_cents`, `shortfall_cents`, `loss_cents` (beyond the sale), `platform_cents`, `tickets`, `delta`, `user_id`, `deposit_kind`, `at`, `dispute_fee_cents`, `funded` and `funded_at` (set at the reversal or at the landing that funds its deposit), `released` and `released_by` (the one path that ended it), `cash_txn` (the balance transaction that posted its cash side), `payout_id`. Its part is in `pending_reversal_cents` exactly while `funded` is true and `released` is false |
| `revx:{po_id}:{rev key}` | Index of the reversals a landing must visit, written as soon as the reversal and the stamp both exist: under the payout that stamps the reversal's deposit (for funding) and under the payout that stamps the reversal's own row (for release). The reversal pass of 4.4 lists it by payout |
| `dw:{dp_id}` | Dispute watch: `status`, `amount_cents`, `deposit_ref`, `next_read_at` |
| `unbooked:{ref}` | `ref`, `kind`, `user_id`, `object_id`, `tries`, `next_at` |
| `agg:{YYYY-MM}` | Receipts by kind, gift fees borne, `accrued`, `paid` (by rail), `withheld`, `wire_fees`, `returned`, `swept` |
| `po:{po_id}` | `amount_cents` (signed), `destination`, `rows_expected`, `rows_applied`, `net_sum`, `pool_in`, `pool_out`, `pool_restored`, `reserve_in`, `reserve_out`, `reserve_restored`, `part_cents`, `swept_cents`, `deferred [{dep_ref, cents, reversed_cents, reason}]` (`reversed_cents` are cents a reversal has taken back, waiting for the installment that carries that reversal's row), `match_key` (`trace` or `amount`), `bank_account` (EE, or BT when it landed there), `state` (`open`, `composed`, `bank_confirmed`, `swept_except_deferred`, `swept`, `failing`, `superseded`, and `legacy` for a payout in transit at the genesis instant; Stripe's own `failed` status lives on `sp:`), `failure_txn`, `carried_by` |
| `pobt:{po_id}:{txn}` | Marker: this row is counted in this payout |
| `sweep:{po_id}:{n}`, `sweep:topup:{date}:{n}` | `covers [{po_id, pool_cents, reserve_cents}]` (empty for a top-up), `fund_cents`, `deficit_applied_cents`, `carry_used_cents`, `topup_cents`, `transfer_cents`, `key`, `state` (`planned`, `submitted`, `landed`, `failed`, `manual`), `debit_txn_id`, `credit_txn_id`, `return_txn_id`, `relay_receipt`, `planned_at`, `landed_at`, `rev_pass` (`open` or `done`) with its cursor |
| `xo:{id}` | Expected outflow: `kind` (`carry_return`, `invest_repay`, `invest_return`, `suspense_refund`, `suspense_transfer`, `vendor_payment`), `account`, `amount_cents`, `ref`, `expires_at`, `planned_by`, `matched_txn`; for `invest_return` also `gross_cents`, `withholding_cents` and `withholding_kind` (`amount_cents` is the net sent); for a kind that pays a counterparty outside the payee path, the `screen` that cleared it (5.5) |
| `btdebit:{txn}` | An unexplained BT debit charged to carry: `amount_cents`, `cause`, `dispute_ref`, `state` (`in_dispute`, `recovered`, `written_off`), `recovery_txn?`. For the remainder of a stray payment: `payee`, `covered_cents`, `netted_cents` and the state `owed_by_payee` |
| `acc:{payee}:{score_date}:{e or v}` | Evidence: `cents`, `score`, `plan`, `form_seq`, `line`, `character`, `mature_on`, `value_us_cents` and `counted_tickets` (v rows), `hold_seq?`. Moving fields: `adjusted_cents`, `folded`, `us_cents` (the US-source stamp of 6.4) |
| `lot:{payee}:{year}:{line}:{character}` | `remaining_cents`, `us_cents`, `folded_cents`, `consumed_cents`, `oldest_open_on`, `withholding_settled_cents` |
| `payee:{id}` | `maturing_cents`, `payable_cents`, `held_cents`, `clawback_due_cents`, `stray_due_cents`, `first_qualified_at`, `suspended {reason, since}?`, `rail`, `waiting_reason`, `not_before`, `first_payable_at`, `oldest_matured_on`, `last_pay_id`, `last_sent_at`, `first_payment {pay_id, dest_version, state}`, `open_request`, `paid_unverified_cents`, `underwithheld_due_cents`, `last_contact_at`, `state_or_country` |
| `payq:{rail}:{oldest_matured_on}:{payee}` | Run order index |
| `est:{payee}` | Shadow-mode estimate; no claim |
| `pay:{pay_id}` | `rail`, `payee`, `period`, `lines [{lot, line, character, cents, us_cents, settled_cents, withheld_cents}]`, `gross_cents`, `withholding_cents`, `withholding_kind`, `withholding_reason`, `wire_fee_cents`, `net_cents`, `netted [{ref, cents}]` (the clawbacks and stray remainders the build write netted), destination snapshot `{recipient_id, version, rail, bank_country, last4, bank_fp, bank_fp_v}`, tax snapshot `{form_type, form_seq, tin_status, rules_hash}`, `state` (`reserved`, `sending`, `submitted`, `awaiting_approval`, `sent`, `return_pending`, `settled`, `returned`, `failed`, `void`, `review`), `first_to_dest`, `do_not_approve?`, `park_requested {reason, by, at}?`, `unknown_seen`, `key`, `memo_code`, `bank_txn_id`, `bank_tracking`, `request_id`, `fee_txn_id`, `relay_receipt`, `attempts`, `last_attempt_at`, `last_answer`, `first_unknown_at` (written once, at the first attempt that ended unknown), `last_error`, `return_reason`, `return_txn_id`, `returned_cents`, `return_loss_cents`, `reopened_at?`, `tax_year_crossed?`, a timestamp per state. `attempts`, `last_attempt_at` and `sending` are committed in a put of their own before each relay call, and only after the record has been read again in the same turn and found `reserved` with no `park_requested` |
| `payidx:{rail}:{payee}:{period}` | The payment id; written in the same put as the payment |
| `payx:{payee}:{inverted ms}:{pay id}` | Index of one payee's payments, for history without a scan |
| `phold:{payee}:{seq}` | A hold, under a prefix pre-journal code never lists: `cents`, `remaining_cents`, `source` (accrual date range, lot lines `[{lot, cents}]`, or a payment id), `reason`, `status` (`held`, `review`, `blocked`, `released`, `returned_to_pool`), `dest_version`, `since`, `review_reason`, `set_by_key_fp?`, `resolved_by`, `case_ref` |
| `claw:{id}` | `payee`, `reversal_ref`, `purchase_id`, `cents`, `netted_cents`, `state` |
| `uwh:{payee}:{seq}` | Tax under-withheld on cents a late US-work report showed US source: `cents`, `us_cents_found`, `pay_ids`, `report_seq`, `recovered_cents`, `state` (`open`, `recovered`, `borne`) |
| `f8233:{payee}:{year}` | The use of an accepted Form 8233: `exempt_cents` (copied from the form when it is recorded), `pending_cents` (reserved by payments built and not yet debited), `used_cents` |
| `ytd:{payee}:{year}:{form_seq}:{line}:{character}` | `form_seq` is the one in the payment's tax snapshot, never the accrual row's. `gross_cents`, `withheld_cents`, `wire_fee_cents`, `net_cents`, `payments`, `returned_cents`, `netted_cents`, `us_gross_cents`, `us_returned_cents` (the US-source part, from the payment lines' `us_cents`, written in the same put as the debit or the return; Form 1042-S and the Form 1042 worksheet read only these), `state_code`, `correction_flags` |
| `yagg:{payee}:{year}:{family}` | `gross_cents`, `withheld_cents`, `pending_cents`, `us_gross_cents`, `us_returned_cents` |
| `run:{rail}:{period}` | `phase`, `cursor`, `built`, `sent`, `held`, `waiting`, `total_net_cents`, `waiting_reason`, `anomaly_ack?`, `started_at`, `finished_at`, `errors` |
| `due:{stream}` | `next_at`, `backoff_ms`, `last_ok_at`, `last_error` |
| `adminq:{id}` | `act`, `args`, `by`, `key_fp`, `signature`, `queued_at`, `effective_at`, `frozen_ms`, `freezes_under` (`payouts`, `all` or `none`; an unhalt is `none`, and an act that adds cover, explains a Suspense item or clears a cause is `all`), `recheck` (the precondition and bank-fact binding re-run at the effective time), `cancelled_by?`, `cancel_reason?`, `done_at?`, `cents` |
| `act:{seq}` | Hash-chained log of every administrator act and every signed read: `act`, `args_digest`, `by`, `at`, `prev_hash`, `hash`. For a signed read, `act` is the route and `args_digest` covers the opaque target it read |
| `rl:{ts}:{id}` | One relay call: `route`, `key`, `cents`, `outcome`, `status`, `mercury_id`, `receipt_digest` |
| `inv:{id}` | Section 9 |
| `tr:{creator}` | Shadow Ticket credit ledger, section 10 |
| `taxyear:{Y}` | `status` (`open`, `draft`, `furnished`, `filed`), `rules_hash`, `report`, `filed {at, receipt, by}` |
| `tax_rules:{Y}`, `entity_tax:{Y}`, `state_rules:{Y}` | The activated rules files with their canonical hashes |
| `legacy:connect:{user}` | Archived `acct_` id and removal time |
| `job:{date}`, `u:{date}:{id}`, `vs:...`, `active`, `queue:{date}` | The settlement's own records. `u` rows carry `gate_reasons`. `vs:` rows live `VALUE_MATURITY_DAYS` plus 30 |

| Key | Fields |

| `sp:{po_id}` | `amount_cents` (signed), `status`, `arrival_date`, `trace_id`, `descriptor`, `automatic`, `reconciliation_status`, lifecycle, `mercury_txn`, `match_key`, `failure_code`, `failure_txn` |

A row with no `posted_at` is pending: tracked, never submitted, never "unapplied", and, for a debit on any of the three accounts, checked against the book at once (R3, R5). Never a routing or account number |
| `cursor:{source}` | Paging cursor for `stripe_bt`, `stripe_payouts`, `mercury_events` |
| `bal:{account}` | `available_cents`, `current_cents`, `as_of` |
| `wall:{account}` | The last quiescent read: `determinate`, `result` (`equal`, `explained`, `shortfall`, `surplus`), `bank_cents`, `available_cents`, `book_cents`, `early_cents`, `explained_by [txn]`, `at`, `indeterminate_run`, `shortfall_run` |
