| `exprule:{key}` | The key is a `counterpartyId`, a card merchant, or a counterparty name where the row carries no id. `account`, `vendor_id`, `kind` (`card` or `pulled`), `max_cents`. Never matched against a payment Eustress sent (8.4) |
| `vendor:{id}` | `name`, `w9_on_file`, `w9_r2_key`, `corporation`, `ytd_cents` by year |
| `evt:{source}:{event id}` | An observation written after the event's work committed. Never read to skip work |
| `wr:{request id}` | Wire request status, the resulting transaction id, the last gate re-check |
| `susp:{id}` | `source`, `account`, `amount_cents`, `why`, `at`, `resolved {by, at, as}` |
| `recon:{date}`, `tick:{YYYY-MM-DDTHH}` | Each check's result; each tick's result |
| `rules:{date}`, `stripe_settings:{date}` | Snapshots of Mercury auto-transfer rules and Stripe balance settings |
| `sdn` | `data_as_of`, `synced_at`, `r2_key`, `entries`, `accepted`, the parse cursor of a sync in progress |
| `relay_health:{date}` | The health answer and the audit digest comparison |
| `meter:{unit}` | Counters of 3.6 for `tickets` and `delta` |
| `mev:{ref}` | Meter event marker, pruned after 30 days |
| `geo:{YYYY-MM}:{country}:{region}` | `count`, `gross_cents`, `tax_cents` |
| `due:{stream}` | The Cashbook's scheduler table |

### 12.4 TaxVault records (one object per payee)

| Key | Fields |
|---|---|
| `profile` | `payee_id`, `payee_ref` (the opaque reference printed in the account number box of every information return), legal name as certified, business name, classification, addresses, citizenship, tax residence, `us_person`, `dob`, `form_seq`, `form_type`, `expires_at`, `tin_last4`, `tin_type`, `tin_match_status`, `self_certified_backup`, backup-withholding state and reason, sanctions status with list version, `sealed_at?` |
| `form:{seq}` | Immutable submission: type, revision, every field except a TIN, each TIN the form carries as `{key_version, key_fp, wrapped_key, nonce, ciphertext, commitment}`, payload hash (over the commitment-bearing payload), certification text hash, signature block, `signed_at`, `expires_at`, IP, session, key epoch, `superseded_by?`. Beside it, outside the immutable part: `tin_format_verified` (null, true, or false with a reason) and `checked_at`, written by the offline envelope check |
| `canary:{key_version}` | An envelope of a known test value, and the record of each offline drill |
| `attest:standing`, `attest:{YYYY-MM}` | The foreign-services certification; a monthly one when `FOREIGN_ATTEST_MODE` is `monthly` |
| `uswork:{seq}` | A report of US work: the dates it names, when it was made, and the rows it stamped |
| `form8233:{year}` | An accepted Form 8233, recorded by an administrator: the treaty article and paragraph it claims, the exempt amount for the year, the acceptance date |
| `loc:{YYYY-MM}` | Sign-in country and region per day; no raw address |
| `consent:{seq}` | E-delivery: `version`, `text_sha256`, `given_at`, `withdrawn_at?` |
| `terms:{version}` | `accepted_at`, `ip`, `session`, `text_sha256` |
| `auth:{seq}` | Payout authorization: `text_version`, `at`, `ip`, `dest_version` |
| `bnotice` | State, notice dates, deadlines, `mailed_at`, evidence key |
| `screen:{seq}` | `at`, `data_as_of`, the inputs as checked (encrypted), `outcome`, `matches`, `resolved {by, at, decision, reason}?` |
| `case:{seq}` | Payee case: `kind`, `pay_id`, `text`, `opened_at`, `state`, `resolved {by, at, note}?` |
| `doc:{year}:{form}` | No stored copy: the marker that a year-end document is available, its rendering inputs' hash, and the log of each signed read |
| `log:{seq}` | Hash-chained record of every write and every export of the profile. Holds digests of commitment-bearing payloads only |

### 12.5 Additions to other objects

**AccountAuth `state`:**

```
kyc_facts: { legal_name, dob, verified_at, id_type, issuing_country, id_hash, name_source }
ban:       { banned, by, at, reason }          account_type: human | agent | staff
sanctions_block: { on, since, screen_seq }     money_migrated: { at, from }
plan:      { plan, status, subscription_id, customer_id, price_id, interval,
             paid_through, cancel_at_period_end, read_at }
plan_history: [ { from, to, plan, paid_through } ]
dest:      { v, state: none|invited|pending|active|needs_reconfirm|under_review|name_mismatch|
                      needs_attention|bad|removed,
             recipient_id, invite_id, invite_at, rail: ach|wire, bank_country,
             account_type, last4, bank_fp, bank_fp_v, set_at, first_seen_at, usable_after,
             proven_at, signed_by_key_fp }
dest_history: [ ... ]
money_notices: [ { kind, at, last4?, until } ]
```

Operations: `ban_set`, `ban_clear` (the source of record for a ban; the KV user record mirrors it, and each call notifies the Treasury through `account_event`, which applies the parking rule of 5.6 to payments in flight and announces a ban of an enrolled payee); `sanctions_block_set`, `sanctions_block_clear` (called only by the Treasury's block and unblock); `plan_apply`, which also seeds an empty record from a subscription read at the account's first plan read (3.5); `dest_invite`, `dest_set` (checks the signature, the pin, the freeze and the age of the verified address, writes history and queues the notices in one put), `dest_reconfirm`, `dest_remove` (always allowed; counted when unsigned), `dest_mark`; `key_replaced` marks the current destination `needs_reconfirm`, whatever its age; `money_view` (one strong read returning `banned`, `account_type`, `sanctions_block`, `money_migrated`, `value_frozen_until`, `payout_pinned_until`, `key_changed_at`, `key_fp`, `email_verified_at`, `dest`, `plan`, `plan_history`, `kyc_facts`). Challenge purposes: `tax_form_sign`, `tax_doc_read`, `dest_invite`, `dest_set`, `dest_read`, `plan_portal`, `kyc_reverify`, `admin_act`, `admin_read`. An `admin_read` challenge is bound to one route and one target reference and is single use. `AccountAuth` also holds the one-time code of the sign-in hand-off (15.4): single use, 60 seconds, bound to the main site's origin and to the hash of the verifier the main site began the sign-in with. The notice outbox accepts `MONEY_NOTICE_KINDS`.

**Bringing existing accounts in.** `payeeGate` reads ban state, account type and KYC facts from `AccountAuth` and nowhere else, and on 2026-11-12 none of the three is there: bans live in the KV user record, and KYC facts live in KV `KYC_STATUS` records whose `ocr_name` is "extracted or self-typed" and whose unlinked records expire after 365 days. A one-time, resumable, audited backfill (`POST /api/admin/finance/accounts/backfill`, a delayed act that runs in bounded steps) and the same step run lazily inside `AccountAuth` on first money use copy `banned`, `account_type` and the KYC facts from KV, run one `plan_apply` for each account whose KV `plan:{id}` names a subscription its `AccountAuth` record lacks (the subscription is read from Stripe; nothing is copied from the mirror), and then set `money_migrated`. The legal name is taken only from the document read (`grok_analysis.extracted_name` with `name_matches` true), recorded as `name_source: "document"`; an account whose stored name was typed, or whose record lacks the document read, gets no legal name and is routed to the narrow reverification of 5.4. `payeeGate` refuses any account whose state lacks `money_migrated`, at both stages. Tests: an account banned in KV before the step is refused (mutation: skip the `money_migrated` check); a self-typed name never becomes a legal name (mutation: copy `ocr_name`).


**Sessions.** Session tokens are signed with `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY`, a key used for nothing else: not for vault key derivation and not for join tickets. One module, `sessions.mjs`, signs and verifies every session token through `signSession(env, claims)` and `verifySession(env, token)`. No other module touches the key or passes a secret to a signer or a verifier, and a source check fails when one does. On 2026-11-12 sessions are signed and verified with `JWT_SECRET` in five modules (`index.js`, `fresh_auth.mjs`, `passkeys.mjs`, `identity_routes.mjs`, and `actingAccount` in `kyc_identity.mjs`); from build step 1 every one of them calls `sessions.mjs`, so the key changes in one place. The move to the new key cannot log everyone out or fail every sign-in: tokens are signed with `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY` when it is set and with `JWT_SECRET` when it is not, and a `JWT_SECRET` token keeps verifying through an overlap.

- **The overlap is deploy configuration, not stored state.** `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER` is the UTC time McKale records when he puts the new secret. A `JWT_SECRET` token verifies only when its `iat` is before that time and its lifetime is at most 72 hours, so the overlap closes by itself 72 hours after the cutover, with nothing for an isolate to remember and no KV key to delete or reset. With the new key set and the variable unset, old-key tokens still verify, `/health` reports the overlap as having no end, and `session_overlap_open` fires after a day: forgetting the variable costs an alert, never a mass sign-out. `/health` reports which key signs and when the overlap closes. No release removes the `JWT_SECRET` branch: the overlap ends by itself, since a `JWT_SECRET` token verifies only when its `iat` precedes `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER` and its life is at most 72 hours, and `JWT_SECRET` signs only while `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY` is unset. From the step 6 release, `session_key_unset` fires each day the new key stays unset.
- **`JWT_SECRET` itself stays set for good.** Only its use for sessions ends. It still derives the evidence vault key, identity tickets and guest join tickets, image URL signatures, Space delete codes, unsubscribe tokens and visit hashes, and removing or rotating it makes sealed evidence unreadable.
- **A token says how it was obtained.** `amr` is `key` (a sign-in by identity file, which happens on the payments origin), `passkey`, or `handoff` (the main site's session, minted by the hand-off of 15.4, which carries no key). The payments-origin routes and every finance route refuse a `handoff` token.

Nothing that sets a destination, signs a tax form, opens the billing portal, reads a tax form, a year-end document or a bank digit, or performs an administrator act rests on a bearer token alone; each needs the Ed25519 signature above. The two stated exceptions for bank digits are in 5.11.

**Wallet:** `lots [{ref, remaining}]` for paid Tickets; each purchase records `lots_drawn`; `paid_today {day, usd_cents}` and `load_reserve [{session_id, usd_cents, expires_at}]` (the day cap's reservation; the creation record of every session is the Treasury's `cs:`); the wallet value limit of 3.6, evaluated on every Ticket the wallet holds (bought, granted by a plan and earned); `frozen` under a sanctions block; `paid_balance` clamped; the index `lotp:{lot ref}:{n}` of the purchases each lot funded; the operation `lot_reversal {ref, tickets}`, which debits the lot and, in the same put, writes the one cursor record `lotrev:{reversal_ref}` that the alarm advances a purchase at a time (5.10); an alarm that runs at most `OUTBOX_STEPS_MAX` steps before it re-arms; meter events in the outbox. `Wallet.refund` takes no `now` argument.

**CommerceHub:** `cancel_settle {purchase_id}`, `reverse_score {purchase_id}` (answers with the stored `score_day`); `settleDue` reads the buyer's copy before filing, files `buyer_country` and stores `score_day` on the sale record; integer 70 percent.

**DeltaAccount:** meter events in the outbox (`purchase`, `plan`, `trial`, `tool`, `expire`, `reversal`); `frozen` under a sanctions block, during which no lot is used, expires or burns, so the blocked-property inventory of 5.5 stays whole.

### 12.6 KV and R2

| Store | Key | Use |
|---|---|---|
| KV `USERS` | `plan:{id}`, the user record | Display mirrors of the plan record and of ban state |
| KV `PAYOUTS` | `payout:{date}`, `payout-month:{YYYY-MM}` | The public day record of 5.1 (the day's pool alone, rounded to two significant digits) and the monthly statement (two rounded figures under the monthly rule). No per-person rows, no gate reasons. Display only |
| KV `AUDIT_LOG` | audit entries | A copy of each administrator act; the record is `act:{seq}` in the Treasury |
| R2 `KYC_BUCKET` | `sanctions/raw-{date}`, `sanctions/index-{dataAsOf}.json` | The downloaded lists and the screening index. Destination claims live in the `DestClaims` objects and year-end documents are rendered on request, so a credential that can list this bucket learns neither who has a destination nor who received a form |
| R2 `KYC_BUCKET` | `tax-evidence/{opaque ref}/{n}`, `tax-docs/vendors/{id}` | Second B-notice evidence and vendor W-9s, encrypted to the offline key |
| R2 `FINANCE_BACKUPS` (private, used for nothing else) | `book-backups/{date}/full-NNNNN.json`, `delta-NNNNN.json`, `manifest.json` | The backup of every Treasury key: a full pass and the deltas after it, encrypted under `BACKUP_ENC_KEY`, each prefix with its own manifest (15.3) |
| R2 `FINANCE_BACKUPS` | `pay-intents/{pay_id}` | One encrypted object per payment intent, written before the send |
| R2 `SCENES` | `ledger-backups/{score_date}/` | The BLS ledger's own backup, written by the settlement under its own manifest. No money record |

No KV key is a source of record for money or for authority over it. The tax export bundle is streamed and never stored.

### 12.7 Treasury operations

Receipts and reversals: `deposit`, `refund`, `dispute`, `dispute_won`, `refund_failed`, `watch_dispute`, `apply_stripe_row`, `compose_payout`, `fail_payout`, `classify_stripe_row`, `checkout_record`, `checkout_get`.

Bank and sweep: `apply_bank_row`, `reclass_bank_row`, `plan_sweep`, `sweep_step`, `revpass_step`, `sweep_fail`, `sweep_manual`, `plan_outflow`, `legacy_fund`, `charge_bt_debit`, `recover_bt_debit`, `write_off_bt_debit`, `apply_unlinked_return`, `apply_stray_payment`, `net_stray_payment`, `classify_own_transfer`.

Settlement and payables: `start_settlement`, `run_steps`, `mature_step`, `start_run`, `run_step`, `apply_payment_status`, `park_payment`, `void_payment`, `holds`, `resolve_hold`, `set_hold`, `release_hold`, `reverse_value`, `adjust_payable`, `net_clawback`, `write_off_clawback`, `record_underwithheld`, `bear_underwithheld`, `block_outside`, `legal_hold`, `block_payable`, `unblock_payable`, `account_event` (a ban, a key replacement or a payee case reached the Treasury).

Control: `tick`, `halt`, `unhalt`, `cancel_unhalt`, `ack_run_anomaly`, `resolve_dest_claim`, `ack_dest_removals`, `admin_act`, `cancel_admin_act`, `undo_acts_by_key`, `purge_acts_of_key`, `verify_step`, `lock_period`, `post_adjusting`, `activate_rules`, `backup_step`, `anchor`, `restore_from_backup` and `rebuild_payments` (callable only while `RESTORE_MODE` is set by a deploy).

Tax and investments: `year_report`, `year_export`, `tax_deposit`, `wire_fee_none`, `wire_fee_late`, `invest_record`, `invest_match`, `invest_accept`, `invest_plan_repay`.

Reads: `period_totals`, `snapshot`, `overview`, `history`, `statement`, `roster_export`. One-time: `connect_archive`, `accounts_backfill`, `genesis` (the cutover of 12.1).

### 12.8 Modules

| Module | Role |
|---|---|
| `ledger.mjs` | Chart, `post()`, hash chain, balances, checkpoints, verify, the book identity, period lock. Pure; storage injected |
| `scheduler.mjs` | The `due:` table and the single alarm of each object |
| `treasury.mjs` | Composes operations; the settlement with accrue phases; no provider call |
| `payables.mjs` | Accrual rows, lots, runs, payments, holds, clawbacks, year totals |
| `payee_gate.mjs` | `payeeGate` |
| `cashbook.mjs` | The `Cashbook` class: ingest, matching, walls, checks, meters |
| `stripe_client.mjs`, `stripe_ingest.mjs`, `stripe_webhook.mjs` | The Stripe client of 3.4; balance transaction classification and payout ingest; the webhook handler |
| `checkout.mjs` | Every checkout handler, the pinned price tables and the creation records of 3.2 |
| `sessions.mjs` | The only signer and verifier of session tokens (12.5) |
| `handoff.mjs` | The sign-in hand-off: mints the one-time code on the payments origin, bound to the main site's verifier hash, and exchanges it with the verifier for the main site's `handoff` session (15.4) |
| `payout_rate.mjs` | `handlePayoutRate`: the public pool and emission figures, rounded as 5.1 states |
| `kyc.mjs`, `admin_users.mjs` | KYC submit and `requireVerifiedAdult`; the ban, delete and stats handlers |
| `dest_claims.mjs` | The `DestClaims` class |
| `mercury_read.mjs` | Read-only client with the field allowlist |
| `relay_client.mjs` | Signs and sends relay requests; classifies answers |
| `bank_adapter.mjs` | The provider-neutral seam (`send`, `queue`, `transfer`, `lookup`, `invite`); the Mercury implementation and the fake |
| `bank_match.mjs` | Matches Mercury rows to payouts, sweep legs, payments, fees, returns, tax deposits, expected outflows, investments, expenses |
| `tax_vault.mjs`, `tax_forms.mjs`, `tax_withholding.mjs`, `tax_yearend.mjs` | Section 6 |
| `sanctions.mjs` | List sync with the streaming tokenizer, index, screen |
| `finance_admin.mjs` | `requireFinanceAdmin`, the signed, delayed and announced act |
| `finance_tick.mjs`, `finance_alerts.mjs`, `finance_routes.mjs`, `payout_routes.mjs` | Section 8; the payee routes |
| `business_days.mjs` | US bank holidays and business-day arithmetic in Arizona time |
| `canonical.mjs` | The canonical text and JSON forms every hash is taken over |
| `route_policy.mjs` | Concatenates the route rows each module exports for its own routes, ahead of the prefix rules. No step and no other session edits a shared table (13) |
| `infrastructure/relay/*` | Section 7 |
| `tools/yearend/*` | The offline tools, each built by the step that first needs it (16.2): the journal replay; the key-generation scripts, the envelope opener with its commitment check and the key drill; the roster comparison, which reads Mercury's dashboard export from a file and holds no bank credential, with the earlier rosters it keeps, its map of customer id to recipient across them and its `ROSTER_GROWTH_MULTIPLE`; the year-end filing export. Run only by McKale, on the offline machine |
| `tools/deploy-tripwire/*` | The scheduled job of 15.4 that runs outside the Worker and the relay |

`index.js` holds one dispatch line for each of these handlers and none of their bodies. The site has two bundles. The payments bundle, served from the payments origin, holds every page that creates or reads an identity file (sign-up and sign-in by identity file, email change, passkey add and remove, key replacement), the tax step, the destination steps, year-end documents and the administrator finance page, and nothing that renders what another account wrote. The main bundle holds everything else and no identity-file code: `identity_file.rs`, the identity-file picker and `sign_challenge_ed25519` are linked into the payments bundle alone, and a bundle test holds that (15.4).

---

## 13. Routes

Classes are those of `route_policy.mjs`: PUBLIC (the route proves itself), SESSION (the handler verifies the session), FRESH (the `AccountAuth` object is asked), VALUE (fresh, and refused with 423 while value is frozen). Every row is explicit. Each module exports the rows of its own routes, with the class, the signature requirement and the origin each one needs, and `route_policy.mjs` concatenates them into `RULES` ahead of the `/api/payouts/*` and `/api/admin/*` prefixes, so adding a route never edits a table another session is editing. `tests/route_policy.test.mjs` walks the concatenated table as its "routes that matter" list and fails on a path two modules claim and on a finance path that only a prefix covers. A destination or tax-form route left to a FRESH prefix would let a frozen account redirect a payout, so the test asserts each by name and its mutation changes VALUE to FRESH. A route that sets where money goes, signs a tax form, opens the billing portal, reads a tax form, a bank digit or a year-end document, or replaces KYC facts also requires an Ed25519 signature by the account key over a one-time challenge; the class alone is never enough, FRESH included, since FRESH is still a bearer class. A read carries its signature in a request header. The route-policy test names each of these routes, and its mutation drops the signature requirement. The two bearer reads of a bank digit that 5.11 states are named in the same test as exceptions, so the table and the test agree. The routes marked "payments origin" follow one rule: a request whose `Origin` header is present and is not that origin is refused, a request that carries no `Origin` header is accepted, and a `handoff` session is refused either way. Studio, the Player and the CLI sign in by identity file through these same routes, natively, and send no `Origin` header (15.4); requiring the header would refuse every installed build. The `Origin` check binds a browser and nothing else: a client outside a browser sends whatever `Origin` it likes, or none, so no defence rests on that check alone.

### 13.1 Payee and public routes

| Path | Method | Class | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/api/stripe/webhook` | POST | PUBLIC | Stripe events; signature; read before crediting |
| `/api/mercury/webhook` | POST | PUBLIC | Mercury events; HMAC; enqueue only |
| `/api/payouts/rate` | GET | PUBLIC | `treasury_usd`, `daily_drip_usd` and the emission figures; every dollar figure rounded to two significant digits (5.1). No dollars-per-BLS field, no `platform_usd`, no `deposit_count` |
| `/api/payouts/status` | GET | SESSION | The five gates, balances (or, with `SHADOW_VISIBLE`, the estimate of an account that holds a qualifying plan), next date, rail, plan, the payee's own gate reasons. No bank digits |
| `/api/payouts/history` | GET | SESSION | One payee's payments from `payx:`: amounts, dates, rail and status. No bank digit and no name (5.11) |
| `/api/payouts/statement/{id}` | GET | SESSION | One payment's statement by its opaque id. The owner comes from the session; every miss is the same 404 |
| `/api/payouts/terms` | POST | FRESH | Accept the contributor terms |
| `/api/payouts/destination/invite` | POST | VALUE | Identity-file session, signed challenge `dest_invite`, verified email aged 30 days, pin check, gates 1 to 3; returns Mercury's hosted link. `not_open` while `ENROLLMENT_MODE` is `off` |
| `/api/payouts/destination/pending` | GET | VALUE | For the payee's own open invite, for the 60 minutes it lives: the last four digits and bank country the relay read, and the challenge to sign. A stated exception to the signed-read rule (5.11) |
| `/api/payouts/destination/confirm` | POST | VALUE | Signature `dest_set` over the recipient, fingerprint, last four, bank country and version; writes the destination version. Rate-limited |
| `/api/payouts/destination/reconfirm` | POST | VALUE | The current key signs a `needs_reconfirm` destination again, after the freeze |
| `/api/payouts/destination` | GET | FRESH | Rail and state of the current destination. Bank country and the last four digits only with a signed challenge (purpose `dest_read`), on the payments origin; owner only |
| `/api/payouts/destination` | DELETE | FRESH | Removes or disables the destination. Any session; exempt from the freeze and the pin; notice. Unsigned removals are counted (5.4) |
| `/api/payouts/case` | POST | FRESH | Open a payee case (`statement_wrong`, `not_received`, `not_my_account`). Any session; exempt from the freeze and the pin. Unsigned `not_my_account` cases are counted with unsigned removals (5.4) |
| `/api/tax/status` | GET | SESSION | Form type, status, expiry. No TIN digits |
| `/api/tax/form` | GET | FRESH | Form type, revision, status and expiry. The certified fields (name, address, the TIN as its last four digits) only with a signed challenge (purpose `tax_doc_read`), on the payments origin; owner only |
| `/api/tax/interview` | POST | FRESH | Selects the form; returns the exact text to be signed, the form sequence, the pinned key version and the signing challenge. It returns no legal name and no date of birth |
| `/api/tax/w9` | POST | VALUE | Identity-file session; envelope, commitment and key signature. `not_open` while `ENROLLMENT_MODE` is `off` |
| `/api/tax/w8ben` | POST | VALUE | Same |
| `/api/tax/w8bene` | POST | VALUE | 404 while `TAX_ENTITIES_ENABLED` is off |
| `/api/tax/us-work` | POST | FRESH | Report US work by date; stamps `us_cents` on the unpaid cents of those dates, and for cents of those dates already paid records them as US source in the paying payment's year total and the tax not withheld on them as under-withheld, recovered from the next payments (6.4) |
| `/api/tax/attest` | POST | FRESH | Monthly certification; used only when `FOREIGN_ATTEST_MODE` is `monthly` |
| `/api/tax/edelivery` | POST, DELETE | FRESH | Give or withdraw e-delivery consent |
| `/api/tax/documents` | GET | FRESH | List of year-end documents (year and form only); owner only |
| `/api/tax/documents/{year}/{form}` | POST | FRESH, payments origin | A recipient copy with a truncated TIN, rendered on request inside the vault and never stored. Requires a signed challenge (purpose `tax_doc_read`); owner only; a bearer token alone reads nothing |
| `/api/kyc/reverify` | POST | VALUE | Only for an account verified with an empty legal name. Identity-file session and signed challenge `kyc_reverify`; same-person check or administrator review |
| `/api/plans/checkout` | POST | SESSION | Plan checkout (`/api/subscriptions/checkout` is an alias) |
| `/api/plans/portal` | POST | FRESH, payments origin | Stripe billing portal session for the stored customer. Requires a signed challenge (purpose `plan_portal`); a bearer token alone opens none (3.5) |
| `/api/auth/handoff` | POST | FRESH, payments origin | Mints the one-time code (single use, 60 seconds, bound to the main site's origin and to the verifier hash the main site sent) for the session that asks. Carries no key |
| `/api/auth/handoff/exchange` | POST | PUBLIC | The main site exchanges the code, together with the verifier it kept, for a bearer session of its own, marked `amr: handoff`. A code whose stored hash is not the verifier's is refused (15.4) |

Payments-origin routes: the destination routes (`invite`, `pending`, `confirm`, `reconfirm`, and the GET), the tax form routes (`interview`, `w9`, `w8ben`, `w8bene`, `form`), `/api/kyc/reverify`, `/api/plans/portal`, the document route, `/api/auth/handoff`, and every existing account route that takes an Ed25519 signature by the identity key (sign-up and sign-in by identity file, email change, passkey add and remove, key replacement). The Worker refuses them from any other `Origin`, and accepts a request that carries none (13).

### 13.2 Administrator routes

Every route here requires `requireFinanceAdmin` (an identity-file session of an account in `FINANCE_ADMINS` whose key fingerprint matches), sits under `/api/admin/` and is served to the payments origin only. A GET is a read; a read marked "signed read" also needs the `admin_read` challenge and is logged (8.8). Every other route is a **signed, delayed and announced act** (8.6) unless the table marks it "stop-only" or "no delay". The route-policy test enforces that default route by route, and fails any GET whose declared response carries an account id, a name, a bank digit or a hold reason per payee without the "signed read" mark (8.8).

| Path | Method | Kind | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/api/payouts/daily` | POST | act (no delay; it runs only what the tick would run) | Start a settlement by hand |
| `/api/admin/finance/overview` | GET | read | The admin view of 8.8 |
| `/api/admin/finance/runs`, `/runs/{id}`, `/payees`, `/wires`, `/sweeps`, `/suspense` | GET | read | Counts and opaque references; bank and tax data masked |
| `/api/admin/finance/payees/{id}` | GET | signed read | One payee's payments and statuses, masked |
| `/api/admin/finance/roster/export` | POST | act (no delay; signed, announced and counted) | The roster of payees and amounts, with each payee's Stripe customer id, for the offline month-close comparison (7.7), encrypted to the offline key, streamed once. It is the Worker's own account and is compared, not trusted |
| `/api/admin/finance/halt` | POST | stop-only | Instant halt |
| `/api/admin/finance/unhalt` | POST | act | Lift a halt; lists the acts that resume |
| `/api/admin/finance/unhalt/cancel`, `/api/admin/finance/acts/{id}/cancel` | POST | stop-only | Cancel a pending unhalt or act |
| `/api/admin/finance/runs/{id}/ack-anomaly` | POST | act (its clock runs under the halt the anomaly set) | Acknowledge one run's anomaly: the cause is false for that run id, and the run finishes after the unhalt (8.6) |
| `/api/admin/finance/suspense/classify` | POST | act | Classify a Suspense item, on a bank account or at Stripe, under the bank-fact rules of 8.6 |
| `/api/admin/finance/suspense/charge-debit`, `/suspense/write-off` | POST | act | Charge an unexplained Bliss Treasury debit to carry as a debit in dispute; write off one the bank did not return |
| `/api/admin/finance/acts/undo` | POST | act | Reverse everything one key fingerprint signed since a stated time |
| `/api/admin/finance/suspense/refund`, `/suspense/transfer` | POST | act | Plan the return of a Suspense credit to its sender; or, for a credit on Bliss Treasury that cannot be returned, its move to Checking as a payable to its sender (8.6) |
| `/api/admin/finance/sweeps/manual` | POST | act | Settle a planned or submitted installment against a Suspense-classified EE to BT transfer of its exact amount (a reclass of both rows) |
| `/api/admin/finance/carry/plan` | POST | act | Plan a carry return of an exact amount, bounded by the carry a sweep may use; the amount is reserved from sweeps until it is matched or expires (4.7) |
| `/api/admin/finance/legacy/fund` | POST | act | Fund a legacy pool figure from carry |
| `/api/admin/finance/wires/manifest` | POST | act (no delay; it moves nothing) | Ask the relay to issue the ready-to-approve manifest |
| `/api/admin/finance/payments/record` | POST | act | Record the Mercury transaction found for an `outcome_unknown` payment |
| `/api/admin/finance/payments/void` | POST | act | Void a payment after the confirming read of both lists, taken again at the effective time; on either rail, only with no open request |
| `/api/admin/finance/wirefee/none` | POST | act | Record Mercury's confirmation that a wire carried no fee |
| `/api/admin/finance/clawback/write-off` | POST | act | Write off a clawback |
| `/api/admin/finance/legal-hold` | POST | stop-only to set (counted); act to lift | A legal or estate hold with a case reference |
| `/api/admin/treasury/holds` | GET | read | Counts by reason and opaque hold references. No payee, no reason per hold, no bank digit |
| `/api/admin/treasury/holds/{ref}` | GET | signed read | One hold: its payee, its reason, its cents and a masked destination (last four, bank country, rail) |
| `/api/admin/finance/dest-claims` | GET | signed read | Open destination conflicts, each naming its two accounts |
| `/api/admin/finance/dest-claims/resolve` | POST | act | Decide a destination conflict on a bank document recorded by case reference: release the losing claim and return the winner's own signed destination to `pending` (5.4). A body naming a destination is refused |
| `/api/admin/finance/dest-removals/ack` | POST | act | Acknowledge the removal tripwire and send `destination_reconfirm` to every payee whose destination it parked (5.4) |
| `/api/admin/treasury/holds/resolve` | POST | act | `pay` or `return`; a body naming a destination is refused |
| `/api/admin/sanctions/queue` | GET | signed read | Potential matches |
| `/api/admin/sanctions/resolve` | POST | act | Clear or block, with a reason. The money is already stopped by `sanctions_hold`; a block starts the reporting clock and sets `sanctions_block` on the account when it takes effect |
| `/api/admin/sanctions/unblock` | POST | act | Undo a block, with an OFAC licence number, delisting notice or counsel reference |
| `/api/admin/finance/accounts/backfill` | POST | act | The one-time copy of ban state, account type and document-read KYC facts from KV into `AccountAuth` (12.5) |
| `/api/admin/kyc/reverify/resolve` | POST | act | Resolve a reverification that was not provably the same person |
| `/api/admin/tax/review` | POST | act | Resolve a tax form review |
| `/api/admin/tax/cp2100`, `/api/admin/tax/bnotice/mailed` | POST | act | Start the B-notice clock; record the mailing |
| `/api/admin/tax/form8233` | POST | act | Record an accepted Form 8233 for a payee and year, with its treaty article and exempt amount |
| `/api/admin/tax/underwithheld/bear` | POST | act | On or after the Form 1042 due date, bear the under-withheld tax that later payments did not recover (6.4) |
| `/api/admin/tax/withholding/remitted` | POST | act | Reclass an IRS deposit out of Suspense: IRS counterparty, and an amount for each named withholding payable within that payable's balance |
| `/api/admin/tax/envelopes/export` | POST | act (no delay; signed, announced and counted) | The envelopes not yet checked, encrypted whole to the offline key, streamed once (6.6) |
| `/api/admin/tax/envelopes/result` | POST | act | Record `tin_format_verified` per form from the offline check. Delayed like any act, because a true result lifts the payment limit of 6.6 |
| `/api/admin/tax/key-drill` | POST | act (no delay) | Record an offline key drill |
| `/api/admin/tax/year/{Y}/report` | GET | signed read | The pre-filing report; no TINs |
| `/api/admin/tax/year/{Y}/export` | POST | act (no delay; signed, announced and counted) | The export bundle, encrypted whole to the offline key, streamed once, `no-store`, never stored |
| `/api/admin/tax/year/{Y}/filed` | POST | act | Record the IRIS receipt |
| `/api/admin/tax/rules` | PUT | act | Activate a `tax_rules`, `entity_tax` or `state_rules` file by hash. Only a hash the deployed bundle contains is accepted |
| `/api/admin/expenses` | GET | read | Expense rows |
| `/api/admin/expenses/categorize`, `/api/admin/expenses/rules` | POST, PUT | act | Classify; maintain rules and vendors. A rule covers card charges and vendor-pulled debits within its ceiling and never a sent payment; when it takes effect it reclasses the Suspense debits it covers (8.4) |
| `/api/admin/expenses/plan` | POST | act | Plan a vendor payment from Engine Expenses or Checking: an exact amount, the account, the vendor and an expiry (an `xo:` record) |
| `/api/admin/books/close` | GET | read | The month's trial balance, balance sheet and journal totals by account and day. No entry line and no payee |
| `/api/admin/books/export` | GET | signed read | The general ledger detail and trial balance, with opaque payee references |
| `/api/admin/books/lock`, `/api/admin/books/adjust` | POST | act | Lock a month; post an adjusting entry on the allowlist |
| `/api/admin/investments`, `/{id}/accept-funding`, `/{id}/match`, `/{id}/accept`, `/{id}/repay` | GET, POST | read; act | 404 while `INVESTMENTS_ENABLED` is off |
| `/api/admin/connect/archive` | POST | act | One-time archive of `stripe_connect_id` values (section 11) |

The ban route (`/api/admin/users/{id}/ban`) is a moderation route: it sits outside the finance prefixes, behind the moderation role (`requireAdmin`), and it stays there, because a ban must be quick and moderators are not finance administrators. It writes `ban_set` in `AccountAuth` first and notifies the Treasury in the same request. What it can do to money is bounded by design, not by who may call it: a ban suspends a qualified payee's shares into a hold and never forfeits them (5.2), every ban of an enrolled payee is announced on the finance alert path, and a burst raises `ban_tripwire`. `ban_clear` has the same callers. Returning a banned payee's hold to the pool is a finance act, signed and delayed.

Site: `/payouts` (the checklist, statements and history on the main site; the tax step with in-browser TIN encryption to the pinned key, the destination steps, the billing portal link and year-end documents on the payments origin), every page that creates or reads an identity file on the payments origin with the hand-off back to the main site, the administrator finance page on the payments origin, the in-product money notice, a `PayoutStatus` type read from `/api/payouts/status`, `safe_mercury_url` beside `safe_checkout_url`. When a response shape changes, the Worker serves the earlier and the current fields side by side for one site release.

---

## 14. Secrets and configuration (names only)

Code that reads a secret ships before the secret is set, because `wrangler secret put` deploys at once. `/health` reports each secret and binding as a boolean, never a value, and reports the active tax key fingerprint. Every name is documented in the `wrangler.toml` secrets comment block with its exact put command for both environments.

**Worker secrets (McKale, `wrangler secret put NAME`, and again with `--env staging`):** `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` (a restricted key), `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `MERCURY_READ_TOKEN`, `MERCURY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `RELAY_SIGNING_KEY`, `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY`, `BACKUP_ENC_KEY`, and, only when the Access fallback of 7.1 is in use, `RELAY_ACCESS_CLIENT_ID` and `RELAY_ACCESS_CLIENT_SECRET` (the Access service token). `JWT_SECRET` stays set for good. Only its use for sessions ends (12.5): it still derives the evidence vault key and other keys that are not session keys, and removing or rotating it makes sealed evidence unreadable.

**Worker plain configuration, `[vars]` and `[env.staging.vars]`:** `DEPLOY_ENV`, `PAYOUTS_MODE`, `ENROLLMENT_MODE`, `SWEEP_MODE`, `WIRE_MODE`, `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE`, `MERCURY_ENV`, `MERCURY_BASE_URL`, `MERCURY_EE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `MERCURY_CHK_ACCOUNT_ID`, `MERCURY_BT_ACCOUNT_ID`, `STRIPE_API_VERSION`, `RELAY_SIGNING_KEY_ID`, `FINANCE_ADMINS`, `TAX_PUBLIC_KEY_V1`, `TAX_KEY_ACTIVE`, `FINANCE_ALERT_MAILBOX`, `ACH_CADENCE`, `PLAN_GATE_AT_SEND`, `FOREIGN_ATTEST_MODE`, `INVESTMENTS_ENABLED`, `TAX_ENTITIES_ENABLED`, `NRA_WITHHOLDING_ENABLED`, `TICKET_RESERVE_MODE`, `TICKET_CASHOUT_LIVE`, `WALLET_LOTS_LIVE`, `RESTORE_MODE`, `SHADOW_VISIBLE`, `LEGACY_CONNECT_PAYOUTS`, `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET`, `STRIPE_EE_BANK_ID`, `WALL_PENDING_CLASSES`, `SANCTIONS_SCREEN_SCOPE`, `PAYMENTS_ORIGIN`, `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER`, `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE`, the Workers VPC service id of `BANK_RELAY`, and every setting of section 5.8. Every switch that opens a money path defaults to off in both environments, and every switch that governs a stream which sells today defaults to its present behaviour, so a deploy with defaults changes nothing a buyer can see; an unset Mercury value fails closed.

**Pinned in source, with a test each:** the SHA-256 fingerprint of every permitted tax public key version (in the Worker and in the site bundle); the canonical hash of every rules file in the bundle, the treaty table included; the public half of each permitted `RELAY_RESPONSE_KEY` by key id; the pinned Stripe price ids of every catalog entry.

**Worker bindings:** `TREASURY`, `CASHBOOK`, `TAX_VAULTS`, `ACCOUNT_AUTH`, `DEST_CLAIMS`, `WALLETS`, `COMMERCE_HUBS`, `DELTA_ACCOUNTS`, `HOLDER_LEDGER`, `BANK_RELAY`, `KYC_BUCKET`, `FINANCE_BACKUPS`, `SCENES`, `KYC_STATUS`, `USERS`, `PAYOUTS`, `AUDIT_LOG`, `EMAIL`.

**Relay secrets (McKale, `fly secrets set`):** `MERCURY_SEND_TOKEN`, `MERCURY_REQUEST_TOKEN`, `BANK_FP_KEY`, `RELAY_RESPONSE_KEY`, `TUNNEL_CREDENTIALS`, `ALERT_WEBHOOK`.

**Relay configuration:** `WORKER_VERIFY_KEYS`, `MERCURY_BASE_URL`, `EE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `BT_ACCOUNT_ID`, `RELAY_ENV`, `RELAY_ARMED`, `CAP_ACH_PAYMENT_CENTS`, `CAP_FIRST_ACH_CENTS`, `CAP_ACH_DAY_CENTS`, `CAP_ACH_DAY_COUNT`, `CAP_ACH_WEEK_CENTS`, `CAP_ACH_30D_CENTS`, `ACH_RUN_WEEKDAY`, `ACH_RUN_WINDOW_DAYS`, `SELF_DISARM_MULTIPLE`, `SELF_DISARM_FLOOR`, `BANK_FP_VERSION`, `CAP_ACH_RECIPIENT_PERIOD`, `CAP_FIRST_PAYMENTS_DAY`, `CAP_WIRE_REQUEST_CENTS`, `CAP_FIRST_WIRE_CENTS`, `CAP_WIRE_MONTH_CENTS`, `CAP_SWEEP_CENTS`, `CAP_SWEEP_DAY_CENTS`, `CAP_INVITE_DAY_COUNT`, `RECIPIENT_MIN_AGE_HOURS`, `WIRE_MANIFEST_TTL_MINUTES`, `WIRE_LIVE_RECHECK_MINUTES`.

**Held offline by McKale, on no server:** the tax private key for `TAX_PUBLIC_KEY_V1` (generated on the offline machine; two copies, away from the daily workstation); a copy of `BANK_FP_KEY`; a copy of `BACKUP_ENC_KEY`. No Mercury credential is held there: the offline roster comparison reads a file McKale exports from the dashboard (7.7).

**Deploy credentials are bank credentials.** A Fly token that can deploy or run a command in the relay Machine reaches the send token; a Cloudflare token that can deploy the Worker reaches the relay signing key; a Cloudflare token or dashboard role that can edit Tunnels, Zero Trust or the Workers VPC service can put itself between the Worker and the relay; a credential that can deploy the payments origin can change the page that holds keys and TINs. None is a standing login on the daily workstation (15.4).

**Files in the repository, signed off by the accountant:** `tax_rules.{year}.json`, `treaty_table.{year}.json`, `entity_tax.{year}.json`, `state_rules.{year}.json`. **Dated configuration:** the blocked-country list, `wire_countries.json`, `sales_regions.json`, `payout_regions.json`, the certification texts by form revision, the contributor terms and payout authorization texts by version. Every one is hashed in the canonical form of 6.3.

---

## 15. Failure modes

### 15.1 The register

"Halt" is the Treasury halt of 8.6. This table is the review checklist Security uses.

| # | Failure | What happens (what prevents or bounds it) | How it is detected | How it recovers |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Compromised Worker (code execution, a leaked signing key, or hostile code in a dependency of a build McKale himself deploys) asks the relay to pay an attacker, including recipients it enrolled itself through the invite route | The relay rebuilds every request from a strict schema; account ids come from relay configuration; one lock and a ledger written first make the caps hold under a burst; a recipient must come from an invite, be announced on McKale's channel and wait 72 hours after that; the first payment is 25 USD, five first payments a day, and nothing more to a recipient until the first has aged; one ACH per recipient per period; ACH only inside the run window; one run's worth per ISO week and five per rolling 30 days in total; the relay disarms itself on a surge of first payments or recipients | Only what runs outside the Worker counts here, since the attacker writes matching `pay:` records, R3 reads clean and the exported roster is the attacker's too: the relay's recipient announcements, its weekly per-recipient digest, its caps and self-disarm; at month close, the offline map of customer to recipient, McKale's notice from his own mailbox to the email on each new recipient's customer's earliest paid plan invoice dated before the enrollment, which no portal session can change, with every customer whose current billing email differs from it listed, and his reading of every new recipient and every jump against the rosters kept offline (R25), with the stated limit that every announced name is one the Worker supplied (7.7); Mercury's own notifications. The script hash against a second-machine build (15.4) finds a tampered build machine or cache and nothing else: a hostile version pinned in the lockfile builds to the same hash everywhere, and its only control is the line-by-line lockfile review before the deploy. A patient attacker inside the caps is bounded in amount by the caps and found by a person reading names, not by a machine | `RELAY_ARMED=false`; rotate `RELAY_SIGNING_KEY` (two key ids); incident runbook 15.2 |
| 2 | Compromised Worker reads bank data with the read token | It can read balances, transactions and recipient bank details from Mercury. Eustress stores none | Mercury token usage; not detectable in the Worker | Rotate the token; narrow its scope if Mercury offers one (section 21) |
| 3 | Compromised Worker redirects Stripe revenue | The Stripe key is restricted: no write on external accounts, payouts or balance settings | Settings drift monitor; `account.external_account.updated` | Rotate the key; McKale checks the Dashboard |
| 4 | Compromised relay host, or a stolen credential that can deploy or run a command on it | No public port; standard-library service; its own Fly organization; deploy tokens short-lived and off the daily workstation; the relay's caps do not bind such an attacker, and the send token reaches Engine Expenses and Checking as well as Bliss Treasury (residual risk 7.11) | For a hostile release, the deploy tripwire, which reads the image digest from Fly's own API (`deploy_drift`); it sees nothing of a command run inside the Machine. For either path, the outflow checks on all three accounts within one poll of a pending debit appearing, with the Mercury transaction ids to cancel while they can still be cancelled: R3 halts for Bliss Treasury, and R5 raises `unmatched_outflow` for Engine Expenses and Checking and books the debit to Suspense, never as an expense. No expense rule covers a sent payment, so a recipient named after a known vendor or after the IRS alerts like any other. Mercury notifications; a silent digest | Kill switch 4: delete both tokens or the allowlist entries in Mercury; cancel the pending transactions the checks named |
| 5 | Stolen administrator session (a lifted token, or one forged with a leaked session key) | Every non-GET finance route needs a fresh key signature. Reads are bounded too: list views (holds and the books close among them) return counts, totals and opaque references; a per-payee view, one hold's detail, the sanctions queue, the destination conflict queue, the tax report and the books export need a signed read challenge bound to one route and one target, are written to the act chain and are rate limited. The route-policy test fails any finance GET that returns per-payee data unsigned. Admin routes cannot name a destination, raise a cap or read a TIN | The act log; `admin_read_bulk`; `admin_act_pending` on both channels | Halt; rotate `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY` |
| 6 | Stolen administrator key | Acts wait until the next Arizona business morning at least 12 hours out, are announced out of band, cancellable, capped per day, bound to bank facts that are checked again when the act takes effect, and can only reduce a payable or act through the send gates. A key removed from the configured list can make nothing fire: its queued acts are purged | `admin_act_pending`; `unhalt_pending`; `admin_act_cancelled` | Cancel the act; redeploy `FINANCE_ADMINS` without the key; bulk undo of everything it signed; halt |
| 7 | Stolen contributor session (a bearer token lifted by malware or script, or forged with a leaked session key) redirects a payout or reads payee data | Setting a destination needs an Ed25519 signature by the account key over the last four digits the payee was shown; a tax form needs the same key; the tax form on file, a year-end document and the destination's last four digits are each read only with a signed challenge. A token alone sets nothing, opens no billing portal (where Stripe shows the billing name, address and card digits, and where the plan that gates cash-out can be cancelled), and reads no legal name, no address, no TIN digit, no tax document and no bank digit, with two stated exceptions: the in-product notice shows the last four digits of a destination changed in the last 30 days, and `destination/pending` shows the payee's own open invite its last four digits and bank country for 60 minutes. A token does read that one account's balance, payment history and statements (amounts, dates, rail, status), so a leaked session key exposes those figures for every account it forges a session for. That is accepted and stated (5.11); rotation of the key ends it. Unsigned destination removals and unsigned `not_my_account` cases are counted, and past the limit they park the destination without removing it, without a dispute hold and without mailing anyone | Notice to every address and in the product; fingerprint mismatch at send; `dest_removal_tripwire` | Account freeze; hold; rotate `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY`; the tripwire's acknowledgement sends each parked payee a reconfirm notice |
| 8 | Stolen identity file: the thief sets a destination, the owner then replaces the key, days or months later | Every key replacement marks the current destination `needs_reconfirm`, whatever its age and however many payments have settled to it; the owner can remove it at once, during the freeze and the pin. A destination can be enrolled only when the account's verified address is 30 days old and the product has shown its notice all that time | The key-replacement notice, by mail and in the product, names the last four and carries the disable control | The current key re-signs or removes it; nothing is paid meanwhile |
| 9 | Payee enrolls an account that is not theirs, or mistypes into a live account | First payment cap; one bank account per payee; signed authorization | A return; a `not_my_account` case | Destination disabled; hold; counsel's rule on a misdirected payment |
| 10 | Forged, replayed or redelivered webhook | HMAC, time window; a webhook only schedules a read; no event record is used to skip work, so a redelivery after a failed read does the whole job | Duplicate event counter | Nothing to recover: the read decides |
| 11 | Replayed relay request | Signature covers environment, method, path, time, nonce and body hash; random persisted nonces; a repeated payment key always returns the existing transaction | Relay log of replays | Nothing to recover |
| 12 | Double payment (retry, crash, lost answer, two runs, a send interleaved with a ban or a void) | One payment id per payee per period; constant key; the attempt committed before every call by check-and-set, so a payment found mid-send is unknown and an event delivered during the awaited gate stops the send; no park or void of a payment with an attempt before a confirming read, and none at all of a payment whose answer was lost; look-up before every send in the relay, which drops a request that waited past its clock; read of both lists before resend, with a request matched to one payment by memo code and creation time; the unknown latch; the resend age, measured from the first unknown attempt, inside Mercury's 24 hour guard; a Mercury refusal ending resends at once; one object serializes sends | R4 | Automatic halt on the first duplicate. A duplicate that could not be cancelled is booked as a stray payment to that payee against the payable, with its year totals, and nothing is credited to the pool; a remainder the payable cannot cover rests in 1210 under the payee and is netted from later earnings or returned on request (8.6) |
| 13 | Outcome of a send unknown beyond the resend age | The payment stays `reserved` and goes to review; never resent, never released. Before that age too, a park, a gate failure and the close of its window only record a request on it | `outcome_unknown` | An administrator reads Mercury, then records the transaction or voids (delayed). The void reads the transaction list and the request list again when it takes effect and is cancelled if either shows the payment |
| 14 | A payee's standing changes between build and send, or while a wire waits for approval (a ban, a sanctions match, a `not_my_account` case, a replaced key, a changed destination) | The send gate and the snapshot check run immediately before every relay call, including resends and resumed runs, and on every poll of a wire awaiting approval | `wire_do_not_approve` on both channels naming the request | The payment is parked and voided to the hold the gate names |
| 15 | A lost answer duplicates a wire request | The relay lists open requests before creating one and refuses a second for a recipient; the Worker resolves from the request list, never the transaction list alone | R4 (two open requests) | Reject the duplicate in Mercury; a void needs zero open requests |
| 16 | Bank details are edited at Mercury while a wire, or an ACH payment Mercury turned into an approval item, awaits approval | The relay re-verifies every pending request's fingerprint hourly, before each manifest and every 5 minutes while a manifest is live, and voids the manifest on any change; the manifest expires in 30 minutes; a post-send check compares the transaction's routing details. The window before approval is narrowed to one re-check interval and closed only if a send-money request snapshots bank details (Q7) | "Do not approve request X"; `wire_details_mismatch` | Reject the request; the destination is `needs_attention` |
| 17 | Returned ACH or wire | The one payment. One return per payment, whichever shape Mercury uses: a linked credit, a status change, both, or a credit Mercury did not link | Mercury status or a linked return credit, read by poll; an unexplained credit in Suspense holds settlement of the payments it may belong to | `payret:{pay_id}`, by the row or by the bound classification `payment_return_unlinked`; year totals reduced by the cash that came back; destination disabled; hold; no automatic retry; a return after settlement reopens the payment |
| 18 | A posted ACH is later marked failed with no linked credit | Any failed, reversed or cancelled status after posting is a return; settlement requires a fresh read still showing `sent` | The status change; the bank wall | `return_pending`, then the return applied |
| 19 | Wire rejected, held or returned | The one wire; the fee rules of 5.7 | Poll of the request and the transaction | Hold `wire_rejected`; `return_loss_cents` recorded |
| 20 | Wire not approved (approver unavailable), or the wire run cannot start on the 3rd | Payments wait in `reserved_cents`, counted in coverage; the month stays open for the run until the 24th (the 19th in December) | `wires_awaiting_approval`, `wire_approval_overdue`, `run_waiting` | Stand-in approver (D2); payees told after the deadline; a rejected request returns to the lots |
| 21 | Payment to a sanctioned person, bank or region | Country block at enrollment; SDN screen at enrollment, at every build, before every send and on list change; the wire recipient's bank screened against the entity list; region match; sign-in history; Mercury's own screening behind it | `sanctions_hit`; Mercury `blocked` | `sanctions_hold` or `blocked_cents`; the 10 business day report; the annual report |
| 22 | Sanctions list stale, truncated or wrongly parsed | List age is a gate; a sync is accepted only with a plausible entry count, a date not earlier than the last, and the canaries present | `sanctions_list_stale`, `sanctions_sync_rejected` | The previous index stays current; sends resume when a sync is accepted |
| 23 | Refund or dispute after the pool share was swept, or between the payout's creation and its sweep | The pool gives back what it holds; a reversal is funded into `pending_reversal_cents` at the reversal or at the landing that brings its deposit's share in, and released exactly once; `cents` and the drip base never go below zero | The reversal's row in a later payout; R13 recomputes the counter from the records | Netted from the next sweep through `carry_cents` and `sweep_deficit_cents`; any shortfall is Eustress's, booked |
| 24 | A refund is followed by a chargeback, a dispute is only an inquiry, or a refund stays pending and executes at another USD amount than the webhook knew | Cash is posted only from the balance transaction; the claim side is trued to the row's actual amount when the cash moves, so 2410 clears; an inquiry is a watch record; cash beyond the sale goes to 4910 | Aging of 2410; the Stripe wall | Nothing to repair: the book never ran ahead of Stripe |
| 25 | Stolen card buys Tickets, spent with a colluding creator; or a creator freezes their own account to dodge the wait | Value rows mature at 120 days whatever holds they pass through, and only with lots live; the reversal is keyed per purchase and recomputes the capped score; value score caps | Dispute rate; the lot trace | `reverse_value`; clawback netted from what is unpaid, and again in every build write, so cents that return to the payable later are netted before anything is paid; maturing paused at 0.5 percent |
| 26 | Wrong or stolen TIN | W-9 name must equal the KYC legal name; format checks in the browser and the offline tool | IRS CP2100, matched by the payee reference on the return; the offline duplicate check; the pre-filing report | B-notice clock; backup withholding |
| 27 | Foreign status claimed falsely, or a foreign payee works from the United States | KYC indicia cross-check; source stamped on every row at accrual by its line's own rule (for services, from the sign-in location of its score date and from US-work reports; for a US-source line, its basis), so cents earned in the United States are US source in whatever month they are paid | Change-of-circumstances triggers; sign-in history | `tax_hold`; the form is refused; US-source cents are withheld on, or wait under `us_presence` while the nonresident branch is off; a US-work report that arrives after payment records the tax as under-withheld, recovered from the next payments and otherwise borne by Eustress |
| 28 | TIN or bank data leak from storage, logs or backups | TINs encrypted in the browser to an offline key; salted commitments only; bank numbers never stored; no recipient copy of a year-end form and no destination claim stored in any bucket; response allowlists; planted-value log tests; encrypted backups in a private bucket | The leak tests; vault `log:` | Ciphertext, commitments and last four only; breach steps in 15.2 |
| 29 | Compromised Worker tries to collect TINs in transit, or re-asks every payee | The Worker never receives a TIN; the page that encrypts is served from the payments origin, to a pinned key, and its bundle hash is watched from outside; a tripwire stops mass invalidation and re-ask notices | `tax_reask_tripwire`; `deploy_drift` | Halt the notices; redeploy the reviewed version |
| 30 | A wrong or hostile tax public key reaches configuration | Fingerprints pinned in source for the Worker and the site; a mismatch refuses submissions and accrual | `tax_key_mismatch`; the fingerprint in the daily digest | Restore the pinned key |
| 31 | Loss or corruption of the tax private key | Two offline copies; a quarterly drill and a drill after every rotation and before January 2 | `tax_key_drill_overdue`; a failed drill | Restore from the good copy while one exists; otherwise affected payees certify again |
| 32 | Mercury outage or relay unreachable | Every bank fault is "ours" or unknown, never a recipient fault; a definite refusal before Mercury has no resend age; accrual needs no bank | Health probe; `relay_unreachable`; `worker_silent` from the relay | Payables wait; runs resume at the next tick with the gate re-check |
| 33 | Mercury token downgraded or deleted | No token is created long before its first write; sweeps and weekly ACH keep the send token in use | 401 or 403 on a write; days since last write in health | The affected rail pauses; a send-token refusal halts; McKale reissues by the runbook |
| 34 | Mercury refuses the use case, or closes the account | Gate G1 holds enrollment, accrual and payouts off until the written yes | Mercury's answer | `bank_adapter.mjs` is the seam; decision D22 |
| 35 | Stripe payout fails, or the bank account goes `errored` | A sweep needs a matched deposit; the failure row carries the contents to a later payout; a swept payout that fails is marked `failing`, waits for its installment in flight, and then posts the reverse of what was swept in the write that moves its contents | `payout.failed` schedules a read; R8; the transit identity | McKale resumes payouts in the Dashboard; the contents are swept once |
| 36 | Negative Stripe payout cannot debit Engine Expenses, or a large swept gift is disputed | The computed floor with dispute cover; a Stripe minimum balance sized from it; a large deposit is deferred on its payout until covered or until it is 120 days old, while everything else in that payout and behind it is swept; a deferred deposit that is reversed nets inside transit and never draws on `cents` | Signed payout amount; R15, R16; `large_deposit_waiting_cover` | The plan of 4.8; refunds wait meanwhile |
| 37 | Stripe API changes shape | Money reads use GA endpoints with a pinned version; every response is shape-checked | Shape check failure | No sweep; observation continues |
| 38 | Books drift from cash, a bank read races a posting, or pending payments make the balance and the book disagree | The book identity is pure arithmetic; a wall is compared only at a quiescent read, with the pending classes Mercury deducts early in the equation; one provider row is one cash posting, at Stripe as at the bank | R2, R5, R6, R13 | A determinate, unexplained difference halts; one that tracked pending rows explain corrects the setting; an indeterminate read waits for the next poll; Suspense rows are classified |
| 39 | A Cashbook row never reaches the Treasury | Idempotent keys; the replay sweep | R21 (a row unapplied for an hour) | Resubmitted; nothing is lost |
| 40 | McKale's own Mercury automation moves money the sweep needs | n/a; his rules are his | R15 | Alert with the safe-to-move figure; the installment waits or fails cleanly and is packed again |
| 41 | A sweep leg fails, a transfer limit blocks it, or a gift exceeds the cap | Installments under the cap; a `failed` state; the next installment only after a read proves no transfer exists | Aging of 1130 and of `submitted` | The next installment; `sweeps/manual` |
| 42 | Investment money, or any outside credit, dripped to contributors | Principal has its own bucket outside the drip base; bank credits post to Suspense; carry can only be an internal transfer; a match needs code, amount and sender name; classified carry waits for the month lock | R13; the ring-fence tests | `INVESTMENTS_ENABLED` off; a wrong-sender credit is returned to its sender |
| 43 | An outside party pulls a debit from Bliss Treasury | A debit block or filter at Mercury; instructions shown only after acceptance; tax deposits paid from Checking | `external_debit`, with the dispute deadline | Dispute at Mercury inside the deadline (15.2) |
| 44 | Misreported year-end forms | Year totals and withholding written with the bank debit, by posting date, under the certificate on file when the payment is made; thresholds tested on the per-payee aggregate; returns take back only the cash returned; netted clawbacks recorded | R12; the pre-filing report | Filing is a deliberate act by McKale; corrected returns |
| 45 | Staging pays real money | `MERCURY_ENV` must match the token, the base URL and `DEPLOY_ENV`; the environment label is inside the relay signature; the staging relay is a separate app with a sandbox token | Health line | Fails closed; a mismatch halts |
| 46 | Plan record wrong (late, forged or out-of-order event) | `plan_apply` reads the subscription at apply time; the event is only a trigger | n/a | One accrual day at most |
| 47 | Earning inputs inflated | Stable chunk ids; server caps; decision D12 before accrual | n/a | Daily score cap 3,200 |
| 48 | A KV write grants administrator rights, or a concurrent rewrite of the KV user record drops a ban | Finance administrators are a configured list checked against the account key; ban state and account type live in `AccountAuth`, copied there from KV once by the audited backfill, and the gate refuses an account that has not been migrated. KV grants nothing over money | `admin_set_changed`; the test that a KV flag gets 403 | Nothing to recover |
| 49 | Journal tampered with or broken | Hash chain; checkpoints; the relay recomputes the chain from its own stored head and refuses a history that does not extend it | R14; `anchor_fork`; the monthly offline replay | Halt; restore from backup (15.3) |
| 50 | Treasury object lost or corrupted, including just after a run | Every key the Treasury writes is backed up through its change log, after every run step and sweep landing and before every anchor, so rows, lots, holds, year totals, deposits and reversals come back with the buckets; no anchor runs ahead of the backup, so a restored journal extends the relay's witness; each payment intent stored outside the object before its send; payments of the gap rebuilt from bank outflows | Verify; walls; R13 in full after a restore; the outflow-without-record check | Restore drill 15.3 with payouts halted; no run starts until every outflow has its record |

The main site never reads an identity file or holds a private key: it signs in by hand-off and receives a bearer session marked `handoff`, which the payments-origin and finance routes refuse. That hand-off limits what the honest bundle holds. It does not limit what injected code can ask for: an injected script brings its own file input, and one file pick hands over the raw key. ### 15.2 Incident response

For a suspected compromise or a wrong payment, in order:

1. **Stop.** Halt (any finance administrator); a halt also freezes every delayed act that releases money, and a halt of scope `all` freezes the top-up and every delayed act except the unhalt. If the Worker is suspect: `RELAY_ARMED=false`, which refuses payments, wire requests, sweeps and invites alike. If the relay or a deploy credential is suspect: delete both Mercury write tokens. Then, in Mercury itself, cancel every pending transaction the outflow checks named, on any of the three accounts, before its cutoff, and reject every pending send-money request, because no switch reaches what is already in flight.
2. **An outside debit on Bliss Treasury.** Dispute it with Mercury the same banking day. The return window for an unauthorized debit on a business account is about two banking days, and the `external_debit` alert states the deadline.
3. **Preserve.** Export the relay audit file, its head list and the day's digest; note the journal head; leave the Durable Objects untouched.
4. **Rotate.** `RELAY_SIGNING_KEY` (add the fresh key id, remove the old), `MERCURY_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, the Mercury tokens, the Stripe restricted key, `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY` if sessions are suspect, every deploy token.
5. **Recover money.** For an unauthorized or erroneous ACH, contact Mercury at once; a recall is time-bound (a Nacha reversal exists only for a duplicate, a wrong receiver or a wrong amount, within five banking days). Wires are final; ask Mercury for a recall attempt.
6. **Account.** Reconcile every outflow from BT since the last determinate wall; post corrections in the open period; record `return_loss_cents` or an adjusting entry for any loss.
7. **Notify.** Affected payees by notice. If TIN ciphertext, identity data or tax profiles left the system, counsel decides the breach notifications (section 19).
8. **Resume** only through the delayed unhalt with its announcement, after Security has reviewed the cause. The announcement lists the delayed acts that will resume; cancel any that were not McKale's.

Three further runbooks:

- **The relay's volume is lost.** Disarm. Restore the newest snapshot to a fresh volume. For recipients the snapshot lacks, let the relay prove age from Mercury's history, then load the restore file built from the recipient announcements in the out-of-band channel. Check the head list against the last daily digest. Re-arm. No payee enrolls again and no destination changes state; the Treasury sees `refused_wait` at worst.
- **A deploy must be rolled back.** Never to a version older than the rollback floor. The floor is the step 1 release, which carries the forward guard, until McKale sets a switch that older code cannot honour; from then it is the release that introduced that switch: the step 3 release once `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` is set (older code writes no creation records, so a subscription it sold would never be credited again), the step 4 release once `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET=3` has run, and the step 6 release once `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER` is set. Each floor is recorded here with its version id when it takes effect. A rollback across the floor needs the book halted with scope `all` first and the orchestrator's review.
- **The payments origin shows an unexpected bundle hash, or serves a policy header that is absent, report-only or changed.** Halt enrollment (`ENROLLMENT_MODE=off`), redeploy the reviewed bundle, treat every key used on the page since the last good report as exposed, and run the end-to-end drill of 6.6 before reopening.

A wrong tax filing follows the corrected-return path of 6.8. A failed Stripe payout is McKale's act in the Dashboard.

### 15.3 Backup and restore drill

A payment is an intent the Treasury created, not a fact a provider can replay. A backup taken at 6:00 AM cannot describe a run that started at 7:00 AM. Four things close that gap.

- **Backup after every step that creates an intent, and of every key.** `backup_step` runs after every run step and every sweep landing, before every anchor, and once more at the end of each day. It ships no chosen list of records. Every Treasury put appends the keys it wrote or deleted to the change log (`chg:{put seq}`, in that same put), and `backup_step` ships the current value of every key the log names since the last backup, and a tombstone for each deleted key, in bounded steps, to `FINANCE_BACKUPS` under `book-backups/{date}/`, encrypted under `BACKUP_ENC_KEY`. That is the journal segment and exactly what the puts behind it wrote: accrual rows, lots, payee rows, the run index, holds, year totals and their aggregates, clawbacks, deposits with their payment-intent index, reversals, payout and installment records, checkout creation records, payments, expected outflows, the administrator queue and act log, and the payment id key. The first backup is a full pass over every key, and a full pass is taken again at each month lock, so a restore never applies more than a month of deltas. A backup is complete at the step that empties the log in the same turn in which it reads the journal head. Its manifest names that head, its hash and the put sequence, so the objects of a prefix, applied in order, reproduce the Treasury at exactly that head. The bucket is private and holds nothing else; no route that serves published packages can reach it.
- **No anchor runs ahead of the backup.** The relay is given digests only up to the head a completed backup names (7.7). A restore therefore always loads a journal that contains the relay's head; the entries posted after it are replayed and extend it, and the witness accepts the next anchor. Nothing ever resets the relay's head, and no route exists that could.
- **Each payment intent is stored outside the object before its send** (`pay-intents/{pay_id}`), and the relay keeps its own receipt in its audit file.
- **The bank is the last witness.** Every payment's note is its payment id.

The `TaxVault` and `AccountAuth` objects are their own records of record; the Cashbook can be rebuilt from Stripe's and Mercury's history.

**Restore.** With payouts halted and `RESTORE_MODE` set by a deploy:

1. `restore_from_backup` loads the last full pass and every delta after it, in order: every key the Treasury held at the backed-up head, sub-ledger and journal alike.
2. The offline replay verifies the chain from `open:genesis` and the head against the relay's own head list.
3. **`rebuild_payments`** covers only the gap after the backed-up head: it rebuilds every `pay:` and `payidx:` record created since, from the intent objects, the relay audit file and Mercury's Bliss Treasury outflows (the note carries the payment id; the row carries amount and recipient). The same pass rebuilds sweep installments from their notes.
4. The Cashbook re-reads Stripe balance transactions and Mercury transactions since the backup's `as_of` and resubmits them; idempotent keys make the replay exact, and a `paydebit` finds the payment record step 3 rebuilt. Every anchor backs up first, so the gap holds at most an hour of work: provider rows, which this step resubmits; payments, which step 3 rebuilt; a settlement, which runs again under its own idempotent keys; and administrator acts queued in that hour, which were announced out of band and are signed again. A sale of that hour is booked again by the webhook's own read path (3.2), run over the sessions and invoices Stripe lists as paid since the backed-up head, since Stripe redelivers no event it was answered for. A session whose `cs:` record was itself written inside the gap has no record to match: its charge row goes to Suspense and is classified as a sale the Worker created (4.3), on the goods credit that the buyer's Wallet, Delta account or plan record holds under that object's ref.
5. The book identity, the walls, the conservation identity and R13 in full must hold to the cent: payee rows sum to `owed_cents`; each payee's accrual rows and lots equal its owed part and its holds its held part; each payee's year totals equal the sum of its payment lines; the transit identity; `pending_reversal_cents` recomputed from the `rev:` records; every `dep:` and `rev:` with its `jref:`. The first three checks read buckets and pass on an empty sub-ledger, so they are never the whole test.
6. `RESTORE_MODE` is cleared and the halt is lifted through the delay.

`start_run` is refused, in a restore and at any other time, while any Bliss Treasury outflow since the last backup's `as_of` has no `pay:` record. A restored book can therefore never build a second payment for a period the bank has already seen, whether or not Mercury still honours the old keys.

**Drill.** Run on staging before `PAYOUTS_MODE=live` (gate G8) and once a year. Three cases: a copy of the Treasury is wiped and restored at rest; the Treasury is lost one hour after a run, with payments submitted and not yet posted; and the Treasury is lost after a deposit was booked and an anchor taken since the last run. In each, the checks of step 5 pass, no payee is paid twice, and the relay accepts the next anchor. In each, one named payee's accrual rows, lots, holds and year totals equal their values before the loss, as do a `legal_hold`, a deposit's `dep:` and `pi:` records and a subscription's `csub:`. The drill has two mutations and must fail under each: back up the book state and the journal only; anchor to the current head. The drill asserts that the book manifest and the BLS ledger's own manifest are both complete. The orchestrator session prepares it; McKale deploys.

### 15.4 Operator and deploy security

- Hardware second factor on Cloudflare, Fly.io, Mercury, Stripe, GitHub and the chat provider behind `ALERT_WEBHOOK`.
- **The out-of-band channel holds the roster.** The relay announces every recipient there by legal name, country and fingerprint tail, and posts each manifest with its amounts. It is the one place outside the Worker where who is paid can be read in the clear, by design, because it is also the only second pair of eyes. Its account is therefore a credential: McKale alone, a hardware second factor, no bank digit ever posted, and a history limit of 90 days. McKale saves each month's announcements to the offline machine at the month close, and the restore file of 7.5 and every later confirmation read them there. The provider is named in the published processor list (16.2, step 12).
- **The payments origin.** Every page that creates or reads an identity file (sign-up and sign-in by identity file, email change, passkey add and remove, key replacement), the tax step, the destination steps, year-end documents and the administrator finance page are served from a dedicated origin (`PAYMENTS_ORIGIN`, for example `pay.eustress.dev`) and from nowhere else. Its bundle is minimal: no forum text, profile text, listing text or user image. Text that someone else wrote and that a page there must show (a Mercury counterparty name, a vendor name, a legal name read from a document, a payee's case text) is rendered only as text, a text node and never parsed markup, under the enforced Trusted Types policy. A test plants markup in a counterparty name, a KYC name and a case text and requires that none is parsed; its mutation renders one of them as markup. Its content security policy is enforced, not report-only: `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`; no inline script and no inline style; `connect-src` the API alone; `img-src 'self'`; `frame-ancestors 'none'`; `require-trusted-types-for 'script'`. It keeps its own token storage, so a token lifted on the main site is not the one it uses, and it loads the identity key for one signing act and drops it; the key is never held in page memory between acts.
- **The main site never reads an identity file and never holds a private key.** A person signs in by identity file on the payments origin, and the main site receives its session by a hand-off that the main site itself begins. It makes a random verifier, keeps it in its own storage for that tab, and sends the browser to the payments origin with the verifier's SHA-256 hash. The payments page passes that hash to the Worker when it asks for the one-time code (single use, 60 seconds, bound to the main site's origin and to the hash), the browser carries the code back, and the main site exchanges it together with the verifier for a bearer session of its own, marked `amr: handoff`. The exchange refuses a code whose stored hash is not the hash of the verifier presented, and the main site exchanges nothing when it holds no verifier of a sign-in it began. A code that someone minted for their own account and planted in another person's browser therefore signs nobody in: that browser holds no verifier for it, so a victim is never left buying a plan or a pack inside the attacker's account. The code carries no key and no signature that could be replayed, and a `handoff` session is refused by every payments-origin route and every finance route. Passkey sign-in stays on the main site, because a passkey's private key is never readable by a page. Studio, the Player and the CLI read the identity file natively, sign in through the same routes with no `Origin` header, and are outside this rule. Moving those pages changes where honest code runs, not what injected code can ask for. A stored script on a forum or listing page brings its own file input, draws "Load your identity file to continue" on the origin people trust most, and one file pick hands it the raw key, with which an attacker needs no browser, no `Origin` and no stolen token. So the main site enforces the two directives that stop it: `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'`, and a `connect-src` limited to Eustress's own hosts. The page ships no inline script, so nothing honest breaks. Its style and image rules, which allow inline styles and any HTTPS image, may stay report-only.
- **Three checks hold that line.** A policy test fails when the policy the payments origin is served with is absent, report-only or wider than the list above; it reads the `_headers` file of the Pages project, or the response of the Worker route when the fallback of section 20 serves the origin, so it covers whichever path is in use. The same test fails when the main site's `script-src` or `connect-src` is absent, report-only or wider than stated. A bundle test fails when the main bundle links `parse_identity_file`, `sign_challenge_ed25519` or the identity-file picker; its mutation imports the picker into a main-site page.
