And the deploy tripwire reads the live origin every hour (below). The Worker also refuses the routes marked "payments origin" from any other `Origin` and accepts a request with no `Origin` header, which is what a native client sends (13); the check binds browsers only and is never the defence by itself. Gate G6 requires the policy test for both origins, the bundle test and the live read of both before `ENROLLMENT_MODE` rises above `off`.
- **Deploy credentials are bank credentials.** There is no standing `fly` or `wrangler` login on the daily workstation, where agent sessions and everyday software run. McKale deploys from a separate machine or operating-system user, with short-lived tokens: a Fly deploy token with a one hour expiry, a per-deploy scoped Cloudflare token. The relay lives in a Fly organization that holds nothing else. Only McKale runs `wrangler deploy` and `fly deploy`.
- **A deploy tripwire runs outside both systems.** A scheduled job (`tools/deploy-tripwire`, run on a third host) compares the relay's image digest as Fly's own API reports it, the Worker's version id, the site's version and the hash of the payments origin's bundle with the values McKale recorded at his last deploy, and posts any difference to `ALERT_WEBHOOK` (`deploy_drift`). Every hour it also fetches the live payments origin and the live main site as any visitor would and compares the `Content-Security-Policy` header each is served with the reviewed value; a header that is absent, report-only or different is `origin_policy_missing`. An unexpected release of any of them, or a policy that stopped being enforced, is seen within the hour by something none can silence. Its tokens are scoped to exactly what it reads: Workers Scripts read and Pages read at Cloudflare, and a read-only Fly token for the relay's organization, which holds nothing else and exposes no secret value. It never asks the relay about itself: the health route has no public port, answers only a request the Worker signed, and would be the watched thing vouching for its own digest. A hostile release is therefore seen. A command run inside the Machine changes no digest and is not, and the outflow checks are what find it (7.11). It holds no R2, KV, Durable Object or DNS permission, so the host that watches the deploy can read no payee data.
- **The build is reproducible enough to compare, and the comparison is asked for no more than it can give.** Before a production deploy the orchestrator's build of the reviewed commit and a build made on a second machine must produce the same script hash, and the tripwire compares the deployed script's hash with that figure. That finds a tampered build machine or build cache: code that enters on one machine changes the hash there and not on the other. It does not find a hostile version that is pinned in the lockfile itself, which builds to the same hash on every machine. Against that the controls sit before the build: every lockfile diff is reviewed by Security line by line with the commit; installs run from the lockfile with integrity checks and with lifecycle scripts off; and the Worker's dependencies are kept few, each named with its reason in the review.
- **Rights over the path between the Worker and the relay are bank credentials.** Cloudflare roles and tokens that can edit Tunnels, Zero Trust, Access policies or the Workers VPC service are held like deploy credentials: McKale only, hardware second factor, no standing token. The tunnel runs from a configuration file in the relay image with a locally managed credential, never from dashboard-managed routing.
- The relay image digest is read from Fly's API on the deploy machine and verified against the reviewed commit before the allowlist is set.
- The tax key pair is generated on the offline machine. The private key is kept offline in two places, never on the daily workstation; the offline tools run on a machine that is offline while the key is loaded, and none of them holds a bank credential: the month-close roster comparison reads Mercury's outflows from a dashboard export McKale carries to it (7.7); IRIS files are uploaded from a browser profile used for nothing else and then destroyed.
- The second Mercury user creates the request token and holds no other duty. Mercury's approval rules require McKale's approval for any payment that user starts from the dashboard; whether that user's role can exclude recipient edits is asked of Mercury (Q7). R3 halts on any outflow from Bliss Treasury the book does not expect, and R5 alerts on one from Engine Expenses or Checking.
- Mercury's own payment notifications to McKale are on.
- One person is administrator, wire approver, deployer, key holder and cap setter. Decision D19 names an independent reviewer for the month close, a cap raise and the year-end filing, and a second holder of the cancel. Until then administrator acts wait for the next business morning, and the relay's out-of-band channel and witness are the only second pair of eyes.

---

## 16. Build plan: build order

This section is the build plan (see the note at the head of section 11).

### 16.1 Rules for every step

**Fakes.**

- Fakes only. No test touches Stripe or Mercury. One `globalThis.fetch` replacement routes by host to the Stripe fake and the Mercury fake. In Worker tests `env.BANK_RELAY.fetch` is bound to the real relay handler, imported from the copy of `infrastructure/relay` that `loadWorker` makes beside the Worker copy, in front of the one Mercury fake. The contract is never two fakes agreeing with each other, and a relay mutation named for a Worker contract test is applied to the code that test runs. `infrastructure/relay` does not exist before step 4: until then `loadWorker` copies a fixture relay handler from `tests/helpers/fixture_relay` in its place, which is what step 1's runner tests mutate, and from step 4 the copy is the real tree.
- The Mercury fake models: asynchronous settlement; 409 on a repeated key for direct sends; a request-send-money endpoint that ignores the key and creates a second request; the 24 hour duplicate refusal as a bare 400; failure before the debit; a return after it in all three shapes (a linked credit, a status change alone, both at once); a posted transaction later marked failed; a linked wire fee (equal, lower, higher, absent, late, on another account); approval requests that stay pending; a lost answer; a late completion; a transaction list that lags a just-created transaction; a balance that moves between two reads; pending debits that leave `currentBalance` early, and ones that do not; a direct send that Mercury turns into an approval item; a return that arrives as an unlinked incoming credit for less than was sent; a recipient whose `countrySpecific` block or correspondent bank changes after enrollment; a transaction whose amount changes after posting; 401 on a downgraded token; an approved send-money request left from an earlier period for the same recipient and amount; an unexpected debit on Engine Expenses and on Checking; a payment sent from Checking to a recipient named after a vendor that has an expense rule; a transaction of a `kind` the client does not know; a card authorization pending when the genesis read is taken; an internal transfer into Bliss Treasury of an amount no installment has; a second debit to one recipient that cannot be cancelled; an outside credit of a few cents on Bliss Treasury; and full bank numbers in responses, so the allowlists are exercised.
- The Stripe fake models: a payout failure with its `failure_balance_transaction` riding a later payout; a dispute that is only an inquiry; a refund followed by a chargeback; a refund that stays pending and then fails; equal payouts on consecutive days, the older of which fails; a charge row that arrives before its webhook; a session created outside the Worker with a genuine signed event; a payout whose destination is not Engine Expenses; a refund booked between a payout's creation and its sweep; a payout that fails before it composes; a subscription and a session created before the creation records; an `invoice.paid` delivered before its session's completion event; a session presented in a currency other than USD; a refund announced by `charge.refunded` alone; a refund that stays pending and executes at another USD amount; a proration invoice; an invoice paid outside the Worker; a refund of a taxed sale.
- Shared fakes in `tests/helpers`: a KV fake that counts operations and honours metadata and expiry; Durable Object storage with the 128-key put limit, `storage.transaction` and an alarm driver. The KV fake's strict mode (one write per second per key) needs a clock, so it is opt-in with an injected clock for money tests and report-only elsewhere.

**The mutation runner.**

- **A money test is defined by a registry, not by judgment.** `tests/money_modules.json` lists the money modules: the Treasury, the ledger, payables, the Cashbook, books, economics, delta, commerce, sponsor, subscriptions, the holder ledger, purchases, bliss, the Stripe webhook and checkout, the payee gate, the tax modules, sanctions, the relay client, bank matching, finance administration, the money operations of `AccountAuth`, and everything under `infrastructure/relay` and `tools/yearend`. Any test that loads one of them is a money test. The runner derives the list and nobody maintains it by hand: it scans every test file for `workerModule(name)` calls and for static and dynamic import specifiers, and resolves each against the registry.
- Every money test takes each module under test through `workerModule(name)` from the temporary copy that `loadWorker` makes of the tree the test runs in, which on a step's branch is that branch. No money test imports from `../src/` directly, and the runner enforces it: a test file that reaches a registry module through `../src/`, by a static or a dynamic import, fails the suite.
- **The existing money tests are counted, not assumed.** On 2026-11-12 they are about 190 tests in 12 files (treasury 34, commerce 37, delta 16, bliss 16, purchases 15, subscriptions 13, holder ledger 11, books 10, economics 10, sponsor 10, auth and webhook 9, value freeze 8), none with a mutation and several without `loadWorker`. Step 1 migrates all of them to `workerModule` and writes mutations for the files that guard deposits, reversals and splits (treasury, books, economics, sponsor, auth and webhook). The rest, 116 tests, are named in `tests/money_grandfathered.json`, each entry as `{file, test, expires_step}`. The list expires by machine, not by intention. The runner reads the build step recorded in `tests/build_step.json`, which the orchestrator writes in each step's landing merge, and fails any entry whose `expires_step` has landed. It fails any entry that is absent from `tests/money_grandfathered.step1.json`, the copy pinned at step 1 and never edited, so the list can only shrink. And each step names the files it retires: step 3 delta, bliss and subscriptions; step 6 value freeze; step 8 commerce and purchases; step 9 the holder ledger, whose module no earlier step rewrites. Step 9's gate requires the list to be empty. The legacy tests that guard deposits and reversals are never left unproven behind a quiet exemption.
- Every money test names its mutation in `tests/money_mutations.json` as `{test, file, find, replace}`, or has its line in the grandfather list until that line expires. `loadWorker` applies the mutation named in an environment variable after it has copied the tree. The runner never writes under `src`.
- The runner normalises line endings before matching (the working tree is CRLF, the archive LF), requires `find` to match exactly once, requires the mutated module to import, and requires the named test to fail on an assertion. A mutation that breaks the import is a broken mutation, not a passing one. A money test with neither an entry nor a grandfather line fails the suite.
- `infrastructure/relay` and `tools/yearend` have their own entries in the same runner and their own `node --test` targets.
- From step 2 on, the conservation identity, the book identity and the transit identity are asserted after every operation in every money test. Step 1 has no journal to assert on: it keeps the assertions the existing tests already make, and step 2 adds the identities to every treasury test that step 1 migrated, when `ledger.mjs` lands. Month and year boundaries run under `npm run test:clock-shift`. No test asserts on wall-clock time. Each scheduler step is proven under 1,000 KV operations and 128 keys a put.

**Shipping.**

- Every step is deployable to staging alone and leaves production correct with every switch at its default. A default never closes a stream that sells today (locked decision 1): `sales_regions.json` ships with every selling place open and `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE` ships true. Security reviews before any production deploy. McKale deploys. Rust changes in `eustress/crates/web` are compiled by the orchestrator's build.
- **A clean tree comes first.** On 2026-11-12 `index.js`, `treasury.mjs`, `account_auth.mjs`, `commerce.mjs`, `route_policy.mjs` and `wrangler.toml` are modified and uncommitted, and `books.mjs`, `sponsor.mjs`, `subscriptions.mjs` and `holder_ledger.mjs` are untracked while `index.js` imports them. A mechanical move cannot be "one commit, no behaviour change" on that tree without sweeping in other sessions' hunks. The written precondition of step 1: the owning sessions commit or hand over those files, and `git status` is clean for every file the finance build touches.
- **Files have owners, and a step lands by a merge.** McKale deploys whatever the tree holds, and any session's hotfix can be the reason. So a step is never built in the shared tree: it is built on its own worktree branch, tested there, and landed by the orchestrator with one git merge when the step is complete. No sibling file exists. A whole-file swap would discard whatever another session changed in the live file after the fork; a three-way merge keeps those hunks and stops on a conflict instead of overwriting. Before the landing the branch takes the main line in, and the full suite runs on the merge result, so a fix Security landed in `account_auth.mjs` on day 10 is in the tree, and under test, when the finance step lands on day 30. A module that two steps change is split between them by named function in their Owns lines, and a later step branches after the earlier step's landing. A module `index.js` does not yet import is not imported until its step lands. A deploy of the tree at any moment ships whole steps only.
- **No shared table to edit.** Each module exports the route rows of its own routes and `route_policy.mjs` concatenates them (13). Step 1 makes that change, with the test that walks the concatenated table. No step and no other session adds a row to a list someone else is editing, and a landing cannot drop a row another session added.
- **`index.js` has one writer after step 1.** Step 1 moves out every function a later step rewrites, so no later step edits a body in `index.js`. Every later edit to it is a named one-line insertion made by the orchestrator.
- **An irreversible migration waits for a deploy variable.** The book's schema migration runs only when McKale has set `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET=3`; the default is unset, so a deploy of the tree never migrates. The same rule holds for any later migration of stored money state.
- **`wrangler.toml` and `tests/deploy_bindings.test.mjs` have one writer, the orchestrator.** A binding, its migration tag and its class export land in one commit at step completion, after McKale has created the outside resource the binding names (a bucket, a tunnel, a VPC service). No session adds a binding early to make its tests pass: tests take bindings from the fakes. A migration that names a class the bundle does not export, or a bucket that does not exist, fails every deploy for every session, including a Security fix.
- **The site deploys separately**, as two bundles: the main site and the payments origin. Each step lists its `.rs` files. When a response shape changes, the Worker serves the earlier and the current fields for one release, and a route is retired only after the site release that stops calling it.

**Mutations that must exist, at minimum:** drop one line of a posting; post the pool share to 2000 instead of 2001; omit `pending_reversal_cents` from the drip base; drop the clamp of the drip base at zero; subtract `pending_reversal_cents` in the reversal capacity; release a pending reversal at row apply; let a negative sweep part take `cents` below zero; skip the day-pool reserve; post a matched bank row a second time under its business key; post a transaction that has no `postedAt`; compare a wall on unequal balance reads; credit 1010 for a reversal from the webhook amount; book a dispute inquiry; call the relay before writing the intent; change the payment key between attempts; treat 409 as a failure; classify the 24 hour duplicate refusal as a recipient fault; resend without a read; resend past the age limit; skip the gate on a resend; skip the gate re-check of a wire awaiting approval; release a payment to a hold after an unknown attempt; resolve a wire request from the transaction list only; key a return by its transaction id; make a released hold payable at once; stamp accrual rows one by one in a payment; key a value reversal by the reversal only; restore the wire fee on a wire Mercury charged; refund a wire fee on a timer; book a fee over-collection to income; credit withholding at reserve; reverse the whole gross on a return; test the floor on one line's total; withhold on the payee's own certification for services; omit `chargeType`; remove the relay's lock; reject a seen payment key; drop the environment label from the signed string; classify a destination or tax route as FRESH; accept a destination confirm without the signature; mark only a young or unproven destination at a key replacement; read the freeze from KV; read the ban from KV; accept a KV administrator flag; let an accrual through without a tax form; commit to a TIN with an unsalted hash; accept a tax key whose fingerprint is not pinned; drop the response allowlist; use a float for the dollar conversion; include `invest_principal_cents` in the drip base; classify an outside credit as carry; match an investment without the sender name; count a returned payment in the year totals; key year totals by score date; take plan state from the event body; ignore an unmatched BT outflow; lift a halt without the delay; let a stream set the alarm itself; perform a provider read inline in `financeTick`; remove a phase alias; skip the `jref:` sweep on open; hash raw bytes; write the attempt after the relay call; park a payment that has an attempt before the confirming read; resolve an ACH from the transaction list only; treat a relay timing refusal as a recipient fault; count a period by posting date; pause the run for a deferred first payment; subtract signed Suspense in coverage; decide `funded` only at the reversal; release a reversal twice; post 1010 from both the webhook and the payout row; hash SWIFT and IBAN only; settle on the status word with an unlinked credit in Suspense; build the wire month in a single pass; require an unapplied row for the manual sweep; credit 1110 twice on a tax deposit; apply a delayed act without the re-check; fire an act of a removed key; block the top-up under a halt; lift a halt whose cause still stands; accept an unsigned relay answer; credit goods from session metadata; use the payee's treaty rate; test the day's load only; render an estimate beside a plan link; gate only the payable on a sanctions block; forfeit a banned payee's share; publish the daily split; migrate without the deploy variable; run every due stream in one alarm; ignore pending rows in the outflow check; keep the old key in the legacy replay; skip the `money_migrated` check; verify sessions with the old key only; default a selling region closed; freeze the unhalt's own clock under a halt; freeze the anomaly acknowledgement under the halt it set; take the reversing part of a failed payout before its installment lands; pack only `bank_confirmed` payouts; take a deferred deposit's reversal from `cents`; leave a Stripe payout that landed on Bliss Treasury in Suspense; count carry bound to a Stripe payout as unlocked carry; return the unaccrued remainder to the drip base nightly with exact public pool figures; import the identity-file picker into a main-site page; mint a `key` session from a hand-off; serve a tax form's fields or a destination's last four to a session alone; bind a signed read to its route only; return the hold list with payees unsigned; let an earlier unproven claim outlast the review; count the relay's weekly cap by posting date; select a treaty row by the payee's stated income type; ignore `sell_tickets` in checkout creation; key the annual blocked-property alert on `blocked_cents`; let one caller pass `env.JWT_SECRET` to a session verifier; restore one line of payout copy; return an unknown payment to the lots at the window close; measure the resend age from the last attempt; match a request on recipient and amount alone; write the attempt from the stale copy after the awaited gate; check the relay's clock at receipt only; return a wire fee credit as ordinary payable; build a December wire after the 19th; require a creation record for every invoice; require an account for a gift; require the catalog amount on an invoice; hold a payout uncomposed for a known-type Suspense row; post a payout's cash at compose; replay legacy deposits into transit at the cutover; post 1010 for a balance transaction created before the genesis instant; debit 4900 for the tax on a refund; book a no-rule debit on Engine Expenses to 6999; hold every payment in its watch on a credit that names none; read the relay's digest from its own health route; ship the main site's `script-src` report-only; open the billing portal on a bearer token; post a bank digit to the out-of-band channel; key year totals by the accrual certificate; require an envelope on every W-8BEN; apply the W-8 wire conditions by rail; fail the accrual gate for a missing rules file; run a lot reversal's loop inline in the webhook; let the later of two route rows for one path win; back up the book state and the journal only; anchor to the current head; tag every returned cent `withholding_settled`; reduce a payable by `adj` for a duplicate payment; classify a Bliss Treasury credit as carry by itself; repack a planned installment; open the book while a bank row is pending; update the reversal records in the landing write; key a negative part by the payout alone; write one outbox key per returned purchase in a reversal's put; fold 50 rows a write; credit 2040 for withholding on an investor return; leave a claim side at the webhook amount; let an expense rule explain a sent payment; accept any debit named IRS; compare each month's roster alone; queue an export as a delayed act; exchange a hand-off code without the verifier; prefill the tax interview from `kyc_facts`; test the month of the build for US presence; require `csub:` to exist before an invoice; compare a session's presentment subtotal with the catalog price; require an `Origin` header on a payments-origin route; ignore `charge.refunded`; take gross from the lots without netting open receivables; count a return in its own posting year; apply the reversing entry of 2.2 to a status change; plan an investor's principal and return as one record; leave planned carry returns in usable carry; transfer the carry deficit with a negative part; require Suspense and 1130 to be zero as of the month's last day; address the notice to the current billing email; re-check a live manifest hourly only; render a counterparty name as markup on the payments origin; stamp `services` rows only; report the whole gross on Form 1042-S; stamp unpaid cents only on a US-work report; plan a refund or a transfer without a counterparty screen; match the funding without the screen; exempt the whole slice under any Form 8233; take the genesis read before the refusal; refuse `checkout_record` during the migration; require the new key to sign; read the ban from the KV record the archive rewrites.

### 16.2 The steps

**Step 0. Long-lead items (McKale and the professionals; starts at once, runs in parallel).** Section 17.2 items 1 to 10, except the IRIS application of item 4, which has its own later date (gate G5). Gate: written answers on file.

**Step 1. Harness, the mechanical move, and the old rail stopped.**
- Precondition: a clean, committed tree for every finance file (16.1).
- Owns: `tests/helpers/*`, `tests/money_mutations.json`, `tests/money_modules.json`, `tests/money_grandfathered.json` with its pinned copy `tests/money_grandfathered.step1.json`, `tests/build_step.json`, the runner, `business_days.mjs`, `canonical.mjs`, and `route_policy.mjs` with `tests/route_policy.test.mjs`, turned into a concatenation of the route rows each module exports (13). Landed by the orchestrator alone, in one commit with no behaviour change: the move out of `index.js` of every function a later step rewrites, each to a named module, leaving one dispatch line each. `handleStripeWebhook` to `stripe_webhook.mjs`. `handleStripeCheckout`, `handleTicketCheckout`, `stripeRequest`, `STRIPE_PRICES` and `TICKET_PACKAGES` to `checkout.mjs`. `handlePayoutRate` to `payout_rate.mjs`. The Connect handlers and the payout handlers to `payout_routes.mjs`. The accounting handlers and `handleCronHealth` to `finance_routes.mjs`. The finance part of `scheduled()` to `finance_tick.mjs`. `handleKycSubmit` and `requireVerifiedAdult` to `kyc.mjs`. `handleAdminBan`, `handleAdminDeleteUser` and `handleAdminStats` to `admin_users.mjs`. `createJwt`, `verifyJwt` and the sign-in token helpers to `sessions.mjs`, behind `signSession(env, claims)` and `verifySession(env, token)`; and every session call site is pointed at those two, passing no secret: the register and login handlers, `verifyAuth`, `bannedBearer`, `authMethod` and the wiring of `freshGate` and `kycGate` in `index.js`, and the session lines of `fresh_auth.mjs`, `passkeys.mjs`, `identity_routes.mjs` and `actingAccount` in `kyc_identity.mjs`. Both functions still use `JWT_SECRET`, so nothing changes for anyone, and step 6 changes the key in one module. In commits of their own: the three guards of section 11, item 1, and the forward guard of 12.1. Site: the neutral payout copy of section 11, item 7 on every page it names (`pages/bliss.rs`, `docs_earning.rs`, `economics.rs` with its pinned test, `pages/premium.rs`, `pages/trust_registry.rs`, `pages/support.rs`, `pages/currency.rs`), and the copy test that walks every page.
- Builds: the shared fakes; `workerModule`; the money-module registry; migration of the existing money tests off direct `../src/` imports, with mutations for the files that guard deposits, reversals and splits and the grandfather list for the rest; the mutation runner, which copies `infrastructure/relay` beside the Worker copy when that tree exists and the fixture relay handler of `tests/helpers` until step 4 creates it; the pinned copy of the grandfather list and the recorded build step; removal of `src/index.js.bak`; `LEGACY_CONNECT_PAYOUTS` (default off) with `payout_setup` skipping to `backup`; the 410 on Connect onboarding; the early return in `syncKycDocsToStripe`; the forward guard (pre-journal code refuses every operation when `book:state` exists).
- Tests: the runner fails a money test that survives its mutation, fails one with neither an entry nor a grandfather line, fails a test file that imports a registry module from `../src/`, fails a grandfather entry whose `expires_step` has landed and one that the pinned step 1 copy lacks, fails a mutation that matches twice or breaks the import, matches a two-line `find` in a CRLF file, and applies a relay mutation to a Worker contract test on the fixture relay. No module but `sessions.mjs` passes a secret to a session signer or verifier (a source check; mutation: one caller passes `env.JWT_SECRET`). With the legacy switch off no Stripe transfer is attempted (mutation: default it on). Connect onboarding answers 410 and no file is uploaded to Stripe (mutation: remove the guard). Pre-journal code refuses a deposit when `book:state` exists (mutation: remove the guard). The pinned site copy says payouts are not open and names no figure, and the copy test fails on any page string that mentions payouts without that wording (mutation: restore one old line). A path that two modules claim fails the route-policy test (mutation: let the later of two route rows for one path win).
- Staging gate: the suites pass on the shared fakes; under the strict KV mode with its injected clock the one-write-per-second defects (the burn counter, the double cache write) reproduce before they are fixed.
- Production after: the only change a buyer can see is the neutral payout copy. The Connect rail and its second identity check are off, and no page of the site sells a plan on a payout. This release is the first rollback floor of 15.2; the floor rises with each later switch McKale sets.

**Step 2. The journal, schema 3 and the scheduler.**
- Before: McKale creates both `FINANCE_BACKUPS` buckets (17.2).
- Owns: `ledger.mjs`, `scheduler.mjs`, the book state and its migration in the Treasury (on the step's branch, 16.1), `backup_step`, the act log.
- Builds: `ledger.mjs` with the chart of 2.5; every Treasury operation that exists today (deposit, refund, dispute, the settlement) posts its entry and moves buckets in one put, in its present shape, which step 3 then replaces; the book under `book:` keys, migrated only under `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET=3`, with the pre-journal path served whole while `book:state` is absent; the halt on a legacy `state` key and the `jref:` sweep on open, for records made after genesis; holds under `phold:`; `transit_cents`, the split Suspense accounts with the two parts of Bliss Treasury's, the carry account; `pending_reversal_cents` as a sum over records; the book identity, the transit identity and bucket-to-account equality in `verify_step`; the cutover of 12.1 as one resumable migration that takes its five cash figures as inputs (step 4 supplies the read that feeds it in production): `open:genesis` with all five cash accounts, the refusal of deposit and reversal operations before the genesis read, with `checkout_record` and `checkout_get` served throughout, and the legacy figure read from the pre-journal `state.cents` inside that refusal, credited to 2001 and mirrored in `legacy_unfunded_cents`, a `jref:` and the `legacy` stamp on every earlier `dep:` and `rev:`, legacy reversal keys rewritten to refund ids with no posting, and 3100 in the place of 1010 for a balance transaction created before the instant; `legacy_fund` as the one audited operation that funds the legacy figure from carry; the scheduler with its `due:` table, one alarm and one stream step per invocation; halt scopes; an unknown settlement phase fails loudly; checkpoints; the change log every put writes and the encrypted backup of every key to `FINANCE_BACKUPS` under `book-backups/`, as a full pass and deltas; the head record.
- Tests: the conservation identity, the book identity and the transit identity after every operation, added here to every treasury test that step 1 migrated (mutation: post the pool share to 2000). An unbalanced entry is refused (mutation: drop a line). A locked month refuses a posting. A deposit made after genesis by pre-journal code against a schema 3 object halts on open and is replayed into transit (mutation: skip the `jref:` sweep). The cutover fixture of 12.1 (40 legacy deposits, a legacy pool, a Stripe balance and a payout in flight) leaves every wall and identity true, and no legacy deposit is replayed (mutation: replay legacy deposits into transit at the cutover; mutation: post 1010 for a balance transaction created before the genesis instant). A legacy refund is found under its Stripe refund id and is not booked again at the next read (mutation: keep the old key). A deploy with `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET` unset migrates nothing (mutation: migrate on open). A sale paid after the refusal begins and before the genesis read books after the cutover, into transit and not into the legacy figure, and a checkout session created during the migration is served (mutation: take the genesis read before the refusal; mutation: refuse `checkout_record` during the migration). A settlement stuck in backoff does not delay another stream (mutation: let a stream set the alarm itself). Every stream due at once stays under the operation limit in each invocation (mutation: run every due stream in one alarm). A restore from the backup reproduces every key the Treasury held, its `dep:`, `rev:` and `cs:` records among them, and R13 passes on the result (mutation: back up the book state and the journal only).
- Staging gate: `verify_step` green after replaying every treasury test; a restore from the backup on staging; both manifests (book and BLS ledger) intact.
- Production after: deployed with `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET` unset. The Treasury keeps its pre-journal state and serves every operation by its pre-journal path; the journal runs on staging. The book opens in production at the cutover of step 4, the first release that can read both providers at one instant.

**Step 3. Money-in truth (no bank code).** It posts through `ledger.mjs` from its first line; every account and bucket its tests assert on exists from step 2. In production its Treasury operations sit behind the same door as step 2's: the pre-journal path answers until the cutover, and the webhook hands the Treasury the same facts on both sides of it.
- Before the production deploy: McKale records decisions D26, D27 and D28, and adds the events of 3.4 to the live webhook endpoint (17.2, item 11). The handler in production answers 200 to a type it does not know, so the endpoint is edited first, and `charge.refunded`, which is subscribed and booked today, stays a trigger of the refund read: in neither order of the two acts is a refund dropped. At the deploy he sets `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` to its time.
- Owns: `stripe_client.mjs`, `stripe_webhook.mjs`, `checkout.mjs`, `payout_rate.mjs` (until its landing; step 7 branches from it afterward), the deposit and reversal operations of the Treasury, with two entry points that later steps call and never rewrite (the one cash posting per balance transaction behind `sbt:`, `sbtx:` and `sbtc:`, and the funding and release of reversals at a landing, written as one bounded step over the `revx:` index), `plan_apply` in `AccountAuth` with its seed from a subscription read, the checkout creation records `cs:` and `csub:` in the Treasury (served on both sides of the cutover), the Wallet's load reservation and value limit, `sales_regions.json`, and the parts of `delta.mjs`, `sponsor.mjs`, `subscriptions.mjs`, `commerce.mjs`, `bliss.mjs` and `books.mjs` it changes (checkout creation through the one Stripe client, integer 70 percent, the clamp, the burn counter), each on the step's branch; site: the checkout pages, `pages/bliss.rs`.
- Builds: the Stripe client and the restricted-key check; read before crediting; one creation record per session in the Treasury for every kind, with or without an account, the pinned price set and the recorded product of a line built inline, `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` for objects older than the records, and invoices matched through their subscription at the amount Stripe charged, a first invoice finding its session by a read when it arrives ahead of the session event, a pinned price checked by its id and quantity and never by a presentment total, so that only what the Worker sold is credited and nothing that sells today stops; gifts and sponsorships booked with no account; the tax part of a refund; the event record written after commit; untyped sessions to Suspense; one cash posting per balance transaction (`sbtx:`, `sbtc:`) and the classify rule for Stripe rows; the currency rule and `unbooked:`; actual fees; the per-kind deposit record with the gift fee on its own field; two-leg reversals keyed by Stripe object with account 2410, triggered by the refund events and by `charge.refunded` alike, funded at both events and released once; the dispute watch record; losses beyond the sale; the fee true-up that re-bases a reversal, and the same re-base of a claim side to its row's actual amount; the payment intent of subscription first payments; `plan_apply` from a subscription read, with plan history, a stable Stripe customer that Checkout creates and the Worker stores from its own read, under a key with no write on customers, and the seed of an empty record at the first plan read; `/api/plans/portal` (it moves behind the account-key signature in step 4, when the payments origin exists); integer 70 percent; the `paid_balance` clamp; the burn counter fix; the daily Ticket cap with reservations and the wallet value limit; `sales_regions.json` with every selling place open and `sell_tickets` true wherever Tickets sell today, and the `tickets_not_open` refusal in Ticket checkout creation; `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE` true; buyer country on deposits and value filings; `score_day` on the hub's sale record; no dollars-per-BLS field (the Worker serves `rate_display` for one more site release).
- Tests: a refunded 9.99 pack leaves Eustress down exactly 0.59 (mutation: reverse only the platform share). A 9.99 pack sold with 0.80 USD of tax and refunded in full leaves 2300 at zero and Eustress down exactly the fee (mutation: debit 4900 for the tax on a refund). Estimate, refund, true-up nets the pool zero (mutation: skip the re-base). A 20.00 EUR pack settled at 21.60 USD whose pending refund executes at 21.10 USD leaves 2410 at zero (mutation: leave the claim side at the webhook amount). Two refunds before either is paid out both return their full part (mutation: subtract pending in the capacity). A refund booked between a payout's creation and its sweep is held out of the drip from the landing on (mutation: decide `funded` only at the reversal); this test and the Suspense test below call step 3's two entry points directly on seeded `po:` stamps, steps 4 and 5 call the same functions from ingest and from the reversal pass that follows a landing, and neither rewrites them, so each mutation's `find` keeps matching exactly once. A refund that fails and a dispute that is won each release their pending amount once, in either order (mutation: release at both). A forged `checkout.session.completed` credits nothing (mutation: credit from the event body). A paid session the Worker did not create, with a genuine signed event and a large pack in its metadata, credits nothing (mutation: credit from session metadata). A plan bought before `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` renews after it, grants the plan and books its deposit, and a session created before that time and paid after it is credited once (mutation: require a creation record for every invoice). The `invoice.paid` of a new plan delivered before its `checkout.session.completed` grants the plan's Tickets and Delta and books the deposit once, and the session event that follows changes nothing (mutation: require `csub:` to exist already). A 9.99 USD pack that Stripe presents to a buyer in euros is credited, and books when its balance transaction states the USD amount (mutation: compare `amount_subtotal` with the catalog price). A refund announced by `charge.refunded` alone is booked, and one announced by `charge.refunded` and by `refund.created` is booked once (mutation: ignore `charge.refunded`). A 50.00 USD gift from a visitor who is not signed in books whole to the pool (mutation: require an account for a gift). A plan changed in the billing portal bills a proration that books at its own amount and grants no Tickets (mutation: require the catalog amount on an invoice). Suspense at the webhook and then the charge row at ingest leave 1010 equal to Stripe (mutation: post both). A webhook whose read fails is redelivered and credits once (mutation: skip a seen event). A dispute inquiry posts nothing (mutation: book it). A refund then a chargeback leaves 1010 equal to Stripe (mutation: credit 1010 from the webhook amount). A plan event dated in the future changes nothing the read does not show (mutation: take state from the event). A gift books with a fee and no negative share; a sale with a negative platform share is refused (mutation: drop the per-kind check). Thirty open Ticket sessions cannot pass the day cap (mutation: count paid only). Three daily loads cannot pass the wallet value limit (mutation: test the day's load only). With the shipped defaults a buyer in Canada, a buyer in Arizona and a gift checkout all succeed (mutation: default a selling region closed). With `sell_tickets` false for a place, a Ticket checkout from it is refused with `tickets_not_open` while a plan checkout from it succeeds (mutation: ignore `sell_tickets` in checkout creation). Retires from the grandfather list: delta, bliss and subscriptions.
- Staging gate: Stripe test mode purchases of every kind, a refund, a dispute, an inquiry, a non-USD payment, and a plan whose invoice event is delivered ahead of its session event.
- Production after: goods are credited only after the Worker's own read, and only for what the Worker sold. Nothing that sells today stops selling: every region and the gift checkout stay open unless McKale closed one under D26 to D28 before this deploy, and every plan, gift and sponsorship that predates the creation records renews and settles as before. Receipts are booked by the pre-journal path until the cutover of step 4, and at actual fees through the journal from then on. Every mode is `off`. From the moment `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` is set, this release is the rollback floor (15.2).


**Step 4. The Cashbook, read only, the cutover, the administrator frame, and the relay with no token.**
- Owns: `cashbook.mjs`, `stripe_ingest.mjs`, `mercury_read.mjs`, `bank_match.mjs`, `books.mjs`, `finance_admin.mjs`, `sanctions.mjs` (the list sync and `screenCounterparty`, named functions; step 6 adds the payee screen), `finance_alerts.mjs`, `finance_routes.mjs` (with `handleCronHealth`), `admin_users.mjs` (the stats handler), `finance_tick.mjs`, the payout and bank-row operations of the Treasury (`apply_stripe_row`, `compose_payout`, `apply_bank_row`, `reclass_bank_row`), `handoff.mjs`, the `handoff` method in `sessions.mjs`, the hand-off code record and the `admin_act`, `admin_read` and `plan_portal` challenge operations in `AccountAuth`, the `amr` stamp at the session issuing sites of `passkeys.mjs` and `identity_routes.mjs`, and the billing portal handler in `checkout.mjs` (each a named function; this step branches after step 3's landing, and step 6 branches after this step's landing); the genesis read in the Cashbook; `infrastructure/relay/*` with its `health`, `anchor` and `announce` routes, its signed answers and its head list, and with no Mercury token and no money route in the image; `relay_client.mjs` for those three calls; `tools/deploy-tripwire/*`; `tools/yearend/replay`, the offline journal replay; site: the payments origin (its bundle, its served policy and their test), the administrator finance page on it, the identity-file pages moved to it (`pages/login.rs` for sign-up and sign-in by identity file, `email_settings.rs`, `passkeys.rs`, `replace_key.rs`, `replacement_key.rs`, with `identity_file.rs` and `components/identity_file_picker.rs`), the hand-off on both bundles, the main-bundle test, `pages/economics_live.rs`. Before: McKale creates the payments origin, the relay's Fly app in its own organization, its tunnel and the Workers VPC service, and hands the service id to the orchestrator, who alone writes the `BANK_RELAY` binding (17.2); the `holds` and `snapshot` operations are read in production.
- Builds: Stripe payout and balance-transaction ingest; `apply_stripe_row`, `compose_payout`, tie-out; the payout failure row and the move of a failed payout's contents; the Mercury read client, events poll and webhook; posted rows only; `apply_bank_row` as the one cash posting, the reclass, and the reversing entry for a changed row; equal-amount pairing; the quiescent wall; reconciliation in observe mode; expected-outflow records; `FINANCE_ADMINS`, `requireFinanceAdmin` and the signed, delayed, announced act as the default for every finance route; the finance alert path; the expense ledger; posted books, lock, adjust on the one allowlist, export; the admin view; the cron-health rewrite; `financeTick` as two calls; drift monitors; unit meters and location totals with the exposure figure; retirement of the legacy accounting routes after the site release that stops calling them (section 11, item 3); the wall equation with its pending classes and the `explained` result; the outflow check on pending Bliss Treasury debits; IRS debits to Suspense and the tax deposit as a reclass; own transfers through 1130, between Engine Expenses and Checking and, as one classification of two rows, into Bliss Treasury; planned installments that never change once written; the destination check on every Stripe payout and the posting for a payout that lands on Bliss Treasury; the two parts of Bliss Treasury's Suspense in coverage, `suspense/charge-debit` and `suspense/write-off`; `sweeps/manual` as a Suspense reclass; halt scopes; the unhalt, whose clock runs under a halt of either scope, judged against the one stated meaning of false for each cause, narrowing an `all` halt whose `all` causes have cleared, and applied after `sweep` and `adminq` at the same hour; the re-check of every delayed act at its effective time, the purge of a removed key's acts, bulk undo and the stop-act counter; signed, logged administrator reads bound to one route and one target, the hold list as counts with its signed detail route, the books close as totals with no payee, and the declared response fields the route-policy test reads; the enforced policy of the payments origin and its policy test; every page that creates or reads an identity file on the payments origin, the sign-in hand-off with its verifier and the `handoff` session; the `Origin` rule of 13, which accepts a request with no `Origin` header; the main site's enforced `script-src` and `connect-src`, under the same policy test; `/api/plans/portal` behind the `plan_portal` signature on the payments origin; the payout's own balance transaction as the cash posting of a payout, with a compose that posts nothing; a compose held only by a row of an unknown reporting category, and the `other_receipt` classification; the outflow check on Engine Expenses and Checking, with a debit no rule covers booked to Suspense, rules that cover card charges and pulled debits under a ceiling and never a sent payment, and the planned vendor payment; the closed exits for a Suspense credit on each bank account, the planned transfer to Checking among them; the genesis read and the cutover in production; the relay deployed with `RELAY_ARMED` false, no Mercury token and three routes that move nothing, with signed answers and their verification and the tunnel from a configuration file in the image, so that every act is announced out of band and every journal entry is anchored from the first; the anchor held to the backed-up head; the dead-man's switch; the deploy tripwire, reading the relay's digest from Fly's API and the live policy header of both origins; the offline journal replay against the relay's head list; the streaming sanctions list sync with its acceptance checks, and the counterparty screen that gates every Suspense refund and every transfer of a Suspense credit to Checking from the first.
- Tests: an unknown BT debit halts (mutation: ignore unmatched rows). A payout whose rows do not sum is not composed (mutation: skip the tie-out). A failed payout's contents are swept once under the later payout (mutation: leave them on the failed payout). A row classified late and then matched posts 1100 once (mutation: post under the business key as well). A card authorization that settles at another amount leaves 1110 equal to the bank (mutation: post the pending row). A debit that posts between two balance reads does not halt (mutation: compare unequal reads). Two equal payouts and one credit confirm the older (mutation: require uniqueness). A KV administrator flag gets 403 on every finance route. A scheduled run that has spent 990 operations still delivers both ticks (mutation: a Stripe read inline). A pending Bliss Treasury debit with no record halts and names the transaction to cancel (mutation: ignore pending rows in the outflow check). An unknown BT debit beside an unexplained credit of the same size: coverage fails and the run preflight refuses (mutation: subtract signed Suspense). Thirty pending payments that Mercury deducts early are an explained difference, not a shortfall (mutation: compare the balance with the book alone). A tax deposit leaves the Checking wall equal (mutation: credit 1110 twice). The shadow-mode manual settlement completes with its real delay (mutation: require an unapplied row). A payout that confirms overnight leaves a planned installment's amount unchanged (mutation: repack a planned installment). An internal transfer of 400.00 USD into Bliss Treasury against a planned installment of 412.37 USD is refused by `sweeps/manual` and settled as an own transfer: 1130, 2910 and 2930 return to zero, carry rises by 400.00 USD and the month locks (mutation: classify the BT credit alone). A void queued on a read that goes stale overnight is cancelled when it would take effect (mutation: apply without the re-check). An act queued by a key that is then removed never fires (mutation: check the list only at queue time). An unhalt queued under a halt of each scope reaches its effective time (mutation: freeze the unhalt's own clock under a halt). A charge to carry and an unhalt due at the same hour are applied in that order (mutation: judge the unhalt first). A payout whose destination is not Engine Expenses raises `stripe_settings_drift`, composes, and is packed into no sweep until its bank row is matched where it landed (mutation: pack it before the match). The payments origin's policy test fails on a report-only policy (mutation: ship the policy report-only). The main bundle links no identity-file code (mutation: import the picker into a main-site page). A hand-off code works once and yields a session that the payments-origin and finance routes refuse (mutation: mint a `key` session from a hand-off). A code minted for one account cannot be exchanged by a browser that did not begin that sign-in (mutation: exchange without the verifier). A sign-in by identity file that carries no `Origin` header succeeds, as Studio's does, and one that carries the main site's origin is refused (mutation: require the header). A payout whose destination is Bliss Treasury is matched on a BT row by its trace, posts to carry bound to it and leaves R8 and R26 green (mutation: leave the row in 2930). A stolen administrator session reads no hold reason and no journal line that carries a payee (mutation: return the hold list with payees unsigned), and an `admin_read` challenge signed for one payee reads no other (mutation: bind the challenge to its route only). A payout that stays uncomposed over a weekend leaves 1010 equal to Stripe, and its failure on Monday posts the exact mirror and leaves 1030 at zero (mutation: post a payout's cash at compose). An invoice paid outside the Worker lands in 2900, its payout composes and the other sales in it are swept, and `other_receipt` clears the row (mutation: hold a payout uncomposed for a known-type Suspense row). An unknown ACH debit on Engine Expenses raises `unmatched_outflow` while it is pending and posts to 2910 (mutation: book a no-rule debit on Engine Expenses to 6999). A 9,000.00 USD ACH sent from Checking to a recipient named after a vendor that has a rule raises `unmatched_outflow` while it is pending and posts to 2920 (mutation: let a rule explain a sent payment). A card charge above its rule's ceiling alerts (mutation: ignore the ceiling). A pulled debit named IRS above the open deposit worksheets alerts (mutation: accept any debit named IRS). A 5,000.00 USD owner contribution on Checking is classified to 3100, two micro-deposits on Bliss Treasury leave by the planned transfer to Checking, and the month locks (mutation: classify an outside credit as carry). The production cutover fixture passes on the figures the genesis read supplies, and a balance that moves between its two reads is read again (mutation: read the Stripe balance once). A card authorization pending at the genesis read postpones the cutover (mutation: open the book while a row is pending). An act queued in this step is announced through the relay, and a rewritten journal is refused by the witness (mutation: accept any head). A deposit booked after the last backup is backed up before its digest is anchored, and a Treasury lost after that anchor is restored to a journal the relay's next anchor accepts (mutation: anchor to the current head). The tripwire reports `deploy_drift` when Fly's digest differs while the relay's own health route reports the reviewed one (mutation: read the relay's digest from its own health route). The main site's policy test fails on a report-only `script-src` (mutation: ship the main site's `script-src` report-only). A forged session opens no billing portal (mutation: open the billing portal on a bearer token). The offline replay of a month verifies against the relay's head list, and a rewritten entry fails it (mutation: skip the head comparison). A counterparty name, a KYC name and a case text that carry markup are shown on the payments origin as text and none is parsed (mutation: render a counterparty name as markup on the payments origin). A sweep whose Engine Expenses leg posts on October 31 and whose Bliss Treasury leg posts on November 1, beside a charge row reclassed on November 1, lets October lock once both exits have posted, and a Suspense item dated in November holds no lock of October (mutation: require Suspense and 1130 to be zero as of the month's last day). A truncated sanctions list is rejected and a full-size one parses inside the buffer bound; a Suspense refund to an originator on the list is held, and a confirmed match blocks the credit in 2016 (mutation: plan a refund or a transfer without a counterparty screen).
- Staging gate: sandbox data reconciles; a seeded unknown transaction lands in Suspense; a seeded mismatch raises exactly one alert; the failing Stripe test bank (`000111111116`) proves the payout failure path.
- Production after: McKale sets `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET=3`, the cutover runs at one quiescent instant with its legacy figure computed inside the refusal and announced and the journal is live: pool shares book to `transit_cents`, and nobody was being paid, so nothing regresses. Then `SWEEP_MODE=shadow`. The first real Stripe payout is matched to its Engine Expenses deposit with no money moved by code, and the winning match key is recorded. Sign-in by identity file happens on the payments origin, and the main site holds hand-off and passkey sessions only. From the cutover, this release is the rollback floor (15.2).

**Step 5. The relay and the live sweep.**
- Before: the relay of step 4 is deployed, announcing and anchoring. McKale creates the send token in the week the live sweep starts (17.2).
- Owns: the money and enrollment routes of `infrastructure/relay/*` (`sweep`, `ach`, `invite`, `invite-status`) with the lock, the ledger and the caps, the money calls of `relay_client.mjs`, `bank_adapter.mjs`, the sweep operations.
- Builds: the relay's lock, its day ledger, its first-seen records, its templates and exact look-ups, the request look-up by memo code and creation time, the clock checked again before every Mercury call, and the recipient announcement with no bank digit; `bank_adapter.mjs`; sweep installments, the carry a sweep may use less open carry return plans, the negative part with no transfer, `sweep_deficit_cents`, the `failed` state, the computed floor; the kill-switch drill, including a disarmed relay refusing sweeps and invites, a halt freezing queued acts, and the in-Mercury cancel and reject steps; periods counted by token; the weekly and 30 day caps, the run window and the self-disarm; `refused_wait`; the versioned fingerprint over the whole routing tuple; the first-seen rebuild from a snapshot, from Mercury's history and from the announcement log; the top-up installment and its passage under a halt of scope `payouts`; the cadence table of 7.5 and the R27 preflight; deferral of a large deposit on its payout with its three exits and the netting of a reversed deferred deposit; funding and release of reversals after the landing, by the `revpass` stream through step 3's entry point, with `accrue_setup` waiting on an open pass; the cover limit of an installment; negative parts keyed by installment counter; the `failing` state, `fail_payout` waiting for the installment in flight and reversing in the write that moves the contents, and the re-point of a mispaired credit; the sweep of a payout that landed on Bliss Treasury from the carry bound to it.
- Tests: a repeated installment id sends once (mutation: remove the exact look-up). `eus-sweep-po_1` never matches `eus-sweep-po_12`. Fifty parallel requests cannot pass the day cap (mutation: remove the lock). A gift larger than the cap is swept in installments. A debit leg that posts and returns ends `failed` and the next installment lands once. A negative part never takes `cents` below zero and transfers nothing, and a carry it leaves negative is repaired by a top-up installment (mutation: apply it whole; mutation: transfer the carry deficit with a negative part). A carry return planned for the whole usable carry leaves the next landing's `use` at zero, and the planned transfer leaves carry at zero (mutation: leave planned carry returns in usable carry). A reversal's pending amount leaves at the covering landing (mutation: release at row apply). An installment whose payouts carry 300 reversals lands in one put inside the key limit, the pass funds and releases them in bounded steps, and the night's drip waits for it (mutation: update the reversal records in the landing write). A payout that was the newest of a net-negative installment and then fails posts its reversing part under a fresh counter and is superseded (mutation: key a negative part by the payout alone). A staging-signed request is refused by a production-labelled relay (mutation: drop the label). A request the relay holds past 60 seconds is not posted to Mercury (mutation: check the relay's clock at receipt only). A recipient announcement carries no bank digit (mutation: post a bank digit to the out-of-band channel). A resend at one minute and at thirty both return the first transaction (mutation: reject a seen payment key). A fake Mercury that returns account numbers leaks none (mutation: drop the allowlist). Cents to dollars is exact (mutation: use a float). An answer with no relay signature is unknown, and an invite answer without one shows no link (mutation: accept an unsigned answer). A W46 payment sent in W47 does not use W47, and a November wire approved in December does not use December (mutation: count a period by posting date). A lost volume does not restart 72 hours for a recipient Mercury's history proves (mutation: restart every clock). The relay's ISO week cap refuses a second run's worth inside one week, counted by period token (mutation: count the weekly cap by posting date). The rolling 30 day cap refuses the sixth run's worth under the weekly cadence (mutation: drop the 30 day check). An ACH outside the run day and its window is `refused_wait {outside_run_window}` and the book returns it to the lots (mutation: accept any day). First payments above three times the trailing median, over the floor, disarm the relay, and a surge below the floor does not (mutation: ignore the floor). A run is not started when the relay's schedule or weekly cap does not fit `ACH_CADENCE` (mutation: check the token kind only). A mutation in `infrastructure/relay` fails the named Worker contract test through the copy. A recipient whose `countrySpecific` block changes fails the fingerprint (mutation: hash SWIFT and IBAN only). A carry past its bound is topped up under a halt and the unhalt then takes effect (mutation: block the top-up under a halt); an unhalt whose cause still stands is cancelled (mutation: lift on acknowledgement alone); an unhalt whose only standing cause has its top-up `submitted` waits for the landing and then lifts (mutation: cancel at the first judgment). A 1,500.00 USD gift with too little cover is deferred while the payouts behind it are swept (mutation: wait on the oldest payout). A deferred gift is swept on day 121 with no added cover and its payout ends `swept` (mutation: pack only `bank_confirmed` payouts). A deferred 1,500.00 USD gift refunded on day 10 leaves `cents` and the drip base unchanged (mutation: take the negative part from `cents` while the deferred cents stay in transit). A payout that fails while its second installment is in flight is funded once, and the transit identity holds after every write of the sequence: the `failing` mark, the landing, the reversing part with the move, the carrier's sweep (mutation: take the reversing part before the installment lands). The older of two equal payouts fails after its sibling's credit was paired to it: the credit is re-pointed after the same wait, the sibling keeps its own part and is funded once (mutation: re-point with an installment in flight). A payout that landed on Bliss Treasury funds its part from the carry bound to it with a transfer of zero, and the month locks (mutation: count that carry as unlocked carry).
- Staging gate: sweeps land in the Mercury sandbox; the observed egress address equals the allowlisted one; cap refusals and each kill switch are exercised; the note of an internal transfer is confirmed to appear on the BT credit; Security reviews the relay.
- Production after: `SWEEP_MODE=live` with the starting caps. Three consecutive payouts are swept and reconciled to the cent. Bliss Treasury holds the funded pool.

**Step 6. Identity, tax and enrollment.**
- Owns: `payee_gate.mjs`, `tax_vault.mjs`, `tax_forms.mjs`, `sanctions.mjs` (the payee screen; the list sync and `screenCounterparty` are step 4's), `payout_routes.mjs` (the destination, payout and case routes), `dest_claims.mjs`, `sessions.mjs` (the key and the overlap; every caller already goes through it since step 1), `kyc.mjs`, `admin_users.mjs` (ban and delete), the other `AccountAuth` additions, and the rest of the session code of `identity_routes.mjs`, `fresh_auth.mjs` and `passkeys.mjs`, each on the step's branch; `tests/helpers/load_worker.mjs` (the test token helper); `tools/yearend/keygen`, `tools/yearend/envelopes` (the opener with its commitment check) and `tools/yearend/drill`; site: `/payouts`, and on the payments origin the tax step with in-browser encryption and the destination steps.
- Builds: `payeeGate`; ban state and account type in `AccountAuth`; `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY`; KYC name enforcement, the narrow reverification and prompt hardening; `kyc_facts`; the destination record, the signed invite, pending, confirm, reconfirm and remove routes; `needs_reconfirm` at a key replacement; the fingerprint claim with its generic answer; `TaxVault`; the forms pages; browser encryption to the pinned key, commitments, the canary envelope; the canonical hash form; the payee screen with stored inputs and the bank entity screen, on the list sync of step 4; sign-in location records; contributor terms; money notices by mail and in the product; `ENROLLMENT_MODE`; the session key cutover inside `sessions.mjs` (sign with the new key when set, verify old-key tokens issued before `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER`, `/health`, `session_overlap_open` and `session_key_unset`); the audited backfill of ban state, account type and document-read KYC facts, and `money_migrated`; a ban as a suspending state with its announcement and tripwire; `sanctions_block` on the account; `DestClaims` with the incumbent rule, the review and `dest_conflict` alert at every conflict, the uniform pending state, and the `dest-claims/resolve` act; signed reads of the tax form on file and of the destination's last four; the unsigned `not_my_account` rule past the limit and `dest-removals/ack`; every key replacement marking the destination; the aged-address rule; the counted, parking removal; the envelope export and result routes; year-end documents rendered on request behind a signed challenge; the offline key-generation script, the envelope opener with its commitment, signature and last-four checks (the US format for a US TIN, the commitment and non-emptiness for a foreign one) and the key drill, so that the pinned production key, the canary and the weekly sitting each have their tool before the first form is taken; the W-8BEN that needs no TIN; the plan seed in the audited backfill.
- Tests: a frozen account gets 423 on destination invite and on `/api/tax/w9` (mutation: class FRESH), and can still delete its destination. A confirm without the signature is refused (mutation: accept it). A proven destination set 40 days before a key replacement is unpaid until the current key signs it again (mutation: mark only a young or unproven destination). A fingerprint conflict leaves the incumbent on its path, proven or not, opens a review and raises `dest_conflict` the first time, and the newcomer sees the same pending state as a clean enrollment for the whole cooling period (mutation: park both). Someone who claimed a stranger's numbers first and never proves them does not hold the true owner past the review: `dest-claims/resolve` releases the claim and the owner's destination returns to `pending` (mutation: let the earlier unproven claim stand after the act). The tax form's certified fields and the destination's last four are not served to a session alone (mutation: drop the signature requirement on either read). An attacker holding all storage and the last four cannot recover a planted TIN (mutation: an unsalted commitment); planted TINs appear in no log or write in any encoding. A key whose fingerprint is not pinned is refused. A W-8BEN from an account with a US document goes to review. A known SDN name and a Crimea address are caught. A ban read from KV fails its test. A reverification with another person's document goes to review and holds. An account banned in KV before the step is refused by the gate (mutation: skip the `money_migrated` check); a self-typed name never becomes a legal name (mutation: copy `ocr_name`). A token signed with the old key verifies during the overlap and not after it (mutation: verify with the old key only). With the new key set, a token signed with it is accepted by `verifyAuth`, `freshGate` and `kycGate`, and every issuing route (register, login, passkey, key rotation) signs with it (mutation: one caller still passes `env.JWT_SECRET`). With `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY` unset after this release, sign-in still signs with `JWT_SECRET` and `session_key_unset` fires (mutation: require the new key to sign). A banned payee's shares accrue into a hold and come back on `ban_clear` (mutation: forfeit them). A blocked account can neither spend nor receive Tickets (mutation: gate only the payable). A year-end document is not served to a bearer token alone. The eleventh unsigned removal of a day parks its destination and sends no notice, and an unsigned `not_my_account` case past the limit parks without setting `payee_dispute` (mutation: set the dispute hold); the tripwire's acknowledgement sends each parked payee a reconfirm notice. An account blocked with a zero payable and a Wallet balance raises the annual blocked-property alert (mutation: key the alert on `blocked_cents`). A W-8BEN with the "not legally required" box and no envelope is accepted, accrues and is verified by rule (mutation: require an envelope on every W-8BEN). An envelope made by the deployed page is opened by the offline tool and its commitment verified, and a junk envelope fails (mutation: skip the commitment check). `/api/tax/interview` answers a forged session with no legal name and no date of birth (mutation: prefill from `kyc_facts`). An export is streamed in the answer to its signed request and nothing is written to any store (mutation: queue the export as a delayed act). Retires from the grandfather list: value freeze.
- Staging gate: a test account completes all five gates against the sandbox; Security reviews the vault and the routes; counsel reviews the form text.
- Production after: built, with `ENROLLMENT_MODE=off`. `staff` needs gate G1; `live` needs G1 in writing, the terms half of G2, G6 and G7. Nothing accrues. From the moment `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER` is set, this release is the rollback floor (15.2).

**Step 7. Accrual, and the Connect removal.**
- Owns: `payables.mjs`, the settlement phases of `treasury.mjs`, `payout_rate.mjs` (the rounded public figures, branched after step 3's landing), the Connect removal of section 11 item 2 (one change, together with this step), and `connect_archive` with its route (section 11, item 4); site: `pages/bliss.rs`.
- Builds: the accrue phases; `init` and `total` as 5.1 states them; the day pool; accrual rows as evidence, with the `us_cents` stamp of 6.4 on every row of a W-8 filer, by each line's own source rule; lots and the `mature` stream, its writes sized in keys; the split gate with suspended holds and their limit; payee rows; `payq:`; history; shadow mode with its stated gate and `SHADOW_VISIBLE`; the unaccrued remainder parked in 2003 with its monthly fold, and every payee-dependent return to the pool credited there; the public day record with the rounded pool alone, the closed list of rounded public figures on `/api/payouts/rate` and `/api/ledger/summary`, and the monthly statement under its three conditions; the phase aliases; conversion of any legacy hold; the accrual run's wait on a year with no activated rules file, and `tax_rules_missing` from December 1; the one-time `connect_archive` of section 11, item 4, run only after step 6 has made `AccountAuth` the source of record for bans. The payable model is fixed here, before any row exists in production.
- Tests: a refund landing mid-settlement does not shrink a planned share (mutation: skip the day-pool reserve). An enrolled share accrues once across a resumed step and an unenrolled share returns to the pool (mutation: drop the row-exists check). An accrual without a tax form is refused (mutation: remove the condition). A payee under a sanctions review for four nights accrues into a hold and is paid on release (mutation: forfeit a suspended share). A settlement run late judges the plan as of the score date. A released hold is not payable before its rows mature (mutation: payable at once). A payee with 400 days of backlog and a hold larger than the first-payment cap is built inside one put (mutation: stamp rows one by one). One night's effort rows of 60 different payees fold in writes that each stay inside the key limit (mutation: fold 50 rows a write). A job stored in each retired phase resumes (mutation: remove the alias). A day older than six days accrues USD with emission skipped. An account banned while `connect_archive` rewrites its KV record stays banned (mutation: read the ban from the KV record the archive rewrites). Membership cannot be rebuilt from the public records and public scores over a multi-day series with single-member changes. The fixture builds that series from the real `drip`, `dripend` and `fold` arithmetic over consecutive nights with no sweep landing and no reversal, and the attacker uses everything on `/api/payouts/rate` and the day records, including returned(t) = base(t+1) - base(t) + pool(t). Two mutations, and the test fails under each: publish the daily split; return the remainder to the drip base nightly with exact public pool figures. No page that renders an estimate carries a plan checkout link, and an account with no qualifying plan has no estimate (mutation: render both). A settlement for January 1 with no rules file for that year waits, and accrues in full once the file is activated (mutation: fail the accrual gate for a missing rules file). A W-8 filer with US sign-ins in January who is paid in June has `us_cents` on January's services rows, and those cents are withheld on or wait under `us_presence` (mutation: test the month of the build). A W-8 filer's value row on a royalty line the rules mark US source with basis `slice` carries its `value_us_cents` as `us_cents`, and those cents are withheld on or wait under `us_presence` (mutation: stamp `services` rows only).
- Staging gate: two weeks of nightly accrual with both identities holding.
- Production after: `PAYOUTS_MODE=shadow` at once if McKale wants estimates computed for the administrator view. Showing an estimate to any account needs gate G13 and `SHADOW_VISIBLE`. `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue` needs gates G1, G2, G3 and G11. Payables build; nothing is paid.

**Step 8. Commerce integrity.**
- Owns: the Wallet lots, `CommerceHub` settle operations, `reverse_value` and the clawback operations.
- Builds: Ticket lots in the Wallet; the per-lot purchase index, and `lot_reversal` with its one cursor record, advanced a purchase a step on a bounded alarm; `cancel_settle`, `reverse_score`, `reverse_value` keyed per purchase with the capped-score recomputation; clawback, netting at once and write-off with the recoverable bucket; the settle step reading the buyer's copy; `SCORE_SETTLE_DAYS`; the meter check; `TICKET_RESERVE_MODE=shadow`.
- Tests: a disputed pack cancels the pending settle and shrinks the creator's maturing value row (mutation: skip `reverse_value`). One dispute, three purchases, three score dates shrink three rows (mutation: key by reversal only). A reversal at day 100 finds the day it scored. A reversal under a binding per-buyer cap leaves the row unchanged. A 4.00 USD clawback against 900.00 USD matured pays 896.00 USD (mutation: block on any clawback). A clawback of a paid row leaves the book identity true (mutation: credit the funded pool). A refund in the last minute of the window never scores (mutation: file without reading the buyer's copy). A disputed pack that funded 600 purchases reverses every one under the operation-counting fake, with the webhook inside its budget and the reversal's put inside the key limit (mutation: run a lot reversal's loop inline in the webhook; mutation: write one outbox key per returned purchase in the reversal's put). Retires from the grandfather list: commerce and purchases.
- Staging gate: the chain runs end to end on staging purchases.
- Production after: `WALLET_LOTS_LIVE=true`. A stolen card cannot mature into a payable.

**Step 9. US ACH runs.**
- Owns: the run and payment operations of `payables.mjs`, `bank_match.mjs` payment classes, statements, `tax_withholding.mjs`, `tools/yearend/roster`; site: statements and history on `/payouts`.
- Builds: runs, payments and their states with opaque ids and `payidx:`; the intent object; the gate re-check before every send; the unknown resolver with `first_unknown_at`, the latch under which no automatic path releases an unknown payment, a Mercury refusal ending resends, the confirming read and resume rules; run order; holds, release in part, review and delayed acts; caps and the armed tripwire; returns keyed by payment in all three shapes; settlement on a fresh read; statements, history and payee cases; `withholdingFor` with the aggregate, the floor, the whole-line basis, the Form 8233 rule with its exempt amount and its use, the under-withheld record of a late US-work report and its recovery from the next payments and the treaty row selection by residence and by the line's own income code (the nonresident branch behind `NRA_WITHHOLDING_ENABLED`), called for every line with the form of the payment's tax snapshot; year totals and the aggregate keyed by that snapshot's form, and withholding at the debit; the deposit clocks of 6.5; the offline roster comparison, reading Mercury's dashboard export from a file and keeping the map of customer id to recipient across rosters; backup after each run step and `rebuild_payments`; the attempt committed before each call by check-and-set, and the `sending` state; the parking rule; the send-money request list in every ACH read and the `awaiting_approval` state on this rail, with a request matched to one payment by memo code and creation time; the builder's timing rules and the run window; `refused_wait` back to the lots; the unlinked return classification, the settlement hold for a credit that names a payment, and the reopening of a settled payment; the per-line withholding on the payment record and the tag for returned cents that tax was taken on; the stray payment classification with its netting; the netting of every open clawback and stray-payment remainder in the write that builds a payment; the payment limit while a TIN envelope is unchecked.
- Tests: a lost answer followed by a retry pays once (mutation: change the key per attempt). The 24 hour duplicate refusal leads to a read, never a release (mutation: classify it as a recipient fault). A payee banned between reserve and resend is not sent to (mutation: skip the gate on resend). After an unknown attempt, a later `bank_rejected` goes to review (mutation: release to a hold). A return shown as a status change and as a linked credit holds the amount once (mutation: key by transaction id). A posted payment later marked failed is a return. A 300.00 USD returned payment is repaid as 25.00 USD and the rest later. A payment with 24.00 USD withheld that is voided never touches 2040 (mutation: credit withholding at reserve). A return of net 76.00 USD on gross 100.00 USD leaves gross 24.00 USD and withheld 24.00 USD (mutation: reverse the gross). The same debit, after its month's withholding was deposited, turns `returned` with no linked row: it posts 76.00 USD to 1120 once and leaves 2030 and 2040 untouched (mutation: apply the reversing entry of 2.2 to a status change). A payment returned after a 100.00 USD clawback arose on its cents is repaid, once its hold is released, net of the clawback (mutation: take gross from the lots without netting open receivables). A 300.00 USD payment that carried no withholding returns, the payee's B notice then reaches `withholding_on`, and the repayment withholds 24 percent (mutation: tag every return). A second 485.00 USD debit to one recipient that could not be cancelled is booked as a stray payment: the payee's year totals carry 970.00 USD, R12 ties and 2003 is unchanged (mutation: reduce the payable by `adj`). A run capped at 50 reaches the 20 it missed first next week. The Treasury lost an hour after a run is restored with no second payment, and one payee's accrual rows, lots, holds and year totals equal their values before the loss. A statement for another payee's id is the same 404 as a missing one. A ban delivered during the awaited send pays once and holds nothing; an object reset mid-send resumes as unknown (mutation of both: write the attempt after the call). A send that Mercury turns into an approval item is not sent again and cannot be voided while the request is open (mutation: resolve from the transaction list only).
