The week-2 build after a first payment is skipped, not refused, and the destination is untouched (mutation: treat a relay timing refusal as a recipient fault). The sixth first payment of a day waits a day while the payee behind it is paid (mutation: pause the run). An unlinked credit holds its amount once and blocks settlement (mutation: settle on the status word alone); a return after settlement takes the amount back out of `paid_cents`. A payee whose envelope is unchecked is paid up to the limit and no further. A run anomaly halts, its acknowledgement finishes its clock under that halt, and the unhalt then takes effect and the run finishes (mutation: freeze the acknowledgement's clock under the halt). A ban delivered during the awaited gate pays nothing (mutation: write the attempt from the stale copy after the awaited gate). A send that times out on the last day of its window is still `reserved` when its debit posts twenty minutes after an empty read, and goes to review at the resend age when nothing posts (mutation: return an unknown payment to the lots at the window close). A first attempt that landed unseen and is answered by duplicate refusals is in review before the guard lapses (mutation: measure the resend age from the last attempt). A capped payee's 500.00 USD payment in the week after an approved 500.00 USD request is sent, and is not captured by that request (mutation: match a request on recipient and amount alone). A credit of three cents that names no payment holds no settlement (mutation: hold every payment in its watch on a credit that names none). Withholding uses gross and integer basis points (mutation: withhold on net). Two lines of 1,500 USD under a B notice are withheld as one 3,000 USD payee, and a payment in flight counts (mutation: test the floor on one line). A payee's own item 2 answer withholds nothing on services (mutation: ignore the character). Rows earned under a W-8BEN and paid after a W-9 is on file are in the Form 1099 total and the threshold aggregate, and rows earned under a W-9 and paid under a W-8BEN on a US-source line are withheld at 30 percent (mutation: key year totals by the accrual certificate). US-source services with a treaty claim and no Form 8233 withhold 30 percent (mutation: apply the treaty rate). A payee with an accepted Form 8233 exempting 1,000.00 USD is withheld nothing on the first 1,000.00 USD of US-source services and 30 percent above it, and two payments in flight never exempt the same dollar (mutation: exempt the whole slice under any Form 8233). A US-work report covering 600.00 USD already wired as foreign source adds 600.00 USD to that payment year's `us_gross_cents`, records 180.00 USD under-withheld, and the payee's next payment withholds it first (mutation: stamp unpaid cents only on a US-work report). A resident of a country with no treaty row who claims 0 percent is withheld at 30 percent (mutation: use the payee's rate). A resident of a country with two royalty rows at different rates who claims the lower-rate subtype is withheld at the rate of the line's own income code (mutation: select the row by the payee's stated income type). September withholding raises an October 15 deposit (mutation: wait for 2,500 USD). The offline roster comparison lists a customer id that appears with a second live recipient (mutation: compare each month's roster alone). It addresses the month's notice to the email on the customer's earliest paid plan invoice dated before the enrollment, and lists a customer whose current billing email differs from it (mutation: address the notice to the current billing email). Retires from the grandfather list: the holder ledger, with mutations written here.
- Staging gate: a weekly run in the sandbox; a forced return, a cap hit and each failure of 5.6 on fakes; the restore drill of G8; `tests/money_grandfathered.json` is empty.
- Production after: built, `PAYOUTS_MODE` still `accrue`. `live` needs gates G1, G2, G6 and G8. The first run pays McKale's own enrolled account, including one deliberately returned payment (G7); then caps rise.

**Step 10. International wires.**
- Owns: the wire run, `/v1/wire-request` and `/v1/wire-manifest` in the relay, `wire_countries.json`.
- Before building: the sandbox test of a repeated key on `request-send-money`.
- Builds: the wire run with its open month; the request-list resolver; the live approval gate, the do-not-approve set and the expiring manifest; the relay's hourly fingerprint re-check, the re-check every 5 minutes while a manifest is live with the manifest voided on a change, and the post-send check; fee clearing on evidence, the fee credit that lowers the payment's year totals and is tagged `withholding_settled` only when its payment carried withholding, and `wirefee-late`; the December rule (nothing built after the 19th, every open request flagged from the 20th); the W-8 conditions applied by form, never by rail, with source read from the lot lines and never from the month of the build; the country `trial` state; the approval deadline notices; trace cases; the daily re-examination of skipped payees while the month is open; `do_not_approve` cleared when the gate passes again; the month token on both sides.
- Tests: `chargeType` is always present (mutation: omit it). A timed-out request is not created twice against a fake that ignores the key (mutation: resolve from the transaction list only). A payee matched by a list update while awaiting approval is marked do-not-approve (mutation: skip the re-check). A payable under the minimum after withholding waits. A fee lower than the deduction returns to the payee (mutation: book it to income). Paid again, the credited cents of a wire that carried withholding are not withheld on or reported a second time (mutation: return a wire fee credit as ordinary payable). A fee that arrives after `wirefee-none` comes back out of the payee's payable, and the book identity holds. A fee that has not appeared in 10 business days stays in clearing (mutation: refund on a timer). A fee billed on Checking leaves the book identity true. A halt on the 3rd that lifts on the 9th still runs that month. A first wire to a `trial` country is built once. Wires awaiting approval never show as surplus (mutation: leave `reserved_cents` out of `claims_bt`). A payee whose November request is approved on December 4, and whose November debit posts on December 8, is built on the first business day after December 8 and inside December (mutation: build the month in a single pass). A do-not-approve mark set by a stale sanctions list clears when the list is fresh. A correspondent bank added while a request waits stops the approval, and one added ten minutes after a manifest is issued voids that manifest before the approval (mutation: re-check a live manifest hourly only). A wire marked do-not-approve by a clawback that reached its cents is rejected, and the next month's build nets the clawback before it takes gross (mutation: take gross from the lots without netting open receivables). A payee whose first wire settles on December 22 is built in January (mutation: build a December wire after the 19th). A US citizen banking in Portugal on a W-9 is wired with no foreign-services certification and through a US sign-in (mutation: apply the W-8 wire conditions by rail).
- Staging gate: a queued wire, an approval and a fee match in the sandbox where it allows; the rest on fakes.
- Production after: needs gate G4 and the accountant's ruling on foreign lines; one supervised wire end to end, which also settles when and where Mercury bills the fee.

**Step 11. Year-end.**
- Owns: `tax_yearend.mjs`, `tools/yearend/export` (the filing export and the hard copies).
- Builds: what a filing season needs, and nothing an earlier step depends on: `TaxFiler` with `iris_csv` for the 1099 series and Form 1042-S, with the line's own income code on every Form 1042-S row; the offline filing export, with the duplicate-TIN check and the hard copies; recipient copies with the payee reference; the pre-filing report; the B-notice machine; the Form 945 and 1042 worksheets; the vendor report; per-state lists. The tools the year runs on are already in place: the journal replay (step 4); key generation, the envelope opener and the key drill (step 6); `withholdingFor`, the deposit clocks and the roster comparison (step 9).
- Tests: a full tax year under the shifted clock, including a return that crosses the year boundary and a payee who changes from W-8 to W-9 mid-year with unpaid rows on both sides of the change: the Form 1099 carries everything paid under the W-9, whenever it was earned (mutation: key year totals by score date; mutation: key year totals by the accrual certificate). A clawback of prior-year income netted this year adds to this year's gross. A return posted in January of a payment debited in December reduces December's year and sets its correction flag, a clawback of that payment netted in February adds `netted_cents` to the new year, and R12 ties for both years (mutation: count a return in its own posting year). The 1042-S export matches the year's IRIS template and prints each line's own income code. A W-8 filer paid 2,400.00 USD in a year, 300.00 USD of it US source with 90.00 USD withheld, has a Form 1042-S of 300.00 USD and 90.00 USD (mutation: report the whole gross on Form 1042-S).
- Staging gate: a dry run on staging data against the tax year's own IRIS templates.
- Production after: ready by January 2 of the year after the first payout.

**Step 12. Final copy, documents and legal text, in the release that makes them true (with step 9).** The neutral copy of step 1 has stood since the old rail stopped; this step replaces it.
- Owns: the site copy files and their pinned tests; the documents named below.
- Builds: payout copy on the Bliss, Earning, Economics and Premium pages (the public Economics page says "bank transfer" and keeps its banned-word tests unless McKale lifts them); the wire fee, cap and minimum disclosures; Terms sections 2, 5 and 7 and the payout, tax, fee, hold, suspension and plan clauses (counsel first); privacy disclosures and the processor list (Stripe for payments; Mercury for payouts, with legal name and email as the data sent; Fly.io; and the chat provider behind the out-of-band channel, with legal name and country as the data sent); the gift page's not-deductible line; the pinned copy tests in the same commit, each shown able to fail; `CURRENCY.md`, `CREATOR_CASHOUT.md`, a current Worker infrastructure document; `SUBSCRIPTIONS.md` archived.
- Staging gate: copy tests updated and mutated.
- Production after: public wording matches the system.

**Step 13. Gated plumbing.** Built and tested with each switch off; each waits for its own sign-off. Investment records with the match on a wire's originator name, one code format, acceptance, repayment to the account that funded, the return posting with its withholding credited to the operating payable of its kind and its year totals, and the holder ledger follow-through. The Ticket cash-out line. Entity payees. Nonresident withholding switched on. Tests: an ACH credit carrying an investor's name and code is not matched (mutation: match on the typed name); a 1,000.00 USD return to a W-9 holder with 240.00 USD withheld credits 2045, leaves 2040, carry and the conservation identity untouched and writes the holder's year total (mutation: credit 2040); the same return to a W-8BEN holder withholds 300.00 USD into 2046 on the Form 1042 clock (mutation: post every return's withholding as backup); one 360.00 USD deposit clears 120.00 USD of 2040 and 240.00 USD of 2045 by name, and the conservation identity holds (mutation: clear the whole deposit from 2040); a repayment of 10,000.00 USD principal and a 500.00 USD return under a B notice writes two records, and the 380.00 USD Checking debit matches its `invest_return` record and posts 5300 and 2045 (mutation: plan an investor's principal and return as one record); an investor who matches the list at the funding match is held, and a confirmed match moves the funds to 2016 (mutation: match the funding without the screen).

**Step C1. Clients (parallel).** Stable chunk ids across a restart; `capped` handling; the site base for "Get Tickets"; CLI key validation and a private config write; payout wording in the bliss crate. Test: a chunk credited before a crash is not credited again (mutation: a fresh id on restart).

When the build completes, sections 11 and 16 move to `docs/monetization/archive/FINANCIAL_BACKBONE_BUILD_PLAN.md` and this document keeps only the description of the system.

---

## 17. What McKale does himself

Agents do none of this.

### 17.1 Decisions, each option defined

"Default" is what the build assumes until McKale says otherwise.

| # | Decision | Options, in plain words | Default |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | International wires need a person at Mercury | (a) Accept one approval sitting a month in the Mercury dashboard: wires are queued automatically and McKale approves the batch against the relay's manifest. (b) Ask Mercury for direct international send and hold international payouts until it is granted. (c) Use another rail for international payees, which reopens locked decision 3 | (a), with (b) asked in the same email as the use-case question |
| D2 | The second Mercury user, and a stand-in approver | Who creates the wire request token so McKale is the approver (an accountant, a bookkeeper or a trusted person with their own Mercury login). Whether a third person may approve when McKale cannot | Required; no default |
| D3 | When the plan is checked | (a) At accrual and at send: a lapsed plan pauses payment until the plan is active again; what was earned stays owed. (b) At accrual only: what was earned while the plan was active is paid even after it lapses. Counsel is asked whether (a) is lawful, since it makes payment of a debt wait on a subscription (section 19, item 27) | (a), the literal reading of locked decision 8, subject to counsel's answer |
| D4 | US ACH cadence | Weekly: one batch each Tuesday, a 7 day window for reversals, the token stays in use. Daily: many small irrevocable payments. Monthly: fewest runs, same day as wires | Weekly |
| D5 | Minimums, the wire fee, and the fee on a split balance | ACH minimum; wire minimum; flat wire fee. A higher wire minimum means the fee is a smaller share of each payout and more balances wait. And: when Eustress's own cap splits one balance over several months, (a) the payee bears the fee of each wire, stated on the payout page before the first one; or (b) Eustress bears the fee of every wire after the first that a cap forced | 10.00, 50.00 and 15.00 USD; (a), the literal reading of locked decision 5 |
| D6 | When a creator's earnings from Ticket sales become payable | 120 days after they are earned: covers the card dispute window, so a stolen card cannot reach an irrevocable payment. A shorter period pays creators sooner and accepts chargeback losses that netting may not recover | 120 days |
| D7 | Shares of people who cannot be paid | Two parts. People who never enrolled: (a) their shares stay in the pool and are shared by everyone later (no liability to people who may never enroll); or (b) accrue for everyone (a growing liability and a claim before any contract). Enrolled payees in a transient state on Eustress's side (a name-similarity review, a bank return, a form renewal, a key freeze): (c) their shares accrue into a hold for up to 30 days and are paid when the state clears; or (d) those shares return to the pool like anyone else's | (a) and (c) |
| D8 | Stripe's fee on a treasury gift | Eustress bears it: the gift is credited whole, as the page says. Or the pool bears it: the gift is credited net | Eustress bears it |
| D9 | A Stripe minimum balance | Set one in the Dashboard, sized from the computed Engine Expenses floor (4.8): refunds clear at once and Stripe rarely debits the bank; every payout carries hold and release rows. Or none: a card refund can wait for the next sale to settle and a dispute can debit Engine Expenses | Recommended: set one. The build works either way |
| D10 | The 10 percent rule: its formula and its lever | Formula: (a) profit over Eustress's own net revenue, monthly and trailing three months (section 8.7); (b) profit over total net receipts; (c) profit as 10 percent of expenses. Lever when below the floor: (a) alert, block investor returns and reserve rate increases; (b) the same, and pause the funding of the Ticket reserve; (c) the same, and change the 50 percent split, which changes the published rule | Formula (a), lever (a) |
| D11 | Entities as payees | Natural persons only at launch. Or entities (W-8BEN-E, business W-9) with a one-time review of the signer | Natural persons only |
| D12 | The self-declared Full-node 1.1 bonus | Keep it as it is. Require server-observed proof before the bonus applies. Remove it. It bears on whether earnings follow a payee's own work (section 19, item 26) | No default. The answer is required before `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue` (gate G11) |
| D13 | Which account is Checking | The Mercury account id of the operating account, or a statement that one of the two named accounts serves as Checking | Read as a third account |
| D14 | Starting caps and holds | The values of 5.8 and 7.5: first ACH 25 USD, first wire 100 USD, ACH 500 USD, wire 2,000 USD, run 2,000 USD, five first payments a day, cooling 72 hours, first payout hold 14 days. Lower is safer and slower | As listed |
| D15 | May a filing vendor receive TINs | No: the offline tool and the IRIS portal, one sitting a year. Yes: a vendor adapter behind `TaxFiler` for TIN matching, state filings and mailed copies | No |
| D16 | Where Mercury is read from | The Worker, with the narrowest read token: detection stays independent of the relay; a Worker compromise can read bank data from Mercury. Or the relay, behind its allowlist: no bank data reachable from the Worker; the outflow check then depends on the relay it watches | The Worker |
| D17 | The Ticket reserve at launch | `shadow`: numbers only. `funding`: the 15 percent earmark moves into Bliss Treasury from the first sale. Payout terms wait for counsel either way | `shadow` |
| D18 | An entity tax set-aside | Show a target and subtract it from "safe to move from Engine Expenses". Or show nothing | Show and subtract |
| D19 | A second administrator or independent reviewer | Name a person who receives every announcement, can cancel a delayed act, and reviews the month close, a cap raise and the year-end filing. Or stay alone: administrator acts then wait for the next Arizona business morning at least 12 hours out, and the relay's out-of-band channel is the second witness | No default; alone until named |
| D20 | Who bears a currency conversion fee on a wire | The payee, as a disclosed percentage. Or Eustress | Decided when the sandbox shows whether wires leave in USD |
| D21 | The foreign-services certification | A standing certification at W-8 signing with a duty to report US work. Or a click every payout month | Standing |
| D22 | The fallback if Mercury refuses the use case | Another bank with an ACH and wire API under Eustress's own KYC. A payouts provider that accepts reliance on Eustress's KYC. No international payouts until one exists | No default; `bank_adapter.mjs` is the seam |
| D23 | Stripe's default bank if a savings account cannot be debited | Keep Engine Expenses with a Stripe minimum balance. Or point Stripe's default at Checking. Stripe's prefunded balance is not offered: the book keeps no account for a second Stripe balance (4.8) | Keep Engine Expenses |
| D24 | Public figures | Whether `platform_usd` appears on the public `/api/payouts/rate` and `/api/ledger/summary`, given that it is real bank money. And how exact the public pool figures are: (a) rounded to two significant digits, which keeps the pool series from showing who is enrolled (5.1); (b) exact to the cent, which is more transparent and lets an observer work out enrollment from the series | `platform_usd` does not appear on public routes; (a) |
| D25 | Who bears a refund | Both halves in proportion. Or Eustress alone | Both halves in proportion |
| D26 | Where paid checkout stays open before the accountant rules on sales tax and VAT | (a) Keep selling everywhere that sells today and carry the accrued exposure, which comes out of Eustress's half and shows in the admin view. (b) Close paid checkout outside the United States and in Arizona until gate G9 closes, by marking those places closed in `sales_regions.json`. The EU and the UK have no registration threshold for a seller established elsewhere, and Arizona nexus exists from the first Arizona sale | (a): a stream that sells today is never closed by a default. McKale records his choice before the step 3 production deploy |
| D27 | The treasury gift checkout in production | (a) Keep it open, as it is today, while counsel answers the gift question and Stripe confirms the use. (b) Turn `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE` off until gate G10 closes; the stream stays built and tested meanwhile. A gift is money taken from one person and passed whole to others with nothing sold, which is the shape counsel's money-transmission analysis and Stripe's crowdfunding rules look at | (a), for the same reason. McKale records his choice before the step 3 production deploy |
| D28 | Ticket checkout while counsel's stored-value question is open (gate G12) | (a) Keep Ticket checkout open wherever it sells today, with the wallet value limit and the daily load cap enforced. (b) Close it, everywhere or in named places, by `sell_tickets` in `sales_regions.json`, until counsel answers. If counsel later answers that Tickets are not closed loop, every Ticket sale made in the meantime was a sale of stored value without a licence, and nothing in the code would have stopped it | (a), for the same reason, with the risk stated here in plain words. McKale records his choice before the step 3 production deploy |
| D29 | What a large gift costs in tied-up cash | A gift whose pool part is 1,000.00 USD or more needs its own amount held in Engine Expenses as dispute cover until the deposit is 120 days old, counted from the deposit's date and not from its sweep. (a) Leave it deferred: the gift waits unswept until Engine Expenses happens to hold the cover, and at 120 days at the latest it is swept with none. (b) Fund the cover: McKale keeps that much of Eustress's own money idle in Engine Expenses so the gift reaches the pool at once | (a); the alert names the figure each time so (b) is one transfer away |

### 17.2 Acts, in the order that unblocks the build

Items 1 to 10 start at once, except item 4, which has its own date.

1. **Email Mercury** (api@mercury.com) with the exact flow (Stripe revenue into Engine Expenses, an internal transfer to Bliss Treasury after each payout, weekly ACH and monthly wires to KYC-verified contributors paid as Eustress's own obligation, recipients created by API through hosted invites) and the questions of section 21, including the debit block and receive-only number for Bliss Treasury and a per-token daily limit. The written yes is gate G1 and blocks enrollment as well as payouts.
2. **Engage counsel** with section 19. The memo is gate G2.
3. **Engage the accountant** with section 18. The signed rules file is gate G3. Counsel and the accountant are given each other's questions and close the value-line relationship together.
4. **Apply for the IRIS Transmitter Control Code well before the first filing season; not urgent this week.** Allow 45 days for the code. IRIS is the only e-file system for information returns from January 1, 2027. Eustress's first returns are for the first tax year in which it pays a contributor, which is tax year 2027, due in early 2028, since no contributor was paid in 2026. Apply once the payer identity is settled with the accountant (section 18, item 10), and sooner if payouts begin and a return is owed sooner, or if the accountant finds a return owed for 2026 to one of Eustress's own vendors (section 18, item 14). November 19, 2026, the last filing day of the older FIRE system, is not Eustress's date: it has never filed through FIRE.
5. **Add a second Mercury user** with send-money permission (D2). Set Mercury's approval rules so that user's own dashboard payments need McKale's approval.
6. **Read in Mercury and report:** the kind (checking or savings), partner bank and live ACH and wire limits of ****0474 and ****8817; which account is Checking; whether ****0474 accepts ACH debits from Stripe and how many transfers a cycle it allows; the current auto-transfer rules.
7. **Decide the items of 17.1**, and tell the sessions that hold modified or untracked finance files to commit them or hand them over: a clean tree is the precondition of step 1 (16.1).
8. **Create a Mercury sandbox organization and a Stripe sandbox** for staging. Create the outside resources each step's bindings name, before that step completes, and tell the orchestrator, who alone writes `wrangler.toml`: both `FINANCE_BACKUPS` buckets (production and staging) before step 2; the payments origin (a DNS name such as `pay.eustress.dev` with its own Pages project), the relay's Fly app, its tunnel and the Workers VPC service, with its service id, before step 4.
9. **Operator security:** hardware second factor on Cloudflare, Fly.io, Mercury, Stripe and GitHub; a separate machine or operating-system user for deploys, with no standing `fly` or `wrangler` login on the daily workstation; a Fly organization that holds only the relay; an offline machine for the tax key; Cloudflare roles that can edit Tunnels, Zero Trust, Access or the VPC service held by McKale alone; read-only tokens for the deploy tripwire host scoped to Workers Scripts read, Pages read and a read-only view of the relay's Fly organization, and nothing else; a hardware second factor on the chat provider behind `ALERT_WEBHOOK`.
10. **Enroll in EFTPS** on Checking (or set up the IRS business account). It takes about a week by mail and the first deposit can be due on the 15th of the month after the first withholding.
11. **Stripe, live (all his writes):** create the restricted key of 3.4 and set it as `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`; add the webhook events of 3.4, before the step 3 production deploy and with `charge.refunded` left subscribed; read the account's API version and give it as `STRIPE_API_VERSION`; set the payout statement descriptor to 10 characters or fewer; set a minimum balance if D9 says so; turn off customer information updates in the billing portal's default configuration (7.7); create Stripe Tax registrations where the accountant says; leave the payout schedule daily automatic; confirm the default external account is Engine Expenses and give its id as `STRIPE_EE_BANK_ID`. Record decisions D26, D27 and D28 before the step 3 production deploy.
12. **Mercury read side:** create the read token with the narrowest scopes offered; `wrangler secret put MERCURY_READ_TOKEN` (and `--env staging` with the sandbox token); set the three account id vars; create the Mercury webhook endpoint and put its secret.
13. **Generate keys:** the tax key pair on the offline machine, with the script step 6 ships (public half and its fingerprint into the repository, private half offline in two places); the relay signing pair (private half into the Worker, public half into the relay); `BANK_FP_KEY`; the relay response pair (generated on the relay; its public half into the repository, pinned by key id); `SESSION_SIGNING_KEY`, put after the step 6 deploy, with `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER` set to that time in the same sitting; `JWT_SECRET` is never removed or rotated, because the evidence vault key and other keys derive from it (12.5, 14); `BACKUP_ENC_KEY` (a copy offline). Set `FINANCE_ADMINS` to his own account id and key fingerprint.
14. **Relay:** before step 4, create the two Fly.io apps in their own organization, allocate the egress pairs, deploy the reviewed image with a short-lived token and with no Mercury token set, verify the digest that Fly's API reports against the reviewed commit, read the observed egress addresses from the health route; create the tunnel as a locally managed tunnel and set its credential as a relay secret; set the volume's snapshot retention to 14 days. Start the deploy tripwire and record the digests it compares against.
15. **Mercury write side, each token in the week of its first live use:** the send token (custom scopes) with the two addresses allowlisted, when the live sweep is ready; the request token, created by the second user, when the first wire is ready. `fly secrets set` each with the account ids and caps. Keep `RELAY_ARMED` false until the staging gate passes.
16. **Out-of-band channel:** set `ALERT_WEBHOOK` to a channel only he reads, on an account with a hardware second factor and a 90 day history limit (15.4); save each month's recipient announcements to the offline machine at the month close; turn on Mercury's own payment notifications.
17. **Automations:** leave the floor the system reports in Engine Expenses; put no automation on Bliss Treasury.
18. **Deploy each step** after Security's review, record the version for the tripwire, and flip each switch: `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` at the step 3 deploy; `BOOK_SCHEMA_TARGET=3` at step 4, where the cutover runs on a morning with no row pending on any Mercury account (12.1); then `SWEEP_MODE`, `ENROLLMENT_MODE`, `PAYOUTS_MODE`, `WALLET_LOTS_LIVE`, later the gated ones. Record the step 1 version as the rollback floor, and move the floor to the step 3, step 4 and step 6 versions as he sets `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE`, runs the cutover and sets `SESSION_KEY_CUTOVER` (15.2), and before each production deploy compare the script hash with the second-machine build (15.4).
19. **Prove the chain on himself (gate G7):** enroll under `ENROLLMENT_MODE=staff`, export his own tax envelope and open it offline, receive one ACH, cause one return. Then one supervised wire before wires open to payees.
20. **Connect:** tell the three account holders, then delete or remove the three accounts (section 11).
21. **Compliance paper:** sign the one-page sanctions policy and the written ACH fraud-monitoring procedure, each with a yearly review date; after the first filing season, enroll in IRS TIN Matching.
22. **Recurring acts, none daily:** approve the international batch once a month against a manifest issued in that sitting, rejecting anything on the do-not-approve list; reject unapproved requests by December 20, and in the same sitting confirm that next year's tax rules are activated; write an expense rule for each new card or pulled-debit vendor the outflow alert names, and plan each vendor payment he sends from Checking the day before, or classify it after its alert; recategorise expenses, download the month's Bliss Treasury transaction export from the Mercury dashboard and carry it to the offline machine, run the offline journal replay and the roster comparison, send the month's notice from his own mailbox to the email on each new recipient's customer's earliest paid plan invoice dated before the enrollment, list every such customer whose current billing email differs from it, read each new recipient and each jump the tool lists against the relay's announcement and a paid plan invoice in the Stripe Dashboard, and lock the month; open the week's fresh TIN envelopes on the offline machine while enrollment is open; deposit withheld tax by the 15th of the following month when any exists; mail B notices and paper year-end forms when any exist; run the tax key drill each quarter; run the year-end tool and upload to IRIS once a year; add next year's rules each November; replace a Mercury token when its warning arrives; act on a failed Stripe payout in the Dashboard; raise caps when volume warrants; run the restore drill and the sanctions test once a year; file the OFAC annual report if anything is blocked on June 30.

---

## 18. Questions for the accountant

Blocking items first. Counsel receives this list, and the accountant receives section 19: the signed rules file and counsel's memo cite each other and close together (gates G2 and G3).

1. **Character of each income line.** The effort share of the drip, the value share (earned from sales of a creator's own content), later the Tickets line, bounties, any prize or bonus: nonemployee compensation (Form 1099-NEC), royalty (Form 1099-MISC box 2) or other income? Every row keeps the effort and value parts apart so either answer can be applied.
2. **Foreign payees: source under that character, per line.** For each line, given its character: is it sourced where the work is done (services), where the property is used (royalty), or by the payer's residence (a prize, an award or other fixed income paid by a US payer, which is US source wherever the recipient lives)? If any line is US source for a foreign payee: is the basis the US-buyer slice or the whole line; may Eustress withhold only on that basis; and is the buyer's recorded country an acceptable sourcing method for a royalty? The engine supports every one of these answers. If the answer makes every foreign payee a Form 1042-S recipient, international payouts launch with that branch on. If any line is US source, name its Form 1042-S income code in `tax_rules.{year}.json` (for a royalty line, which of 10, 11 and 12), and sign `treaty_table.{year}.json`: for each treaty country, income character and income code, the article, the paragraph and the rate. The engine selects the row by the payee's country of residence and the line's own character and income code, takes the rate from that row and never from a payee's entry, prints the line's income code on every Form 1042-S, and withholds 30 percent for a country with no row.
3. **Evidence of foreign source, and Form 8233.** Is a standing certification, plus a source stamp on each day's services earnings taken from the payee's sign-in location on that score date, enough, or is a statement per payment needed? The design treats the cents of a US-located or reported day as US source in whatever month they are paid: confirm that, and that the latest sign-in on or before the day is an acceptable proxy for where the work was done. Confirm that a treaty claim on US-performed services needs Form 8233, and the procedure for accepting and filing one.
4. **When a payment is made.** At accrual, at maturity or when cash leaves Mercury? Does a matured payable the person could collect create constructive receipt? The design reports, and dates the withholding liability, by bank posting date.
5. **Gross or net.** Confirm that forms report the gross before the 15.00 USD wire fee, any conversion fee and any backup withholding, and that the fee recovered offsets Eustress's wire expense. Confirm also that a wire fee Mercury did not charge, returned to the payee, lowers the reported gross of that payment and is reported when it is paid.
6. **Third party settlement organization.** Is Eustress one for creator earnings (Form 1099-K), or always a payor of its own obligations? This answer and counsel's money-transmission position must be consistent (item 26).
7. **Backup withholding.** Confirm: that for services, royalties and other income the only triggers are a missing TIN and an IRS notice of an incorrect TIN, so a payee's own item 2 certification withholds only on interest and dividends; the floor for services and none for royalties; that the floor is tested on the payee's aggregate for the year including the payment that crosses it, and whether the whole crossing payment is withheld; that the prior-year test is per payee; the deposit rule (monthly from the first withholding, by the 15th); and whether withheld cash should sit in its own bank account.
8. **Thresholds.** 2,000 USD for 2026 and 2,100 USD for 2027; royalties 10 USD.
9. **Returns and netting.** A payment returned in a later tax year than it was reported; a return of a payment that carried withholding (the design takes back only the cash that returned, so the gross left equals the withholding); a wire that returns at a different dollar value; a clawback of prior-year income netted from this year's earnings (the design reports the netted amount as paid); a duplicate payment that could not be recalled (the design books it as a payment to that payee when it posts, and nets from later earnings whatever the payable did not cover); a returned payment that carried no withholding and is repaid after the payee's status changed (the design judges the repayment on the documentation then held): booking, reporting and who bears each difference.
10. **Payer identity.** The LLC's tax classification, and the legal name and EIN on Forms 1099, 945, 1042 and the IRIS application.
11. **Year-one TIN diligence** without IRS TIN Matching: is format validation plus the KYC name match enough for reasonable cause, given that each envelope is opened and checked offline within about a week and a payee's payments stay under the reporting threshold until then? May B notices be delivered electronically? May a W-8BEN filer with no TIN and no treaty claim be paid on a US-source line at the full 30 percent, or must a TIN be on file first? The design holds that line at zero until an envelope passes.
12. **States.** Which states require a direct filing for payees living there, at what thresholds; whether any state requires backup or nonresident withholding by an Arizona payer; confirmation that nothing is owed to Arizona.
13. **A payee whose status changes mid-year** (W-8 to W-9 or the reverse), and a payee who dies: forms and payee of record. The design applies to every cent of a payment the certificate on file when the payment is made, whenever the cents were earned, and a switch review first sets the source of unpaid rows earned under the earlier status (US work days for a former W-9 filer). Confirm both.
14. **Eustress's own vendors.** Which need a W-9 on file and a Form 1099-NEC (counsel, the accountant, contractors).
15. **Eustress's income tax.** Estimated payment calendar, federal and Arizona; a set-aside rate; provision entries.
16. **Sales tax and VAT (blocks paid checkout outside the United States and in Arizona, gate G9).** For each product separately (Tickets as prepaid value, Delta, plans): is it taxable in Arizona, in other US states, in the EU, in the UK and elsewhere; which registrations are needed before the first sale there (the EU non-Union One Stop Shop, UK VAT, Arizona transaction privilege tax); should Stripe Tax be on; which places may be sold to without collection and at what exposure. Is the tax on a refunded sale reversed in proportion (the design debits 2300 for it), and is the tax on a lost dispute recoverable?
17. **Revenue recognition.** Cash or accrual basis; prepaid Tickets and Delta as revenue at sale or deferred revenue with breakage; the 1,000 Delta trial; plan grants on zero-revenue invoices.
18. **The pool and the reserve.** Liabilities or designated equity; deductible when funded or when paid.
19. **Fees and losses.** Booking of the gift fee, the fee Stripe keeps on a refund, dispute fees, a chargeback after a refund (account 4910), negative payouts, `return_loss_cents`.
20. **Interest** earned on the Bliss Treasury balance: the book treats it as Eustress income. Counsel is asked the same question from the other side (section 19, item 30); the default stands until both answer.
21. **The 10 percent rule.** Does the formula of 8.7 match what the books can prove; is labor a real expense line?
22. **Investments.** For a note: liability and interest, the reporting form and threshold for a private note, backup withholding on interest. For a unit: what entity classification results and from what date; what replaces Form 1099-INT (Form 1065, Schedule K-1, section 1446 withholding on a foreign holder, state composite filings); what holder data must be collected at subscription; and what happens to the payer identity of item 10 in the year the classification changes. A return is paid from an operating account and its withholding is a payable on the operating side, 2045 for backup withholding and 2046 for a foreign holder (section 9), deposited with the contributors' withholding on the Form 945 or the Form 1042 clock: confirm that booking.
23. **The Ticket reserve yardstick** that values the Tickets part of a plan invoice.
24. **Is BLS itself reportable**, given it is non-transferable, cannot be bought and only burns?
25. **Export format** for the trial balance and general ledger that the tax software takes, and the tie from book income to the return.
26. **The legal relationship behind the value line (asked of counsel too).** Is a creator's value earning (a) Eustress's payment for a license of the creator's content, which Eustress sells as principal; (b) the proceeds of an agency sale Eustress makes for the creator; or (c) a discretionary bonus Eustress pays by formula? That one answer fixes character, source, third party settlement status and the money-transmission analysis. The design stores each creator's US-buyer slice; say whether keeping that trace is consistent with the answer.
27. **Form 1042.** How Form 1042 itself is filed when 10 or more returns are filed (approved software or the IRIS portal), and the deposit schedule for a first-year filer.
28. **Gifts and sponsorships.** Are treasury gifts and engine sponsorships gross income to Eustress, and is the amount designated to the pool deductible when designated or when paid?
29. **Tax found under-withheld after payment.** When a late US-work report shows cents already paid to a W-8 filer to be US source, the design reports them on Form 1042-S for the year paid, withholds the tax from the payee's next payments (Reg. 1.1461-2(b)) and bears what is unrecovered by the Form 1042 due date. Confirm that, on which year's Form 1042-S the recovered tax is reported, and how Form 1042 is amended for the year paid.

---

## 19. Questions for counsel

The accountant receives this list and counsel receives section 18.

**Money transmission and banking (a written memo before `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue`):**

1. Is Eustress LLC a money transmitter under 31 CFR 1010.100(ff)(5), or under Arizona's or any other state's law, when it sells as principal through Stripe, books the proceeds as its own revenue and pays contributors a pooled formula share from its own Mercury account? If any slice is transmission, which exclusion covers it? Which states and countries are open for payouts at launch, and which are blocked for sales or for payouts? The answer becomes `payout_regions.json`.
2. What contract and interface wording keeps a payable an unsecured obligation of Eustress and never funds held for others, given Mercury's terms against using the account for third parties? Is "payout of earnings" the right name? Do the shadow-mode estimates and their wording create any claim, and may a personal estimate be shown at all before the program opens (gate G13)?
3. Is BLS a convertible virtual currency under FinCEN guidance? Does any public statistic read as a price or a return?
4. **Tickets as stored value (gate G12).** Are Tickets closed-loop prepaid access under 31 CFR 1010.100(ff)(4)(iii)(A), and within A.R.S. 6-1201's "goods or services provided by the issuer or its affiliate", when third-party creators list the goods and receive 70 percent of the Tickets? The federal exclusion caps the value that can be associated with a wallet on any day at 2,000 USD, which is a ceiling on value held, not on value loaded: the design enforces it at purchase (`TICKET_WALLET_MAX_USD_CENTS`) and limits loads to 1,000 USD a day. What must happen when earned Tickets alone take a creator's wallet past the ceiling: refuse the sale, hold the excess outside the wallet, or something else? Until this is answered Ticket checkout stays open where it sells today unless McKale closes it (decision D28); say plainly whether it should be closed meanwhile. Would a Ticket cash-out break the closed loop?
5. Nacha: is Eustress the Originator and not a Third-Party Sender; what authorization record must be kept and for how long; does the written fraud-monitoring procedure (cooling, first payment cap, fingerprint, caps, tripwires) meet the rule in force since June 2026; what SEC code applies to credits to consumer accounts; who owns a Notification of Change when Mercury holds the recipient?
6. May a W-8 filer ever be paid by domestic ACH to a US account under the international ACH definition in force since 2026-09-18?
7. Is netting a clawback against unpaid and future earnings enforceable, is the set-off of a duplicate payment against the same payee's unpaid and later earnings enforceable, and is 120 days the right maturity for value earnings?
8. The wire fee deduction, the fee on each wire of a balance a cap splits, the minimums and the warning that the amount received can be lower: the disclosure and consent that make them enforceable; whether any destination country requires local registration.
9. Bank account ownership: who bears a payout sent to an account the payee enrolled that is not theirs, or mistyped; what the terms must say; whether an account validation step is required. When two accounts enroll the same bank account, the design decides ownership on a bank statement header or bank letter in the claimant's KYC legal name with the same last four digits, viewed and recorded by case reference and not stored (5.4): is that evidence sufficient, and may Eustress ask for it?
10. Unclaimed property: payables below the minimum, undeliverable payouts, dormant holds, unspent Tickets; the dormancy clock per state, the due-diligence letter, the remittance. May a banned account's payable return to the pool? An outside credit on Bliss Treasury that cannot be returned to its sender is moved to Checking and held as a payable to that sender (8.6): what becomes of it?
11. Legal process: the procedure for an IRS levy, a garnishment, a bankruptcy stay and a deceased payee's estate against a payable.
12. Mercury's terms: does paying contributors by API fit "paying your own obligations"; what must McKale tell Mercury; does keeping a bank write credential on Fly.io meet Mercury's and an insurer's conditions?

**Tax:**

13. Do the substitute W-9 and W-8BEN screens and the key-signature method, in which the signed payload carries a salted commitment to the TIN, meet the electronic form and signature rules?
14. Which KYC indicia defeat a W-8, and what cure is accepted?
15. Entities as payees: support at launch or hold back?

**Sanctions:**

16. Is the program of 5.5 a defensible risk-based program for a company this size? Should access itself be geo-blocked, not only payout? Should Kherson, Zaporizhzhia, Russia or Belarus be blocked as policy? Eustress holds the document name, date of birth and issuing country of every verified account, buyers and sellers as well as payees. Is screening only payees defensible, or should every account be screened at verification and on each list change (`SANCTIONS_SCREEN_SCOPE`)? No second identity check is involved either way.
17. When a true match has an accrued payable: is it blocked property to hold and report within 10 business days and in the annual report; must it move to a separate blocked, interest-bearing account at a US institution, and who opens that account; what must the terms say in advance? Which of the blocked person's Wallet Tickets (bought, plan-granted and earned), Delta lots and paid plan are blocked property to inventory and report beside the payable; what is done with the subscription, which the code neither cancels nor refunds; must a renewal charge received after the block be blocked or rejected; and what record supports an `unblock`?
18. If Mercury's screening asks for a recipient's identity document, may Eustress forward the one it holds, and under what consent?

**Securities (before `INVESTMENTS_ENABLED`, before any wording):**

19. Is the treasury investment a note, an investment contract or both; which exemption fits (Rule 506(b), Rule 506(c), Regulation Crowdfunding); what may any surface say before a filing?
20. Must investor principal sit in its own bank account and not in a sub-ledger of Bliss Treasury? How are returns worded and ranked against contributor payouts? Is the 10 percent rule disclosed? What do the terms say about a receipt that arrives short of the subscribed amount?
21. Do gifts taken through Stripe Checkout stay outside the securities laws once an investment exists beside them, and does Stripe's restricted-business list allow either?

**Privacy and incidents:**

22. The retention and deletion schedule for tax profiles, payment records, screening inputs, sign-in location records and KYC images, including rejected and abandoned sessions; GDPR and UK GDPR basis and transfer terms; the Arizona SSN statute.
23. Breach notification duties if TIN ciphertext, tax profiles or identity data leave the system.
24. Notice or record duties toward the three Connect account holders for identity data given to Stripe.
25. Terms of service sections 2, 5 and 7 and the payout, tax, fee, hold, suspension and plan clauses.

**The contributor program itself (blocks `PAYOUTS_MODE=accrue`, gate G2):**

26. **Is the program a security or a regulated business opportunity?** Locked decision 8 makes a paid plan the condition for a pro rata share of a pool that is 50 percent of company net revenue, funded by other people's purchases and gifts. Is a paid plan on those terms an investment contract or a "certificate of interest or participation in any profit-sharing agreement" under federal law and A.R.S. 44-1801(27), or a business opportunity under the FTC rule and Arizona law? What facts keep it outside: the independent, consumptive value of the plan; earnings tied to the payee's own work; no earnings figure, pool balance or payout example near a plan purchase; the removal or proof of passive scoring such as presence time and the self-declared Full-node bonus (decision D12)? Until answered, plan and pricing pages carry no payout figure. May a personal estimate be shown at all before the program opens, and to whom? The design computes one only for accounts that already hold a qualifying plan and never shows it on a surface that carries a plan link or price (gate G13).
27. **May payment of an accrued payable wait on a current subscription** (decision D3, option a)? Consumer protection, wage and unclaimed property law: does it read as a fee to collect money already owed, and does the answer differ for an amount below one month's plan price?
28. **The gift stream.** Nothing is sold; 100 percent of the gross goes to the contributor pool; Eustress bears the Stripe fee; the page says the whole amount is credited. Is that receiving money for transmission under FinCEN's definition or A.R.S. 6-1201; third-party funds under Mercury's terms; crowdfunding for a for-profit under Stripe's rules? Would this alternative be sound: the gift is revenue of Eustress, and Eustress by its own published policy designates an equal amount to the pool, with copy that makes no pass-through promise to the donor? What disclosure is required, including that a gift is not tax deductible? This is gate G10.
29. **Platform reporting outside the United States.** Does EU DAC7, the UK's platform reporting rule or another adopter of the OECD model rules reach Eustress for creator or effort earnings paid to residents there? The answer is a condition of enabling each country in `wire_countries.json`.
30. **Interest on Bliss Treasury.** The book treats it as Eustress income. Would crediting it to the pool support a reading that the account holds funds for others?
31. **Worker classification.** Can paying people by formula for time spent (the effort line) make any of them employees, or create state wage duties, under federal and Arizona law?
32. **The legal relationship behind the value line** (section 18, item 26, asked of both): a license to Eustress which sells as principal, an agency sale for the creator, or a discretionary bonus. The contributor terms state whichever relationship counsel and the accountant agree on.
33. **Cancelling a plan.** The billing portal opens only after a signature by the account key on the payments origin (3.5), because the plan gates cash-out and the portal shows billing data. Does that step meet the rule that cancelling be as easy as subscribing, or must a cancel-only control stay open to any session?

---

## 20. Assumptions resting on unverified research

Each is isolated behind a setting, an adapter or a gate, so a wrong assumption changes configuration, not structure.

| Assumption | If wrong | Checked by |
|---|---|---|
| Mercury approves the use case, including recipients created by API through hosted invites | No enrollment and no contributor payouts from Mercury; `bank_adapter.mjs` is the seam; decision D22 | Gate G1 |
| International wires need a dashboard approval by a user who is not the token creator, and no bulk approval exists | Fewer clicks; `WIRE_MODE=direct` if granted | Mercury's answer |
| A custom send token sends ACH with no approval hold, and no per-user or per-token daily send limit turns a send into an approval item | Designed for either way: every ACH read includes the send-money request list, a match is `awaiting_approval`, and a void needs zero open requests | Sandbox; Mercury's answer (Q9) |
| `POST /transfer`, recipient invites and wire requests work from the relay's tokens | Routes move between relay and Worker | McKale at token creation; sandbox |
| A repeated `/transfer` key is safe, and the note of an internal transfer appears on the BT credit | Neither is relied on alone: the relay looks up by exact note before sending, and the Worker's resolver also matches by amount inside the time window on both accounts | Sandbox, step 5 |
| Internal transfers settle within a business day, and both legs can be read | The installment waits for both rows; a leg that fails shows as a return credit on EE; the aging threshold changes | Sandbox; a 1 USD live test |
| A Stripe payout appears in Mercury with a tracking number equal to Stripe's `trace_id`, or a recognisable counterparty | The match falls to amount and date; equal amounts pair oldest to oldest, so no day waits for a person | The first real payout in shadow mode |
| The balance transactions of a payout, less its own row, sum to its amount, minimum-balance rows included; a dispute fee appears on the dispute's row or as its own row | The tie-out formula is adjusted before any sweep runs | Stripe sandbox, step 3 and step 4 |
| A failed automatic payout is recorded by its own balance transaction (`failure_balance_transaction`, category `payout_reversal`) that rides a later payout, while its original rows stay attached to the failed payout id | The carry-forward rule of 4.3 is revisited; no sweep meanwhile | Stripe sandbox with the failing test bank `000111111116`, step 4 |
| A dispute object lists a balance transaction exactly when funds have been withdrawn, and an inquiry lists none | The booking trigger moves to `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn` | Stripe sandbox, step 3 |
| `GET /v1/balance_settings` works on Eustress's own account at a GA version | The drift monitor uses the Dashboard read McKale makes | Sandbox |
| Engine Expenses (savings) accepts Stripe debits and enough outgoing transfers | The plan of 4.8; decision D23 | McKale's dashboard read; Mercury's answer |
| Bliss Treasury may originate ACH and wires at the needed volume, and can carry a debit block | A limit increase, or the payout source changes; without a block, `external_debit` and the dispute deadline are the control | McKale's dashboard read; Mercury's answer |
| The hosted invite can be completed only through the link, lets the relay read the recipient's bank details, and lets the recipient name be compared | The fallback capture of 5.4 for ACH; the fingerprint check degrades to a recipient-id check | Sandbox, step 5 |
| Mercury's `currentBalance` moves only when a transaction posts, and `availableBalance` reflects pending debits | Not relied on: the wall equation carries the pending classes Mercury deducts early (`WALL_PENDING_CLASSES`), and a difference that tracked pending rows explain is never a shortfall | Sandbox where it simulates status changes; otherwise the gate G7 payment and the first supervised wire |
| Mercury's transaction list can lag a transaction just created | Already designed for: the relay's caps take the larger of its own ledger and the list | Sandbox |
| API wires leave in USD and cost 15.00 USD with `chargeType: "ours"` | The fee setting and decision D20 change | Sandbox; the first supervised wire |
| When and on which account Mercury bills a wire fee | Nothing is assumed: clearing clears only on evidence across all three accounts | The first supervised wire |
| A returned ACH shows as a status change on the original, as a linked credit, or as both; a returned wire may come back as an unlinked incoming credit; `reasonForFailure` carries the return code | All four are handled: the fourth is classified `payment_return_unlinked` under five bank facts and holds settlement meanwhile | Sandbox; Mercury's answer |
| Stripe sends `account.external_account.updated` for the platform's own account, and a restricted key can read external accounts | Neither is relied on: every payout's own `destination` is compared with the Engine Expenses bank id at ingest | Stripe sandbox, step 4 |
| Mercury's recipient and transaction objects expose every delivery field of a wire (`countrySpecific`, `correspondentInfo`, `bankDetails`) to the relay | The fingerprint covers what is exposed; a country whose delivery fields are not all readable stays `disabled` in `wire_countries.json` | Sandbox, before step 10 |
| The Worker's read token can read a recipient invite by id | The relay's signed answer alone vouches for the invite | Sandbox, step 5 |
| The originator name on an incoming wire is the sending bank's record of its customer | Investment funding needs a further proof of the funding account before the offer opens | Mercury's answer; securities counsel |
| Fly keeps daily volume snapshots for the configured retention | The relay's first-seen records are rebuilt from Mercury's history and the announcement log alone | Step 5 kill-switch drill |
| A second origin can be served with its own enforced headers and its own bundle | The payments pages are served by a Worker route with the same policy; the policy test reads that route's response and the tripwire's live read is unchanged | Step 4 |
| The sign-up, sign-in, email, passkey and key replacement pages can move to the payments bundle whole, and a one-time code can hand a session to the main site with no shared storage | The affected flow stays on the payments origin end to end and the main site links to it; no identity-file code returns to the main bundle | Step 4 |
| A passkey registered for the site works on the payments origin (the relying party id covers the subdomain) | Sign-in on the payments origin is by identity file only | Step 4 |
| KV `KYC_STATUS` records hold the document-read name (`grok_analysis.extracted_name`, `name_matches`) for most verified accounts | More accounts go through the narrow reverification before they can enroll | The backfill's audit, step 6 |
| Mercury answers a repeated idempotency key on a direct send with 409 and the existing transaction for at least 20 hours | The look-up before every send and the read-first rule already stand behind every resend | Mercury's answer on key retention |
| `request-send-money` honours a repeated key | Not relied on: the wire rail resolves and counts against the request list, and the fake ignores the key | Sandbox, before step 10 |
| A send-money request can be listed by account and status, and carries the recipient and amount | The resolver falls back to the request ids the relay recorded in its ledger | Sandbox, before step 10 |
| Mercury's API rate limits allow a 10 minute poll with a quiescent read of three accounts | `BANK_POLL_MINUTES` rises; the detection window grows with it | Mercury's answer |
| Workers VPC stays available and affordable | Tunnel plus Access; the signature is unchanged | Built and tested both ways |
| Fly.io egress addresses are stable and its prices are as quoted; a deploy token can be limited to one hour | The allowlist is updated; the cost line changes; the deploy procedure of 15.4 is adjusted | Health route; the invoice |
| The 2,000 and 2,100 USD thresholds, the form layouts, IRIS filing for the 1099 series and Form 1042-S, IRIS as the only e-file system from January 1, 2027, 45 day TCC processing (the last two read on irs.gov on 2026-11-12) | Rules files and exporter templates are per year; the application date of gate G5 moves with the processing time | IRS pages each January, and before the application |
| IRS TIN Matching is unavailable before a first filing | If available sooner, the offline tool already writes its bulk file | McKale's application |
| The character and source of each income line | Every answer of 6.4 is supported; a line stays closed to foreign payees until it is ruled on | Accountant, with counsel |
| A bank statement header or bank letter in the claimant's legal name is obtainable and is enough to decide a destination conflict | The review falls back to counsel's rule; both destinations stay as they are meanwhile | Counsel (section 19, item 9) |
| OFAC's territory list is as configured (Syria list-based since 2025), its list endpoints stay public, and its exports (tens of megabytes; the measured sizes are entered in this row at the first staging sync) parse inside the streaming buffer bound | Dated configuration; list age and the acceptance checks block sends | Counsel; the daily sync; the full-size parse test |
| The sandbox does not simulate returns, approvals or webhooks | Those paths are proven on fakes and on the supervised first payments of steps 9 and 10 | Step 5 |
| Checking is a third Mercury account, and EFTPS can be enrolled on it | One account id fewer; deposits are made from the account EFTPS accepts and `taxdep` records which | Decision D13; McKale's enrollment |
| Stripe's balance, its payouts in flight and the three Mercury balances can be read at one quiescent instant with no row pending on a Mercury account, and a balance transaction's `created` time orders it against that instant | The genesis read is taken again until it is quiescent; the cutover waits and the pre-journal path keeps serving | The staging cutover, step 4 |
| Production holds no hold from the Connect rail at cutover | Any hold found is converted to a payable first (section 11, item 4) | `holds` and `snapshot` read before step 4 and step 7 |
| Stripe states `created` on every session and subscription, and nobody can create one with an earlier stamp | Objects older than the records are listed once from Stripe and written as records by an audited pass | Stripe sandbox, step 3 |
| A Payout object names its own balance transaction from the moment it is created | The row is read at the first listing of balance transactions of type `payout`, and 1010 is late by that interval only | Stripe sandbox, step 4 |
| A direct send that Mercury turns into an approval item keeps the external memo of the send, and a request states when it was created | The memo term is dropped for such an item, and the match rests on recipient, amount, creation time and the rule that a request belongs to one payment | Sandbox; Mercury's answer (Q9) |
| A read-only Fly token for the relay's organization reads the Machine's image digest and no secret value | McKale reads the digest on the deploy machine at each deploy, and the tripwire watches the Worker and the site alone | Step 4 |
| An invoice's `customer_email` is fixed when Stripe finalizes it, and a restricted key with Invoices read cannot change it | McKale reads the address from the invoice PDF Stripe rendered at payment, which no later edit of the customer changes | Stripe sandbox, step 4 |
| Mercury's dashboard export of an account's transactions carries each row's note and counterparty | The roster comparison matches on amount, date and counterparty name | The first month close |
| The main site runs under an enforced `script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'` with no inline script | The offending script moves to a file before the policy is enforced; enrollment waits on gate G6 | Step 4 |
| Mercury's transaction `kind` tells a payment Eustress originated (ACH, wire, check, real-time payment) from a debit a vendor pulled and from a card charge, and a pulled debit carries a `counterpartyId` | An unknown kind counts as sent and alerts until it is planned or classified; a pulled debit with no id is matched by name under its ceiling | Sandbox; the first month of Checking rows in shadow mode |
| Stripe Checkout creates the customer under a restricted key that has no write on customers, and the billing portal opens for that customer | The key keeps Customers write, and a `subscription_create` or `subscription_cycle` invoice whose cash paid is below the pinned price less the proration lines on that invoice grants nothing and goes to Suspense | Stripe sandbox, step 3 |
| `GET /v1/checkout/sessions?subscription=` returns the session that created a subscription, under the restricted key | The first invoice of a subscription whose `csub:` is missing answers 500 until the session event has written it | Stripe sandbox, step 3 |
| Stripe's v2 preview reads may change | Nothing that moves money depends on them | n/a |

One point is design, not assumption: Mercury's recipient object carries no creation or modification time, so the relay ages a recipient from the invite's `createdAt` and from its own first-seen record (7.5).
