## 22. Review notes

The design review of 2026-11-12 raised findings against every section, in six rounds. Each is resolved in the text above. The points below are the ones resolved in a different form than the finding proposed, or not adopted, with the reason. Notes 1 to 15 come from the first round, notes 16 to 30 from the second, notes 31 to 42 from the third, notes 43 to 54 from the fourth, notes 55 to 63 from the fifth and notes 64 to 71 from the sixth.

1. **Splitting the build plan into its own file at once.** The change narrative is out of the descriptive sections; sections 11 and 16 are marked as the build plan and name their archive path. They stay in this file while the build runs, because the build order must change in the same commit as the design it implements and every reviewer and session addresses one path. They move when the build completes.
2. **A posting that reverses a failed sweep.** No entry is posted when a sweep is submitted: account 1130 moves only on a bank row. A failed installment therefore has nothing to reverse. `sweepfail` is the marker on the row that returns the money to Engine Expenses, and the `failed` state frees the payouts for the next installment.
3. **Bounding a transfer by the payouts' amounts plus the gift fees.** Superseded by the cumulative bound (4.5), which also covers a day on which a sponsorship refund makes the payout smaller than the pool part.
4. **A signed challenge to remove a destination.** Removal carries no signature: it is allowed from any session and during a freeze or a pin, because it can only stop money. Setting and reconfirming a destination carry the signature.
5. **Halt and cancel as the only undelayed administrator acts.** One more kind of act is undelayed on the same principle, that it can only stop money: setting a legal or estate hold (a levy cannot wait for the next morning). Lifting one is delayed. Blocking a confirmed sanctions match is delayed: `sanctions_hold` stops the money at once, and the block itself starts a reporting clock on a named person. Three acts that move nothing (a manifest request, a key drill record, a manual settlement start) are undelayed as well, and so are the three exports, each encrypted whole to the offline key and streamed in the answer to its signed request. The list is closed and held by the route-policy test, and the undelayed holds are counted per day.
6. **Eustress bearing the fee of a wire its own cap forced.** Locked decision 5 has the recipient bear the wire fee, so the default keeps one fee per wire and discloses the split before the first wire. The alternative is option (b) of decision D5.
7. **`ENROLLMENT_MODE` values.** The values are `off`, `staff` and `live`. The staging environment runs `live` against the sandbox; `staff` exists so McKale can prove the chain on his own account in production (gate G7) before anyone else enrolls.
8. **Carry as a liability account.** Carry is the second cash account of Bliss Treasury (1121, signed), so that every movement of it is ordinary double entry and the book identity reads journal balances only. It has the same effect as a due-to and due-from account.
9. **The gift checkout and paid checkout by region.** Both streams are built in full, as locked decision 1 requires, and neither is closed by a default: `GIFT_CHECKOUT_LIVE` ships true and `sales_regions.json` ships with every selling place open, because both sell in production today and a stream McKale asked for is closed only on his word. Decisions D26 and D27 record his choice, and gates G9 and G10 reopen what he closes. The first round proposed shipping both closed; the second showed that a routine deploy with defaults would then have stopped live revenue, including his own purchases in Arizona.
10. **A wire approved in a later year than it was built.** Of the two remedies proposed, the document takes the first: no request stays open past December 20. Recomputing the tax snapshot at approval is not built.
11. **A large gift disputed after its sweep.** Both proposed remedies are combined: the Engine Expenses floor carries dispute cover for large swept deposits, and a large deposit waits unswept until that cover exists.
12. **The bank wall halt.** Both proposed rules apply together: a wall is compared only at a quiescent read, and a determinate shortfall halts on its second consecutive poll.
13. **The relay's one-ACH-per-recipient rule.** It follows the configured cadence (`CAP_ACH_RECIPIENT_PERIOD`), a week by default, a day under daily cadence and a month under monthly cadence, so the relay never refuses a payment the cadence allows.
14. **"Relay unreachable" as a definite refusal.** Only a request that was never issued is definite (`not_sent`): a signing failure, a spent operation budget, a preflight refusal. Any error once the fetch is issued, a failed connection included, is unknown like any other lost answer, because a Worker cannot prove through a binding or a tunnel that nothing was delivered.
15. **Measured sizes of the OFAC exports.** Not measured in this revision. The row in section 20 receives the figures at the first staging sync, and the full-size parse test enforces the bound in the meantime.
16. **Stripe's prefunded balance.** The finding offered two remedies: give the prefunded balance an account, a wall and a sweep path of its own, or remove it. It is removed from the operating plan (4.8, D23). Account 2400 is gone with it, and 1010 is a signed account.
17. **Investor returns.** Of the two paths offered, the manual path is kept and given its posting: the return leaves an operating account, and its withholding is a payable on the operating side by kind (2045 or 2046), with year totals written by the matched bank row. Returns as lines of the pay runs are not built, because principal and returns go to the account that funded, never to a payout destination.
18. **Investment funding.** Wire only, the first of the two remedies. Fingerprinting inbound transactions on the relay is not built; the relay stays as small as it is.
19. **A subrequest limit reached during a send.** The finding asked for that exception to be classed `not_sent`. The step checks its budget before it issues a call, so the limit is a preflight refusal and leaves no attempt; an exception from a fetch that was issued stays unknown, because a Worker cannot prove a request never left. This agrees with the rule that every transport error is unknown.
20. **A gate on Ticket checkout.** Gate G12 and decision D28 exist, and the wallet value limit is enforced in code. Ticket checkout itself stays open by default, for the reason in note 9, with the consequence of a "not closed loop" answer written into D28. Earned Tickets past the ceiling raise an alert and refuse the wallet further purchases; no sale to a creator is refused until counsel says which remedy applies.
21. **Who may ban.** The ban route stays a moderation route. The remedy is in what a ban can do to money (suspend, never forfeit; announced; counted), not in moving moderation behind the finance key.
22. **Pausing unsigned destination removals.** Past the daily limit an unsigned removal parks the destination instead of removing it. It is not refused, because stopping money must always be possible from any session.
23. **Where the neutral copy ships.** With step 1, not with the journal step, because the guard that stops the old rail is in step 1 and the copy must change in the release that makes it true.
24. **Backing up the relay's volume.** Fly's snapshots, plus three rebuild sources the Worker cannot forge. No second store and no extra credential are added to the relay.
25. **Two findings on `refused_recipient`**, one from the ledger review and one from the build review, are resolved as one change: `refused_wait`, the builder's own timing rules, period tokens, and the first-seen rebuild.
26. **"Skip a payee with any non-terminal payment from an earlier period."** Implemented as any earlier payment whose bank debit has not posted. A posted payment inside its return watch does not block an established payee, or every payee would be paid every other week. The first payment to a destination is the exception: nothing follows it until it settles.
27. **An acknowledged R3 row.** It fires again on every poll. Nothing is suppressed; the unhalt requires the row to be gone.
28. **The payment limit while a TIN envelope is unchecked.** Tied to the lowest reporting threshold of the payee's open lines. Where a line is a royalty that figure is under the ACH minimum, so such a payee is unpaid until the weekly check has passed. That is intended.
29. **The order of steps 2 and 3.** The journal precedes money-in truth, and the old rail is stopped in step 1 rather than with the journal. References to step numbers elsewhere in this document follow that order.
30. **Rate-limiting stop-only acts.** Halt and cancel are never limited, since they are the response to every incident. Legal and estate holds are counted and then delayed, not refused.
31. **A separate key for money and administrator acts.** Of the two ways to keep the account key off the main origin, the document takes the first: every page that creates or reads an identity file is served from the payments origin, and the main site signs in by a hand-off that carries no key. A second key, or a WebAuthn credential bound to the payments origin, is not built: it would itself be enrolled under the account key, so it moves the problem and adds a key lifecycle.
32. **An unproven claim that expires.** A claim on a bank account does not lapse on a timer. An honest payee with small earnings can stay unproven for months, and an expiry would hand that payee's claim to whoever enrolled the same numbers later. Order of arrival decides only who waits; the review decides who owns, on a bank document.
33. **Hiding a destination conflict from the newcomer for longer.** The membership test is stated as a residue and not removed. Keeping a conflicted destination looking active until a first payment would leave a true owner unpaid for weeks without a word.
34. **Payment history behind a signature.** History, statements and the balance on the status page stay readable by a session, and the exposure is stated: amounts and statuses of one account per forged or lifted session, no bank digit, no name, no tax data. The tax form on file and the destination's last four digits sit behind a signed read. A signature for every look at a balance was judged worse for payees than the exposure it removes.
35. **A roster the Worker cannot write.** No such roster exists: accruals, payments and the export all come from the Worker. The document says so, keeps the earlier months' rosters offline where a later Worker cannot rewrite them, and makes McKale's confirmation of each new recipient and each jump a condition of the month lock. Deriving expected payees offline from Stripe was not adopted, because who earned what is decided by scores that only the Worker holds.
36. **The remainder of the day pool.** It is parked and folded monthly, and public pool figures are rounded. Spreading the fold over the following month was considered and not built: the pool's trend over a month of quiet nights shows the same coarse figure either way.
37. **The run-anomaly cause.** Both proposed exits are in: the acknowledgement, whose clock runs under the halt, and the end of the run with its payments back in the lots.
38. **The reversing part of a failed payout.** The `failing` payout stays in the transit identity with its own part and its own swept cents, the first of the two forms proposed, and one write reverses, moves and supersedes. A carrier does not compose while a payout it carries is `failing`, which keeps the identity exact without a correction term.
39. **The two step 3 tests that touch later steps.** They stay in step 3 and call step 3's own entry points on seeded payout stamps; steps 4 and 5 call the same functions. Moving the tests would have left step 3's funding rule without a test that can fail.
40. **Where the session overlap starts.** A deploy variable holds the cutover time, and an old-key token is judged by its own `iat`. A KV key written at the first new-key signature was not adopted: KV holds no authority here, and a deleted key would have reopened the overlap.
41. **`handlePayoutRate`.** It has a module of its own, `payout_rate.mjs`, owned by step 3 and then by step 7, never by two steps at once.
42. **When the IRIS code is needed.** The application is tied to the first filing season, not to a date in November 2026: that date is the last day of the FIRE system, which Eustress never used.
43. **A fee that arrives after `wirefee-none`.** The finding proposed that Eustress bear it. Locked decision 5 has the recipient bear the wire fee, so the credit is undone as far as the payee's payable allows (`wirefee-late`), and Eustress bears only what the payable cannot cover. The clawback kind `wire_fee`, which had no posting, is gone either way.
44. **Letting the settlement hold lapse at the end of the watch window.** A hold that ends when the window ends holds nothing, since no payment settles sooner. The hold is kept for a credit that names a payment and dropped for one that names none, and the reopening of a settled payment covers a return proven later.
45. **The 24 hour duplicate refusal as its own trigger.** The relay cannot prove which bare 400 is the duplicate refusal, so every Mercury 400 that is not a provable schema error ends automatic resends. The effect asked for is the same and no answer is added to the closed set.
46. **`destination/pending` behind a signature.** It is named as the second stated exception instead: the digits must be seen before they can be signed, the invite was opened by a signature, and it lives 60 minutes.
47. **Where the creation record lives, and seeding legacy subscriptions.** One record per session in the Treasury, as the second of the two findings proposed. Legacy subscriptions are not listed and seeded: Stripe's own `created` stamp against `CHECKOUT_RECORDS_SINCE` judges them for as long as they live, which needs no pass and cannot be forged. The plan record is seeded by `plan_apply`, at first read and in the backfill.
48. **When the book opens.** Of the two sources offered for the opening figures, the document takes the Worker's own quiescent read, so the cutover runs at step 4 and steps 2 and 3 serve production through the pre-journal path. A balance transaction created before the instant posts 3100 in the place of 1010; no finding asked for that, and the lag of a webhook behind its charge requires it.
49. **A halt on an unexpected debit from Engine Expenses or Checking.** Not adopted: a halt stops payee sends and sweeps, and nothing on those two accounts. The check alerts with the ids to cancel and books the debit to Suspense.
50. **The offline journal replay.** One finding placed it in step 4 and one in step 5. It is in step 4, with the relay's witness, which that step deploys, because the month lock of step 4 needs it.
51. **The relay's second clock check.** Offered as optional and adopted: it is what keeps a stalled request from landing after an administrator has read Mercury and voided.
52. **The billing portal behind the account key.** Adopted as proposed, with the cancellation question put to counsel (section 19, item 33), since the same step stands between a subscriber and a cancel.
53. **A W-8BEN with no TIN on a US-source line.** The finding's rule is kept: that line pays nothing until an envelope passes. The accountant is asked whether that is stricter than the law requires (section 18, item 11).
54. **The main site's whole policy.** Only `script-src` and `connect-src` are enforced there, as the finding proposed. The style and image rules stay report-only while the site uses inline styles.
55. **Withholding on an investor return posted to 2040 or 2041, with its claim funded from carry.** One finding asked for the posting to follow the kind `withholdingFor` returns and left the carry funding in place. The posting follows the kind, in another place: return withholding has left Bliss Treasury for two payables on the operating side (2045 and 2046), as a second finding proposed, because 2040 and 2041 are whole buckets of the pool's conservation identity and one account cannot carry two identities. The carry funding, its bound at planning and the `source: return` tag are gone with it.
56. **Deriving `funded` and `released` from the payout's state.** Two findings bounded the landing write. The reversal pass is taken. Keeping pending and unfunded sums on `po:` and deriving the flags is not: the flags on each `rev:` record are the evidence R13 recomputes the counter from, and a deferred deposit is funded apart from the rest of its payout.
57. **A mail for each new recipient.** Adopted as one blind-copied notice a month from McKale's own mailbox, sent at the close he already performs, and not as a message per enrollment. Locked decision 6 keeps routine taps out of the payout path, and the notice conditions the month lock, never a payment. The residue is stated in 7.7: a mule with a paid plan of its own is bounded by the caps alone.
58. **Exports.** Of the two forms offered, the exports are undelayed, signed, announced and counted. A single-use armed download is not built: it adds a state for no gain, since a bundle is ciphertext to everything but the offline machine.
59. **The tax step's prefill.** Dropped, the first of the two remedies. A signed challenge ahead of the interview is not built: typing a name the server compares costs the payee one line and leaves no route that returns a legal name.
60. **A floor on what a plan invoice must charge.** Not built. The key holds no write on customers, so no credit balance exists that Stripe's own proration did not create. The floor is recorded in section 20 as the fallback if Checkout cannot create the customer without that permission.
61. **A vendor paid by transfer.** The finding's rule (no expense rule ever explains a sent payment) left such a vendor with an alert on every invoice. A quiet path is added: the payment is planned first (`expenses/plan`, an `xo:` record), or it alerts once and is classified by a signed act for that one row. Nothing covers it in bulk.
62. **Withholding on a stray payment.** The finding's posting is taken: the payee's payable against Suspense, the remainder to 1210, the year totals in the same put. One thing is added: where the payee is under withholding, the payable consumed is the gross whose net is the cash, so a duplicate does not pay out untaxed what the original was withheld on.
63. **Findings raised twice.** The backup that could not restore the sub-ledger, the anchor that ran ahead of the backup, the `withholding_settled` tag and the unbounded landing write were each raised by two reviewers, and each is one change.
64. **A return posted in a later year**, raised by two findings, is one change: a return always reduces the totals of its payment's debit year, and R12 subtracts `netted_cents`. The reversal of 2.2 is kept for changes of amount only.
65. **The monthly ACH cadence.** Kept, the first of the two remedies offered: the relay's token kind gains the month, so every value of `ACH_CADENCE` has a token that fits.
66. **The approval window of a wire.** Narrowed, not closed, by the second form offered: a 30 minute manifest and a re-check every 5 minutes while it is live, with the manifest voided on a change. Only Mercury can close the window, by a request that snapshots bank details (Q7); the residue is stated in 5.7 and 7.3.
67. **Text others wrote on the payments origin.** The text rule is taken. A second origin for the administrator page is not built: the rule covers it, and a third origin would bring its own policy, bundle and tripwire read.
68. **The legacy pool figure.** Computed inside the refusal, and `LEGACY_POOL_CENTS` is removed, not kept as an acknowledgement: a typed figure would have to equal a number that changes until the refusal starts.
69. **The billing portal configuration.** McKale turns customer information updates off in it, and nothing relies on that, because a key that writes portal sessions may write configurations. The invoice's fixed email is the defence.
70. **Blocked outside money** moves to an account of its own (2016), not to `blocked_cents`: 2015 is a term of the pool's conservation identity, and an investor's funds or an outside credit were never the pool's.
71. **Tax found under-withheld after payment** is a payable on the operating side (2047, against 1220) and moves into 2041 only as later payments recover it, for the reason of note 55: 2041 is a whole bucket of the pool's identity. On which year's Form 1042-S the recovered tax belongs is put to the accountant (section 18, item 29).
